# RouteIQ - Project Handbook

RouteIQ is the daily dispatch planner of **NMWC** (National Mineral Water Co., Muscat, Oman). Every evening it turns tomorrow's sales orders into truck loading plans and delivery routes: which truck carries which orders, in how many loads, in which stop order and at what times.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Built for** | NMWC dispatchers (planners and supervisors) and the admins who keep the master data (customers, trucks, drivers, products, settings) |
| **Status on 25 Sep 2026** | **Live in production** on Railway since 24 Sep 2026 (PR #25). `main` is at `f2f4099` (PR #30) |
| **Repository** | `github.com/rahmanmansoori244-droid/routeiq` (**private**) |
| **Stack** | pnpm monorepo: `apps/web` (Next.js 14 App Router + Prisma 5 + PostgreSQL), `apps/solver` (Python FastAPI + Google OR-Tools routing and CP-SAT), `packages/shared-types`, `infra/osrm` (self-hosted OSRM road routing), `docs/` |

**How to use this handbook**

- **People:** [section 1](#1-where-everything-is) gives quick answers. [Section 3](#3-daily-dispatch-process-flow) walks through the daily workflow as the code runs it. [Section 5](#5-running-testing-deploying-operating) covers running, testing and deploying.
- **AI code reviewers:** start with [section 7.3](#73-guide-for-an-ai-code-reviewer). It covers where to start reading, how to run the tests, the invariants that must hold, the riskiest code, what is out of scope and what you must not do. [Section 7.4](#74-review-leads-found-while-writing-this-handbook) lists the leads already found. Check them; do not assume they are confirmed.
- **Citations.** Each statement names a repo-relative path, and where useful a function, so you can jump straight to it. The facts come from `main` at `f2f4099`, the git history, the merged pull requests and `docs/`. Facts marked **(per work log)** come from the operator's build notes and are not recorded in the repo.
- **What wins.** Where this handbook, older documents and the code disagree, **the code wins**, then `docs/`. `CLAUDE.md` (the May 2026 SaaS spec) and `OVERNIGHT_REPORT.md` are historical.
- **Conventions.** P1 is the highest priority and P5 the lowest. Times of day are minutes from local midnight in the tenant timezone (default `Asia/Muscat`), so 390 means 06:30. Money is OMR and distances are km. A "plan" is one `RunPlan` row: one depot, one delivery date, one version.
- **No secrets, no customer data.** This handbook names environment variables but never gives their values, and it contains no real customer data. Keep it that way when you edit it.
- **Stabilization release.** The security part (PR1) changed sign-in, sessions, roles, the driver app and several env vars. [`docs/SECURITY.md`](./SECURITY.md) describes the result and the owner runbook; the sections below are updated to match and mark the review items fixed. PR3 (plan lifecycle and concurrency) added database locks for every plan change, copy-forward re-plans that keep the previous plan when the optimization fails, the stale-day guard on the dispatch screen and the solve admission ([2.7](#27-background-job-model), [3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions), [3.14](#314-status-values-and-transitions)). PR4 (review F08, F04, L23) freezes the facts each plan was made with (stop, truck and settings snapshots: a later pin or hours correction is shown as "changed after planning", never applied silently) and adds the dispatch feasibility gate: the optimizer re-checks every timetable it returns, the web re-checks each truck-day, and a truck whose times break a rule cannot be locked, loaded or dispatched until it is re-planned ([3.8](#38-step-6-load-states), [3.10](#310-step-8-exports), [4.11](#411-post-solve-stage)).
- **Audit of 27 Sep 2026.** An external audit (findings F01-F26 and the earlier-open items E1-E5) was verified independently; the consolidated assessment and the owner's decisions are in `.dev/audit-2026-09-27/VERIFIED-ASSESSMENT.md` (local, `.dev/` is not in git). Fixed so far: **A2 "Dispatch screen: never save the wrong thing"** (F06, F07, F13, F16, F17) and **A3 "Intake and master data"** (F02-F05, F20, F24-F26, with the migration `20260930093000_master_data_no_orphans`), both merged in main (their [7.4](#74-review-leads-found-while-writing-this-handbook) blocks come before A1's); **A4 "Recovery, races, readiness"** (audit PR4; F09-F11, F15, F19, F21: a plan left "optimizing" and **Reset stuck plan**, races between password resets, between admins and between a plan read and "Use instead", the `/api/health` readiness gate, the OSRM health check), merged in main (its blocks come before A1's); and **A1 "Quick hardening"**: F01's first part (image endpoint off, no Server Actions enforced in CI), the public source maps, Node 22, and the E2 quick fix for uploads, with its review fixes (a crafted file of a few KB could still block the app for minutes; the upload caps bound one upload's work, but a file near them still blocks the app for 5-11 s until the worker of assessment PR 5). **A1 v2** closed the rest: a crafted old .xls could still make `XLSX.CFB.read` itself use memory that grows with the square of the file size (E2 for .xls), an "ID"-column CSV sent as Excel was wrongly refused, and decision 4's localhost binding was documented but `next dev` still listened on every interface. **A1 v3** closed four more ways a small upload under every cap could still block the app for minutes or run it out of memory (a long number format on many cells, `xl/metadata.xml`, a threaded-comment person list, self-closing typed cells) and a semicolon "ID" CSV still wrongly refused. **A1 v4** closed the files that made SheetJS read one part many times while the caps counted it once (two sheets naming one worksheet part: a 121 KB file took up to 108 s and 3.4 GB, or ran the app out of memory; a comments part or a drawing named again and again; an external link listed many times) and chart sheets, whose chart points SheetJS turns into cells past every cap. **A1 v5** made that replay number the sheets the workbook's relationships do not name as SheetJS does (from the text of `xl/worksheets/sheet.xml`, not its size): a part holding only a byte-order mark made it count the wrong sheet's parts, and a 3.5 KB file of repeated comments blocked the app for 10-22 s ([7.4](#74-review-leads-found-while-writing-this-handbook), A1's six blocks, after A4's; [`docs/SECURITY.md`](./SECURITY.md) section 9). And **A5 "Owner rules: correct locations, every order has a depot"** (the owner's standing rules of 27-28 Sep 2026, answering what A2 and A3 left open): a reading that is not exact is saved only as a pin placed by hand and checked again on the server, an import never stores such a pair as usable, a LOW location nobody confirmed is not planned, no load goes out with a stop at a point that is not usable or was replaced, and every order and order file has a depot (migration `20260930120000_orders_always_have_depot`); imported locations nobody confirmed (HIGH or MEDIUM) are still planned, with a note; a pair a dispatcher types or pastes counts one zero at the end like the import ("Same rule everywhere"); a pin corrected after planning does not block the load, the driver gets the new pin ("Allow, driver gets new pin") (owner, 28 Sep). Branch `audit-5-owner-rules`, with main `2a20cd7` (A4 and A1) merged in ([7.4](#74-review-leads-found-while-writing-this-handbook), A5's blocks). And **P5 "Uploads read in a separate process"** (assessment PR 5: E2 fully fixed, owner decision 16; called P5 because A5 is the owner-rules PR): every uploaded file (order file and late orders, customer import, baseline) is read in its own short-lived process with a heap cap and a time limit that really stop it, at most two at once, so no file can freeze or crash the web process; a file over those limits is refused in plain words and nothing is saved (new optional variables `UPLOAD_WORKER_MAX_HEAP_MB`, `UPLOAD_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `UPLOAD_PARSE_CONCURRENCY`, [2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service)); since its review the rows the parser sends back are at most 128 MB, each text sent once, and since its second review a CSV sent as text has A1's 200-column and 2,500,000-cell caps too, no longer row or more cells reach the web process, and the web process keeps the rows compact (about 20 MB for the widest legal file, under 1 MB for NMWC's), so a small file cannot fill the web process either. Branch `audit-p5-upload-worker`, on main `3451f4d` ([7.4](#74-review-leads-found-while-writing-this-handbook), P5's blocks, the last ones).

## 1. Where everything is

| You are looking for | Where it is |
|---|---|
| **All source code** | GitHub, private repo `rahmanmansoori244-droid/routeiq`, branch `main` (local `main` = `origin/main` = `f2f4099`). Everything needed to build, test and deploy is tracked |
| **Web app** (screens, REST API, background optimize jobs) | `apps/web`: pages in `app/`, API handlers in `app/api/**/route.ts`, server logic in `lib/` ([2.2](#22-the-web-app)) |
| **Planning logic on the web side** (orders → solver request → loads) | `apps/web/lib/dispatch/plan-service.ts` (`buildDispatchRequest`, `applyScenario`, `createNextVersion`, `refreshPlanFacts`), `apps/web/lib/dispatch/start-optimize.ts`, `apps/web/lib/jobs/dispatch-job.ts` |
| **Optimizer logic** | `apps/solver/dispatch_solver.py` (OR-Tools route search, scenarios), `apps/solver/load_repack.py` (CP-SAT load re-assignment, exact timing), `apps/solver/providers.py` (road distances). See [section 4](#4-optimizer-logic); `docs/OPTIMIZER_DESIGN.md` explains it in plain language |
| **Web ↔ solver contract** | `packages/shared-types/src/dispatch.ts` (TypeScript) mirrors `apps/solver/dispatch_models.py` (Python) field for field for `/optimize-dispatch`. The `/route-geometry` models (`GeometryRequest` / `GeometryResponse`) have no TypeScript mirror; `callRouteGeometry` in `apps/web/lib/solver-client.ts` declares its own inline response type |
| **Process flow** (upload → locations → optimize → review → lock/dispatch → exports → late orders) | [Section 3](#3-daily-dispatch-process-flow). The screen code is in `apps/web/app/t/[slug]/dispatch/`; the dispatcher's guide is `docs/DISPATCHER_GUIDE.md` |
| **Database** | PostgreSQL through Prisma: `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma` (27 models, 15 enums) and 20 migrations in `apps/web/prisma/migrations/` ([3.15](#315-data-model-used-by-the-dispatch-flow), [5.5](#55-database-migrations)) |
| **Road routing** | `infra/osrm/` (Docker image with the Oman + UAE map); runbook `docs/OSRM_SETUP.md` |
| **Tests and CI** | `apps/web/tests/`, `apps/solver/tests/`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `osrm.yml` ([5.3](#53-test-suites), [5.4](#54-ci-github-actions)) |
| **Deployment** | Railway project `routeiq`; runbook `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md`. Service settings live in the Railway dashboard, not in the repo ([5.6](#56-deployment-on-railway)) |
| **Environment variable names** | [2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service) is the complete list; `.env.example` has placeholders for the main ones only. The **values** live only in Railway (production) and in git-ignored local `.env` files |
| **Documentation** | `docs/` (current, Sep 2026) and the root `README.md`. See the [Document map](#document-map) |
| **Local only, never in git** (`.gitignore`) | `.dev/` holds a portable Postgres, logs, start scripts, the benchmark harness `.dev/bench` and **real NMWC order data**. It is private: never copy it anywhere. `.env`, `.env.local` and `.env.*.local` hold real configuration values. Build output and dependencies (`node_modules/`, `.next/`, `apps/solver/.venv/`, `__pycache__/`) are also ignored |

### Is all of it on GitHub?

- **Yes: all of the code is on GitHub.** `origin` is `github.com/rahmanmansoori244-droid/routeiq` (private), and everything built so far is merged into **`main`**. Production deploys from `main`. When this handbook was written (25 Sep 2026), `main` was at PR #30 (`f2f4099`) plus this handbook (PR #31).
- **Other branches:** each feature was built on its own branch (for example `nmwc-dispatch-mvp`, `split-deliveries`, `driver-pack`, `optimizer-fewer-trucks`). Those branches are all merged, so treat `main` as the only source of truth. The Dependabot branches are open dependency-update PRs that have not been merged.
- **Tracked:** all source code, migrations, tests, CI, Dockerfiles and docs.
- **Not tracked on purpose:** see the last row of the table above. An online reviewer sees the complete code but no real data and no configuration values. Production values are Railway service variables.

## 2. Repository map & architecture

RouteIQ is a pnpm/Turborepo monorepo with three runtime pieces:

- a **Next.js 14 web app** (`apps/web`), which serves the UI and the REST API and runs the background optimization jobs;
- a **Python FastAPI optimizer** (`apps/solver`), built on Google OR-Tools;
- a **self-hosted OSRM road-routing server** (`infra/osrm`).

All data is stored in PostgreSQL through Prisma. Production runs on Railway ([5.6](#56-deployment-on-railway)).

### 2.1 Repository tree

```
routeiq/
├── apps/
│   ├── web/                    Next.js 14 app: UI pages, REST API, background optimize jobs (TypeScript)
│   └── solver/                 FastAPI optimizer service: OR-Tools dispatch planner (+ legacy PyVRP)
├── packages/
│   └── shared-types/           TypeScript wire types shared by web and solver contract
├── infra/
│   └── osrm/                   Docker image of the OSRM road-routing server (Oman + UAE map baked in)
├── docs/                       Current runbooks and design docs (Sep 2026) - these win over CLAUDE.md
├── .github/                    CI workflows, Dependabot config, PR template
├── CLAUDE.md                   HISTORICAL: original v1.3 multi-tenant SaaS build spec (May 2026)
├── OVERNIGHT_REPORT.md         HISTORICAL: May 2026 build/bug-hunt report from the PyVRP era
├── README.md                   Entry point; links to docs/
├── .env.example                Placeholders for the main web and solver variables (the full list is in section 2.9)
├── docker-compose.yml          Local dev only: Postgres 16 + PostGIS, and the solver container
├── package.json                Root scripts (turbo dev/build/lint/typecheck/test, db:*), engines Node 22.x (A1), pnpm>=9
├── .nvmrc, .node-version       Node 22 (A1): read by CI (setup-node), nvm/fnm and Railpack
├── pnpm-workspace.yaml         Workspaces: apps/*, packages/*
├── pnpm-lock.yaml              Locked JS dependency versions (lockfile v9)
├── turbo.json                  Turborepo task graph (build/dev/lint/typecheck/test; .env is a global input)
├── .npmrc, .prettierrc, .editorconfig, .gitignore
└── .dev/        (git-ignored)  Local only; not on GitHub; contains real data - do not copy
```

> **Historical documents.** `CLAUDE.md` is the May 2026 SaaS specification, and many code comments still cite "CLAUDE.md §N". `README.md` says that wherever `CLAUDE.md` disagrees with the `docs/` files or the code, **the docs and code win**. `OVERNIGHT_REPORT.md` describes the PyVRP build of 12 May 2026. The dispatch planner has since replaced that engine.

### 2.2 The web app

`apps/web` is a Next.js 14 App Router app.

```
apps/web/
├── middleware.ts           Edge middleware: unauthenticated /t/* and /admin -> /login (query string kept); signed-in users bounced off /login,/signup to /
├── instrumentation.ts      Startup hook: loads Sentry config per runtime; logs missing production settings; starts the in-process janitor (Node runtime only)
├── next.config.js          Security headers (HSTS, nosniff, DENY framing, baseline CSP), images.unoptimized (/_next/image off, A1),
│                           instrumentationHook, Sentry wrapper (browser source maps only with an upload, deleted after it, A1)
├── app/                    Pages (React Server Components) + *-client.tsx client components
├── app/api/                64 route files. Most return the { data, error } JSON envelope; the exceptions are
│                           /api/health (readiness) and /api/health/live, the NextAuth handlers, the Excel/PDF exports and
│                           the job-debug download (files), and /api/orders/sample (CSV)
├── lib/                    All server-side logic (tables below)
├── components/             App shell (sidebar, mobile-sidebar, topbar, user-menu, nav-link, page-shell),
│                           maps (plan-map, pin-map, map-picker: MapLibre + OSM), empty states, ui/ (shadcn/Radix primitives)
├── prisma/                 schema.prisma, migrations/, seed + fixture scripts, synth-data/
├── scripts/                (A1) remove-public-source-maps.mjs (last step of `pnpm build`); build-guards.ts + check-build-output.ts:
│                           CI check after the build (no Server Actions, no .map in .next/static; since P5 the upload parser);
│                           (P5) build-upload-parser.mjs (a `pnpm build` step) and upload-parser-build.cjs: esbuild bundle of the
│                           upload parser into .next/upload-parser/parse.cjs; upload-parser-dev.cjs: the same, built on use, for dev and tests
├── tests/                  Vitest: lib/ (pure unit), tenant-isolation.spec.ts (real DB), integration/ (HTTP; web + solver running)
├── types/modules.d.ts      Declares the mapbox-gl CSS module
├── sentry.{client,server,edge}.config.ts   Sentry init per runtime. Only the client and server configs scrub the
│                                           authorization/cookie/x-solver-token/x-janitor-token headers (beforeSend);
│                                           sentry.edge.config.ts has no scrubber
├── sentry.config.example.ts                Template/instructions
└── vitest.config.ts, tailwind.config.ts, postcss.config.js, components.json (shadcn), tsconfig.json, .eslintrc.json
```

**Pages (`app/**/page.tsx`, 30 in total).** Every tenant page goes through `app/t/[slug]/layout.tsx`, which runs `getCurrentTenant(slug)`. A slug that does not match the user's tenant returns 404.

| Route | Files | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `/` | `app/page.tsx` | Sends the user to `/login`, `/admin` (SUPER_ADMIN) or `/t/<slug>`. A session the server no longer accepts goes through `/api/auth/end-session` first, so there is no redirect loop |
| `/login`, `/signup`, `/forgot`, `/reset` | `app/{login,signup,forgot,reset}/*` | Sign in (`callbackUrl` reduced to a same-origin page path, `lib/safe-redirect.ts`), self-service tenant signup (open unless `SIGNUP_MODE=closed`; always a TENANT_ADMIN), password reset (`/forgot` says "Reset by email is not available" when production has no `RESEND_API_KEY`; the admin reset on the Users screen then applies) |
| `/admin` | `app/admin/page.tsx` | Platform page, SUPER_ADMIN only (404 for everyone else) |
| `/driver`, `/driver/manifest` | `app/driver/*` | **Retired** in PR1: a notice that also clears the old sign-in data from the phone. Was the driver phone PWA (legacy "Module C") |
| `/t/[slug]` | `page.tsx`, `kpi-card.tsx`, `trend-chart.tsx` | Dashboard (KPIs from `lib/dashboard.ts`) |
| `/t/[slug]/dispatch` | `dispatch/page.tsx`, `dispatch-client.tsx`, `plan-view.tsx`, `late-order-dialog.tsx`, `location-dialog.tsx`, `customer-dialog.tsx`, `client-api.ts` | **Daily dispatch, the main NMWC workflow** ([section 3](#3-daily-dispatch-process-flow)) |
| `/t/[slug]/dispatch/plan/[id]` | `plan/[id]/page.tsx`, `plan-version-client.tsx` | One plan version (older versions stay viewable) |
| `/t/[slug]/{depots,trucks,drivers,regions,products}` | `*-table.tsx`, `*-form.tsx`, `add-*-button.tsx` | Master data CRUD |
| `/t/[slug]/customers`, `/customers/[id]`, `/customers/import` | `customers-client.tsx`, `customer-editor.tsx`, `import-form.tsx` | Customer master, location editing (the customer page's **Set location** opens the ADD LOCATION dialog, audit PR A5), bulk import. The table's Active / Priority change shows at once and then what the server has (audit F24): refused (the app's 4xx answer) - rolled back with the error; no answer, a server error (5xx, also a gateway 502 / 504) or an answer that is not the app's JSON - reloaded from the server, or rolled back and marked **not confirmed** until the page is reloaded (never "nothing was saved"); never re-sent |
| `/t/[slug]/upload`, `/upload/[batchId]` | `upload-dropzone.tsx`, `upload-tabs.tsx`, `batches-table.tsx`, `orders-table.tsx`, `validation-report.tsx` | "Upload orders" menu: batches and orders list (legacy screen, see [3.17](#317-legacy-and-unused-surfaces-still-in-the-repo)) |
| `/t/[slug]/runs`, `/runs/new`, `/runs/[id]`, `/runs/[id]/live` | `run-detail.tsx`, `routes-tab.tsx`, `map-tab.tsx`, `baseline-tab.tsx`, `scenario-cards.tsx` | "Plan history". `runs/[id]` redirects dispatch plans to `/dispatch/plan/[id]` and still renders **legacy** (pre-Sep 2026) runs; `live` is a "retired" notice (PR1) |
| `/t/[slug]/{audit,users,settings,onboard,help}` | `audit-client.tsx`, `users-client.tsx`, `settings-form.tsx`, `onboard-wizard.tsx` | Admin screens and help. The audit, users, settings and onboard pages redirect users below TENANT_ADMIN to `/t/[slug]` (`canManageMasterData`); help does not. This matches the roles table in [2.10](#210-multi-tenancy-authentication-and-roles) |

There are **no Server Actions**, and until the Next.js upgrade there must be none (owner decision 4 of the 27 Sep 2026 audit, A1): a unit test fails on any `'use server'` in the app's code and CI fails the build when `.next/server/server-reference-manifest.json` lists an action (`scripts/check-build-output.ts`). The unused `experimental.serverActions.bodySizeLimit` setting is gone from `next.config.js` (A1). Server Components read the database directly through `tenantDb`. Client components call `/api/*` with `fetch`; `api()` in `app/t/[slug]/dispatch/client-api.ts` unwraps the `{ data, error }` envelope.

**API route handlers (`app/api/**/route.ts`).** 46 of the 64 route files use `withTenantApi()` from `lib/api.ts`. In this table, "any" means any signed-in tenant role, VIEWER included. The full matrix is checked in: `apps/web/tests/lib/api-role-matrix.spec.ts` fails CI when a handler is added, removed or re-gated without updating it.

| Group | Endpoint | Methods → minimum role | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| Auth / platform | `auth/[...nextauth]` | GET, POST → public | NextAuth handlers (credentials sign-in, session) |
| | `auth/signup` | POST → public (5/min/IP); 404 when `SIGNUP_MODE=closed` | Creates Tenant + TenantConfig + first user, **always TENANT_ADMIN** (PR1: never SUPER_ADMIN) |
| | `auth/end-session` | GET → public | Clears a session cookie the server no longer accepts and redirects to `/login?reason=session`, with `?next=` (the dispatch page) as `callbackUrl`. A session the server still accepts (fresh DB reading) is never signed out: it goes to `/` (PR1) |
| | `auth/forgot`, `auth/reset` | POST → public (5/min/IP) | Password-reset token issue (delivered after the response) and consume (one transaction with the password update) (`lib/password-reset.ts`) |
| | `health` | GET → public | **Readiness** (audit PR4, F15): DB + dispatch readiness (an authenticated `GET /ready` on the solver, never an optimization) + routing + email; 503 `not_ready` when the DB is down or dispatch is misconfigured, 200 `degraded` (`ok: false`) when the solver is only unreachable or answers another error (a 5xx, or a 403 from a proxy in front of it) ([5.8](#58-health-endpoint)) |
| | `health/live` | GET → public | (Audit PR4) **Liveness**: the web process answers; checks nothing else |
| | `cron/janitor` | GET, POST → `X-Janitor-Token` or `Authorization: Bearer` = `JANITOR_TOKEN` (production: never `SOLVER_TOKEN`) | Runs `reapStuckJobs()` and `reapStaleShifts()` |
| | `tenant/config` | GET, PATCH TENANT_ADMIN (GET was any before PR1) | Tenant settings the dispatch planner uses. PATCH (PR5): only the fields sent, strict (the old controls answer 400), bounds of the optimizer's contract, `expect` = the values the page showed (a newer value answers 409 `SETTINGS_CHANGED`) ([3.12](#312-settings-that-change-the-plan)) |
| | `users`, `users/[id]` | GET, POST, PATCH TENANT_ADMIN (GET was any before PR1) | User admin. Refuses to grant SUPER_ADMIN, and (PR1) refuses to change a SUPER_ADMIN unless the caller is one. Inviting an existing email is 409 |
| | `users/[id]/reset-password` | POST TENANT_ADMIN (PR1) | Admin password reset: a new one-time password returned once; old password, open sessions and reset links end; `PASSWORD_RESET_BY_ADMIN` audit row. 403 for a SUPER_ADMIN target (non-super caller), 400 for yourself |
| | `audit` | GET TENANT_ADMIN (no role gate before PR1) | Audit log query; credential hashes redacted from before/after JSON. PR5: filters from the audit catalog, unknown ones 400 `INVALID_FILTER`, Muscat days ([3.13](#313-audit-log)) |
| | `dashboard/kpis` | GET any | Dashboard numbers |
| Master data | `depots`, `trucks`, `drivers`, `regions`, `products` (+ `/[id]`) | GET any, POST/PATCH/DELETE TENANT_ADMIN | CRUD. A truck used on a plan is deactivated instead of deleted. Since the audit PR "Intake and master data": a depot that anything refers to (trucks, regions, plans, orders, order files) is deactivated, never deleted (F03), and a driver is always deactivated, never deleted (F20; the answer names the trucks that keep them as default driver). In a POST or PATCH a field left out stays unchanged and an empty or null optional text or reference clears it (F26: a driver's phone, a region's depot, a depot's address, a truck's description; the customer routes too). Driver rows never carry the PIN hash (`DRIVER_PUBLIC_SELECT`) |
| | `drivers/[id]/pin` | POST → **410** (retired in PR1) | Was: set or rotate a legacy driver-app PIN |
| | `customers`, `customers/[id]` | GET any, POST/PATCH PLANNER, DELETE TENANT_ADMIN | Customer master |
| | `customers/[id]/location`, `locations/parse` | PUT/POST PLANNER | Parse a pasted location (`lib/dispatch/location-input.ts`) and save it |
| | `customers/import` | POST PLANNER (checked in the handler; 401 only without a session, 403 without a tenant) | Bulk customer import |
| Orders | `orders/upload` | POST PLANNER (in handler, 60/h/user) | Check a sales-order Excel/CSV and create an UploadBatch; no orders are written yet |
| | `orders/[batchId]/confirm` | POST PLANNER | Write customers, products and orders (`lib/dispatch/intake-server.ts`) |
| | `orders`, `orders/batches`, `orders/[batchId]` | GET any, DELETE PLANNER | List orders and batches, delete a batch |
| | `orders/sample` | GET any | Sample order file from the tenant's master data (comment says "dev-only") |
| **Dispatch planner** | `dispatch/day` | GET any | Day overview for a depot + date (`lib/dispatch/day-overview.ts`) |
| | `dispatch/plan` | POST PLANNER | Get or create plan v1, and optionally start optimizing (`startDispatchOptimize`) |
| | `dispatch/late-order` | POST PLANNER | Record a late order; the next re-plan plans it |
| | `dispatch/carry-over` | GET any, POST PLANNER (PR9) | Bring forward: GET lists the orders of the 7 days before a day (never after the company's today; today's own marked `ofToday`, a group of their own on screen, unticked) whose cases were not delivered (`carryOverPreview`); POST carries the selected ones to that day - an order of today only when sent with `today: true` (`bringForward`, [3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)) |
| | `runs/[id]/optimize` | POST PLANNER (solve admission, [2.7](#27-background-job-model); the old 30/h/tenant limiter is gone since PR3) | Start a background optimize → 202 + `runJobId` |
| | `runs/[id]/replan` | POST PLANNER | New plan version that keeps frozen loads, then optimizes (`replan()`) |
| | `runs/[id]/reset-stuck` | POST SUPERVISOR (audit PR4, F09, owner decision 17) | **Reset stuck plan**: a version OPTIMIZING whose job has ended or was lost by a restart goes back to FAILED (a lost job is failed "Reset by a supervisor"), audited `PLAN_RESET`; 409 `NOT_STUCK` / `JOB_RUNNING` / `JOB_STARTING` (`resetStuckPlan` in `lib/dispatch/stuck-plan.ts`) |
| | `runs/[id]/status`, `runs/[id]/plan` | GET any | Poll job status; full plan detail (`getPlanDetail`) |
| | `runs/[id]/choose-scenario` | POST PLANNER | Apply MIN_TRUCKS or MIN_DISTANCE instead of RECOMMENDED |
| | `runs/[id]/loads/[loadId]` | PATCH PLANNER (dispatch/complete need SUPERVISOR, checked in `plan-service.ts`) | Load status and driver per load |
| | `runs/[id]/load-geometry` | GET any | Road polylines per load, through the solver's `/route-geometry`: 4 loads at a time, one 20 s deadline, road shapes cached in memory; a load without its shape comes back straight with `estimated` and a `reason`; each row carries its path's fingerprint `pointsKey`, and a load with no located stop away from the depot is `noPath` (`lib/dispatch/load-geometry.ts`) |
| | `runs/[id]/export/excel`, `export/pdf` | GET any | Dispatch workbook / driver sheets for dispatch plans; legacy route sheets for legacy runs |
| | `runs/[id]/jobs/[jobId]/debug` | GET SUPERVISOR (any before PR1) | Raw `requestJson` / `responseJson` / `errorJson` of a RunJob; 404 for a job of another run |
| Legacy runs | `runs`, `runs/[id]` | GET any, POST PLANNER | List runs; "New run" opens the existing plan for that depot and day. `GET runs/[id]` returns job status only, never the solver JSON (PR1) |
| | `runs/[id]/dispatch`, `runs/[id]/unlock` | POST SUPERVISOR | Mark a run dispatched, or unlock it back to READY |
| | `runs/[id]/routes/[assignmentId]` | PATCH/DELETE PLANNER | Manual move / unassign (`lib/route-adjust.ts`) |
| | `runs/[id]/baseline` | GET any, POST PLANNER | Upload the manual baseline to compare against |
| | `runs/[id]/route-geometries` | GET any | Legacy Map tab polylines per truck, through the solver's private `/route-geometry` (PR5, review F22; the web calls no routing service), else straight lines. A dispatch plan answers 409 `USE_LOAD_GEOMETRY` |
| | `runs/[id]/live` | GET → **410** (retired in PR1) | Was: latest driver-app GPS per truck |
| Driver PWA (retired) | `driver/login`, `driver/manifest`, `driver/ping`, `driver/stop`, `driver/shift/end` | **410 Gone** before any auth or DB work (`lib/driver-app.ts`, PR1) | Was: PIN login, manifest, GPS ping, stop done + signature, end shift |

**Server logic (`apps/web/lib`).**

| File | Purpose / key exports |
|---|---|
| `db.ts` | The single `PrismaClient`, shared through `global.__prisma` so the instrumentation bundle reuses the same pool |
| `tenant.ts` | `tenantDb(tenantId)` (Prisma extension that scopes queries to one tenant), `getCurrentTenant(slug)`, `validateSlug()` |
| `auth.ts` | NextAuth config (`handlers`, `auth`, `signIn`, `signOut`), `hashPassword()`, `isSuperAdmin()`. The jwt callback stamps `authTime` and `pwf` at sign-in, enforces the 12 h absolute lifetime (edge too) and, in Node, refreshes role/tenant from the DB |
| `session-principal.ts` | (PR1) `loadPrincipal` (30 s cache), `evaluatePrincipal`, `refreshSessionClaims`, `invalidatePrincipal` / `invalidateTenant`, `passwordFingerprint`, `effectiveRole` (SUPER_ADMIN needs `SUPER_ADMIN_EMAILS` too) |
| `auth-credentials.ts` | (PR1) `verifyCredentials`: soft ip+email / per-IP throttle, `LOGIN_THROTTLED` alert, dummy bcrypt for unknown emails, one generic message |
| `session-redirect.ts`, `safe-redirect.ts`, `signup-policy.ts` | (PR1) `redirectToSignIn` via `/api/auth/end-session`; `safeCallbackUrl`, `endSessionUrl` / `sessionEndedLoginUrl` (the page to come back to after an ended session); `signupMode` (`SIGNUP_MODE`) |
| `client-ip.ts`, `crypto.ts`, `janitor-auth.ts`, `startup-checks.ts`, `temp-password.ts` | (PR1) client IP from the proxy-appended hop (`TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`, `CLIENT_IP_HEADER`; one-time warning when it is internal or unresolved), the only reader of forwarding headers; `constantTimeEqual`; the janitor token rule; startup `[config]` warnings (audit PR4: an error when `SOLVER_URL` or `SOLVER_TOKEN` is missing); the one-time password for invites and admin resets |
| `health.ts` | (Audit PR4, F15) readiness for `/api/health`: `checkDispatchReadiness()` (config present, read by `solverEnv()` exactly as the optimize call reads it, a URL every call can use (`solverUrlUsable()`), a token every call can send (`tokenCanBeSent()`), then the solver's authenticated `GET /ready` with a 4 s timeout for the whole answer, body included, and without following a redirect: `ready`, `misconfigured` or `degraded`, with a reason code), `overallReadiness()` (503 only for a definite fault) |
| `driver-app.ts`, `driver-fields.ts` | (PR1) `driverAppGone()` (410 for the retired driver app); `DRIVER_PUBLIC_SELECT` |
| `master-data-delete.ts`, `customer-inline-update.ts` | (Audit PR "Intake and master data") What "Delete" does to a depot (`DEPOT_REF_COUNT`, `depotDeleteOutcome`, the dialog's words) and the driver deactivation warning (F03 / F20); the Customers table's inline Active / Priority change that always ends in the server's state (`runInlineUpdate`, F24) |
| `api.ts` | `withTenantApi()` wrapper (auth, tenant, role, rate limit, error mapping), `ok`/`fail`, `parseBody`, `hasRole`, `notFoundIfNull`, `HttpError` (PR1: an expected failure with its status) |
| `rbac.ts` | `canManageMasterData`, `canPlan`, `canApproveOverride`, `canView`, `requireRole` (used by pages) |
| `driver-auth.ts` | Retired driver app (PR1): PIN login, `requireDriverShift()`, `endShift()`, `generatePin()`. Nothing calls it; kept until the driver-app tables are dropped |
| `password-reset.ts` | Reset tokens: 32 bytes, SHA-256 hash stored, 24 h TTL, 3 per hour, only the newest link works; `resetPasswordWithToken()` (one transaction); (audit PR4, F10) `lockUserCredentials()`: issuing, using and an admin reset lock the user row first, and a link is consumed only while unused and unexpired (checked in the `DELETE`); `deliverResetEmail()` sends through Resend and never logs the link in production |
| `rate-limit.ts` | In-memory fixed-window `RateLimiter` (swept, capped at 20,000 keys) + `LIMITS` (auth 5/min, uploads 60/h, optimize 30/h, sign-in throttles). `RATE_LIMITS_DISABLED` is ignored on Railway ([5.10](#510-operating-runbook-pointers)) |
| `solver-client.ts` | HTTP client to the solver: `callDispatchSolver()`, `callRouteGeometry()`, `postJsonLong()` (the legacy `callSolver()` was removed in PR5). Reads `SOLVER_URL` / `SOLVER_TOKEN` through `solverEnv()` (review of audit PR4) |
| `solver-env.ts` | (Review of audit PR4) `solverEnv()`: reads `SOLVER_URL` (spaces around it and trailing slashes dropped) and `SOLVER_TOKEN` (spaces and line breaks around it dropped; empty = not set) ONE way for the optimize and road-line calls, the readiness check (`health.ts`) and the startup log, so `/api/health` checks exactly the URL and token an optimization uses. (Third review of audit PR4) `tokenCanBeSent()`: only a plain ASCII token (letters, digits, punctuation, a space or a tab inside it) is sent the same way by `fetch` and `node:http`; any other is refused by all of them before anything is sent. (Fourth review of audit PR4) `solverUrlUsable()`: the URL must be plain ASCII without spaces, read by the URL parser as `http:` or `https:`, without a user name or password and without `?` or `#`; any other is refused the same way |
| `http-error.ts` | (PR5) `HttpError`: an expected failure with its status; `PlanError`, `RouteAdjustError` and `BatchRaceError` extend it, so `handleError` maps an uncaught one to its status and code (review L16) |
| `audit-catalog.ts` | (PR5) the one catalog of audit actions and entities with labels (`AUDIT_ACTIONS`, `AUDIT_ENTITIES`, `loadStatusAction`), used by the writers, the audit API and the Audit log page (review F23) |
| `planner-bounds.ts`, `settings-fields.ts`, `countries.ts` | (PR5) the setting bounds (a copy of `packages/shared-types/src/planner-bounds.json`, inside the optimizer's contract), the fields Settings edits and `changedFields`, the company country pick-list and what it means for routing (review F21) |
| `audit.ts` | `audit()` writer (strips credential hashes, `redactForAudit`) and the `AuditAction` union |
| `schemas.ts` | Zod request schemas |
| `csv.ts`, `workbook-guard.ts` | Upload parsing limits and allowed MIME types: 10 MB, 50,000 rows on the sheet that is read, at most `READ_ROWS` (50,100) rows read per sheet or CSV; a workbook is measured by unpacking it (`checkWorkbookZip`: at most 50 MB unpacked and 1,000 parts) and may have at most 10 sheets, both checked before any sheet is read (A1, audit E2). A1 review: `guardSpreadsheet` lets SheetJS read only an .xlsx, an old .xls or CSV text (it picks its reader from the first bytes), refuses more than 2,500,000 cells with a value, 10,000 comments or links over 200,000 cells before SheetJS reads the file, checks each zip part's two names, hides binary parts; `parseExcelSheets` reads without formulas and checks each sheet's range (200 columns, 2,500,000 cells over all sheets) before any sheet is turned into rows; since the P5 second review a CSV sent as text (Papa Parse) has the same two caps (`parseCsv`: its header before Papa makes a row object; `checkCsvCells`: each line's values, and all values with the header's, after the row limit; the two caps now live in the dependency-free `upload-limits.ts`). A1 v2: an old .xls that is a compound file has its structure checked in linear time first (`checkCompoundFile`), so `XLSX.CFB.read` cannot be made to use time and memory that grow with the square of the file size; a CSV whose first header is "ID" is read as CSV, not refused as SYLK. A1 v3: a file that begins with "ID" is judged by replaying SheetJS's SYLK reader over the whole file (`idFileReader`: refused when it reads as SYLK or the reader would do more than 100,000 steps before it gives up); a cell is counted as SheetJS makes one (a type or a value, also on a tag that closes itself); at most 1,000 metadata entries of each kind and 1,000 comment authors; SheetJS reads with `cellText: false` (no display text; since A5 was merged with A1, the customer import alone keeps a reference to the number format or, for CSV sent as Excel, the text of its lat / lng cells, and works out the decimals they show on the sheet it reads only, in the one column per name the rows keep, trying each format once per upload (at most 20, each at most 64 characters): `shownSources`, `ShownFormats`, A5 fourth review; a CSV sent as Excel whose first header is "ID" keeps its text too, unless SheetJS's SYLK reader would format a value in it first, `idFileFormatsCells`, A5 fifth review). A1 v4: `sheetReads` replays which parts SheetJS reads for each sheet (its relationships, comments, drawing of notes; external links as often as they are listed), a part read again counts again against every cap, two sheets that read one worksheet part are refused as damaged, a chart, dialog or macro sheet is refused, and `parseExcelSheets` checks that SheetJS reads the sheet list the guard replayed (`sameSheetList`). A1 v5: the replay takes SheetJS's "Numbers iOS" numbering (`xl/worksheets/sheet.xml`, `sheet1.xml`, ...) and its `xl/workbook.xml` fallback only when that part holds text as SheetJS reads it, as SheetJS does. `parseUpload` itself has no timeout: it is synchronous (the old 10 s timer could never fire). Since P5 only the upload parser process runs it for an upload (`upload-parse/`, next row); a file near the caps took the web process 5-11 s (A1's measure) and more on a busy PC |
| `upload-parse/` (`index.ts`, `config.ts`, `protocol.ts`, `handler.ts`, `child.ts`), `upload-errors.ts`, `upload-limits.ts`, `dispatch/order-headers.ts` | (P5, E2's proper fix) `parseUploadIsolated(file, spec)`: what the three upload routes call instead of `parseUpload`. The file's size and type are checked first (`fileRefusal`, the same messages), then it is read by the unchanged `parseUpload` in a fresh Node process (`child_process.fork` of `.next/upload-parser/parse.cjs`, or of `scripts/upload-parser-dev.cjs` outside production) with `--max-old-space-size=UPLOAD_WORKER_MAX_HEAP_MB`, no secrets in its environment and none of the web's Node flags; it is killed (SIGKILL) after `UPLOAD_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS`. The rows come back in pieces of at most 25,000 cells and about 4 MB, each turned into objects as it arrives, and each text is sent once, so the web process holds it once; the whole answer is at most 128 MB (`MAX_RESULT_BYTES`), counted by both processes before it is turned into objects, and a larger one is refused as `UPLOAD_OUT_OF_MEMORY` (`answerMessages`, `ReplyCollector`, `AnswerTooLargeError`; P5 review). So is an answer with a row of more than 200 cells (`MAX_COLS`) or more than 2,500,000 cells in all (`MAX_CELLS`): the parser stops before it sends such a row, and `ReplyCollector` checks both before it builds a row (P5 second review; `too-large` names what was passed: `bytes`, `columns` or `cells`). Errors come back as data and are thrown again (`encodeError` / `decodeError`: the same message; a `MultipleSheetsError` or `WorkbookRefusedError` again, both now in the dependency-free `upload-errors.ts`). `UploadParseRefused` (`UPLOAD_TIMEOUT` and `UPLOAD_OUT_OF_MEMORY` 400, `UPLOAD_CRASHED` and `UPLOAD_UNAVAILABLE` 500, `UPLOAD_BUSY` 503 with Retry-After 5) and `uploadRefusedResponse`. At most `UPLOAD_PARSE_CONCURRENCY` at once per web process (state on `globalThis.__routeiqUploadParses`), a further upload waits up to 5 s (`QUEUE_WAIT_MS`, at most 8 waiting: `MAX_WAITING`). `checkUploadParser()`: the production startup check. The ParseSpec is data (the order sheet test is built in the parser from `orderSheet.extraAliases`), so `isOrderSheet` and the header aliases moved to `dispatch/order-headers.ts` (no dependencies; `order-intake.ts` re-exports them) |
| `dashboard.ts`, `route-adjust.ts`, `maps.ts`, `nav.ts`, `format.ts`, `error-message.ts`, `observability.ts`, `utils.ts` | KPIs; legacy manual route edits; MapLibre/OSM map config; sidebar items per role; unit labels; API error text; Sentry shim; `cn()` |
| **`dispatch/plan-service.ts`** | **Core of the planner** (1,074 lines). The only place that turns orders into a solver request (`buildDispatchRequest`) and a solver response into loads (`persistDispatchResult`, `applyScenario`). It also owns versions (`getOrCreatePlan`, `createNextVersion`, `currentPlan`, `isLegacyPlan`, `pendingLateOrderIds`) and load changes (`updateLoad`, `changeLoadStatus`, `setLoadDriver`) |
| `dispatch/start-optimize.ts` | `startDispatchOptimize()` (guards, RunJob creation, schedule) and `replan()` (new version, keeps frozen loads). Audit PR4 (F09): both first reset a stuck plan (`unstickIfEnded`, which then reads the plan again), and answer 202 only with a job the database has QUEUED or RUNNING; 409 `PLAN_STUCK` comes only from the locked plan row in the start transaction (review of audit PR4) |
| `dispatch/stuck-plan.ts` | (Audit PR4, F09) plans stuck on "optimizing": `stuckPlanState()` (the current job ended, is missing, or was lost by a restart), `repairEndedJobPlan()` (automatic: back to FAILED with `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` reason `STUCK_PLAN`), `resetStuckPlan()` (the supervisor's **Reset stuck plan**, audited `PLAN_RESET`) |
| `dispatch/order-intake.ts` / `intake-server.ts` | Pure file normalizer and resolver / its server side (loads master data, decides LATE, writes on confirm) |
| `dispatch/customer-attrs.ts` | Effective customer attributes (`effectiveAttrs`: confirmed customer value > customer type > stored or tenant default; see [3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details)), blocking issues and `locationBlocksDelivery()` (audit PR A5: the one test of "no usable location", LOW and never confirmed included), `routingProviderFor()`, `parsePriorityWeights()`, `parseServiceArea()`; `service-area.ts` loads a company's area (`tenantServiceArea`) for the location routes |
| `dispatch/load-state.ts` | Load lifecycle PLANNED → LOCKED → LOADING → DISPATCHED → COMPLETED, with the role for each transition |
| `dispatch/split.ts`, `service-time.ts`, `time.ts`, `reconcile.ts`, `summary.ts` | Split deliveries; unloading time; Asia/Muscat time helpers (`zonedDayStart` since PR5); the cases check (uploaded = planned + unserved); plan summary and version diff |
| `dispatch/time-input.ts` | (Audit PR "Intake and master data", F25) times in form fields: an "until" of 1440 (midnight) shows as 00:00 and 00:00 / 24:00 read back as 1440; a field left as loaded keeps its stored minutes (`truckAvailabilityFromForm`) |
| `dispatch/costs.ts`, `planner-config.ts`, `legacy-runs.ts` | (PR5) the stored cost breakdown of a load and its sums (`loadCostFromSolver`, `readLoadCost`, `costTotals`, `truckDayRows`, `costBasisOf`, `kmLabelFor`; review F17 / F18); the one mapping from tenant settings to the optimizer and the effective values panel (`dispatchConfigFromTenant`, `effectivePlannerValues`, `plannerSettingProblems`; F21); `isDispatchPlan` for the legacy run page and routes (F22) |
| `dispatch/day-overview.ts`, `plan-detail.ts` | Data for the day screen; `getPlanDetail()`, shared by the plan screen and the exports. Since audit PR4 (F21) it reads everything in ONE REPEATABLE READ snapshot on the tenant-scoped client (`PLAN_DETAIL_TX`), and reports a stuck plan (`stuck`). Since PR4 the plan detail shows each stop and truck as planned (snapshots) with `masterChanged` notes, and carries the timetable check (`feasibility`, per load `timing`). Since PR9 both mark orders brought forward (`carriedIn`, `carriedOut`, per stop `carriedFrom` / `carriedTo`), and the plan detail gives the company's `today` (so a load of today's "carried over" badge says today on every plan screen, also the plan version page) |
| `dispatch/carry-over.ts`, `carry-view.ts` | (PR9) Bring forward: `carryCandidates` (pure: what was not delivered, per order line), `checkSelection` (the expected state, idempotency), `carryCopyData` (the copy on the later day), `carryOverPreview`, `bringForward` (one transaction under the intake lock). `carry-view.ts` holds the browser-safe words (badges, the driver-sheet line, the question and the toast, the Today group's heading and warning) and the selection (`carrySelected`: earlier days ticked, today's not) |
| `dispatch/snapshots.ts` | (PR4, review F08) `PlanInputs` (what an optimization was computed with, kept in `ScenarioDetails.inputs`), `StopSnapshot` / `TruckSnapshot` (on `RouteAssignment` / `PlanLoad`), `rulesFrom`, `stopMasterChanges` / `truckMasterChanges` ("changed after planning": pin moved more than 50 m, hours, name, address, truck capacity) |
| `dispatch/feasibility.ts`, `feasibility-view.ts` | (PR4, review F04) `checkPlanFeasibility`: the timetable check per truck-day stored in `RunPlan.feasibilityJson` and enforced by `changeStatusTx`; `feasibilityGateMode` (`FEASIBILITY_GATE`). `feasibility-view.ts` holds the browser-safe helpers |
| `dispatch/load-geometry.ts`, `load-path.ts`, `map-caption.ts`, `plan-map-state.ts` | Plan map road shapes: `resolveLoadGeometries()` (bounded concurrency, deadline, the in-memory `roadShapeCache`), `routingOffReason()` and the reasons a load is drawn straight; `loadPath()` / `loadPathKey()`, a load's path and its fingerprint, built the same way by the route and the map; `roadShapesCaption()` / `shouldAutoRetry()`, the caption under the map; `linesToDraw()`, `drawnShapes()`, `answerIsStale()`, `afterFailedFetch()`, `autoRetryDue()` and `createDrawGate()`, the map component's decisions ([3.7](#37-step-5-reviewing-the-plan)) |
| `dispatch/workbook.ts`, `driver-pack.tsx`, `driver-links.ts`, `pdf-text.ts`, `location-input.ts` | NMWC Excel workbook; driver sheets PDF; Google Maps / WhatsApp links; WinAnsi-safe PDF text; pasted-location parser |
| `exports/excel.ts`, `pdf.tsx`, `route-sheet-data.ts` | Route sheets for **legacy** runs only |
| `jobs/optimize-job.ts` | Shared in-flight registry (`trackInflight`, `isOptimizing`, `whenIdle`), `reapStuckJobs()` (audit PR4: each job, its plan and the audit row in one transaction under the plan row lock) and `repairStuckPlans()` (the F09 repair sweep), `STUCK_JOB_MS` (the legacy `scheduleOptimize` / `buildSolverPayload` were removed in PR5) |
| `jobs/dispatch-job.ts` | `scheduleDispatchOptimize()`: calls the solver, saves every scenario, applies RECOMMENDED, records failures (`failJob`, audit PR4: job, plan and `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` in one transaction under the plan row lock) |
| `jobs/janitor-loop.ts`, `jobs/shift-janitor.ts` | 60-second in-process sweep; closes DriverShifts that have been ACTIVE for more than 18 h |

**Database and scripts (`apps/web/prisma`).**

- `schema.prisma` has 27 models and 15 enums:
  - `Tenant`, `TenantConfig`, `User`;
  - `Depot`, `Truck`, `Driver`, `Region`, `Customer`, `CustomerTypeProfile`, `Product`;
  - `UploadBatch`, `Order`, `OrderLine`, `IntakeLineKey`;
  - `RunPlan` (one plan version per depot + date), `PlanLoad`, `ScenarioResult`, `RouteAssignment`, `RunJob`, `UnservedOrder`;
  - `ManualBaseline*`, `DriverShift`, `TruckLocation`, `DeliveryProof`, `AuditLog`, `PasswordResetToken`.
- The 20 migrations are listed in [5.5](#55-database-migrations). The dispatch data model is in [3.15](#315-data-model-used-by-the-dispatch-flow).
- Scripts:
  - `seed-nmwc-dispatch.ts` (`db:seed:dispatch`): demo tenant; `SEED_PASSWORD` sets the demo logins.
  - `nmwc-dispatch-data.ts`: pure generator of fictional demo data (seeded PRNG).
  - `nmwc-dispatch-fixtures.ts` (`fixtures:dispatch`): writes `tests/fixtures/nmwc/*`.
  - `seed-synth.ts`: loads the `synth-data/` CSVs. `check-priority-drops.ts`: diagnostic.
  - `check-saved-readings.ts` (audit PR A5 sixth review): read-only count, per company, of customers confirmed from a pasted reading whose saved text reads as not exact today; since the A5 seventh review only a text that reads back to the saved point is judged, and customers placed on the map or with typed numbers are listed apart; owner-run before the A5 deploy ([5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy) step 13).
  - `grant-platform-admin.ts <email> [--revoke]` (PR1): the only way to grant or revoke platform admin; owner-run ([`docs/SECURITY.md`](./SECURITY.md)).
  - Deleted in PR1: `seed-nmwc.ts` (hard-coded demo password, review L9) and `smoke-driver-flow.ts` (left driver-app residue in the `nmwc` tenant).

**Tests (`apps/web/tests`).**

- `tests/lib/*.spec.ts` (84 files since P5: 81 after the A5 seventh review (main's 76, after audit PR4 and A1 were both merged (74 + A1's `upload-limits.spec.ts` and `build-hardening.spec.ts`), + A5's `orders-have-depot.spec.ts`, `location-rule.spec.ts`, `location-rule-routes.spec.ts`, `migration-test-db-name.spec.ts` and `check-saved-readings.spec.ts`), + P5's `upload-parse-identity.spec.ts`, `upload-parse-process.spec.ts` and `upload-parse-routes.spec.ts`, which start the real parser process): pure unit tests of `lib/dispatch/*`, schemas, password reset, plan continuity, (PR1) sessions, sign-in, redirects, route guards and repo guards, (PR5) costs, settings, the audit catalog and the error contract, (plan map fix) the plan map road shapes, (PR6) the scenario findings, (PR7) the plan options, (audit A2) the dispatch screen's dialogs, reloads and exports, (audit A3) the customer import, inline edits, master-data deletes and cleared fields, (audit PR4) health readiness and stuck plans, and (audit A5) the owner's depot and location rules. `repo-guards.spec.ts` checks both counts in this list against the files on disk.
- `tests/tenant-isolation.spec.ts`: runs `tenantDb` against a real Postgres.
- `tests/integration/*.spec.ts` (30 files since P5: 27 + A5's `location-rule-db.spec.ts` and `orders-have-depot-db.spec.ts` + P5's `upload-isolation.spec.ts`): HTTP tests against a running web app and solver, and specs on the real database that use neither the web server nor the solver (`carry-over`, `dashboard-db`, `dispatch-screen-db`, `location-rule-db`, `master-data-db`, `orders-have-depot-db`, `plan-lifecycle-db`, `recovery-races-db`, `same-day-plan-db`, `service-time-migration`; library level, except that `service-time-migration` runs one migration's SQL).
- Helpers and fixtures: `tests/integration/helpers.ts`, `tests/lib/plan-detail-fixture.ts`, `tests/fixtures/**`.

### 2.3 The solver

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `main.py` | FastAPI app: `GET /health` (public, includes the cached OSRM probe), (audit PR4, F15) `GET /ready` (token-protected dispatch readiness for the web's `/api/health`: the token check and nothing else, never an optimization), `POST /optimize-dispatch`, `POST /route-geometry`, legacy `POST /optimize`. `_check_token()` checks `X-Solver-Token` (constant-time on bytes since PR1, every endpoint but `/health`) |
| `dispatch_models.py` | Pydantic contract for `/optimize-dispatch` and `/route-geometry`. The `/optimize-dispatch` models are mirrored field for field by `packages/shared-types/src/dispatch.ts` (times are minutes from midnight; money is OMR). `GeometryRequest` / `GeometryResponse` have no TypeScript mirror; `callRouteGeometry` in `apps/web/lib/solver-client.ts` declares its own inline type |
| `dispatch_solver.py` | NMWC engine: `optimize_dispatch()` → `_truck_days`, `_prefilter`, `resolve_matrix`, `_window_prefilter`, `auto_time_limit`, `_run_scenarios` (spawned worker processes), `_post_solve`. OR-Tools routing with guided local search; RECOMMENDED / MIN_TRUCKS / MIN_DISTANCE |
| `load_repack.py` | Post-solve stage: `repack()` reassigns whole loads to trucks and departure times with CP-SAT; `time_plan()` does exact timing; `score()`, `build_candidates()` |
| `feasibility.py` | (PR4, review F04) `check_scenario()`: independent re-check of every returned scenario against the request and the road matrix (capacity, hard windows, drive times, unloading time, exact turnaround, shift start / depot hours / availability, shift span, loads per day, frozen-load overlap) → `DispatchScenario.feasibility` |
| `providers.py` | Road matrix: `OSRMProvider` (`/table` in `OSRM_TABLE_TILE` blocks, default 45×45, fetched `OSRM_PARALLEL` at a time under a deadline; `/route` geometry; snap-distance guard), `HaversineProvider` (straight line × multiplier, flagged "estimated"), `resolve_matrix()` fallback, per-cell provenance and `quality` (PR5), `configured_osrm_url()` |
| `costing.py` | (PR5, review F17) the one cost model: `truck_day_costs` - driver paid for the whole truck day, overtime on top, per-load allocation - used by `_build_scenario` and `load_repack.score` |
| `solver.py`, `models.py`, `distance.py` | **Legacy** v1 PyVRP three-scenario solver behind `/optimize`, its models, and its Haversine/Mapbox/OSRM matrices. The web app no longer calls `/optimize` |
| `scripts/bench_dispatch.py`, `scripts/bench_replan.py`, `scripts/bench_day1.py` | Local benchmarks: synthetic dispatch day / re-plans around locked loads, two versions compared (PR5) / legacy day 1 |
| `tests/test_dispatch.py` (81), `tests/test_repack.py` (39), `tests/test_feasibility.py` (20, PR4), `tests/test_costing.py` (30, PR5), `tests/test_admission.py` (3, PR3), `tests/test_security.py` (17, PR1, audit PR4), `tests/test_solver.py` (27, legacy), `tests/conftest.py` | Pytest. Uses Haversine or a mocked OSRM (`httpx.MockTransport`), so no network is needed. A few tests are parametrised; PR #29 reports 104 collected tests, PR4 144, PR5 188 (166 before its review fixes), PR6 191 (194 with its review fixes), PR7 200 on PR6 (on PR5: 192, 193 with its review fixes, 194 with the search-time floor), PR8 on PR5 190 (193 with its review fixes), PR8 rebased onto PR7 206, 213 with the rebase review fixes (per file in [5.3](#53-test-suites); `repo-guards.spec.ts` checks that table against the test files) |

### 2.6 Runtime architecture

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  subgraph Clients
    B["Dispatcher / admin browser<br/>client components"]
    D["Driver phone PWA<br/>/driver - retired, 410"]
  end
  subgraph WEB["apps/web - Next.js 14, exactly ONE replica"]
    MW["middleware.ts<br/>edge: login redirect"]
    RSC["Server Components<br/>app/t/[slug]/**/page.tsx"]
    API["Route handlers<br/>app/api/**/route.ts<br/>withTenantApi"]
    SVC["Domain logic<br/>lib/dispatch/*, lib/exports/*"]
    JOB["In-process job runner<br/>lib/jobs/dispatch-job.ts<br/>globalThis inflight Map"]
    JAN["Janitor every 60 s<br/>lib/jobs/janitor-loop.ts"]
  end
  DB[("PostgreSQL<br/>via Prisma, tenantDb")]
  subgraph SOLVER["apps/solver - FastAPI, private network"]
    EP["main.py<br/>/optimize-dispatch<br/>/route-geometry<br/>/health"]
    WK["Spawned worker processes<br/>OR-Tools routing + CP-SAT repack"]
  end
  OSRM["OSRM - infra/osrm<br/>/table /route /nearest<br/>no auth, private only"]
  HAV["Haversine x multiplier<br/>fallback, labelled Estimated"]
  EXT["Resend email, Sentry,<br/>Mapbox - all optional"]

  B -- "page requests" --> MW --> RSC
  B -- "fetch /api/* JSON, polls every 2.5-3 s" --> API
  D -. "retired: 410 Gone" .-> API
  RSC -- "tenantDb reads" --> DB
  API --> SVC --> DB
  API -- "202 + RunJob row" --> JOB
  JOB -- "POST /optimize-dispatch, X-Solver-Token, up to 600 s" --> EP
  API -- "POST /route-geometry 15 s, GET /health 4 s" --> EP
  EP --> WK
  EP -- "/table blocks 45x45, 30 s x 2 tries" --> OSRM
  EP -. "OSRM_URL unset or failing" .-> HAV
  JOB -- "persist scenarios, apply RECOMMENDED" --> DB
  JAN -- "fail jobs stuck over 15 min, close shifts over 18 h" --> DB
  API -.-> EXT
```

**Request paths.**

1. **Pages.** `middleware.ts` checks the NextAuth JWT (and its 12 h absolute lifetime) at the edge and only redirects unauthenticated users; the database re-check of the user happens in the Node runtime (`lib/session-principal.ts`). The tenant layout `app/t/[slug]/layout.tsx` calls `getCurrentTenant(slug)`. That loads the tenant by slug, checks it against the session, and returns `{ tenant, user, db: tenantDb(tenant.id) }`. Pages then read through `db` directly.
2. **API.** Handlers are wrapped in `withTenantApi(handler, { role, rateLimitKey })` (`lib/api.ts`). In order, the wrapper:
   1. calls `auth()`, which re-checks the user, tenant and password against the DB (30 s cache): no session → 401; no tenant on the session → 403;
   2. applies the optional rate limit, only when `rateLimitKey` is passed (no route passes it today; the limited routes call `rateLimit()` themselves);
   3. checks the role rank → 403;
   4. builds `ctx = { user, db: tenantDb(session.user.tenantId), ip }`;
   5. maps Zod errors to 400 and Prisma P2002 / P2025 / P2003 to 409 / 404 / 400.

   The tenant of an API call always comes from the **session**; no API URL contains a slug. Much of `lib/dispatch/*` and `lib/jobs/*` uses the unscoped `prisma` client and passes `tenantId` explicitly in each `where`.
3. **Web → solver.** `lib/solver-client.ts` reads `SOLVER_URL` and `SOLVER_TOKEN` through `solverEnv()` (`lib/solver-env.ts`: trimmed, no trailing slash; the readiness check of `/api/health` reads them the same way) and sends the header `X-Solver-Token: $SOLVER_TOKEN`.
   - `callDispatchSolver()` uses `postJsonLong()` (`node:http` / `node:https`), not `fetch`. Node's undici `fetch` gives up after 300 s without response headers, and the solver sends nothing until the plan is ready. The timeout is `DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 600_000`.
   - An HTTP 404 from the solver is reported as "optimizer is being updated", because web and solver deploy independently. For other errors, FastAPI's `{ detail }` is passed through (for example 504 `SolveAborted`).
   - (Third review of audit PR4) No call follows a redirect: `node:http` never does, and the road-line call and the readiness check ask with `redirect: 'manual'`, so the token never goes to the address a redirect names. A redirect fails the optimize call with a message that names `SOLVER_URL`, and `/api/health` answers `degraded` `SOLVER_URL_REDIRECTS`. A token that is not plain ASCII is not sent at all ([2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service)), and (fourth review) neither is anything to a `SOLVER_URL` no call can use, such as one without `http://`.
4. **Solver → roads.** `resolve_matrix()` (`apps/solver/providers.py`) takes the OSRM URL from the request's `config.osrm_url` (from `TenantConfig.osrmUrl`) first, then from the `OSRM_URL` env var. If OSRM is not configured or fails, the plan falls back to Haversine distances labelled Estimated; it is never blocked. The web decides the provider before calling (`routingProviderFor()` in `lib/dispatch/customer-attrs.ts`). Details are in [4.5](#45-distance-and-time-matrix).
5. **Inside the solver.** FastAPI runs the synchronous endpoint in its threadpool. `_run_scenarios()` solves RECOMMENDED first, then the two alternatives warm-started from it. Each scenario runs in a `multiprocessing` "spawn" pool, because OR-Tools holds the GIL and would otherwise freeze `/health`. `_post_solve()` then runs the CP-SAT load repack. The whole request must finish within `SOLVER_BUDGET_SEC` (540 s), before the web's 600 s timeout ([4.9](#49-worker-pool-budget-and-deadlines)).

### 2.7 Background job model

Optimization is asynchronous: the API answers **202** and the client polls. The detailed steps are in [3.6](#36-step-4-optimize); failures and the janitor are in [5.9](#59-job-failures-and-the-janitor).

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
  participant UI as Dispatch screen
  participant RH as Route handler
  participant SO as startDispatchOptimize
  participant DB as Postgres
  participant JOB as dispatch-job in web process
  participant SV as Solver
  UI->>RH: POST /api/dispatch/plan or /api/runs/:id/optimize or /replan
  RH->>SO: tenantId, runId, user
  SO->>DB: refuse if superseded, legacy or already applied
  Note over SO,DB: a job already QUEUED or RUNNING, or in the in-flight map, is not refused - the call returns 202 with that job and starts nothing new
  SO->>SO: buildDispatchRequest in plan-service.ts, then solve admission (quota, slot or queue)
  SO->>DB: one transaction - intake lock, plan row lock, checks again, RunJob QUEUED, RunPlan OPTIMIZING, audit OPTIMIZE_STARTED
  SO->>JOB: scheduleDispatchOptimize registers the promise in the inflight map
  RH-->>UI: 202 with runJobId
  JOB->>JOB: wait for a solver slot when queued
  JOB->>DB: RunJob RUNNING 20 percent
  JOB->>SV: POST /optimize-dispatch, waits up to 600 s
  SV-->>JOB: RECOMMENDED, MIN_TRUCKS, MIN_DISTANCE scenarios
  JOB->>DB: transaction - plan row lock, still OPTIMIZING with this job? save master weights, persistDispatchResult, applyScenario RECOMMENDED, RunJob SUCCEEDED, audit OPTIMIZE_SUCCEEDED
  loop every 2.5 to 3 s while OPTIMIZING
    UI->>RH: GET /api/dispatch/day or /api/runs/:id/plan
  end
```

- **One job per plan.** `trackInflight(runId, …)` in `lib/jobs/optimize-job.ts` keeps an in-memory `Map` on `globalThis.__routeiqInflight`, shared with the instrumentation bundle. A second start for the same run returns the active job. Since PR3 the database decides, not this map: the start transaction locks the plan row and answers 202 with the job that won a race. Since audit PR4 (F09) a 202 always names a job the database still has QUEUED or RUNNING: never an ended job, and never "the in-flight map has something" alone (a job whose promise is just finishing after its commit is over; the new start is chained after it).
- **Guarded finalization (PR3, review F07 / ADD-JOB-AUDIT).** The result is saved in one transaction that first locks the plan row and checks that the version is still `OPTIMIZING` with this job as `currentJobId` and the job still `RUNNING`. Otherwise the result is stale (the version was superseded, reaped, or another job took over): the job is marked FAILED "Stale result" and the plan is not touched. `OPTIMIZE_SUCCEEDED` is written in the same transaction, so a saved plan can never be marked FAILED by a later audit error.
- **On failure.** `failJob()` fails only a job that is still QUEUED or RUNNING, and moves the plan to FAILED only while it is `OPTIMIZING` with this job as current (never over READY or SUPERSEDED); it writes an `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` audit row. Since audit PR4 (F09) the job, the plan and the audit row are ONE transaction that first locks the plan row (the order every plan writer uses: plan, then job): if any write fails nothing changes, and the job, still in progress in the database with no live process, is failed with its plan by the janitor 15 minutes after it started. A stale result is recorded the same way (job and audit row together). A re-plan version holds a copy of the previous plan ([3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)), so after a failure it is FAILED but still usable. When the solver finds no plan at all (RECOMMENDED `NO_SOLUTION`) for a version that already holds a plan, that is a failure too and the plan is kept.
- **Solve admission (PR3, review F16).** `lib/dispatch/solve-admission.ts`, shared by every solve entry through `startDispatchOptimize`:
  - **Quotas:** 15 optimization starts per user and 30 per company in any rolling hour (429 `SOLVE_QUOTA_USER` / `SOLVE_QUOTA_TENANT` with `Retry-After`; only starts that created a job count).
  - **Concurrency:** `SOLVER_MAX_CONCURRENT` (default 2) solves at once in total, and per company one less than that, at least 1 (so 1 per company with the default, 2 with `SOLVER_MAX_CONCURRENT=3`). One company can therefore never hold every slot; waiting solves start fewest-running first, then in the order they were queued (fair order, below).
  - **Queue:** a start beyond the caps is not refused. Its job is created and waits (QUEUED, "Waiting: N optimization(s) ahead") until a slot frees. A company may have at most 2 solves waiting; one more answers 429 `SOLVE_QUEUE_TENANT` with `Retry-After` to that company only. The shared queue holds 10 (`maxQueue`): once it is full, a company that already has a solve waiting gets 503 `SOLVER_BUSY` until there is room, but **a company with nothing waiting is always queued**, so other companies filling the queue never lock it out. Only the absolute cap of 200 waiting (`queueHardCap`, process memory) answers 503 to every start that would wait.
  - **Fair order:** a freed slot goes to the waiting solve of the company with the fewest solves running; among those, first come first served by when the solve was queued, whether or not its company ever ran one; so first come first served within a company. A company at its own concurrency cap keeps waiting. The guarantee: a company with nothing running is never overtaken by a solve queued after its own, so it waits at most for solves that were queued before it. It is only that: once the company runs a solve, solves queued later by companies running fewer start first, and at its own cap (1 with the defaults) its next solve waits for its running one to end; a second start while one of its solves waits can get 503 when the shared queue is full (third review of PR3: the docs promised "the next free slot" and "never refused"). `position()` estimates the place with the same rule, assuming the running solves end in the order they started.
  - Review of PR3: with the first defaults (2 per company = every slot, one shared first-come queue) a sign-up company could take both slots and all 10 queue places, and NMWC's OPTIMIZE got 503 or waited about 45 minutes. Second review: five sign-up companies with 2 waiting each still filled the queue of 10, so NMWC, with nothing waiting, got 503 for as long as they kept it full; and a company that had never run a solve won every tie, so a stream of fresh sign-ups went ahead of NMWC's waiting solve.
  - The solver itself refuses more than `MAX_CONCURRENT_DISPATCH` concurrent solves with 503 "solver busy" (defence in depth during a deploy overlap); the job then fails with "busy, optimize again in a minute".
- **Why exactly one replica.** The in-flight map, the rate-limit buckets, the solve admission and the plan map's road-shape cache all live in process memory. `README.md` and the header of `lib/jobs/optimize-job.ts` therefore require **exactly one web replica**. Plan correctness no longer depends on it: every plan mutator takes database locks (PR3, [3.14](#314-status-values-and-transitions)), which also hold across a Railway deploy overlap.
- **Janitor.** `instrumentation.ts` → `startJanitor()` (unless `ROUTEIQ_DISABLE_JANITOR=1`) runs `reapStuckJobs()` and `reapStaleShifts()` 5 s after boot and then every 60 s. It fails jobs orphaned by a redeploy: every push to `main` redeploys web and kills in-flight solves. The same sweep can be triggered through `/api/cron/janitor`. Audit PR4 (F09): each job is reaped in one transaction with its plan and audit row, and the sweep then repairs plans left OPTIMIZING behind an ended job ([5.9](#59-job-failures-and-the-janitor)).
- **Stuck plans (audit PR4, F09).** A version is OPTIMIZING exactly while its current job is QUEUED or RUNNING; every way a job ends moves the plan in the same transaction. A plan found OPTIMIZING behind an ended (or missing) job - left by the old two-write janitor - is put back to FAILED with an `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` row, reason `STUCK_PLAN`, by the janitor sweep (`repairStuckPlans`) and first thing by every OPTIMIZE and RE-PLAN request for it (`repairEndedJobPlan`), which then start real work. (The screens disable OPTIMIZE and Re-plan while a plan is OPTIMIZING, so for a dispatcher the janitor or a supervisor's **Reset stuck plan** does it, and the plan screen says so; review of audit PR4.) A job lost by a restart (QUEUED or RUNNING, not in this process, older than 2 minutes) can be failed at once by a SUPERVISOR with **Reset stuck plan** (`POST /api/runs/:id/reset-stuck`, audited `PLAN_RESET`, `lib/dispatch/stuck-plan.ts`); the plan screen says which case it is (`PlanDetail.stuck`). `getPlanDetail` asks the in-flight map once, before its read's snapshot starts (second review of audit PR4): a job that saves its plan and leaves the map while the plan is being read is still OPTIMIZING / RUNNING in that snapshot, and asked at the end of the read it looked "lost" (the *server restarted* line and **Reset stuck plan** for one reload).

### 2.8 Timeouts, budgets and limits

| Hop / item | Value | Where |
|---|---|---|
| UI polling while optimizing | 3 s (day view), 2.5 s (plan view) | `dispatch-client.tsx`, `plan-view.tsx` |
| Web → solver `/optimize-dispatch` | **600 s** (raw `node:http`, because undici `fetch` gives up after 300 s without headers) | `DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS`, `postJsonLong()` in `lib/solver-client.ts` |
| Web → solver `/route-geometry` / `/ready` (readiness, audit PR4) | 15 s / 4 s (for the whole answer, body included, since the third review of audit PR4) | `GEOMETRY_TIMEOUT_MS`; `READY_TIMEOUT_MS` in `lib/health.ts` |
| Web → solver `/optimize` (legacy, unreachable) | 240 s | `SOLVER_TIMEOUT_MS`, `callSolver()` |
| **Whole solver request** | **540 s** | `SOLVER_BUDGET_SEC` (env override), `dispatch_solver.py` |
| RECOMMENDED search limit (automatic) | 5 s up to 25 stops, 20 s up to 120, then straight lines to 50 s at 150 and 150 s at 200 stops (175 stops: 100 s), 150 s up to 350, 240 s above (PR7: before, 20 s up to 200 stops jumped to 150 s at 201; so 121-200-stop days now get more and no day size gets less, an owner decision after PR7's first schedule had given 201-299-stop days less). The web always sends `time_limit_sec: null`, so `TenantConfig.solverTimeLimitSeconds` has no effect. The Settings page states the same schedule from `searchTimeSec` in `packages/shared-types/src/planner-bounds.json` (PR7 review), which a pytest checks against `auto_time_limit` for every day size | `auto_time_limit()`, `TIME_LIMIT_POINTS`; web copy `SEARCH_TIME_SCHEDULE` / `autoTimeLimitSec` in `lib/planner-bounds.ts` |
| RECOMMENDED fitted to the budget | min(limit, remaining budget − 20 s) | `_run_scenarios()`, `REC_OVERHEAD_SEC = 20` |
| RECOMMENDED backstop | 2 × limit + 60 s, capped at the budget end; when exceeded, `SolveAborted` → HTTP 504 | `REC_GRACE_SEC = 60`, `_await_worker()` |
| Alternatives (MIN_TRUCKS, MIN_DISTANCE) | half the limit (at least 2 s) when warm-started, plus 20 s grace; skipped with a warning if less than 2 s of budget remains | `_run_scenarios()`, `SOLVER_ALT_GRACE_SEC` |
| CP-SAT repack | each solve min(15, max(3, limit / 2)) s; stage deadline rounds × job budget + 20 s, no later than budget end − 2 s | `REPACK_CAP_SEC`, `REPACK_MIN_SEC`, `STAGE_GRACE_SEC`, `_post_solve()` |
| Solver → OSRM | Whole matrix within `MATRIX_BUDGET_SEC` (min(90 s, 20% of the budget); then estimates, labelled). Per attempt connect 2 s, read min(30 s, time left); 2 attempts, 1 s backoff, no retry without time; `OSRM_TABLE_TILE` coordinates per call (default 90; production can use 1000), `OSRM_PARALLEL` calls at once (default 2); snap limit 5 km (PR5, review F19) | `providers.py` (`OSRM_TABLE_TILE`, `OSRM_PARALLEL`, `OSRM_MAX_SNAP_M`), `dispatch_solver.py` (`matrix_budget_sec`) |
| Stops per optimization | at most 600 (422; the web answers 422 `TOO_MANY_STOPS` first); above 350 the optimizer adds a "Large day" warning (the web adds none) | `MAX_STOPS` in `dispatch_models.py` and `MAX_DISPATCH_STOPS` in `lib/planner-bounds.ts` (the 422s); `LARGE_DAY_STOPS` in `dispatch_solver.py` (the warning; the web's copy in `lib/planner-bounds.ts` only words the Settings "Effective planner values" line) |
| Health probes | web → solver `/ready` 4 s for the whole answer (audit PR4; an unreachable solver is `degraded`, not a failure); solver → OSRM 2 s, cached 60 s; Docker OSRM `healthcheck.sh` 4 s | `lib/health.ts`, `routing_status()` in `main.py`, `infra/osrm/healthcheck.sh` |
| Saving the plan | transaction timeout 120 s, `maxWait` 15 s | `lib/jobs/dispatch-job.ts` |
| Screen actions waiting for a plan row (load change, Use instead, re-plan, new plan, optimize start) | row / day lock wait 5 s (`SET LOCAL lock_timeout`), then 409 "Plan is being saved - retry" (`PLAN_BUSY`); load changes run in a 30 s transaction | `lib/dispatch/plan-locks.ts` (PR3) |
| Solve admission | 15 starts per user and 30 per company per rolling hour; `SOLVER_MAX_CONCURRENT` (2) concurrent in total and one less per company (at least 1); at most 2 waiting per company (then 429 for that company); a shared queue of 10 that refuses (503) only a company that already has one waiting; 200 waiting at most (then 503) | `lib/dispatch/solve-admission.ts` (PR3) |
| Solver concurrent solves | `MAX_CONCURRENT_DISPATCH` (2); more answer 503 at once | `apps/solver/main.py` (PR3) |
| Stuck job / janitor interval | 15 min / 60 s; (audit PR4) a plan behind an ended job is repaired at the next sweep (a plan without any job after 1 min, `STUCK_PLAN_GRACE_MS`); a lost job can be reset by a supervisor after 2 min (`JOB_LOST_AFTER_MS`) | `STUCK_JOB_MS`, `janitor-loop.ts`, `lib/dispatch/stuck-plan.ts` |
| Plan detail / export read | one REPEATABLE READ transaction, `maxWait` 5 s, `timeout` 20 s; it takes no locks (audit PR4, F21) | `PLAN_DETAIL_TX` in `lib/dispatch/plan-detail.ts` |
| Stale driver shift | 18 h | `SHIFT_STALE_MS` in `shift-janitor.ts`; `driver-auth.ts` |
| Web session | 8 h idle, **12 h absolute** (PR1); user re-checked at most every 30 s | `lib/auth.ts`, `lib/session-principal.ts` |
| Upload parse (order file, customer import, baseline) | (P5) **In its own process, never on the web's event loop: killed after `UPLOAD_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS` (15 s) or at a heap of `UPLOAD_WORKER_MAX_HEAP_MB` (512 MB), and the file refused (400); at most `UPLOAD_PARSE_CONCURRENCY` (2) at once, then a wait of up to 5 s (8 waiting at most), then 503 with Retry-After 5; the answer it sends back at most 128 MB (`MAX_RESULT_BYTES`, each text sent once; P5 review), a larger one refused (400) the same way as a file that needs too much memory, and so is one with a row of more than 200 cells or more than 2,500,000 cells, checked in the web process before a row is built (P5 second review)** (`lib/upload-parse`). Before P5: no timeout (synchronous on the event loop; the 10 s timer before A1 could never fire). Bounded by the caps as well: 10 MB, 50 MB unpacked, 1,000 parts, 10 sheets, 50,100 rows read per sheet, and since the A1 review 200 columns, 2,500,000 cells (since the P5 second review for a CSV sent as text too), links over 200,000 cells, 10,000 comments, .xlsx / .xls / CSV only; since A1 v3 1,000 metadata entries of each kind and 1,000 comment authors; since A1 v4 a part SheetJS reads again (for another sheet, another spelling of its name, an external link listed again) counts again against every cap, and chart sheets are refused. The caps bound the work, not the time: in the web process (A1) a file just under them blocked every user for 5-11 s and took about 1 GB (measured then: an .xlsx of 50,000 rows x 49 columns 9-10 s, the same as CSV sent as Excel 7-11 s, ten sheets of 50,000 rows 5-6 s; NMWC's shape at the row limit about 2.5 s); P5's before / after figures are in its 7.4 block | `lib/upload-parse/`, `lib/csv.ts`, `lib/workbook-guard.ts` |
| Railway health checks | 300 s | Railway dashboard |

The chain **solver budget 540 s < web wait 600 s < janitor 15 min** is deliberate: a request never outlives its caller, and orphaned jobs are cleaned up. Measured wall times on the maintainer's machine for all three options (`docs/OPTIMIZER_BENCHMARK.md` §8.3): about 31–57 s for the 60–150-stop days (the syn60 days took 31–32 s) and 252–253 s for 300 stops. These were measured before PR7. Since then **121–200-stop days get more** search time (150 stops 50 s instead of 20, 175 stops 100 s, 200 stops 150 s instead of 20), so expect roughly 1.5 × the extra seconds more wall time there (about +45 s at 150 stops; about +3 min at 200, so a 200-stop day now takes about the 4 min a 201-stop day already took). No day size gets less than before (owner decision, PR7 review): 201–350 stops keep 150 s (a 240-stop day like the re-test's S03 still about 229 s), and up to 120 and above 350 stops nothing changed.

### 2.9 Environment variables by service

Names and purpose only. This is the complete list; `.env.example` has placeholders for the main variables only (not `RESEND_FROM`, the `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` and other Sentry variables, `ROUTEIQ_DISABLE_JANITOR`, `SEED_PASSWORD`, the test and script variables, the solver tuning variables or the OSRM image variables). Production values live in Railway. Which local file each process reads is in [5.2](#52-local-development).

**Web (`apps/web`).**

| Variable | Required? | Purpose / read in |
|---|---|---|
| `DATABASE_URL` | yes | Postgres connection (`prisma/schema.prisma` datasource) |
| `NEXTAUTH_SECRET` | yes | JWT signing secret. next-auth reads it implicitly; app code never references it |
| `NEXTAUTH_URL` / `AUTH_URL` | yes (prod) | Public base URL. Reset links use `AUTH_URL`, else `NEXTAUTH_URL`; production refuses to build one without either (`lib/password-reset.ts`) |
| `SOLVER_URL` | yes | Solver base URL (`lib/solver-client.ts`, `lib/health.ts`, both through `lib/solver-env.ts`). Spaces around it and trailing slashes are ignored (`http://solver:8000/` works; review of audit PR4). Missing: `/api/health` answers 503 `SOLVER_URL_MISSING` and a production boot logs a `[config]` error (audit PR4). It must be a full address, `http://<solver private address>:<port>` (or `https://`), typed as plain text: without `http://` (for example Railway's private domain pasted alone, `solver.railway.internal:8000`), with `http//`, a hidden character or a space in it, a user name and password, or a `?` or `#`, no call can use it, so `/api/health` answers 503 `SOLVER_URL_INVALID`, a production boot logs a `[config]` error, and the optimize and road-line calls send nothing (`solverUrlUsable()`; fourth review of audit PR4) |
| `SOLVER_TOKEN` | yes | Shared secret sent as `X-Solver-Token`, without spaces or a line break around it (`lib/solver-env.ts`; review of audit PR4).
