Plain ASCII only (letters, digits, punctuation): a hidden zero-width space, curly quotes or a non-breaking space pasted with it cannot be sent the same way by every call (third review of audit PR4). Local dev only: also the fallback for `JANITOR_TOKEN`. Missing, not plain ASCII, or refused by the solver: `/api/health` answers 503 (`SOLVER_TOKEN_MISSING` / `SOLVER_TOKEN_INVALID` / `SOLVER_TOKEN_REJECTED`), checked with the solver's `GET /ready` (audit PR4); the first two also log a `[config]` error at a production boot |
| `JANITOR_TOKEN` | yes (prod) for the manual janitor route | Auth for `/api/cron/janitor` (`X-Janitor-Token` / Bearer). In production it is the only accepted token (PR1); unset = every call refused. The in-process janitor does not need it |
| `OSRM_URL` | not read by the web since PR5 | The legacy Map tab goes through the solver (review F22); every map and plan uses the **solver's** `OSRM_URL`. A value left on web does nothing |
| `MAPBOX_TOKEN`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_MAPBOX_TOKEN` | optional | Token passed to the map pickers and map pages (map tiles only; no Mapbox routing since PR5) |
| `RESEND_API_KEY`, `RESEND_FROM` | yes (prod) for reset emails | Password-reset email via Resend. Without the key, production sends nothing and logs only the user id (PR1); development logs the link. `/api/health` reports `email` |
| `SUPER_ADMIN_EMAILS` | optional | Comma-separated list. Since PR1 it grants nothing on its own: SUPER_ADMIN is honoured only for a user who also has the role, granted by `prisma/grant-platform-admin.ts`. Sign-up never grants it |
| `SIGNUP_MODE` | optional | `closed` turns public sign-up off (404, notices); anything else = open (the default, owner decision) (`lib/signup-policy.ts`) |
| `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`, `CLIENT_IP_HEADER` | optional | Client IP for rate limits and audit rows: the X-Forwarded-For entry this many places from the right (default 1), or a single-value header the edge sets (`lib/client-ip.ts`). Confirm once against a LOGIN audit row. If the IP cannot be resolved (`0`, or a header the edge does not send) the log warns once, audit rows have no IP and sign-in uses per-account limits only |
| `SENTRY_DSN`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`, `SENTRY_ORG`, `SENTRY_PROJECT`, `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` | optional | Error reporting (`sentry.*.config.ts`, needs the DSN) and source-map upload at build time (`next.config.js`). Browser source maps are made only when `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`, `SENTRY_ORG` and `SENTRY_PROJECT` are all set, and are deleted from the build after the upload (the last step of `pnpm build` removes any `.map` left under `.next/static`), so they are never served (A1) |
| `ROUTEIQ_DISABLE_JANITOR` | optional | `1` turns off the in-process janitor |
| `FEASIBILITY_GATE` | optional (enforce) | (PR4, review F04) `warn` is an **emergency operator switch**: trucks whose times break a planning rule can then be locked, loaded and dispatched, and each such change records the violations in its `LOAD_*` audit row. Anything else, or unset, enforces the gate. A startup warning is logged while it is `warn` (`lib/startup-checks.ts`) |
| `SOLVER_MAX_CONCURRENT` | optional (2) | Solve admission (PR3): how many optimizations run at once over all companies; one company may use one less (at least 1); more wait in a queue (`lib/dispatch/solve-admission.ts`). Keep it at or below the solver's `MAX_CONCURRENT_DISPATCH`, sized to the solver's CPUs. Set both to 3 to let one company run 2 solves at once |
| `UPLOAD_WORKER_MAX_HEAP_MB` | optional (512) | (P5) Heap cap in MB of the process that reads an uploaded file (`--max-old-space-size`, `lib/upload-parse/config.ts`). A file that needs more is refused "needs too much memory"; the process as a whole uses about this plus 100-150 MB. A whole number from 64 to 16384; anything else takes the default with a `[config]` warning at startup |
| `UPLOAD_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS` | optional (15000) | (P5) Time limit of that process, from its start; then it is killed and the file refused "takes too long". 250 to 600000 |
| `UPLOAD_PARSE_CONCURRENCY` | optional (2) | (P5) Such processes at once in one web process; a further upload waits up to 5 s, then gets 503 "RouteIQ is reading other files right now". 1 to 16. The web service's memory must hold its own plus this many times (heap cap + about 150 MB) ([5.6](#56-deployment-on-railway)) |
| `RATE_LIMITS_DISABLED` | tests only | `1` bypasses the in-memory limiter (CI sets it) and the hourly solve quotas (PR3; the concurrency caps stay on); `NODE_ENV=test` bypasses them too. Ignored on Railway, and logged as an error on any other production server outside CI (PR1) |
| `NODE_ENV`, `NEXT_RUNTIME`, `PORT` | set by the platform | Log level and limiter bypass; instrumentation branch; Railway port |
| `SEED_PASSWORD` | seed only | Demo logins in `prisma/seed-nmwc-dispatch.ts` |
| `TEST_BASE_URL`, `TEST_EXPECT_OSRM` | tests only | Integration test target; assert road distances |

**Solver (`apps/solver`).**

| Variable | Required? | Purpose / read in |
|---|---|---|
| `SOLVER_TOKEN` | yes | Must equal the web's value. If unset, every protected endpoint returns 500 (`main.py`), and the web's `/api/health` answers 503 `SOLVER_NOT_CONFIGURED` (audit PR4) |
| `OSRM_URL` | strongly recommended | OSRM base URL for the matrix, geometry and the `/health` routing probe (`providers.py`). Unset means Haversine, labelled Estimated. The legacy `distance.py` has no public default either since PR1 |
| `OSRM_MAX_SNAP_M` | optional (5000) | Beyond this snap distance a point uses estimated legs |
| `OSRM_TABLE_TILE` | optional (90) | Coordinates per OSRM `/table` call (PR5). Raise it up to the OSRM server's `--max-table-size` (1000 in `infra/osrm`) so a normal day needs one call |
| `OSRM_PARALLEL` | optional (2, max 4) | `/table` calls in flight at once when a matrix needs several (PR5) |
| `MATRIX_BUDGET_SEC` | optional (min(90, 20% of the budget)) | Road routing time within the request; after it the matrix is estimated with a "road routing too slow" warning (PR5) |
| `SOLVER_BUDGET_SEC` | optional (540) | Whole-request time budget |
| `SOLVER_ALT_GRACE_SEC` | optional (20) | Worker grace for alternative scenarios |
| `SOLVER_PARALLEL` | **tests and local debugging only** | `0` solves in-process with no worker processes: **no deadline and no time budget at all** (nothing keeps a solve under the web's 600 s), and `/health` blocks while solving. `main.py` logs a warning at startup when it is set, an error on Railway (PR4). Never set it on a deployed solver |
| `MAX_CONCURRENT_DISPATCH` | optional (2) | At most this many `/optimize-dispatch` solves at once; another one is refused at once with 503 "solver busy" (PR3, `main.py`). Size it to the solver's CPUs (each solve uses up to 3 OR-Tools processes) and keep the web's `SOLVER_MAX_CONCURRENT` at or below it |
| `MAPBOX_TOKEN` | legacy only | Mapbox matrix for legacy `/optimize` (`distance.py`) |
| `PORT` | optional (8000) | uvicorn port (Dockerfile `CMD`) |
| `ROUTEIQ_TEST_{HANG,FAIL,KILL}_SCENARIO`, `ROUTEIQ_TEST_{FAIL,HANG}_REPACK` | **tests only** | Fault-injection hooks in `dispatch_solver.py`; must never be set in production |
| `BENCH_TIME_LIMIT` | script only | `scripts/bench_day1.py` |

**OSRM (`infra/osrm`).** Runtime variables: `PORT` (5000), `OSRM_BIND` (`0.0.0.0`; `::` on Railway for IPv6), `MAX_TABLE_SIZE` (1000). Build args: `OSRM_IMAGE`, `PBF_URL`, `BBOX`, `PROFILE`, `MAP_REFRESH` (cache-buster for the monthly refresh).

**CI.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` sets `DATABASE_URL`, `NEXTAUTH_SECRET`, `NEXTAUTH_URL`, `SOLVER_URL`, `SOLVER_TOKEN`, `JANITOR_TOKEN`, `RATE_LIMITS_DISABLED`, `SIGNUP_MODE=open` and a test `SUPER_ADMIN_EMAILS` at job level, with throwaway CI-only values.

### 2.10 Multi-tenancy, authentication and roles

**Tenant isolation: `tenantDb(tenantId)` in `lib/tenant.ts`.**

- **How it works.** It is a Prisma client extension (`prisma.$extends({ query: { $allModels: { $allOperations } } })`). For every model in `TENANT_SCOPED_MODELS`, it merges `tenantId` into:
  - `where` for `find*`, `count`, `aggregate`, `groupBy`, `update*`, `delete*` and `upsert`;
  - `data` for `create`, `createMany` and `upsert`.
- **Scoped models (18):** TenantConfig, Depot, Truck, Driver, Region, Customer, Product, UploadBatch, Order, RunPlan, RunJob, ManualBaseline, AuditLog, DriverShift, TruckLocation, DeliveryProof, PlanLoad, CustomerTypeProfile.
- **Guard.** An empty `tenantId` throws.
- **Not covered by the wrapper:**
  - `Tenant`;
  - `User` and `PasswordResetToken`: both have a `tenantId` column but are filtered by hand (for example `where: { tenantId }` in `app/api/users/route.ts`);
  - child tables without a `tenantId` column: `OrderLine`, `ScenarioResult`, `RouteAssignment`, `ManualBaselineAssignment`, `UnservedOrder`. They are reached through a tenant-checked parent or filtered with `where: { run: { tenantId } }`;
  - raw SQL (`$queryRawUnsafe` / `$executeRawUnsafe`). The janitor uses raw SQL on purpose, across all tenants;
  - code that imports `prisma` directly. That includes most of `lib/dispatch/*`, `lib/jobs/*` and `lib/driver-auth.ts`, which pass `tenantId` by hand.
- **Resolving the tenant.**
  - Pages use `getCurrentTenant(slug)`. A session is required (without one the browser goes through `/api/auth/end-session` to `/login`), the tenant must exist and be active, and the slug must match `session.user.tenantId` unless the user is SUPER_ADMIN. Any mismatch → `notFound()`, i.e. 404 rather than 403, so tenant existence does not leak. A SUPER_ADMIN viewing another tenant writes one `CROSS_TENANT_VIEW` audit row there per hour (PR1).
  - API routes use `withTenantApi`, which takes the tenant from the session.
- **Tests.**
  - `tests/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` covers Depot, Product, Region, Customer, Truck, AuditLog, TenantConfig, `updateMany`/`deleteMany`, and the empty-tenantId guard.
  - `tests/integration/cross-tenant.spec.ts` is the HTTP-level matrix.
  - `lib/tenant.ts` exports `_internal.TENANT_SCOPED_MODELS` "to prevent drift", but no test uses it.

**Authentication.**

- **Dispatchers and admins** (`lib/auth.ts`):
  - NextAuth v5 beta with a single **Credentials** provider. `verifyCredentials` (`lib/auth-credentials.ts`, PR1): Zod checks the email and password; the user is looked up by lowercase email; the password is checked with bcrypt (cost 12 in `hashPassword`), with a throw-away compare for unknown emails; the user and the tenant must be active; soft throttles per IP + email and per IP; one generic error.
  - `session.strategy = 'jwt'`, `maxAge` 8 h (idle), **12 h absolute** (PR1), `trustHost: true`, sign-in page `/login`.
  - The `jwt` callback stores `userId`, `tenantId`, `role`, `pwf` and `authTime` at sign-in. **Since PR1 every later session read in Node re-checks the user** (`lib/session-principal.ts`, 30 s cache): an inactive user or tenant, a changed tenant or a changed password ends the session, and role/tenant come from the DB. The edge middleware checks only the lifetime. The `session` callback copies the claims onto `session.user`. Details: [`docs/SECURITY.md`](./SECURITY.md).
  - The `signIn` event writes a `LOGIN` audit row (skipped for users without a tenant).
  - Handlers are mounted at `app/api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.ts`. The middleware matcher excludes `api/auth` and `api/health`.
- **Signup** (`app/api/auth/signup/route.ts`) is public (owner decision: stays open; `SIGNUP_MODE=closed` turns it off) and rate-limited. In one transaction it creates the Tenant, a TenantConfig (OSRM for Oman/UAE, otherwise HAVERSINE) and the first user, always a TENANT_ADMIN.
- **Password reset:** `lib/password-reset.ts` with `app/api/auth/{forgot,reset}` (email link), and the admin reset `app/api/users/[id]/reset-password` (Users screen, "Reset password": a one-time password shown once; works without email).
- **Drivers** have no User accounts. The legacy driver phone app (PIN login, `DriverShift` session tokens, `lib/driver-auth.ts`) is **retired** since PR1: its routes answer 410 and the migration `20260926090000_retire_driver_app_scrub_secrets` ended the open shifts and cleared the PIN hashes. Drivers work from the PDF sheets and WhatsApp.
- **Service to service.**
  - Solver endpoints other than `/health` require `X-Solver-Token` (`_check_token()` in `apps/solver/main.py`, `hmac.compare_digest` on bytes).
  - `/api/cron/janitor` requires `JANITOR_TOKEN` (outside production `SOLVER_TOKEN` as the fallback), compared with `constantTimeEqual()` (`lib/janitor-auth.ts`).
  - OSRM has no authentication and relies on private networking.

**Roles.** `enum Role` is in `schema.prisma`. The ranks are in `RANK` in `lib/rbac.ts`, duplicated as `ROLE_RANK` in `lib/api.ts`. The check is hierarchical: a role passes when `rank(user) >= rank(required)`.

| Role | Rank | Can do (as enforced in code) |
|---|---|---|
| `SUPER_ADMIN` | 100 | Everything. `/admin` page. Pages may cross tenants (`getCurrentTenant`, audited as `CROSS_TENANT_VIEW`), but API calls still use the session's own tenant. Since PR1 granted only by the owner-run `prisma/grant-platform-admin.ts` **and** honoured only while the email is in `SUPER_ADMIN_EMAILS`; sign-up and the tenant UI cannot grant it, and a TENANT_ADMIN cannot change such an account |
| `TENANT_ADMIN` | 80 | Master data CRUD (depots incl. hours, trucks incl. default driver, cost per load, km per litre, max loads and availability - PR5 -, drivers, regions, products), delete customers, users, tenant settings (PR5: incl. the planner economics - driver cost per hour, overtime, fuel, road factor, preferred-window penalty, cutoff); audit, onboard, users and settings pages and (PR1) their read APIs (`canManageMasterData`; sidebar `ADMIN_ONLY` in `lib/nav.ts`) |
| `SUPERVISOR` | 60 | Everything PLANNER can do, plus: dispatch/complete loads (`lib/dispatch/load-state.ts`), legacy run dispatch/unlock, the job debug JSON, (audit PR4, owner decision 17) **Reset stuck plan** (`canApproveOverride`) |
| `PLANNER` | 50 | Upload/confirm orders; create/edit customers and locations; optimize, re-plan, late orders, choose scenario; lock/unlock/loading of loads and driver per load; legacy route edits and baseline (`canPlan`) |
| `VIEWER` | 10 | Read-only: every GET endpoint without a role option, and the tenant pages |

---

## 3. Daily dispatch process flow

This section follows one NMWC delivery day through the web app, in the order the dispatcher works:

1. upload tomorrow's orders;
2. fix customer locations;
3. OPTIMIZE;
4. review the plan;
5. lock and dispatch loads;
6. hand out driver sheets;
7. handle late orders.

Each step names the page or component, the API route, the library functions and the database tables involved. The dispatcher's own guide to the same screen is `docs/DISPATCHER_GUIDE.md`; how the optimizer decides is in [section 4](#4-optimizer-logic).

### 3.1 Where the flow code lives

| Layer | Path | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| Page | `apps/web/app/t/[slug]/dispatch/page.tsx` | Server page. Resolves the tenant and role (`canPlan`, `canApproveOverride` from `apps/web/lib/rbac.ts`) and renders `DispatchClient`. "Daily dispatch" is the first menu item in `apps/web/lib/nav.ts` |
| Day screen | `apps/web/app/t/[slug]/dispatch/dispatch-client.tsx` | Steps 1 to 3 (upload, resolve issues, optimize) and the card that holds `PlanView` |
| Plan screen | `apps/web/app/t/[slug]/dispatch/plan-view.tsx` | KPIs, reconciliation bar, plan options, truck loads, drivers, exports, unserved list, map, versions. Also used by `apps/web/app/t/[slug]/dispatch/plan/[id]/page.tsx` + `plan-version-client.tsx` (one plan version on its own page) |
| Dialogs | `location-dialog.tsx`, `customer-dialog.tsx`, `late-order-dialog.tsx` (same folder); their pure helpers `location-requests.ts` (which Read / Save answer may reach the dialog, audit F06) and `customer-details.ts` (the Details form: strict times, unloading minutes, only changed fields, audit F07) | Add a location, edit customer details, record a late order |
| Bring forward (PR9) | `carry-over-panel.tsx` (same folder), in Step 1 of the day screen | The orders of the 7 days before not delivered, with why - today's (when the day is later than today) in their own group "Today (27 Sep) - may still leave today", with a warning, unticked - and "Bring forward to <day>" |
| Client helper | `apps/web/app/t/[slug]/dispatch/client-api.ts` | `api()` fetch wrapper for the `{ data, error }` envelope, time formatters, `REASON_TEXT` labels for unserved reason codes |
| API routes | `apps/web/app/api/dispatch/*`, `apps/web/app/api/orders/*`, `apps/web/app/api/runs/[id]/*`, `apps/web/app/api/customers/[id]/*`, `apps/web/app/api/locations/parse` | Thin handlers, almost all wrapped in `withTenantApi` |
| Business logic | `apps/web/lib/dispatch/*` | Intake, locations, customer attributes, plan building, versions, load rules, reconciliation, summary, exports |
| Background job | `apps/web/lib/jobs/dispatch-job.ts`; shared in-flight map and stuck-job janitor in `apps/web/lib/jobs/optimize-job.ts` and `apps/web/lib/jobs/janitor-loop.ts` | Calls the solver and saves the result |
| Solver client | `apps/web/lib/solver-client.ts` | `callDispatchSolver` (POST `SOLVER_URL` + `/optimize-dispatch`) and `callRouteGeometry` (`/route-geometry`) |
| Wire types | `packages/shared-types/src/dispatch.ts` | `DispatchRequest`, `DispatchResponse`, `DispatchScenario` and friends; the Python side is `apps/solver/dispatch_models.py` |
| Schema | `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma` and `apps/web/prisma/migrations/20260924090000_nmwc_dispatch_mvp` onward | See [3.15](#315-data-model-used-by-the-dispatch-flow) |
| Tests | `apps/web/tests/lib/dispatch-*.spec.ts`, `plan-continuity.spec.ts`, `driver-pack.spec.ts`; `apps/web/tests/integration/dispatch-mvp.spec.ts`, `dispatch-split.spec.ts`, `dispatch-timing.spec.ts`, `driver-pack.spec.ts` | Unit tests for the pure functions; integration tests against a running web server and solver |

### 3.2 The day at a glance

| # | Dispatcher action | UI | API | Main functions | Tables written |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Pick delivery date and depot | `DispatchClient` | `GET /api/dispatch/day` | `getDayOverview` | none |
| 2 | "Check file" | Step 1 card | `POST /api/orders/upload` | `parseUploadIsolated` (runs `parseUpload` in the parser process), `validateIntake` (`normalizeOrderRows`, `resolveOrderLines`) | `UploadBatch`, `AuditLog` |
| 3 | "Add N lines to the day" | `ValidationPanel` | `POST /api/orders/:batchId/confirm` | `revalidateIntake`, `confirmIntake` | `Customer` and `Product` stubs, `Order`, `OrderLine`, `IntakeLineKey`, `UploadBatch`, `AuditLog` |
| 4 | ADD LOCATION | `LocationDialog` | `POST /api/locations/parse`, `PUT /api/customers/:id/location` | `resolveLocationInput`, `coordStatus` | `Customer`, `AuditLog` |
| 5 | Details (priority, type, hours) | `CustomerDialog` | `PATCH /api/customers/:id` | none beyond the route | `Customer`, `AuditLog` |
| 6 | OPTIMIZE | Step 3 button | `POST /api/dispatch/plan` (no applied plan yet) or `POST /api/runs/:id/replan` | `getOrCreatePlan`, `startDispatchOptimize`, `buildDispatchRequest`, `applyWeightChanges`, `scheduleDispatchOptimize`, then `persistDispatchResult`, `applyScenario`, `refreshPlanFacts` | `OrderLine.weightKg` / `weightFromMaster` / `Order.totalWeightKg` (lines that take the product's case weight), `RunPlan`, `RunJob`, `ScenarioResult`, `UnservedOrder`, `PlanLoad`, `RouteAssignment`, `Order.status`, `AuditLog` |
| 7 | Review | `PlanView` | `GET /api/runs/:id/plan`, `GET /api/runs/:id/load-geometry`, `GET /api/drivers` | `getPlanDetail` | none |
| 8 | "Use instead" | Plan options table | `POST /api/runs/:id/choose-scenario` | `applyScenario` | same as 6, same version |
| 9 | Driver, Lock, Loading, Dispatch, Completed | Load row | `PATCH /api/runs/:id/loads/:loadId` | `updateLoad`, `checkTransition`, `checkDriverChange` | `PlanLoad`, `Order.status`, `RunPlan.status`, `AuditLog` |
| 10 | Export Excel, Driver sheets, WhatsApp | Plan header and load row | `GET /api/runs/:id/export/excel`, `GET /api/runs/:id/export/pdf` | `buildDispatchWorkbook`, `driverPackModel`, `renderDriverPackPdf`, `whatsappText` | none |
| 11 | Late order, then Re-plan | "Late order", "Re-plan" | `POST /api/dispatch/late-order`, `POST /api/runs/:id/replan` | `replan`, `createNextVersion` | new `RunPlan` version, then as 6 |
| 12 | Bring forward orders not delivered on earlier days (PR9), then OPTIMIZE or Re-plan | Step 1 box "Not delivered on earlier days" | `GET` / `POST /api/dispatch/carry-over` | `carryOverPreview`, `bringForward` | `Order` (the copy, and `carriedToOrderId` / `carriedAt` / `carriedById` on the original), `OrderLine`, `IntakeLineKey`, `AuditLog` |

**Who can do what in this flow** (role ranks in [2.10](#210-multi-tenancy-authentication-and-roles)):

- **Any signed-in user of the tenant:** read the day, the plan, the drivers list and both exports. Those routes have no role gate.
- **PLANNER or above:** upload, confirm, location, customer edit, optimize, re-plan, late order, choose scenario, set a driver, and lock/unlock/loading.
- **SUPERVISOR or above:** DISPATCHED and COMPLETED. The route admits PLANNER; `changeStatusTx` then checks the role returned by `checkTransition`.
- **TENANT_ADMIN:** settings, the audit log page, truck default drivers and adding drivers.

**Tenant isolation.** The child tables `ScenarioResult`, `RouteAssignment`, `UnservedOrder` and `OrderLine` have no `tenantId` column. The dispatch code reaches them through a run or order it has already scoped, or with an explicit `run: { tenantId }` filter.

### 3.3 Step 1: upload and validate the order file

**UI.** The Step 1 card in `dispatch-client.tsx` has a file picker (`.xlsx`, `.xls`, `.csv`), "Check file" (`upload()`), the `ValidationPanel`, then "Add N lines to the day" (`confirmBatch()`). Nothing is saved as orders until confirm.

**Check file: `POST /api/orders/upload`** (`apps/web/app/api/orders/upload/route.ts`). Multipart form fields `file`, `depotId`, `deliveryDate`. PLANNER role, rate limit `LIMITS.ordersUpload` (`apps/web/lib/rate-limit.ts`).

1. `parseUpload` (`apps/web/lib/csv.ts`) reads CSV or XLSX with the file-size, row-count and content-type guards, since P5 in a separate parser process (`parseUploadIsolated`, `apps/web/lib/upload-parse`; a file that takes longer than 15 s or needs more than 512 MB there is refused with 400 "This file takes too long to read ..." / "... needs too much memory ...", and when two files are already being read and a third waited 5 s, 503 "RouteIQ is reading other files right now. Try again in a moment."; nothing is saved in either case). An Excel workbook (PR6, scenario test S04 / B2): the one sheet whose header row has the order columns (customer code, product code and cases, built-in or tenant aliases; `isOrderSheet` in `order-intake.ts`) is read, wherever it is in the workbook, and every other sheet with data is named in a warning (kept in `validationJson.warnings` with the CSV parse warnings since PR6, so the upload page shows them too). A sheet whose rows are all blank cells (a template) does not count, nor (A1 review) one whose first 50,100 rows hold no value while its used range goes on past them (formatting only). **Order rows on two or more sheets are refused**: 400 `MULTIPLE_SHEETS` with `sheets` (name and rows), e.g. "This workbook has order rows on 2 sheets: "Orders" (1,082 rows), "LateOrder" (116 rows). Nothing was read. Upload each sheet as its own file ...". Nothing is saved (no `UploadBatch`). Before PR6 only the first sheet was read and the others dropped without a word. There is no sheet choice on the screen or the API. A workbook with no order sheet reads its first sheet with rows (its check names the missing columns). The customer import and the baseline upload keep reading the first sheet with rows and name the sheets they did not read in a warning (the baseline upload since the PR6 review: its answer, the upload message on the old run page and `ManualBaseline.notes` carry the note; it used to drop it). The 50,000-row limit counts the rows of the sheet that is read, not of the whole workbook (PR6 review): a large sheet that is not read, such as a customer list next to the orders, is only named in the warning; a sheet that is read with more rows is refused with its name ("Too many rows: 50001 on sheet "Orders". Max 50000."). **Before any sheet is read (A1, audit E2)** an .xlsx is measured by unpacking it with a hard cap (`checkWorkbookZip` in `lib/workbook-guard.ts`; the sizes in the zip headers are never trusted): over 50 MB unpacked, over 1,000 parts, a part whose header size is not its real size, a password-protected part, or an archive that is not an Excel workbook (OpenDocument, Numbers, an archive inside) is refused with a plain message; then a workbook with more than 10 sheets is refused ("This workbook has 12 sheets; at most 10 can be read. ...", from the sheet list alone). **A1 review**, also before SheetJS reads the file (`guardSpreadsheet`): a file read as Excel must be an .xlsx, an old .xls or CSV text, because SheetJS picks its reader from the first bytes and several of its readers do far more work than the file's size; a web page or XML file ("This file is a web page or an XML file, not an Excel workbook or CSV. Open it in Excel, save it as .xlsx and upload that."), an OpenDocument, .xlsb ("This is an Excel binary workbook (.xlsb) ..."), SYLK, DIF, Lotus, dBASE, RTF or SocialCalc file is refused. An .xlsx is also refused with more than 2,500,000 cells with a value, 10,000 comments or hyperlinks over more than 200,000 cells in all ("This workbook has links over 17,179,869,184 cells; at most 200,000 can be read. Remove the links ..."), when a part's name in its local header is not its name in the zip directory ("damaged"), and an .xls with such links; a CSV sent as Excel with more than 2,500,000 cells (counted from its separators). Binary parts (.bin) are hidden from SheetJS. Formulas are not read: each formula cell gives its saved value (one without a saved value reads as blank; it read as 0). Before any sheet is turned into rows (`sheetRanges`), each sheet's range is checked: a range that claims more than its cells (a size record, formatting) is cut to its last cell when it is over the caps, which drops only empty trailing columns, and a sheet wider than 200 columns ("Sheet "Orders" has 16,384 columns; at most 200 can be read. Delete the columns you do not need, ...") or sheets that span more than 2,500,000 cells are refused. **Since the P5 second review a CSV sent as text** (Papa Parse, not SheetJS; most browsers send a .csv so) has the same two caps: a header of more than 200 values is refused before Papa makes a row object ("This file has 1,000,000 columns; at most 200 can be read. Delete the columns you do not need (also empty columns after your data) and upload again."), and after the row limit a line of more than 200 values ("Row 3 of this file has 201 columns; ...", the header being row 1) or more than 2,500,000 values with the header's (the words of a CSV sent as Excel). Every value counts as it is in the file, empty ones too, so a CSV saved from Excel with empty columns after the data can be refused where the same text sent as Excel is read (SheetJS drops those columns). Each sheet is read to at most `READ_ROWS` = 50,100 rows (SheetJS `sheetRows`; a CSV with Papa's `preview`). A sheet that goes on past those rows is **never cut short without a word**: when it is the sheet that is read it is refused ("Too many rows: more than 50000 on sheet ...", or, when the rows read hold 50,000 or fewer because of empty rows, "the sheet goes on past row 50,100 ... If the rows below your data are empty, delete them and upload again"); another sheet is named in the warning as "N rows or more". **Since P5 the file is read in its own process, never on the web process's event loop**: that process is killed after `UPLOAD_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS` (15 s) or ends at its heap cap `UPLOAD_WORKER_MAX_HEAP_MB` (512 MB), the file is refused, and the web process keeps answering every other user meanwhile. The answer it sends back is at most 128 MB, each text sent once (P5 review), with no row of more than 200 cells and no more than 2,500,000 cells, checked in the web process before a row is built, which keeps the rows compact there (P5 second review: the widest legal file holds about 20 MB), so a small file cannot fill the web process either ([2.8](#28-timeouts-budgets-and-limits); how it works and what it costs: the P5 blocks in [7.4](#74-review-leads-found-while-writing-this-handbook)). The caps still bound one upload's work: crafted files that blocked the app for minutes or crashed it before A1 are refused or read in milliseconds.
2. `validateIntake` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/intake-server.ts`):
   - loads `TenantConfig` and the depot (`intakeDepot`, audit PR A5: every order file is for one depot, never a guessed one). The depot sent (`depotId`, the dispatch screen always sends the one on screen) must be an active depot of the company, else 422 `DEPOT_NOT_ACTIVE` ("The depot chosen for this file is not active. Choose an active depot on the Daily dispatch screen, then upload the file again."). Without `depotId` (the legacy `/upload` page) the company's **only** active depot is used; with none, 422 `DEPOT_REQUIRED` ("There is no active depot, so these orders cannot be linked to one. Add a depot under Depots, then upload the file again."), with two or more, 422 `DEPOT_REQUIRED` ("This company has more than one depot. Upload the file on the Daily dispatch screen after choosing its depot, so every order is linked to the right depot."). Nothing is saved. Before A5 the first active depot by code was taken silently, and an unknown or inactive depot answered 400 "No active depot. Create a depot first.";
   - runs `normalizeOrderRows` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/order-intake.ts`, pure). It maps the file headers through `HEADER_ALIASES` plus tenant extras from `TenantConfig.orderColumnMapJson`, and parses dates with `TenantConfig.dateOrder` (DMY by default; ISO dates and Excel serials also work). Every row becomes either a line or a row error:
     - required columns: customer code, product code, cases, and a delivery date (or the date picked on screen);
     - row errors: empty customer or product, cases not a whole number above 0, bad date, priority not 1 to 5 or P1 to P5, non-numeric weight, value or margin;
   - reads the tenant's customers and products and the already confirmed lines for the same dates, then calls `resolveOrderLines` (pure):
     - an inactive customer or product is an error, **except** a line already confirmed with the same cases (PR6, scenario test S05: the customer was deactivated after its orders were added). That line is a duplicate like any re-sent line: "Already confirmed: sales order ..., skipped. Customer X is inactive now: its confirmed orders are not planned until it is reactivated." (a product: "its confirmed lines stay on the day"). The same line with other cases is the inactive error plus the "already confirmed with N cases" text;
     - a line equal to a confirmed one (same date, sales order, customer branch and product; `lineDupKey`, sales order trimmed and upper-case) with the **same cases** is a duplicate and is skipped ("Already confirmed: ..."); with **other cases** it is an error ("changing a confirmed line is not supported yet"), since there is no amendment flow. Since PR6 these messages and the inactive errors name the master codes ("S05-C05", "TEST-W330"), not the file's spelling ("s05-c05");
     - the same sales order already confirmed for **another** delivery date (from `IntakeLineKey`) is a warning;
     - an unknown customer or product is not an error; it is listed as new;
     - the same sales-order line twice in one file (same sales order, customer branch, product and date) is added together, with a warning per row. Since the audit PR "Intake and master data" (F02 / F04) the result does not depend on the row order: the line gets the **highest priority** of its rows (P1 wins; a row without a priority never removes another row's), **every distinct note** (joined with ` | `, each once), and a sales value or margin only when **every** row has one (a blank is unknown, never 0). Each row as it was in the file (row, cases, priority, note, value, margin) is kept with the check in `validationJson` (`ResolvedLine.mergedRows`), and when the rows differ in priority, note or money one more warning names them ("Rows 2 and 3 are one line (sales order 901, ...), 15 cases in all. Different priorities (row 2 P5, row 3 P1): P1 is used, the highest. Notes kept: row 2 "routine"; row 3 "URGENT call receiving"."). Before, the first row's priority and note won and the others were lost (a P1 on a second row after a row without priority disappeared completely), and 100 OMR + a blank was saved as a complete 100 OMR. The notes carry no row numbers (drivers read them); the rows are in the check and the warning. Rows without a sales-order number are never added together;
     - customer identity is `customerKey(code, branchKey)`, case-insensitive. Master rows whose codes differ only in letter case ("twins") resolve to one row, always the same one (`preferredCustomer` / `preferredProduct`: active, then with a location / a case weight, then code, then id), with a warning;
     - weights: a file weight of 0 counts as blank (the product's case weight applies); a merged line keeps the cases of its blank-weight rows in `weightMissingCases` (such a line is weighed from the master at confirm, see below); products (existing without weight, or new) with rows that carry no file weight are listed in `issues.productsWithoutWeight`; a file kg per case under half or over twice the product's case weight is a warning (the column must be kg per line);
   - turns rows whose depot column names another depot into errors;
   - decides late per delivery date: late if `isAfterCutoff(now, date, planningCutoffMin, timezone)` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/time.ts`; default cutoff 18:00 the day before), or if `currentPlan(...)` for that date already has a chosen scenario. Each reason is stored as text in `late.reasons`: "Received after the 18:00 cutoff for D." or (PR6, `planExistsReason`) "A plan (version N) already exists for D (2 of its loads are dispatched, 1 is locked or loading)." - the counts only when there are such loads;
   - computes `contentHash`: SHA-256 of `contentFingerprint(lines)`, the normalized lines (delivery date, sales order, customer, product, cases, kg, value, margin, priority, depot) as text, sorted. Row order, column order and the export do not matter; the delivery date does (also when it came from the screen).
3. The route adds an error ("These orders were already confirmed for D (file ...)"; since PR6 D is the file's own dates when every line was skipped, not "this date") when a batch with the same `fileHash` (= `contentHash`, so for the same dates) was already CONFIRMED for this depot, or (`findSameConfirmedFile`) a batch confirmed before the stabilization release, whose `fileHash` was SHA-256 of the raw rows (`legacyRowsHash`, stored as `validationJson.legacyHash`), for one of the same delivery dates. Without that, a no-SO file confirmed before the deploy could be confirmed again after it (review of PR2). It creates an `UploadBatch` with status `PARSED` (has errors) or `VALIDATED` (no errors), stores the full validation result (including every resolved line) in `validationJson`, and writes audit `CREATE UploadBatch`. The `deliveryDate` form field must be a real date (`isRealIsoDate`).

**Confirm: `POST /api/orders/:batchId/confirm`** (`apps/web/app/api/orders/[batchId]/confirm/route.ts`). Body `{ lateReason? }`.

- **Refusals before the transaction:** a batch that is already confirmed, rejected or deleted (409); a batch with `errorRows > 0` (400, code `FILE_HAS_ERRORS` since PR6: "This file has N errors: nothing was added. Fix the rows ... and upload the file again"); a batch validated by an older version (409); late lines without a reason (400, code `LATE_REASON_REQUIRED`). Since PR6 its message names the real reasons (`lateReasonRequiredMessage` in `intake-server.ts`: "These orders are late. A plan (version 1) already exists for 2026-10-07 (2 of its loads are dispatched). Enter the reason for accepting them."); before, it always said "after the planning cutoff", also for a day that only had a plan (scenario test S04).
- **Re-check inside the transaction** (review F05). The transaction first takes `lockIntake` (`pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('intake:' || tenantId, 0))`, the same lock as the late-order route and batch delete), then `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the batch row, then `revalidateIntake` (`intake-server.ts`). Each conflict answers 409 with a `code`, saves nothing and leaves the batch `VALIDATED`:
  - `STALE_VALIDATION`: the batch is older than `VALIDATED_BATCH_MAX_AGE_HOURS` (24): upload the file again. Also (audit F02, `mergedTheOldWay`) a file checked before the audit PR "Intake and master data" with a line added together from several rows: that line kept only its first row's priority and note, so the file must be uploaded again (`MERGED_THE_OLD_WAY` says so);
  - `MASTER_CHANGED` for the depot (audit F03, `batchDepotProblem`): the file's depot was deactivated after the check ("Depot NZW was deactivated after this file was checked. Nothing was added: reactivate the depot under Depots, or upload the file again for another depot.") or is not found. A file that had no depot before audit PR A5 is on the history-only depot since the migration and is refused with "This file was checked before every order file had a depot. Nothing was added: upload the file again on the Daily dispatch screen for an active depot." (never "reactivate"). The depot row (`code`, `active`, `historyOnly`) is locked `FOR SHARE` until the orders are written, so a deactivation at the same moment is waited for;
  - `DUPLICATE_FILE`: another batch with the same `fileHash` and depot is CONFIRMED (or a pre-release batch with the same raw-row hash for one of the dates);
  - `DUPLICATE_LINES`: a line of the file is now confirmed (another file, tab or late order), with the rows;
  - `MASTER_CHANGED`: a customer or product of the file was deleted or deactivated after the check;
  - `LATE_REASON_REQUIRED`: the cutoff passed, or a plan was applied, since the check, and no reason was sent ("These orders became late after the file was checked." and the reasons, PR6). The day screen then shows the late-reason field;
  - `INTAKE_BUSY`: the transaction timed out (Prisma P2028), e.g. waiting for the intake lock behind another large confirm. Nothing was saved; confirm again. Batch delete, the late order and the optimize start answer the same way.
- **Writes.** `confirmIntake`:
  - creates stub `Customer` rows for new customers (`createdFromUpload = true`, `geocodeConfidence = 'MISSING'`, no coordinates) and stub `Product` rows (`createdFromUpload = true`, case weight 0 = unknown). An existing row whose code differs only in case is reused (a deactivated one is `MASTER_CHANGED`);
  - creates one `Order` per customer branch and delivery date, with one `OrderLine` per SKU line (sales order number, product, cases, kg, value, margin, source row, notes). Line kg (`intakeLineWeight` in `weights.ts`): the file kg when every row of the line had one (`weightFromMaster = false`, never changed later); otherwise cases × the product's case weight, or 0 kg (unknown) when it has none, with `weightFromMaster = true`. A merged line whose rows only partly carry a weight is weighed from the master as a whole: a partial kg (e.g. 100 kg for 15 cases of which 5 had no weight) would count as known and too low for good. Lines weighed from the master follow it: a case weight entered or corrected later reaches them at the next optimize ([3.6](#36-step-4-optimize)). Order totals are summed from its lines;
  - writes one `IntakeLineKey` per line with a sales-order number (`createIntakeKeys`). Its unique index makes a second confirm of the same line fail; the route maps that (Prisma P2002) to 409 `DUPLICATE_LINES`;
  - sets the order's `priority` to the lowest number found in the file for that order, else the customer's; `priorityFromFile` records which. `status = 'VALIDATED'`. `isLate`, `lateReason` and `lateRecordedById` are set for late batches (late as re-checked). `salesValue` and `marginValue` are set only when every line carries one (a merged line with a blank on one row carries none, audit F04). Distinct line notes are joined with ` | `, each note once also when a merged line already holds several (`joinNotes`);
  - marks the batch `CONFIRMED`, stores `lateReason` and `isLate`, and writes audit `UPDATE UploadBatch`.

**Delete: `DELETE /api/orders/:batchId`** (`apps/web/app/api/orders/[batchId]/route.ts`, legacy `/upload` page only; review F20). One transaction under `lockIntake` and the batch row lock. Refused with 409 when the batch is already `DELETED`, when a plan for its depot and date(s) is `OPTIMIZING` (`PLAN_OPTIMIZING`), and when any of its orders has a `RouteAssignment`, an `UnservedOrder` row or sits in the scope of a chosen scenario (`BATCH_IN_PLAN`, naming the dates and plan versions). Otherwise the orders (their lines and `IntakeLineKey` rows cascade), the `DELETED` status and the audit row are written together. The 409 says the file cannot be deleted because a plan keeps every order it was made for, and that removing planned orders is not possible in the app yet. A late order only adds orders and a re-plan plans the same orders again, so neither removes a wrongly uploaded file's orders: until the "cancel orders from this file" flow exists (deferred, owner decision on F20), only an administrator can correct them.

The screen then switches to the first delivery date found in the file.

### 3.4 Step 2: customer locations and details

**What blocks optimizing.** `customerIssues` in `apps/web/lib/dispatch/customer-attrs.ts`, using `coordStatus`.

- **Blocking:** `LOCATION_REQUIRED` (no coordinates) and `INVALID_LOCATION` (0,0 or out of range, or outside the service area and never verified: "Saved location is outside Oman/UAE and was never confirmed. Drop the pin on the customer's exact location." - it said "Confirm or correct it on the map", but the dialog never confirms such a point as it is, A5 review - or - owner's location rule, audit PR A5 - a LOW reading never verified: "Saved location is not exact (low confidence). Drop the pin on the customer's exact location."). One predicate, `locationBlocksDelivery`, is used by the day screen, `buildDispatchRequest`, the load changes LOCK / LOADING / DISPATCH (`locationGate`, [3.8](#38-step-6-load-states), A5 second review) and the legacy run dispatch. Before audit PR A5 no writer stored LOW; since the A5 review the customer import marks an unconfirmed saved point LOW when the file points somewhere else (below), also for a customer already on a plan.
- **Notes only:** `LOCATION_UNVERIFIED` (a HIGH or MEDIUM import not confirmed yet: still planned, by owner decision of 28 Sep 2026, [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)), `NEW_CUSTOMER`, `PRIORITY_UNCONFIRMED`, `TYPE_MISSING`, `NO_RECEIVING_WINDOW`.
- **Service area:** `parseServiceArea` uses `TenantConfig.serviceAreaJson` if set, else an Oman + UAE box (`DEFAULT_SERVICE_AREA` in `location-input.ts`) for tenants based there, else no area check.

**Effective attributes** (`effectiveAttrs` in `customer-attrs.ts`). The `CustomerTypeProfile` used is the one for the customer's `customerType`.

- **Priority:** the customer's own value if `priorityConfirmed`, else the type profile's `defaultPriority` if set, else the customer's stored (unconfirmed) priority. There is no tenant default priority.
- **Service minutes** (since PR5, [3.12](#312-settings-that-change-the-plan) "Default service time"): the customer's confirmed value (`serviceTimeConfirmed`), else the type profile's `serviceTimeMin` if set, else the tenant `defaultServiceTimeMin`. An unconfirmed stored `avgServiceTimeMin` is not used; migration `20260928090100_keep_imported_service_times` confirmed the times earlier imports had stored, and the pre-deploy count is in [5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy) check 11. The customer page shows the value in use and its source (`describeServiceTime`).
- **Hard and preferred windows:** no "confirmed" flag applies. The customer's own four window fields are used if any of them is set, else the type profile's four if any of those is set, else there is no window. There is no tenant default window.

**Add location** (`location-dialog.tsx`, map `apps/web/components/pin-map.tsx` on MapLibre):

1. "Read" calls `POST /api/locations/parse` (`apps/web/app/api/locations/parse/route.ts`), which runs `resolveLocationInput` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/location-input.ts`). Nothing is saved. Rate limit 120 per minute per user.
   - **Accepted inputs:** "lat, lng"; degrees-minutes-seconds; Google Maps URLs (the `!3d...!4d...` pin, `q=`, `query=`, `destination=`, `daddr=`, `/search/`, and the end of a `/dir/` directions link when the end is a point; a map centre - `/@lat,lng`, `ll=`, `center=`, `sll=` - is accepted but needs a pin; A5 third review: `ll=` used to be read as exact, and a directions link was read at its first waypoint whose coordinates, which Google fills with the start when the route starts at "Your location" or a dropped pin, so the dispatcher's own position could be saved as the customer's; now only the end of the route is read, a directions link that does not end at a point needs a pin, and its data part is not read); `geo:` URIs; short share links (`maps.app.goo.gl`, `goo.gl/maps`), which the server resolves by following redirects to Google hosts only.
   - Uncertain results come back with `needsPin`: every LOW reading and the MEDIUM ones (map centre only, fewer than 4 decimals, Google's pin included, degrees and minutes only, a pair with fewer than 4 decimals written as degrees, minutes and seconds, swapped latitude and longitude, outside the delivery area). An accepted reading without `needsPin` is always HIGH.
   - **Decimals: one zero at the end counts** (owner decision of 28 Sep 2026, "Same rule everywhere", [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)). Of the zeros at the end of each coordinate only one counts, as in the customer import, with the import's own function (`countedText` in `location-input.ts`, used by `parseLocationInput` and `readImportedPair`): "23.5800, 58.4100" is 3 decimals and needs a pin, with the warning "Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts)." (the zeros made the difference; a pair written with fewer than 4 keeps "Coordinates have fewer than 4 decimals ..."); "23.5850, 58.4105" is 4 and "23.585900, 58.405900" 5, both exact. The same holds for a pair written as text inside a link: `geo:`, `q=`, `query=`, `destination=`, `daddr=`, `/search/`, `/place/<pair>` and the end of a `/dir/` link. Padding reaches those: `/search/` and `q=` carry the text searched for, as it was typed, and other programs write such links with a fixed number of decimals (Java `%f`, JavaScript `toFixed(6)`), so a rough 23.58 comes out "23.580000". Google's own pin (`!3d...!4d...`) needs 4 decimals too, counted as Google wrote them (A5 sixth review): Google repeats a coordinate searched for as the pin, so "23.58, 58.41" (or "23.5800, 58.4100") searched in Google Maps and shared comes back as `!3d23.58!4d58.41`; Google writes no zeros at the end, so its digits are not cut; a real place marker has 6 or 7 decimals. Before, the pin was read with no decimals check. A map centre needs a pin whatever its digits. RouteIQ's own driver links (`driver-links.ts`) never pad a number. Before, ADD LOCATION and `POST /api/customers` counted every zero, so "23.5800, 58.4100" read as exact, HIGH, and was saved as confirmed. The price, as for the import: a real 4-decimal coordinate ending in 00 (about 1 in 100 per axis) needs its pin placed by hand, or more decimals.
   - **Owner's location rule** (27 Sep 2026, audit PR A5: "locations should always be correct ... no item will be delivered without location"). A reading with `needsPin`, or one that could not be read, is never saved as read: Save location stays off, with "This reading is not exact. Drop the pin on the customer's exact location, then save." (or "This could not be read. ..."; for a pair padded with zeros "Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts). Drop the pin on the customer's exact location.", `notExactMessage`, and the preview does not repeat that reason under it), until the dispatcher clicks the map or drags the pin (`PinMap` reports only a click or a drag, so `pinMoved` means placed by hand). Before, Save was the confirmation and stored the reading as read, verified HIGH. The customer's saved pin, which the dialog opens on, is saved again as it is only when it is exact (`savedPointProblem` in `customer-attrs.ts`, the server's own test: verified, or HIGH and inside the company's delivery area; the day overview passes `geocodeConfidence` and `serviceArea`, the customer page its `tenantServiceArea`). Otherwise Save location stays off with the reason: "This saved location is not exact. Drop the pin on the customer's exact location, then save.", "This saved location is outside the delivery area and was never confirmed. ..." or "This saved location has latitude and longitude swapped. ...". The digits of the stored number are never counted (A5 review): "23.5850" is stored as 23.585, so reading the number again as text called about 1 in 5 exact points "not exact". The dialog used to judge the saved pin with no area at all, so a pin outside the area kept Save on and the server then refused it as "not exact". Save sends the address a short link led to at the Read (`resolvedUrl`) with the text it was read from.
   - Degrees-minutes-seconds (audit F17, `parseDms`): minutes and seconds must be below 60, seconds need minutes, latitude at most 90° (N/S) and longitude at most 180° (E/W). Anything else is refused with the reason ("Latitude: minutes must be 0 to 59 (got 99')") instead of being rolled over into another real point (23°99'00"N used to become 24.65°N, high confidence, no pin asked). Whole degrees only (about 110 km) come back LOW with `needsPin`, degrees and minutes without seconds (about 1.8 km) MEDIUM with `needsPin`, like decimals with fewer than 4 places. Seconds that are a pair with 3 decimals or fewer written another way (both coordinates within 0.05" of such a value, `dmsIsRoughPair`; A5 sixth review) come back MEDIUM with `needsPin` and "These degrees, minutes and seconds are 23.58, 58.41 written another way: fewer than 4 decimals ...": Google shows a searched "23.58, 58.41" as 23°34'48.0"N 58°24'36.0"E, which was read HIGH. A real point written to a tenth of a second looks like that about once in 1,300 (whole seconds once in 324) and its pin is placed by hand; one coordinate alone is not enough (a real point does that once in 36). The same rules apply to DMS inside a Google Maps link.
   - One request at a time, bound to the dialog (audit F06, `location-requests.ts`). Each Read and Save carries the dialog generation (bumped when the dialog opens, closes or shows another customer) and the input generation (bumped when the text changes or a pin is dropped by hand). A Read whose dialog or input changed since is aborted and its answer dropped, so customer A's slow short link can no longer land in customer B's dialog. The Enter key goes through the same guard as the Read button (it used to start a second Read while one ran). A Save answer that arrives after the dialog was closed or opened for another customer is reported for its own customer only (a toast, and the day and plan are refreshed) and never closes the dialog on screen or turns its Save into "Confirm & save". The text saved as the location's input is the text the point was read from, never text that was not read (a pin dropped by hand without a Read is stored as "map pin").
   - A point read earlier is out of date once the text in the box is no longer the text it was read from (A2 review, `textUnread` in `location-dialog.tsx`). The preview turns grey, the dialog says "Text changed - press Read", and Save location stays off until a Read of the text on screen comes back (typing the read text back makes the preview current again). Text typed but never read does the same ("Press Read to find this point before saving"): a customer's existing pin is no longer saved under a link nobody read. An empty box with nothing read still saves the pin on the map as it is, when that saved pin is exact (owner's rule above). A pin the dispatcher drops by hand is the point: Save stays on, with the note that it keeps that pin. The `save()` function checks the same rule as the button. Before, Read text A, replace it by text B and Save stored A's point, with the green "Found A" box next to B.
   - "Confirm & save" (after a 422 `OUTSIDE_AREA`) confirms only the point it was asked for: a new point from a Read or a hand pin puts the button back to "Save location", so a new point outside Oman/UAE is asked about again (before, the next Save sent `confirmOutsideArea` for it unasked).
2. "Save location" calls `PUT /api/customers/:id/location` (`apps/web/app/api/customers/[id]/location/route.ts`) with `{ lat, lng, source, input, resolvedUrl?, confirmOutsideArea? }`. The route enforces the owner's rule itself, whatever the screen sends (audit PR A5; the company's area from `tenantServiceArea`, `lib/dispatch/service-area.ts`):
   - **A reading** (`source` not `MAP_PIN`): `checkManualLocation` (`location-input.ts`) reads the text again with the same parser and **no network call**. A short link is read from `resolvedUrl`, the final Google Maps address the Read found (`rereadSavedInput`: only an http(s) Google Maps address that is not itself a short link; else "Press Read again, or drop the pin on the map"). 422 `PIN_REQUIRED` with the plain remedy when the reading needs a pin, cannot be read or is missing (for a pair padded with zeros, with the dialog's words: "Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts). Drop the pin on the customer's exact location."); 422 `LOCATION_MISMATCH` ("The point sent is not where this text points. Press Read again, or drop the pin by hand, then save.") when the point sent is not the point the text reads as, to the 6th decimal (`samePoint`). The source and confidence stored are the parser's (always HIGH once accepted), never the client's. Resolving the short link again on Save was not chosen: it would be a new outside call per save and could land on a consent page and refuse an honest save; a forged `resolvedUrl` gains nothing a hand pin does not already allow.
   - **A hand pin** (`MAP_PIN`): stored as `MAP_PIN`, HIGH. Two exceptions, 422 `PIN_REQUIRED`: the customer's saved point sent back unchanged is not a hand pin, so it is confirmed as it is only when it is already verified, or HIGH and inside the company's area (`savedPointProblem`, the dialog's test; else 422 with the reason: not exact, outside the delivery area, or latitude and longitude swapped, also when `confirmOutsideArea` is sent; a confirmed saved point keeps its source, e.g. `IMPORT`); and a "hand pin" exactly on the point of a reading that needs a pin was never moved (the message says why, as for the reading).
   - It also accepts `{ input }` alone. It resolves it (short links too, as the Read does) and answers 422 `PIN_REQUIRED` unless the result is exact (was `CONFIRM_ON_MAP` before A5; one code for "needs a pin by hand").
   - `GEOCODER` is no longer accepted as a source (400): nothing geocodes, and a client cannot choose the source stored.
   - A point outside the service area gets 422 `OUTSIDE_AREA` until the dispatcher confirms. Only a hand pin can get there: a reading outside the area always needs a pin.
   - It writes `lat`, `lng`, `locationInput`, `locationSource`, `locationVerified = true`, `locationVerifiedById`, `locationVerifiedAt`, `geocodeConfidence` (a hand pin HIGH, a reading the parser's, a saved point confirmed its own) and audit `CUSTOMER_LOCATION_SET` (before: point, source, verified, confidence; after: point, source, input, confidence and `check` = `HAND_PIN`, `READING` or `SAVED_POINT`), in one transaction since the A5 third review: LOCK, LOADING and DISPATCH read the row's "before" point ([3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)), so a saved pin without its row is not possible. Since the A5 fifth review the "before" is the customer as it is when the change is written (the row locked, `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, and read again in the transaction), not as the route read it at the start: a point a customer file marked LOW in between is recorded as LOW. The customer import writes the same row (`check` = `IMPORT`) when an exact pair from the file replaces a saved point that was not usable; since the A5 fifth review in one transaction with the new pair, the customer locked and judged as it is then (before: the row was written after the pair, so a restart or a failed insert in between left the change without its row, and the decision used the customer as the import read it at the start). A row whose customer is still as the import read it and needs no row is written in one conditional statement (still not confirmed, same point, same confidence); any other goes through the locked transaction.
   - **The customer page and the Customers list** (A5 third review) say when a saved point blocks delivery, with the day card's test and words (`locationIssue` in `customer-attrs.ts`, the company's area): the customer page shows "Pin: ... (not usable)" and a red note, for example "Saved location is not exact (low confidence). Drop the pin on the customer's exact location. Its orders are not planned or sent out until then."; the list marks the row **needs pin** (the reason on hover) and counts them at the top ("N need a pin", next to "N missing geocode"). Before, a point an import had marked LOW looked like any usable import on both, so the blocked customers could only be found once they had orders.
   - **Other writers of coordinates** (audit PR A5): the customer page's **Set location** opens this same dialog and route (`customer-editor.tsx`; the page used to save a map click or two typed numbers through `PATCH /api/customers/:id` as verified HIGH with no check at all: not 0,0, not the area). `PATCH /api/customers/:id` now refuses coordinates (400 `USE_SET_LOCATION`, not silently dropped). `POST /api/customers` (no screen uses it) reads the pair as sent like a Read and refuses one that needs a pin (422 `PIN_REQUIRED`) or one coordinate without the other (400). Since the owner decision of 28 Sep 2026 it counts one zero at the end like the Read: "23.5800" / "58.4100" sent as text is refused with "This location is not exact. Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts). Create the customer without coordinates, then set its location on the map (Set location on the customer page)." (it was created HIGH; each reason is now a sentence of its own, it read "not exact (... (accurate to ~100 m or worse).)."); a JSON number has no zeros at the end. The customer import stores no pair that needs a pin (below). Order-file stubs (`confirmIntake`) and late orders store no coordinates at all (LOCATION REQUIRED).
   - The location stays on the customer master. A later customer import (`apps/web/app/api/customers/import/route.ts`) does not overwrite a verified location, also not one verified while the import runs (audit F05): the import writes a customer's coordinates with their own update whose condition is "still not verified" (`updateMany` where `locationVerified = false`), which PostgreSQL checks on the row as it is at that moment, not on the list the import read at its start; "N customer location(s) confirmed by a dispatcher were kept" counts what those updates did. Before, a pin verified between the import's read and its write was overwritten and stayed marked verified, with the dispatcher's name on the imported point.
   - **The import and the owner's rule** (audit PR A5): each `lat` / `lng` pair is read like ADD LOCATION reads "lat, lng" (the text as in the file, with the company's area; `readImportedPair` in `lib/dispatch/import-location.ts`). A pair that needs a pin (fewer than 4 decimals, swapped, outside the area, 0,0) is never stored as a usable location: a new customer gets no coordinates (`MISSING`, LOCATION REQUIRED on the day). An existing customer keeps its saved point when the file's pair points at the same place within the file's own precision (23.586 stands for 23.5855 to 23.5865) or within 150 m (`fileAgreesWithSaved`); when the file points elsewhere (A5 review: the customer moved, or one of the two is wrong) its saved point, if nobody confirmed it, stays on the map but is marked LOW, so it blocks planning until a dispatcher drops the pin (an update conditional on "still not verified and still at the point compared", like F05). A confirmed location is never changed. A saved point the file agrees with but that is itself not usable (LOW, or outside the area, and never confirmed: `locationBlocksDelivery`, for example after an earlier import marked it LOW) is kept but listed as not used, with its own warning, and is not marked again (A5 second review; it used to say "The location it already has is kept."). The row is listed in `locationsNotSaved` (row, code, reason, `kept`: `SAVED_LOCATION`, `SAVED_LOCATION_NEEDS_PIN`, `SAVED_LOCATION_NOT_USABLE` or none) with the reason in the import's own words ("Fewer than 4 decimals.", "Latitude and longitude look swapped.", "Outside the delivery area.", "0,0 is not a location.", plus "The file points about 12 km from the saved location." when it contradicts the saved point), shown on the import screen with what happens to the customer's location and what to do, plus one warning (and one more for saved points not used: "... Their orders are not planned or sent out until then. Drop the pin on each one ...", which is what planning and `locationGate` enforce; it said "Nothing is delivered to them until then", which neither held before the gate nor holds for a load already on the road, A5 second review); it is not an error, so the rest of the row and the file are imported. The screen's column hints say the rule before the import (at least 4 decimals, inside the delivery area, every decimal typed or pasted, the column formatted as Text when Excel drops a trailing zero). Before, any pair within -90..90 / -180..180 was stored as HIGH, 0,0 included. Of the zeros at the end of a coordinate's decimals **one counts, whatever the file** (`countedText` in `location-input.ts`, A5 fifth review; since the owner decision of 28 Sep 2026 the same function judges ADD LOCATION and `POST /api/customers`, above): 23.5850 is 4 decimals, 23.5800 is 3 (padding is not precision), in a CSV (typed, or saved by Excel from cells formatted to show 4 decimals, which writes each cell as it shows it), sent as CSV or as Excel, in an Excel Text cell, and in a number cell formatted to show more decimals; the row's reason is then "Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts)." when the zeros made the difference. So a rough pair is never made exact by its format, and the verdict no longer depends on whether the workbook or the CSV Excel saves from it is imported (the A5 fourth review applied the one-zero rule to number formats only). The price: a real 4-decimal coordinate ending in 00 (about 1 in 100 per axis) is not exact either; its pin is placed by hand, or the file gives more decimals. Excel: a number holds no trailing zeros (23.5850 is the number 23.585), so for `lat` / `lng` the import reads the text a number cell shows when its number format shows more decimals than the number has (`decimalTextColumns` in `lib/csv.ts`: 23.585 in a cell formatted 0.0000 reads "23.5850", 4 decimals; 23.58 reads "23.5800", counted as 3). A cell in General format shows 23.585 in Excel too, and is read as 3 decimals. A CSV sent as Excel is read as the text in the file, also one whose first header is "ID" (A5 fifth review: SheetJS reads such a file with its SYLK reader first, and every such file was read without its text, so a trailing zero was lost only when the browser sent a semicolon "ID" CSV as Excel; now only a file whose SYLK part formats a value, never a CSV, is, `idFileFormatsCells` in `lib/workbook-guard.ts`). The import's warning and its result box ask for a pin only for the rows whose customer has no usable location after the import (`kept` none, `SAVED_LOCATION_NEEDS_PIN`, `SAVED_LOCATION_NOT_USABLE`); rows whose customer keeps a usable saved location (`SAVED_LOCATION`) are counted on their own line, "nothing to do" (A5 fifth review: every listed row was told to be set on the map, with "No item is delivered without a correct location"). The remedy says to type or paste each coordinate with all its decimals and, if Excel drops a trailing zero, to format the column as Text before typing or pasting (it said "or to show 4 decimals", which turned rough pairs into exact ones).

**Details** (`customer-dialog.tsx`, form helpers in `customer-details.ts`) calls `PATCH /api/customers/:id` (`apps/web/app/api/customers/[id]/route.ts`) with **only the fields the dispatcher changed** (audit F07, owner decision 9): customer type, priority (which also sets `priorityConfirmed`), unloading minutes (`avgServiceTimeMin`) and the hard and preferred windows. A window is sent whole: when its start or its end changed, both ends go as the dialog shows them (A2 review). One end sent alone was merged by the route with the other end as stored, and the day screen does not poll outside an optimization, so a dialog opened with hours changed since (by another dispatcher or tab) could save a window nobody typed: 06:00-10:00 on screen, 12:00-15:00 stored since, end set to 16:00, saved as 12:00-16:00. Now 06:00-16:00 is saved, replacing the other change as a whole. Nothing changed = no request ("Nothing changed."). Audit `UPDATE Customer`.

- **Opening.** A priority or unloading time that is a default (`prioritySource` / `serviceSource` TYPE or DEFAULT, from the day overview) opens as a default, never as the customer's own value: the priority list has "P3 - default, not confirmed" (picking any P, P3 included, confirms it), and the unloading box is empty with the default as placeholder ("15 (customer type default)"). A window ending at midnight (1440) shows "24:00", which reads back (it used to show "00:00 +1", which could not be saved).
- **Checks before anything is sent.** Times with the shared strict `parseHhmm` (`lib/dispatch/time.ts`): "06:90" or "25:00" is refused (the dialog's own parser read "06:90" as 07:30; that `toMinutes` is removed). Both ends of a window or neither, end after start. Unloading time: whole minutes 0 to 480 (`MAX_SERVICE_MIN`); "10 min", "ten" or "1.5" is refused (it used to become 0 minutes, confirmed).
- **Unloading time left empty** = no own time: the customer-type or Settings default applies (`effectiveAttrs`). The dialog sends `avgServiceTimeMin: null` when the dispatcher cleared an own time; the route then stores the column default (10, `CUSTOMER_SERVICE_COLUMN_DEFAULT`) with `serviceTimeConfirmed = false`. An explicit 0 is kept and confirmed. This is the only way to put a customer back on the default: a customer import can give a new own time (a number, always confirmed) but cannot clear one, because a blank `avg_service_time_min` cell or a missing column keeps the stored time and its confirmation, and text such as "default" is refused (`customer-import-service-time.spec.ts`).
- **API.** `customerPatchSchema` takes `avgServiceTimeMin` as a JSON whole number 0 to 480 or null, and the four window fields as JSON whole numbers 0 to 1440 or null: a string ("", "420", "10 min") is 400, never coerced (a coerced "" used to be 0, i.e. midnight or no unloading time). Fields not sent are not changed and not confirmed.
- **Answers after a switch.** A Save answer that arrives after the dialog was closed or opened for another customer is reported for its own customer only.

After either dialog saves, the day **and** the plan below it are read again, the plan in place (audit F13, `afterCustomerSaved` in `dispatch-client.tsx`, `PlanView` `reloadSignal`). A READY plan does not poll, so its "changed after planning" notes, the load's **Changed after planning** badge, its warnings and the "New pin" line of the WhatsApp texts built from it used to appear only after a page reload, also for LOCKED loads, which the day's `outdated` count leaves out.

What the reload does **not** change: a stop keeps the point it was planned with. `getPlanDetail` takes the stop's pin from its snapshot, so the map, the stop's pin link, the route link and the stop's link in the WhatsApp text stay the planned ones (also after a page reload). The WhatsApp text (`whatsappText`) and the driver sheet (`driverPackModel`) add the change and "New pin - ask the dispatcher which one to use: <link>" under the stop, for a pin moved more than 50 m (`PIN_MOVED_M`). The planned pin is replaced only by a re-plan of the load: a PLANNED load at RE-PLAN, a LOCKED or LOADING load after it is put back to Planned. Since the A5 third review a stop still planned at a point that was **not usable** when a new pin replaced it cannot be locked, loaded or dispatched until that re-plan (409 `STOP_PIN_REPLACED`, [3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)); an ordinary correction of a usable point keeps the "New pin" path above (see "Changed after planning", [3.7](#37-step-5-reviewing-the-plan)). That is the owner's decision of 28 Sep 2026 ("Allow, driver gets new pin", [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)): a pin corrected by more than 50 m after planning does not block the load; it can go, and the driver's text and sheet show "New pin".

### 3.5 Step 3: the day overview

`GET /api/dispatch/day?date=&depotId=` runs `getDayOverview` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/day-overview.ts`). The screen re-reads it after every action, and every 3 s while a plan is optimizing. It returns:

- the date (default tomorrow in the tenant timezone), the cutoff, the active depots and the selected depot;
- the orders in scope. `ordersInScopeWhere` in `plan-service.ts` selects the same depot and delivery date. Every order has a depot (owner rule, audit PR A5: `Order.depotId` is NOT NULL since migration `20260930120000_orders_always_have_depot`); before A5 orders without a depot were included when the tenant had exactly one active depot, and the migration gave them that depot. Counts, cases, kg and late count;
- one issue card per customer branch, with effective priority and unloading minutes and where each comes from (`prioritySource`, `serviceSource`: CUSTOMER, TYPE or DEFAULT; the Details dialog opens a default as a default, audit F07), window text, issues and a `blocking` flag, blocking ones first. A deactivated customer that still has open orders (not on a frozen load, not dispatched) shows only `CUSTOMER_INACTIVE` (blocking; its open orders are left unserved at optimize). When the plan in use still has those orders on PLANNED loads (it was made before the customer was deactivated), the card says so and asks for a RE-PLAN. A deactivated customer whose orders are all on frozen loads gets no card (frozen loads keep them);
- weights per order **line**, not per product (`lineWeightStatus` in `apps/web/lib/dispatch/weights.ts`), for the open cases of each line (cases on frozen loads of the plan in use are subtracted per line, as `buildDispatchRequest` counts them, so a partly locked split order is still checked. The open rest of such an order is planned with the product's weight at every optimize and never saved on the line it shares with the frozen part, so a weight entered or corrected since is listed only while a PLANNED part of the plan in use carries another case weight than the product has now - planned at 0 kg, or corrected since - read with the timetable check's rule (`portionPlannedKgPerCase` in `split.ts`: the part's `kgPerCase`, else a 0-kg line whose part weighs no more than its weighed lines). Second review of PR4: these were never listed, so the day screen said "up to date" with RE-PLAN off while the check blocked the PLANNED load they now overload (`CAPACITY_KG_NEW_WEIGHT`)): `productsWithoutWeight` (0 kg and no case weight on the product: counted as 0 kg, the same lines the `WEIGHT_REQUIRED` check names) and `weightsToApply` (the product's case weight gives the line another kg: 0 kg and entered since, or weighed from the master and corrected since; applied at the next optimize), each with lines and cases per product;
- the live plan from `currentPlan`: the highest version for (tenant, depot, date) that is not `SUPERSEDED` or `ARCHIVED` and has no `supersededAt`, with its latest `RunJob`, loads by status, summary and whether the cases reconcile;
- `pending`: orders of the day that are not in the applied scenario's `scope.orderIds` or `scope.frozenOrderIds`, i.e. orders that arrived after the plan was made;
- `openOrders` (PR3): orders with cases not yet on a locked, loading or dispatched load of the plan in use. 0 while the day has orders means there is nothing left to plan;
- (PR9) `carriedIn`: the orders of the day brought forward from earlier days (customer, cases, the date first due, and whether the plan in use contains them yet), the day screen's list with the badge "Carried over from 26 Sep"; `pending.carried`: of the pending orders, those brought forward (a re-plan adds them with reason LATE_ORDER); `carriedOut`: orders of this day brought forward to later days since (how many, their cases, the days). Those orders are not in `orders`, `openOrders`, `pending` or the issue cards any more: `ordersInScopeWhere` leaves out an order with `carriedToOrderId` set. The "Not delivered on earlier days" box has its own request (`GET /api/dispatch/carry-over`, [3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)), read when the day loads and when its orders change, not on the 3 s polls; `plan.loadsOfDay` (PR9 second review): the plan's loads by status without the loads that never left the depot and hold only orders brought forward to a later day (a load counts when it left, holds an order not brought forward, or holds nothing), next to `plan.loadsByStatus` (every load);
- `outdated` (only with an applied plan; the keys of `UP_TO_DATE`, all 0 when up to date): open cases in its scope whose case weight was entered or corrected since (`weightCases`, including the open rest of a partly frozen order above), and orders of customers deactivated since that are still on its PLANNED loads (`inactiveOrders`). The plan view shows the same as warnings (`outdatedNotes` in `plan-detail.ts`). Since PR4 also customers on PLANNED loads whose pin or receiving hours were corrected (`masterChanged`) and trucks with PLANNED loads whose capacity or payload was corrected (`trucksChanged`, PR4 review), both from the snapshots (`plannedLoadsMasterChanged` in `snapshots.ts`). Since audit PR A5's second review also active customers on PLANNED loads whose location is not usable any more (`locationBlocked`: `locationBlocksDelivery` on the customer now, for example a saved point a customer import marked LOW after planning; the snapshots compare only the pin and the hours, so this was not counted and the day said "The plan is up to date"). Step 3 then says "the location of N customer(s) on planned loads can no longer be used (drop the pin on each one, or RE-PLAN to leave their orders unserved)" and offers RE-PLAN; their loads cannot be locked meanwhile (`locationGate`, [3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)). A customer only on LOCKED or LOADING loads is not counted (a re-plan keeps those loads): LOADING and DISPATCH of such a load are refused instead;
- active trucks with their cases per load round, and the last 20 upload batches for the day.

The Step 3 button reads OPTIMIZE when no plan is applied yet and RE-PLAN when one is. It is enabled when the day has orders and there is either no applied plan, at least one pending order, an `outdated` plan (the Step 3 card then says why), or a failed last optimization ("the previous plan was kept"). It is disabled with "nothing left to plan" when `openOrders` is 0 and nothing is pending (PR3, review F03), and while an action of the plan below it runs (PR3 review, [3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)). The words under it come from `dayNothingLeftText` (`carry-view.ts`, PR9 second review), which says what the server's 409 `NOTHING_TO_PLAN` says: when orders of the day were brought forward to a later day (`carriedOut`), "Nothing left to plan: N order(s) of this day were brought forward to 28 Sep and are planned there", that the loads still showing them stay for the record, and, for a LOCKED or LOADING load holding only brought-forward orders (it was loaded), to unload those cases before the loads of 28 Sep are picked and put the load back to Planned - never "To change a load, unlock it first" for such a load, and never "Every load has left the depot" while it is still at the depot (it is not counted in `loadsOfDay`). The unlock advice and Step 4's "every load is out" count `loadsOfDay`. Step 4 shows done when the plan is DISPATCHED, or when every load of the plan in use is out although its last re-plan failed (the version then stays FAILED, holding the plan that was dispatched).

**Stale-day guard** (PR3, review ADD-STALE-DAY-CLIENT). `DispatchClient` loads the day only through the day loader (`app/t/[slug]/dispatch/day-loader.ts`), and every load is for the day selected **now**: the first load, polling, "Try again", and the reload after an action (a load change, OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN, a confirmed file, a saved dialog) that ends after the dispatcher picked another date or depot. Second review of PR3: those reloads closed over the date of the render the action started in, so they reloaded the old day and the screen stayed on "Loading <new date>..." for good (no load on its way, no error, no Try again). As a backstop, an answer for a selection the dispatcher has left, with no other load on its way, is not shown; the selection is loaded instead. The loader uses a gate (`request-gate.ts`): an answer for a date or depot the dispatcher already left is dropped, whatever order the answers arrive in. An answer for the day being loaded is shown when it is newer than the one on screen, even if a later refresh of that day has started, and the 3 s polling while optimizing skips a tick while the previous load of the day is still on its way (at most 5 ticks in a row). Review of PR3: the first version kept only the newest answer, so a day overview slower than 3 s dropped every answer and froze the screen on "Optimizing...". After a date or depot change the screen shows only the pickers and "Loading <date>" until that day has loaded; a failed load shows the error with "Try again" instead of the previous day. A day shown after a failed load (`afterError`) also reloads the plan below it, in place: `DispatchClient` bumps `PlanView`'s `reloadSignal` prop, which runs the plan's own load, so an open late order, the opened loads and a running action stay (fourth review of PR3: this used to bump the plan's key, and one failed day poll during an optimization remounted the plan and closed the late order being typed). The plan is remounted only when its id changes, after OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN reached the server, and after a plan action whose day reload was shown (`refresh()` answers true), so a failed day reload leaves the plan on screen (third review of PR3: the day's Try again brought the day back while Step 4 stayed replaced by an error).
