A file check that answers after the dispatcher picked another day is not offered on the new day, and a confirmed file moves the screen to the file's delivery date only while the dispatcher is still on the day it was added on (`dayAfterConfirm`; third review of PR3: the answer took the screen and the URL back to the file's date). `api()` (`client-api.ts`) never rejects: a request that does not reach the server (offline, a connection reset while Railway redeploys) answers `{ ok: false, status: 0 }` with "The server could not be reached (...). Check the connection and try again." like any refusal (second review of PR3: a rejected request skipped the clean-up, so one network error left OPTIMIZE and every plan action disabled until the page was reloaded). Upload, Confirm, OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN and the plan's actions are enabled only while the day on screen is the selected one; their payloads are built from the loaded day, and OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN send `expect: { date, depotId }` (the server answers 409 `DAY_MISMATCH` for a plan of another day).

### 3.6 Step 4: OPTIMIZE

**Which route runs.** `optimize()` in `dispatch-client.tsx`:

- **No applied plan:** `POST /api/dispatch/plan { date, depotId, optimize: true, allowMissingLocations, expect }` (`apps/web/app/api/dispatch/plan/route.ts`). It calls `getOrCreatePlan`, which returns the live plan or creates version 1 (`status DRAFT`, `reason INITIAL`, audit `CREATE RunPlan`), then `startDispatchOptimize`. Version 1 is created by `createInitialPlan` under the **day lock** (`lockPlanDay`, a PostgreSQL advisory lock per company, depot and date; PR3, review F06): the check for a live plan and the create run in one transaction, so concurrent first requests (two dispatchers, two tabs, the legacy "New run") get the same plan. The legacy `POST /api/runs` uses the same helper and answers an existing plan with 200.
- **Stale screen guard** (PR3, review ADD-STALE-DAY-CLIENT). The day screen sends `expect: { date, depotId }` of the day it shows; `/api/dispatch/plan` and `/replan` answer 409 `DAY_MISMATCH` when the plan is for another day or depot.
- **Plan already applied:** `POST /api/runs/:id/replan` with reason `LATE_ORDER` when late orders are pending, else `REOPTIMIZE` ([3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)).
- **409 `LOCATION_REQUIRED`:** the browser asks whether to optimize anyway (`askOverride` in `client-api.ts`; since the A5 third review the question says what is wrong: "N customer(s) still have no location ... reason 'location missing'" when every one is missing, "N customer(s) have a saved location that cannot be used (not exact, outside the delivery area, or not valid). Their orders will be UNSERVED until the pin is placed on each one." when every one is `INVALID_LOCATION`, and both counts when mixed; it used to call all of them "no location ... location missing"). If yes, it sends the request again with `allowMissingLocations: true`, and those customers' orders become unserved with reason `MISSING_COORDINATES` or `INVALID_LOCATION` (never sent to the optimizer: `locationBlocksDelivery`, owner's location rule, [3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details)).
- **409 `WEIGHT_REQUIRED`** (review F02): open lines still have no weight and an active truck of the depot has a payload (`capacityWeightKg > 0`). The body lists `unknownWeights` (product, lines, cases). The browser asks (`askOverride` in `client-api.ts`: "Cancel, and add the case weight under Products - or optimize anyway (treated as 0 kg)?"; only TENANT_ADMIN can edit products, so PLANNER and SUPERVISOR users read "ask a company admin to add the case weight under Products", `weightFixText`); yes sends `allowMissingWeights: true`. The plan then carries a warning ("Planned without weights for N order line(s) ...") in its scenario warnings, and `OPTIMIZE_STARTED` records the override. The plan's Re-plan button asks the same questions.

`POST /api/runs/:id/optimize` (`apps/web/app/api/runs/[id]/optimize/route.ts`) calls the same `startDispatchOptimize`. The dispatch screen does not use it; the legacy run page does. Its old per-company limiter is gone (PR3): every solve entry goes through the shared solve admission ([2.7](#27-background-job-model)).

**`startDispatchOptimize`** (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/start-optimize.ts`) checks, in order:

1. the plan exists;
2. it is for the `expect`ed day and depot, when the caller sent one (else 409 `DAY_MISMATCH`);
3. it is not superseded (status `SUPERSEDED` or `supersededAt` set);
4. it is not a legacy plan, else 409 `LEGACY_PLAN`. `isLegacyPlan` is true when the plan has `RouteAssignment` rows without a `loadId`, or its chosen scenario has no dispatch `scope` and `loads`;
5. (audit PR4, F09) a version OPTIMIZING whose current job has ended is reset first (`repairEndedJobPlan`: FAILED, `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` reason `STUCK_PLAN`, the user as actor); then, if a job is QUEUED or RUNNING in the database, it answers 202 with that job (before audit PR4 also for an ended job, or when only the in-memory map held the run: a 202 that started nothing); a version still OPTIMIZING without such a job (the reset could not take the row) answers 409 `PLAN_STUCK`, decided only in the start transaction on the locked plan row (review of audit PR4: the plan row read before the job checks may be out of date - a job ending in between moved the plan to FAILED or READY - and was answered `PLAN_STUCK` for a plan that was not stuck; `unstickIfEnded` also reads the plan again after the job checks);
6. it is not already applied (`chosenScenarioId` set, whatever the status - also FAILED, since a failed re-plan still holds the previous plan), else 409 `NEW_VERSION_REQUIRED` (use re-plan). A version a re-plan just created carries a copy of the previous plan; it counts as not applied while it has never had a job (`freshVersion`). Review of PR3: a FAILED version with a plan could be re-optimized in place (a second tab's OPTIMIZE, `POST /api/runs/:id/optimize`), replacing loads the dispatcher had seen without a new version.

It then calls `buildDispatchRequest`, and refuses missing locations and unknown weights (unless allowed, `gate` in `start-optimize.ts`), nothing left to plan (409 `NOTHING_TO_PLAN` when every order is on a locked, loading or dispatched load - its text says to unlock a load only when a LOCKED or LOADING one exists, otherwise that every load has left the depot and a late order can still be planned; 400 "Upload orders first" only when the day has no orders at all) and a depot with no active trucks (400). Then it reserves the solve admission (429 / 503, [2.7](#27-background-job-model)); every answer below that starts no job gives the reservation back.

**Weights from the product master** (`resolveOrderLineWeights` / `masterLineKg` in `weights.ts`). A line weight of 0 means unknown. A line at 0 kg, or weighed from the master (`weightFromMaster`), whose product's case weight now gives another kg, is planned with cases × that weight; the order total is summed again. File weights never change, and orders whose kg lives on the order only (older orders: lines at 0 kg, order total above 0) are left as they are. `buildDispatchRequest` does this **in memory** and lists what it would save in `weightChanges` (orders with no part on a frozen load of this version, not DISPATCHED / DELIVERED; the open rest of a partly frozen order is planned with it but not saved). So a re-plan probe that is refused leaves the live plan's orders, loads and portions exactly as they were (review of PR2). The job saves `weightChanges` only when it applies the plan made with them (step 5 of the background job below, PR3 review): a failed or stale optimization saves nothing, so after a failed re-plan the copied loads still match their orders' kg and the "planned with the old weight" warning stays.

**Start transaction** (timeout 30 s, maxWait 10 s; everything below commits together or not at all - PR3, review F07 / ADD-JOB-AUDIT). It takes `lockIntake` (the lock that confirm, late order and batch delete take), then the plan row (`lockRunForWrite`, 5 s lock timeout) and checks again on the locked row: superseded (409), another job won the race (202 with that job; OPTIMIZING without a QUEUED or RUNNING job is 409 `PLAN_STUCK`, never a 202 - audit PR4), already applied (409 `NEW_VERSION_REQUIRED`), and the frozen loads are still the ones the request was built around (else 409 `LOADS_CHANGED`). It checks that every order of `scope.orderIds` / `scope.frozenOrderIds` still exists (else 409 `ORDERS_CHANGED`: a batch delete committed after the request was built), creates the `RunJob` (`attemptNo` + 1, `QUEUED`, `requestJson`), sets `RunPlan.status = OPTIMIZING` and `currentJobId`, and writes audit `OPTIMIZE_STARTED`. An audit failure therefore leaves no QUEUED orphan, and an immediate retry starts. Once OPTIMIZING is committed, a batch delete for that day is refused. A wait for the intake lock past the timeout (Prisma P2028, e.g. a large confirm holding the lock) answers 409 `INTAKE_BUSY` ("try again in a moment"), and one for the plan row 409 `PLAN_BUSY`, not 500. It then calls `scheduleDispatchOptimize` without waiting and answers 202 (`queued: true` when the job waits for a solver slot).

**`buildDispatchRequest(tenantId, runId, scenarios?, { now? })`** (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/plan-service.ts`) turns the day into a `DispatchRequest`. `now` is the time the plan is made (default: the clock; `startDispatchOptimize` and `replan` pass it through, so tests can fix it):

| Input | How it is built |
|---|---|
| Orders | `ordersInScopeWhere`, oldest upload first, with customer and lines |
| Frozen loads | This run's `PlanLoad` rows with status other than PLANNED. Orders wholly on them go to `scope.frozenOrderIds`. For a split order partly on frozen loads, the frozen cases are subtracted per line (`frozenLineCases`) and only the rest is planned |
| Stops | All open orders of one customer branch become one stop: `stop_id` = customer id, `order_ids` = the order ids, or `<orderId>~open` for the open part of a partly frozen order |
| Priority | Per order (`prOf`): `priorityFromFile ? min(order.priority, effective priority) : effective priority`. The stop takes the lowest `prOf` of its orders, and every order of that stop stores that stop priority in `scope.orderPriority`, not its own `prOf`. Only orders pre-dropped for a missing or invalid location store their own `prOf`. The summary's per-priority service % uses these stored values. `strict_priorities: true` is always sent |
| Late flag | `late` is true if any order of the group is late |
| Weights | Per line: its own kg (`weightKg / cases`), or cases × the product's case weight now for a 0-kg or master-weighed line (see above); for an order whose kg lives on the order only, the order kg per case. Lines still at 0 kg are counted in `unknownWeights` (the `WEIGHT_REQUIRED` gate) |
| Deactivated customers | All open orders of an inactive customer are pre-dropped as `INVALID_CUSTOMER` ("customer deactivated after the order was confirmed - reactivate it ..."), with a request warning. Not blocking. Reactivating the customer plans them again at the next re-plan. Orders on frozen loads are untouched. Deactivated products are planned as ordered, with a warning |
| Missing locations | Pre-dropped (`preDrops`, unserved before the solver runs) and reported as a `BlockingIssue` |
| Cases heavier than any truck | When every usable truck has a payload, a line whose case weight exceeds the largest one is pre-dropped as a portion `EXCEEDS_ANY_TRUCK_CAPACITY` ("One case of X weighs N kg, more than any truck payload (M kg) - check the product weight."), with a request warning. The rest of the order is planned (review F01) |
| Windows | Effective hard and preferred windows. A window that ends before it starts is ignored with a warning (`usableWindow`) |
| Split deliveries | When `TenantConfig.splitDeliveries` is on and no truck that still has a load left can carry the customer's cases or kg (`partCapFor`), `choosePartCapacity` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/split.ts`) picks a part size and `splitIntoParts` cuts the SKU lines into parts. Each part becomes its own stop (`<customerId>#k`) with order ids `<orderId>~k`. The exact lines of every part are kept in `scope.portions` as `PortionRecord`s ([4.4](#44-how-the-web-shapes-the-request)) |
| Service time | `stopService(base, serviceMinPerCase, cases, totalCases?)` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/service-time.ts`): the customer's service minutes plus unloading minutes per case. A split part gets its proportional share of the base time (at least 5 minutes) plus the per-case time of its own cases. Rounded and capped at `MAX_SERVICE_MIN` (480), the solver's `service_min` limit; a capped stop gives a request warning with the minutes it needs. Customer, tenant-default and imported service times accept at most 480 |
| Money | `margin` and `revenue` per stop only when every order carries a value (`portionMoney` for split parts); otherwise `null` |
| Plan continuity | Only when `usesPlanContinuity(run)` is true, i.e. the plan has a `parentRunId` and its reason is not `REOPTIMIZE` (so `LATE_ORDER` or `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT`). Each stop then gets `previous_truck_id`: the truck that carried most of its cases in the parent version (`previousTruckOf`, a vote per order line weighted by cases). The solver charges `change_penalty_per_stop` (default 3.0 in `apps/solver/dispatch_models.py`, not sent by the web) for moving it. A `REOPTIMIZE` sends no previous trucks, so every scenario starts from scratch (commit `19831f0`) |
| Trucks | Active trucks of the depot: cases, kg, fixed, trip and per-km cost, km per litre, availability window, `maxTripsPerDay`, and `frozen_trips` (depart, return and cases of each frozen load) |
| Config | From `TenantConfig` (mapping in [4.4](#44-how-the-web-shapes-the-request)). `time_limit_sec: null` lets the solver choose. The provider comes from `routingProviderFor`. Scenarios: RECOMMENDED, MIN_TRUCKS, MIN_DISTANCE |
| Same-day start (PR8) | When the plan is built **on its own delivery day** in the company timezone (`TenantConfig.timezone`, Asia/Muscat), `sameDayPlanFrom` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/plan-from.ts`) takes now + the turnaround between loads (`reloadMinutes`, the preparation time; at most 24:00). If that is later than the first departure setting and the depot opening, it is sent as `shift_start_min`, a plan warning says "Planned from 09:30 (now 09:00 + 30 min preparation): the plan is for today, so no new load leaves the depot before 09:30. ..." (the first of the optimizer's warnings; the plan screen can show other notes above it), and the plan's settings keep it (`PlanSettings.planFrom`, for the ASSUMPTIONS sheet; `shiftStartMin` stays the setting). Whenever the plan is built on its delivery day (also before the first departure) the time it is made is sent as `loading_from_min` and kept as `PlanSettings.loadingFromMin` (`planDayNowMin`, PR8 review): loading starts then, so every new load leaves no earlier than now + turnaround + loading per case x its cases ([3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)). Frozen trips, truck availability and everything else are sent as before. A plan for a future day is unchanged |

It returns `{ request, preDrops, scope, blocking, warnings, unknownWeights }`. `scope` also records `frozenLoadOrderIds` and `frozenLoadIds`, so the result can later be checked against the loads that were frozen when it was computed.

**Background job** (`apps/web/lib/jobs/dispatch-job.ts`). `scheduleDispatchOptimize` registers the promise in the shared in-flight map (`trackInflight` in `optimize-job.ts`; this assumes one web replica). `runJob` then:

1. waits for a solver slot when the solve admission queued it (PR3);
2. sets the `RunJob` to `RUNNING`, 20 % (only if it is still QUEUED);
3. calls `callDispatchSolver` (`apps/web/lib/solver-client.ts`): POST to `SOLVER_URL` + `/optimize-dispatch` with header `X-Solver-Token`, up to 600 s ([2.6](#26-runtime-architecture)). A 503 from the solver ("busy") fails the job with "busy, optimize again in a minute";
4. stores `responseJson`, 80 % ("Saving plan");
5. in one transaction (timeout 120 s) locks the plan row and checks it is still `OPTIMIZING` with this job as `currentJobId` and the job still `RUNNING` (else "Stale result", nothing applied), saves the request's `weightChanges` with `applyWeightChanges` (set-based `UPDATE ... FROM unnest(...)`, each row only if it still has the kg the request was built from, else the job fails with "The orders of this day changed ... Optimize again"; one audit row `ORDER_WEIGHTS_RESOLVED`, entity RunPlan, each line and order before and after; PR3 review: before, this ran in the start transaction, so a failed re-plan left copied loads with the old kg and no warning; saved weights may change orders on the superseded parent's PLANNED loads, whose stops then show the new kg, never frozen ones), runs `persistDispatchResult`, runs `applyScenario` with the RECOMMENDED scenario, marks the `RunJob` `SUCCEEDED` at 100 % with a message such as "N loads on M trucks, K stop(s) unserved" (on a re-plan with kept loads "N new loads + K kept (locked or dispatched) on M trucks", M counting the kept loads' trucks, read from the version's rows of `scope.frozenLoadIds`: PR7, B3), and writes audit `OPTIMIZE_SUCCEEDED` with per-scenario figures (PR3: in the same transaction).

Any error goes to `failJob` (conditional, [2.7](#27-background-job-model)); lost jobs are reaped by the janitor ([5.9](#59-job-failures-and-the-janitor)).

**`persistDispatchResult(tx, tenantId, runId, built, resp)`** deletes the run's old `ScenarioResult` rows (including the option a re-plan copied from its parent) and writes one per scenario with its totals and `detailsJson`. `ScenarioDetails` is the solver scenario plus engine, distance provider, estimated flag, warnings and `scope`. Each scenario gets its `UnservedOrder` rows: the pre-drops plus the solver's unserved stops, with split parts merged per order and reason into `portionLinesJson`.

**`applyScenario(tx, tenantId, runId, scenarioId, userId, opts)`** turns a scenario into loads:

- Its first statement locks the plan row (`lockRunForWrite`, PR3, review F07). It refuses a superseded version (status `SUPERSEDED` or `supersededAt` set), an `OPTIMIZING` one unless the caller is its current job (`opts.jobId`), and a scenario without dispatch details (legacy). A dispatcher's choice (`opts.requireOptimized`) must be an `OPTIMIZED` option: `NO_SOLUTION` gives 409 `SCENARIO_NOT_USABLE`.
- It refuses when the frozen loads differ from `scope.frozenLoadIds` / `frozenLoadOrderIds` ("Loads were locked/unlocked after this optimization").
- It picks a driver for every new load with `planDrivers` ([3.9](#39-step-7-a-driver-per-load)) before anything is deleted: the evidence is this version's loads as they are then (for the re-plan job, the copies of the previous version's loads). It keeps the driver notes (a trip that lost or changed its driver, a hand-set driver whose trip the plan does not have).
- It deletes this run's PLANNED loads and any legacy `RouteAssignment` without a load.
- It creates one `PlanLoad` per solver load: truck, `loadNo`, depart and return, km, minutes, cases, kg, utilisation, fuel, cost, return leg, estimated flag, `driverId` and, for a driver the dispatcher chose by hand for that truck and trip, its marker (`driverSetById`, `driverSetAt`). The load's kg is `loadKgFromRefs`: the sum of its portions' kg and its whole orders' `totalWeightKg`, the same figures its stops, manifests and driver sheets show. A solver `kg` that differs by more than 0.5 kg is recorded in the `SCENARIO_CHOSEN` audit (`loadKgMismatches`) and logged.
- It creates one `RouteAssignment` per order (or split portion) per stop: `sequenceInTruck` (numbered from 1 within each load), `orderInStop`, `etaMin`, `serviceStartMin`, `departureMin`, `waitMin`, `cumulativeKm`, window flags and the `portionCases` / `portionWeightKg` / `portionLinesJson` columns.
- It sets `Order.status` to `ASSIGNED` (at least one part on a truck) or `UNSERVED`. It never touches orders that are already `DISPATCHED` or `DELIVERED`.
- It sets `chosenScenarioId` and the status by the loads: `DISPATCHED` (with `finalizedAt`) when every load of the version is already out, otherwise `READY` (PR3: before, always READY). The write is conditional (never over a superseded row). It then calls `refreshPlanFacts` with the driver notes and writes audit `SCENARIO_CHOSEN` (with `driverChanges`: truck, trip, from, to, reason, when any). It returns `{ driverChanges }`: `chooseScenario` passes them on (choose-scenario answers `driversChanged`, the count the screen's toast names: "N driver note(s)"), and the job's message adds "N driver note(s) (see the plan)" (on `RunJob.message`; the dispatch screens show a job's message only while it runs or after it failed). This happens on every optimize, not only when the dispatcher switches options.

**`refreshPlanFacts(tx, tenantId, runId, { driverChanges? })`** recomputes and stores on `RunPlan`:

- `reconciliationJson` = `reconcile(orders, planned, unserved)` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/reconcile.ts`). It checks:
  - uploaded = planned + unserved cases, in total, per SKU and per sales order;
  - every order appears exactly once (whole), or in portions that add up per line;
  - no order is planned for another customer or branch;
  - every unserved row has a reason;
  - every order of the scenario's scope (`orderIds` + `frozenOrderIds`) still exists. An order deleted after planning is a problem, so the plan cannot be DISPATCHED until it is re-planned (review F20).

  `ok` is false when any problem is found.
- `summaryJson` = `computeSummary` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/summary.ts`): orders served, partial and unserved; cases; service % per P1 to P5; trucks, loads, km, hours, utilisation, fuel, cost; revenue and margin served (null unless every order has a value); late orders; unserved by reason; loads by status; provider and solver info; `driverChanges`, the driver notes of the applied plan (`DriverChangeNote`: truck, trip, times, `from` and `to` with their names, reason `CLASH`, `INACTIVE` or `TRIP_GONE`, and for `CLASH` the trip that got the driver). `applyScenario` passes the new list (the key is left out when it is empty); a refresh after a load change keeps the list already stored. `createNextVersion` copies the summary with the notes; they are never read as evidence, and the next applied plan replaces them. A `parkedDrivers` list written by earlier builds of this PR is ignored, and the next refresh leaves it out.
- `changeSummaryJson`, for version 2 and later, = `computeChangeSummary`: orders added, assignments changed and unchanged, trucks unchanged, locked loads preserved, and the sentence shown in the blue bar.
- `totalOrders` and `unservedCount`.

It runs after `applyScenario` and after every load status change. It does not run after a driver change, because a driver does not change the plan facts.

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor D as Dispatcher
    participant UI as dispatch-client.tsx
    participant API as POST /api/dispatch/plan
    participant DAY as GET /api/dispatch/day
    participant SO as start-optimize.ts
    participant PS as plan-service.ts
    participant JOB as dispatch-job.ts
    participant SV as Solver /optimize-dispatch
    participant DB as Postgres via Prisma
    D->>UI: Click OPTIMIZE
    UI->>API: date, depotId, optimize true
    API->>PS: getOrCreatePlan
    PS->>DB: live RunPlan, or create version 1 as DRAFT INITIAL
    API->>SO: startDispatchOptimize
    SO->>PS: buildDispatchRequest
    PS->>DB: read orders, lines, customers, type profiles, trucks, frozen loads, TenantConfig
    PS-->>SO: request, preDrops, scope, blocking, warnings, unknownWeights, weightChanges
    alt customers without a location and allowMissingLocations not set
        SO-->>UI: 409 LOCATION_REQUIRED
        UI->>D: window.confirm to plan without them, then resend
    else lines without weight, a truck has a payload, allowMissingWeights not set
        SO-->>UI: 409 WEIGHT_REQUIRED
        UI->>D: window.confirm to plan them as 0 kg, then resend
    end
    SO->>DB: one transaction - intake lock, scoped orders still exist, applyWeightChanges with AuditLog ORDER_WEIGHTS_RESOLVED, RunJob QUEUED with requestJson, RunPlan OPTIMIZING
    SO->>JOB: scheduleDispatchOptimize, not awaited
    SO-->>UI: 202 with runJobId
    loop every 3 s while optimizing
        UI->>DAY: poll the day overview and plan status
    end
    JOB->>DB: RunJob RUNNING 20 percent
    JOB->>SV: POST with X-Solver-Token, up to 600 s
    SV-->>JOB: RECOMMENDED, MIN_TRUCKS, MIN_DISTANCE
    JOB->>DB: RunJob responseJson 80 percent
    JOB->>DB: one transaction - persistDispatchResult, RunPlan READY, applyScenario RECOMMENDED, refreshPlanFacts, RunJob SUCCEEDED
    JOB->>DB: AuditLog OPTIMIZE_SUCCEEDED
    Note over JOB,DB: on any error failJob sets RunJob and RunPlan to FAILED and audits OPTIMIZE_FAILED
```

### 3.7 Step 5: reviewing the plan

`PlanView` loads `GET /api/runs/:id/plan` (`apps/web/app/api/runs/[id]/plan/route.ts`), which returns `getPlanDetail` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/plan-detail.ts`). The same `PlanDetail` object feeds the Excel workbook and the driver sheets, so the screen and the paper always agree. The view polls every 2.5 s while the plan is optimizing, and is read again in place (`reloadSignal`) when the day screen saved a customer's pin or details (audit F13). A failed load of a plan already on screen keeps it, with the error and "Try again" (`planAfterLoad` in `plan-actions.ts`); only a plan that never loaded shows the error alone, also with "Try again" (third review of PR3: once `api()` stopped rejecting, a network error during an action replaced the whole plan, every load button included, until the page was reloaded). Fourth review of PR3: a load's answer is shown only when it is newer than the answer on screen (`createLoadOrder`), so a slow "Try again" read before a Lock never shows the load PLANNED again after the Lock's own reload, and a failed first "Try again" never puts the banner back over the answer of a later load; an older answer that arrives first is still shown, so answers slower than the polling never freeze the screen. "Try again" is disabled while a load newer than the answer on screen is on its way and while an action runs. The banner reads "Could not reload the plan: <message>. The plan below may be out of date." (`planReloadErrorText` adds the period the server's messages leave out). A driver change that fails (a refusal, or an answer lost after the server saved it) reloads the plan like a status change, so the driver list and the WhatsApp link show the driver the server has, or the banner with "Try again".

| Part of the screen | Where the data comes from |
|---|---|
| Header: version, depot, date, status, reason badge, "RECOMMENDED PLAN" or "(alternative)" | `detail.run` |
| Superseded, optimizing and failed banners | `run.status` and the latest `RunJob` |
| Blue bar "Changes vs version N" | `run.changeSummaryJson.text` |
| Yellow warnings | request and solver warnings from the chosen `ScenarioDetails`; the driver notes of `summaryJson.driverChanges` (`driverChangeWarnings` in `driver-links.ts`, fed with `getPlanDetail`'s own load rows and their marker, `driverSetByDispatcher`): "Driver changed by this plan: ..." for a trip that lost or changed its driver, until the dispatcher sets that trip's driver (another driver, **Keep**, or "No driver" on a trip that has a driver; it does not come back after; a trip the plan left without a driver keeps its note until a driver is picked for it, as a re-sent "No driver" changes nothing); "Driver picked by hand, not in this plan: ..." for a `TRIP_GONE` note while the plan has no load for that truck and trip |
| Driver clash warning | `driverClashNotes` in `apps/web/lib/dispatch/driver-links.ts` |
| KPIs | `summaryJson`: Orders served, Cases planned, Trucks · loads, km ("Road km", "Road km (N legs estimated)" when some legs are straight-line estimates - PR5, or "Estimated km"), Hours on road · paid (PR5: time on the road vs paid truck days), Avg utilization, Fuel (l · OMR), Operating cost (PR5: the whole day under the one cost model - every load of the version, carried locked loads included; its tooltip gives the breakdown; `*` = some loads were costed the earlier way, `costBasis` MIXED_LEGACY), P1 to P5 service (P1 or P2 below 100 % is highlighted), Late orders served, Revenue served, Margin served ("not supplied" when null) |
| Green or red reconciliation bar | `reconciliationJson` |
| Plan options table | every `ScenarioResult` of the run, each as the whole day with that option (`getPlanDetail` with `lib/dispatch/plan-options.ts`): **Trucks** (PR7, B3: the physical trucks - its new loads' trucks + the trucks of the locked, loading and dispatched loads it was planned around, counted from the stored loads, so options saved by an older optimizer are right too), **Loads** (PR7: kept + new, "(N new)" on a re-plan), **km** (PR7 review: the whole day - the kept loads' stored km + the option's new loads', rounded like the KPI, so the option in use shows the KPI's km; "(new X)" shows the new loads alone on a re-plan, with the number of estimated legs; before, only the new loads' km, under whole-day trucks, loads and cost), **Day cost** (PR5: the locked, loading and dispatched loads the option was planned around + its new loads, so the option in use equals the KPI; "(new X)" shows the new loads alone on a re-plan), **Preference cost** (PR7, N1: what RECOMMENDED also values, in OMR-equivalent - preferred hours + early delivery of P1/P2 + moved orders, `preference_penalties`; the tooltip splits it; an option saved by an optimizer that did not report the parts - before PR5, so every plan made on main - shows only its preferred-hours part, marked "(hours only)", and is compared as "preferred-hours cost", never as "preference cost": PR7 review), unserved, **Timing checked** (PR4: the optimizer's own check of the option - Yes, "No - N rule(s) broken", "Not verified", or "not checked" for options from before the check), **Search**: the search time and how it ended in plain words (PR6, `solverStatusText` in `lib/dispatch/solver-status.ts`: "best plan found in the time allowed", "... (search stopped at its limit)" for `ROUTING_PARTIAL_SUCCESS_LOCAL_OPTIMUM_NOT_REACHED`, "no plan found in the time allowed"; the longer wording in its tooltip; the raw OR-Tools name is no longer shown), **What it gains** (PR7, N1: `optionTradeoffs`; RECOMMENDED against the cheapest option that is a different plan, each alternative against RECOMMENDED: "vs MIN TRUCKS: P1/P2 delivered on average 40 min earlier, preference cost 19.5 OMR lower; but costs 35.4 OMR more, 87 km more, 1 more truck"; minutes are the average difference in service start over the P1/P2 stops both options serve; "Same plan as RECOMMENDED", "Same plan as MIN TRUCKS" or "Same plan as the other options" when the loads, trucks and stop order are identical); "In use" or "Use instead" (an option whose times break a rule can be used for review, but its trucks cannot be dispatched). The Excel SUMMARY repeats the table under **PLAN OPTIONS** (trucks, loads, day km and day cost with the new loads' part, preference cost or "preferred hours only" for an older option, unserved and the trade-off line) |
| Red "Times not verified" box (PR4) | `detail.feasibility`: the blocking violations per truck (up to 8, all in the Excel SUMMARY), the remedy (`timingRemedy` in `feasibility-view.ts`) and a **Re-plan** button. The remedy is "Re-plan …" unless a blocking violation sits on a LOCKED or LOADING load: a re-plan carries such a load over unchanged, so the box names it and says to put it back to Planned first ("Back to locked", then "Unlock"), then re-plan. Unlock is refused while a later load of the same truck is still locked or loading, so the box lists those loads too, latest first ("put loads T01 L2, T01 L1 back to Planned first, in this order"; second review of PR4). The box's Re-plan button is off, with the reason next to it (`timingReplanOff`), when nothing is left to plan or when every blocking violation is on a locked or loading load (a re-plan would change none of them); the plan's own Re-plan button stays on. Amber lines for warnings (cases planned with no weight, a truck corrected below its planned load, problems on loads already out). Each load row shows "Times not verified" when its truck-day fails (its tooltip says when the load itself must be put back to Planned, with the later locked loads of its truck), and Lock / Loading / Dispatch are disabled with the reason while the gate is on |
| "Changed after planning" (PR4) | `DetailStop.masterChanged` / `DetailLoad.masterChanged`: a pin moved more than 50 m, receiving hours, name or address changed in the customer master, or the truck's capacity changed, since the stop was planned. Windows are compared the way they were planned: a window that ends before it starts was planned as "any time" (`usableWindow` in `snapshots.ts`), so such legacy data is no change. The plan keeps what it was planned with; the note is shown under the stop and as a badge on the load, and a warning says what to do (re-plan a PLANNED load; unlock and re-plan a locked one). "current customer data" marks a stop planned before snapshots existed. **Access notes are not frozen**: like the driver's name and phone they are contact details, printed as they are now (the snapshot keeps the planned notes for the record) |
| Truck loads table | one row per `PlanLoad`: truck · load, driver picker with PDF and WhatsApp links and who chose the driver ("picked by hand" when `DetailLoad.driverHandSet`; else a **Keep** link when `driverPickLink` says so: an active driver on a load not yet out that the dispatcher can change, exactly when the server takes the re-sent driver as a Keep (`isDriverKeep`), [3.9](#39-step-7-a-driver-per-load)), status, depart to return, stops, cases against capacity, utilisation, km ("est. km" when some of its legs are estimates), time, fuel, cost (its tooltip: fixed, trip, distance, fuel, driver share with the paid minutes, overtime; `*` = costed the earlier way), action buttons. Expanding a row shows the loading manifest (SKU totals from `aggregateSkus`) and the delivery route (ETA, window, cases, SKUs, sales orders, km, "Part k of n" for split deliveries) |
| Unserved orders | the chosen scenario's `UnservedOrder` rows with the `REASON_TEXT` label and the reason message; "REST OF SPLIT" when the rest of the order is on a truck. A reason saved by an older solver with a raw status code in it ("... (ROUTING_FAIL_TIMEOUT).") is shown in plain words (`withPlainSolverCodes`, PR6) |
| Map | `apps/web/components/plan-map.tsx` (MapLibre) with `GET /api/runs/:id/load-geometry`, which asks the solver's `/route-geometry` for a road polyline per load and falls back to straight lines flagged as estimated (see **Plan map road shapes** below). Since PR4 it draws through the pins each stop was planned with and from the depot the plan was made from |
| Plan versions | every `RunPlan` of the same depot and date, with reason and change text, linking to `/t/[slug]/dispatch/plan/[id]` |

**Plan map road shapes.** The map draws each load as its road shape (solid) or, when it has none, as straight dashed segments between the stops; the dashed lines are only a drawing and change no km figure (the plan's km come from the solver's distance matrix at optimize time: "Road km" or "Estimated km").

- `GET /api/runs/:id/load-geometry` (`resolveLoadGeometries` in `lib/dispatch/load-geometry.ts`) asks the solver for 4 loads at a time (`callRouteGeometry`, 15 s per call) within one 20 s deadline. A load that fails or that OSRM cannot route is drawn straight on its own; the others keep their shapes. Two answers stop new calls: a clear "not configured" (web without `SOLVER_URL`/`SOLVER_TOKEN`, or solver without an OSRM URL), since every load would get the same; and a timeout, since routing is then hanging and the solver's `/route-geometry` (a sync handler in its 40-thread pool, OSRM timeout 30 s with 2 tries) keeps each abandoned call for up to about a minute after the web gives up. Calls already running may finish and cached loads keep their shapes, so a hanging OSRM costs at most 4 solver calls per request (review: without the stop it was 8 per request, 16 per page view with the automatic retry). Before, the route asked one load after the other and the first failure turned every later load straight, so a solver restart (every deploy) or a slow answer gave a whole day of straight lines.
- Each load's path is depot -> located stops in sequence order -> depot, one stop per sequence at its first order's **planned** pin (the stop snapshot; today's customer pin for rows planned before snapshots existed), from the depot the option was planned from (`ScenarioDetails.inputs`; the live depot for options from before inputs), exactly what `getPlanDetail` shows (PR4, review F08). The route and the map build it with the same `loadPath` (`lib/dispatch/load-path.ts`), so a pin corrected after planning neither redraws a locked or dispatched load nor makes the answer look stale.
- Each straight load carries a `reason`: `ROUTING_OFF` (Settings: distance provider Haversine), `OUTSIDE_COVERAGE` (the company is outside the shared Oman + UAE road map and has no OSRM URL of its own; Settings may still say OSRM), `NOT_CONFIGURED` (also: no `TenantConfig` row); no call is made for these three (`routingOffReason`). Then `NOT_ROUTABLE` (OSRM itself refused that path: `NoRoute` / `NoSegment`, sent as HTTP 400, or an empty route list), `ROUTING_ERROR` (solver or OSRM error, including network trouble between them such as "[Errno 113] No route to host", which is an outage and not a stop far from any road) or `TIMEOUT`; the last two are the ones a retry can fix. A server answering HTTP 400 to every request (a wrong own OSRM URL) also reads as `NOT_ROUTABLE`: the solver's message does not carry OSRM's code, so the web cannot tell the two apart. The rows keep their first fields (`loadId`, `truckCode`, `loadNo`, `estimated`, `coordinates`) and add `pointsKey`, the fingerprint of the path the row is for (`loadPathKey(loadPath(depot, stops))` in `lib/dispatch/load-path.ts`: depot, the located stops in order, depot; exact numbers, 16 hex digits). A load with no located stop away from the depot (none located, or all on the depot's pin) is `noPath: true` with `estimated: false` and no reason, whatever the routing: nothing is routed and it has no line. It used to come back as a road shape with no reason, so the caption counted it "on the road": with routing not set up it said "1 load of 2 ... The other lines follow the road network (OSRM)" instead of "not set up", and alone it said "Lines follow the road network (OSRM)" (review).
- Road shapes (never straight-line estimates) are cached in the web process for 12 h, at most 500 shapes and 300,000 points (about 5 MB), keyed by the routing namespace (the tenant's own OSRM URL, else the solver's default) and the exact coordinate list. The loads always come from the tenant's own run, so a tenant only gets shapes for its own points. The cache is per process (one web replica, [2.7](#27-background-job-model)); a restart empties it, and a moved pin is a new key.
- The screen (`components/plan-map.tsx`) draws only the depot and the stops while the shapes load, then the lines (`linesToDraw` in `lib/dispatch/plan-map-state.ts`: road shape solid, anything else dashed, no line for a load with no located stop away from the depot). A row counts only for the load it was made for: same load id and, when the server sent it, the same `pointsKey` as the map computes from the stops it shows. A load on screen without such a row is drawn straight. The caption comes from `roadShapesCaption` (`lib/dispatch/map-caption.ts`) over what is drawn (`drawnShapes`: one row per line drawn, a load without its row counted straight, `noPath` loads in no count), never over the answer's rows as such: "Lines follow the road network (OSRM)" only when every line drawn is a road shape; otherwise how many loads are straight and why, or "Road shapes could not be loaded - straight lines shown" with a **Retry** button (for `ROUTING_ERROR` and `TIMEOUT`, or a failed request). When some loads came back `ROUTING_ERROR` (or the request failed) the map asks once more by itself after 5 s; the cache makes that second call cheap. Loads that only timed out get the Retry button but no automatic second round, which would put another 4 abandoned calls on a hanging solver. The shapes are kept per run and stop list, so an answer asked for other content is never drawn.
- **An answer for other content than the screen is stale** (`answerIsStale`): a load on screen has no row for its stops as shown, or the answer has a load the screen does not. The shapes are always asked for after the plan was read, so the screen is then behind the server: another dispatcher's "Use instead" (`chooseScenario` → `applyScenario` deletes the PLANNED loads and makes new ones under the same run, and a READY plan does not poll), or the pin of a stop planned before snapshots existed moved (no `stopSnapshotJson`, so its path is today's customer pin: same load id, other path; a stop planned with a snapshot keeps its planned pin, see above). Asking for the shapes again would give the same answer, so the map reloads the plan instead (`onStale`, PlanView's `load`), once per plan content by itself (it takes the place of the automatic retry); new plan content asks for its own shapes, and when the reload brings the same loads the map asks for the shapes once more. **Retry** on a stale answer does the same. Until then those loads are dashed and counted straight. Review: the caption counted only the rows, so after a "Use instead" elsewhere every load on screen was dashed under "Lines follow the road network (OSRM)", and a load whose pin moved (then any stop; since PR4 only a stop planned before snapshots) was drawn solid along the other path.
- **Drawing waits only for the map's style (`style.load`, or a style already loaded), never for its tiles** (`createDrawGate`). Sources, layers and markers can be added once the style has loaded, whatever the base-map tiles do. Not the map's `load` event: in maplibre-gl 4.7.1 it fires only when every tile of the first view has loaded or failed, so with slow OSM tiles the map stayed empty (no depot, stops or lines) until the slowest tile came, and with a tile request that never answers (maplibre has no tile timeout) or fails late it stayed empty until the dispatcher moved the map, while the caption already said OSRM (review, reproduced in headless Chrome: with 3 s tiles the map drew at about 3.5 s, now at about 0.4 s; with hanging tiles never, now at once). Not `map.isStyleLoaded()` either: it is false whenever a tile or GeoJSON source is still loading. The map used to call `isStyleLoaded() ? draw() : once('load', draw)`: when the road shapes arrived right after the map fitted itself to the stops (new tiles loading) or while the dispatcher zoomed, the draw waited for an event that never came again, so the map kept the straight lines (or no lines at all) under a caption saying OSRM, until something else re-rendered it. This, and not the solver, is the likely cause of the "sometimes straight lines" seen in production. The component has no DOM test (jsdom is not a dependency); `tests/lib/dispatch-plan-map-state.spec.ts` tests the helpers, including the gate against a fake map that models maplibre's `style.load` / `load` / pending tiles, and holds the component to them with static guards. The legacy run-detail Map tab uses its own route (`route-geometries`, through the solver's `/route-geometry` since PR5, review F22), one truck at a time and no call after a failed one; it draws a straight 'fallback' tour (no answer, or the solver's own straight-line estimate) dashed.

**Three options and "Use instead".** The job always applies RECOMMENDED; MIN_TRUCKS and MIN_DISTANCE are kept for comparison. "Use instead" calls `POST /api/runs/:id/choose-scenario { scenarioId }` (`apps/web/app/api/runs/[id]/choose-scenario/route.ts`).

- `chooseScenario` (`plan-service.ts`) does every check in one transaction **after** locking the plan row (PR3, review F07): it is refused while the plan is OPTIMIZING or superseded (status or `supersededAt`), when any load was locked in this version (a frozen load whose `carriedFromLoadId` is null), and for an option that found no plan (`NO_SOLUTION`: 409 `SCENARIO_NOT_USABLE`; the screen shows "No plan" instead of the button). A "Use instead" queued behind a re-plan therefore gets 409 once the re-plan has superseded the version, and never writes it READY again.
- Otherwise it runs `applyScenario` on the same version. No new version is created, and `planDrivers` sets the drivers ([3.9](#39-step-7-a-driver-per-load)); the toast says how many driver notes the option left (`driversChanged`).
- While any action of the plan screen runs (a load change, Lock all, Use instead, Re-plan), every other one is disabled, so one dispatcher cannot race themselves (PR3). Two users racing get 409 "Plan is being saved - retry" when one waits more than 5 s for the other.

### 3.8 Step 6: load states

The rules are in `apps/web/lib/dispatch/load-state.ts` (`checkTransition`, `ALLOWED`). They are applied by `updateLoad` then `changeStatusTx` in `plan-service.ts`, reached through `PATCH /api/runs/:id/loads/:loadId { status }` (`apps/web/app/api/runs/[id]/loads/[loadId]/route.ts`).

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> PLANNED: applyScenario
    PLANNED --> LOCKED: Lock (planner)
    LOCKED --> PLANNED: Unlock (planner)
    LOCKED --> LOADING: Loading (planner)
    LOADING --> LOCKED: Back to locked (planner)
    LOCKED --> DISPATCHED: Dispatch (supervisor)
    LOADING --> DISPATCHED: Dispatch (supervisor)
    DISPATCHED --> COMPLETED: Completed (supervisor)
    COMPLETED --> [*]
```

| From and to | Button | Role | Extra rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| PLANNED to LOCKED | Lock, or "Lock all loads" (locks every PLANNED load in load-number order) | PLANNER | every earlier load of the same truck is already frozen |
| LOCKED to PLANNED | Unlock | PLANNER | no later load of the truck is frozen |
| LOCKED to LOADING | Loading | PLANNER | none |
| LOADING to LOCKED | Back to locked | PLANNER | none |
| LOCKED or LOADING to DISPATCHED | Dispatch | SUPERVISOR | every earlier load of the truck is DISPATCHED or COMPLETED, and the plan's stored reconciliation is `ok` |
| PLANNED to LOCKED, LOCKED to LOADING, LOCKED or LOADING to DISPATCHED | as above | as above | **(PR4) the truck-day's timetable passes the feasibility check** (below), and the plan's reconciliation is `ok` for LOCK and LOADING too (409 `NOT_RECONCILED`: locking freezes what later re-plans build on) |
| DISPATCHED to COMPLETED | Completed | SUPERVISOR | none |
| DISPATCHED or COMPLETED to anything else | none | none | refused: these loads are immutable |
| PLANNED to LOCKED, LOCKED to LOADING, LOCKED or LOADING to DISPATCHED | as above | as above | **(PR9) no order of the load was brought forward to a later day** (`carriedOrdersGate`, 409 `ORDERS_CARRIED` naming the customers and the day they went to): it is planned there now, so the load would deliver it twice. The remedy (`carriedLoadRemedy` in `carry-view.ts`, PR9 review) depends on what else the load holds and on its status: with other orders, re-plan this day (a PLANNED load), or unload the brought-forward cases, put the load back to Planned and re-plan (the re-plan leaves carried orders out); with nothing else, a PLANNED load is left as it is - it stays in the plan for the record and was never loaded - while a LOCKED or LOADING one **was loaded** (in NMWC's flow the trucks are loaded the night before): its cases are on the truck but planned on the day they went to, so the answer says to unload them back to stock, or tell the warehouse, before the loads of that day are picked, then put the load back to Planned (PR9 second review: it used to say "never loaded or dispatched" and "needs nothing" for these too). A re-plan of a day with nothing else open answers 409 `NOTHING_TO_PLAN` saying its orders were brought forward (`nothingLeftCarried`, with the same unload advice when such a loaded load exists), never "every load has left the depot" or "upload orders first". A load of the company's **today** is refused the same way when one of its orders was brought forward to tomorrow in the evening (owner decision on today's orders, [3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)); its words then say today: "Re-plan today (27 Sep) to plan its other orders without them.", or, when it was loaded, "Unlock it (put it back to Planned)" and unload those cases (`carriedLoadRemedy` with the load's day; `updateLoad` takes the clock). The way back to Planned is named with the plan screen's own buttons: a LOADING load has no Unlock (LOADING goes only to LOCKED or DISPATCHED), so its words say "Put it Back to locked, then Unlock it", and, since Unlock is refused while a later load of the same truck is still locked or loading, "A later locked or loading load of the same truck must be unlocked first" (review of the owner decision: a LOADING load was told to "Unlock it", a button it does not have). Stepping back and Completed are never refused |
| PLANNED to LOCKED, LOCKED to LOADING, LOCKED or LOADING to DISPATCHED | as above | as above | **(audit PR A5, second review; owner's rule "no item will be delivered without location") every customer of the load has a usable location now** (`locationGate`, 409 `LOCATION_REQUIRED` with `customerIds` and `customers`, message "T01 L1: 1 customer on this load has no usable location: K1. A load cannot be locked, loaded or dispatched while a customer on it has no correct location. ..."). The test is the planner's own, `locationBlocksDelivery` with the company's delivery area, on the customers as they are at that moment, not on the stop's planned pin: a stop is planned with a usable point, but a customer import can mark that point LOW afterwards (the file points elsewhere), and a changed delivery area can put it outside; "Use instead" and a failed re-plan's copy then hold such a stop too. The remedy (`noLocationLoadRemedy`) says to drop the pin on each one (ADD LOCATION, or Set location on the customer page), then RE-PLAN so the stops go to the new pins, after unlocking a LOCKED load (a LOADING one goes Back to locked, then Unlock), or to go without them: RE-PLAN leaves their orders unserved (a loading load's cases come off the truck). Until the A5 third review it said "try again" for a LOCKED or LOADING load, and trying again after the pin sent the load out with the stop, its links and the WhatsApp text still at the old point. **(A5 third review) and no stop is still planned at a point that was not usable when a new pin replaced it** (`stopsAtReplacedPoints`, 409 `STOP_PIN_REPLACED` with `customerIds` and `customers`, message "T01 L1: the stop for K1 still goes to its old point, which was not usable. A new pin was placed after this load was planned. A load cannot be locked, ... RE-PLAN so the stops go to the new pins.", a locked or loading load unlocked first, `replacedPinRemedy`): the stop's planned pin (`stopSnapshotJson`) is more than 50 m (`PIN_MOVED_M`) from the customer's point now, and a `CUSTOMER_LOCATION_SET` audit row (ADD LOCATION, Set location, or a customer import that wrote an exact pair over it) moved the customer out of that point's place while the point it started from blocked delivery (`locationBlocksDelivery` on the row's "before": LOW or outside the area and never confirmed, 0,0). The planned point's place is `PIN_MOVED_M` (50 m) around it, the distance at which this gate and the sheets' "New pin" note count a pin as the same place (A5 fifth review; it was the exact point, within 5 cm). Only the change that moved the customer out of it counts (A5 fourth review): a row that started there and ended there too moved nothing (a flagged point confirmed where it is: its own coordinates typed, or a hand pin a few metres off, which is how a pin confirms a flagged saved point, since a pin exactly on it is refused, `PIN_REQUIRED`), and of the rows that moved it the newest decides, so a point confirmed where it is, or set back by hand, and then corrected is an ordinary correction; a flagged point replaced by a pin more than 50 m away is refused, whatever came after. An ordinary correction of a usable point is not refused (frozen plan facts; the owner's decision of 28 Sep 2026, "Allow, driver gets new pin", [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner): the load can go, and the sheets and the WhatsApp text show the planned stop with the new pin noted). Verified points, and HIGH or MEDIUM points inside the area, pass as before, so NMWC's locked loads are not affected. Stepping back and Completed are never refused |

Around every change, `lockOpenRun` (`lockRunForWrite`) takes a row lock on the `RunPlan` and refuses superseded (status `SUPERSEDED` or `supersededAt` set) and OPTIMIZING plans. The transaction waits at most 5 s for that lock (for example behind a plan being saved) and then answers 409 "Plan is being saved - retry in a moment" (`PLAN_BUSY`), not a 500 (PR3). A version **without an applied plan** (no chosen option; only versions left behind by a failed re-plan before PR3) allows only Unlock (LOCKED to PLANNED), Back to locked (LOADING to LOCKED) and Completed (DISPATCHED to COMPLETED: no plan facts change and no reconciliation is needed), 409 `NO_PLAN_APPLIED` otherwise, so the day can be optimized again and its dispatched loads closed (review F03; the review of PR3 added Completed, which was refused, so a stranded version whose loads were all out had no action left). The `NO_PLAN_APPLIED` text gives advice the dispatcher can follow on that version: OPTIMIZE when a load is still PLANNED, unlock first when only LOCKED or LOADING loads could be freed. After the change:

- an order becomes `Order.status DISPATCHED` once every part of it is on a DISPATCHED or COMPLETED load and no part is unserved;
- when the version has an applied plan, it becomes `DISPATCHED` (and `finalizedAt` is set, kept from the first time) when every load is DISPATCHED or COMPLETED, otherwise `READY`. A DRAFT or FAILED version without one keeps its status: it never becomes READY without a summary and reconciliation (PR3, review L14);
- the audit row is `LOAD_LOCKED`, `LOAD_PLANNED`, `LOAD_LOADING`, `LOAD_DISPATCHED` or `LOAD_COMPLETED`;
- `refreshPlanFacts` runs.

"Frozen" means any status except PLANNED (`FROZEN`, `isFrozen`). A re-plan copies frozen loads exactly as they are.

**The feasibility gate (PR4, review F04).** LOCK, LOADING and DISPATCH (forward moves only: `isGatedMove`) run `timingGate` in `changeStatusTx`, under the plan's row lock. It recomputes the timetable check of the version from the plan's own facts (`loadFeasibilityInput` + `checkPlanFeasibility` in `lib/dispatch/feasibility.ts`; the stored `RunPlan.feasibilityJson` is only compared by its `inputHash`) and refuses the move with **409 `TIMES_NOT_VERIFIED`** (`{ error, code, truckId, truckCode, status, remedy, unlockFirst[], violations[] }`, message "Truck T01: the timetable is not verified … Re-plan to get a timetable that keeps every rule", or, when a blocking violation is on a LOCKED or LOADING load, "… put load T01 L1 back to Planned first ("Back to locked" if it is loading, then "Unlock"), then re-plan": a re-plan copies such a load unchanged, so re-planning alone would bring the problem back. `unlockFirst` also lists every later LOCKED or LOADING load of that truck, latest first, because Unlock is refused while a later load of the truck is frozen: "… put loads T01 L2, T01 L1 back to Planned first, in this order …") when the load's **truck-day** is not OK. Checked per truck-day over every load of the version, frozen copies included, from the plan's own facts (the snapshots, the stored rows and the optimizer's inputs and report). Only two checks read the master data now: the product's case weight for cases planned at 0 kg (`CAPACITY_KG_NEW_WEIGHT`, blocks) and the truck's capacity now (`CAPACITY_CHANGED`, a warning):

- cases per load (from its stops) against the truck it was planned on (`truckSnapshotJson`); **physical kg** (a split portion's own kg, else the order's) against that payload (review F01), with one rounding tolerance (`KG_ROUNDING_TOL` = 0.5 kg in `weights.ts`, the same as `applyScenario`'s kg cross-check and the workbook's Kg check). Cases **planned** with no weight are `KG_UNKNOWN`, a **warning** only: the dispatcher accepted them at optimize ("treated as 0 kg", review F02). "Planned with no weight" is read from the plan's rows, never from today's product master: a split part keeps the case weight each line was planned with (`portionLinesJson[].kgPerCase`); a part stored before that counts its 0-kg lines as unknown when its own kg shows it; a whole order's lines hold the kg it was planned with. When a case weight was entered under Products since and the load would now be over its payload, it is `CAPACITY_KG_NEW_WEIGHT` and blocks like any overload;
- each service start inside the receiving window the stop was planned with (`stopSnapshotJson`; a window that ends before it starts was planned as "any time"), and the stored `hardWindowOk`;
- the turnaround: each load leaves after the previous load's return + reload + loading per case × its cases, with the rules the later load was planned with (`truckSnapshotJson.rules`); first departure after shift start / depot opening / truck availability, returns before the depot closes and the availability ends (each load with its own rules), the whole day within the shift maximum and the loads-per-day limit;
- the optimizer's own report for the option in use (`ScenarioDetails.feasibility`, [4.11](#411-post-solve-stage)): VIOLATED blocks the trucks it names, UNVERIFIED blocks.

Rules: 1-minute tolerance (the solver rounds seconds to minutes). A problem on a load that has already left (DISPATCHED or COMPLETED) is history: a warning that never blocks, so it can never lock the truck's later loads for good. A problem on a LOCKED or LOADING load is flagged `frozen` (the remedy is to put it back to Planned first, with the later locked or loading loads of its truck, see above). A truck whose capacity or payload was corrected since planning below what a load not yet out carries gives the warning `CAPACITY_CHANGED` (the load keeps the truck it was planned with; on a LOCKED or LOADING load the warning names the same unlock order, e.g. "Put T01 L2, T01 L1 back to Planned first, in this order, and re-plan to use the new capacity."; the day screen also says the plan is out of date, [3.5](#35-step-3-the-day-overview)). Stepping back (unlock, back to locked) and Completed are never gated. A version made before the check (options without a report, loads without snapshots) is checked structurally (`LEGACY_STRUCTURAL_ONLY`: windows, overlaps, and for loads of its own successful job the truck and rules from that job's stored request) and blocked only on a concrete violation. `FEASIBILITY_GATE=warn` ([2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service)) lets the move through and records `timing: { ok: false, gate: 'warn', overridden, violations }` in the `LOAD_*` audit row; every gated move records `timing` there. The result is stored by `refreshPlanFacts` (after every applied option and load change) and `createNextVersion`, and shown on the plan screen ([3.7](#37-step-5-reviewing-the-plan)). With a loading time per case of 0 (NMWC today) practically every plan verifies; the gate matters once real loading minutes are entered (backlog #1).

### 3.9 Step 7: a driver per load

- **Default per truck.** `Truck.defaultDriverId`, set on the Trucks page (`PATCH /api/trucks/:id`, TENANT_ADMIN).
- **Per load.** The driver picker calls `PATCH /api/runs/:id/loads/:loadId { driverId }` (`null` = no driver), which runs `updateLoad` then `setDriverTx`.
  - `checkDriverChange` refuses a real change once the load is DISPATCHED or COMPLETED. Re-sending the current driver changes nothing, with one exception, **Keep** (`isDriverKeep`): the driver RouteIQ filled in (not hand-set) re-sent for a load not yet out is marked as the dispatcher's choice, with a `LOAD_DRIVER_SET` row (`kept: true`). The plan screen's **Keep** link sends it; the Driver list cannot (choosing the option already selected fires no change).
  - The driver must exist and be active. Audit `LOAD_DRIVER_SET`.
  - The load is marked as the dispatcher's choice: `driverSetById` (the user) and `driverSetAt` (fourth review of PR3, migration `20260926110000_plan_load_driver_set_by`). "No driver" chosen for a load that has a driver is marked the same way (who, when), so a driver note on that trip ends for good (`driverSetByDispatcher`; review of the simplified rules: "No driver" used to clear both, and a note that had left the trip without a driver came back after the dispatcher picked a driver and then "No driver"). "No driver" for a load that has none changes nothing (`checkDriverChange`: unchanged; the Driver list does not even send it): a note that left the trip without a driver ends only when a driver is picked for it. `createNextVersion`'s copies keep them. A driver is **hand-set** when the load has a driver and either column is set (`isHandSetDriver`: deleting the user clears `driverSetById`, `driverSetAt` stays); "No driver" is never hand-set, there is no driver to keep. `getPlanDetail` gives the screen `driverHandSet`, read from each load row. A driver set before that migration has no marker and counts as filled in by RouteIQ until the dispatcher presses **Keep**. No backfill: a `LOAD_DRIVER_SET` row names the load it was written on; a copy-forward copy links back to it (`carriedFromLoadId`), but a load `applyScenario` created does not, so a backfill would mark some of the choices made before the update and not others.
  - When one request carries both a driver and a status, both run in one transaction and the driver is set first.
  - The picker is filled from `GET /api/drivers` and offers active drivers plus the load's current driver.
- **After every optimize, re-plan or "Use instead".** `applyScenario` calls `planDrivers(trips, evidence, usable)` (`load-state.ts`) before it deletes anything. A **trip** is a truck and load number. The **evidence** is the loads of the version the plan is applied to, as they are right before the apply: for "Use instead", the version's own loads; for the re-plan job, the copies `createNextVersion` made of the previous version's loads, with any driver the dispatcher set on a copy between the new version and the start of its job (nobody can change them while the job runs), never the previous version's own loads. So both read the same kind of evidence, and the same evidence, with the trips in the same order, gives the same drivers and notes. The order matters only on a tie in pass 2 (below): the job and "Use instead" both read the optimizer's list, which is sorted by truck code, then trip. A trip's **evidence load** is the evidence load on the same truck and trip, if any. `usable` = the tenant's active drivers.
  - **Pass 0, frozen loads.** Locked, loading, dispatched and completed loads (any status but PLANNED) stay as they are, drivers included; their times take their drivers.
  - **Pass 1, hand-set drivers.** A trip whose evidence load has a hand-set driver keeps that driver and its marker, even when the new times overlap a frozen load or another hand-set trip of that driver: the overlap shows as the yellow clash warning (`driverClashes`). A filled-in trip at those times gives the driver up (pass 2). The marker is copied from the evidence row (its foreign key keeps `driverSetById` valid; it is never read from JSON). A hand-set driver who is no longer active is not kept: the trip goes to pass 2, and an `INACTIVE` note says why.
  - **Pass 2, drivers RouteIQ fills in.** Every other trip, the one that moved least first (how far its departure moved from its evidence load's; trips without an evidence load last; stable: on a tie - the same move, or no evidence load - the trip the optimizer lists first goes first: it lists by truck code, then trip), gets the first of these that is active and not on an overlapping trip already given out (a frozen load, a hand-set trip, or an earlier trip of pass 2):
    1. its evidence load's driver;
    2. the driver of the same truck's trip in the new plan that is nearest in time (the smallest gap between the two trips, the earlier one on a tie) and already has a driver - a frozen load counts, so the driver of a locked Load 1 takes the new Load 2;
    3. the truck's default driver.

    None of them: no driver, for the dispatcher to fill. So of two filled-in trips that now overlap with the same driver, the one that moved loses it, and a driver RouteIQ fills in never overlaps another trip of that driver.
  - **Overlap** is the planned times alone (`timesOverlap`: back at 10:00 and out at 10:00 is no overlap). Trip 1 and trip 2 of a truck share a driver unless their times really overlap, which a valid plan never has.
  - **The first optimization of a day** has no evidence: pass 2 with 2 and 3 only, so each truck's default driver in the optimizer's order, never one driver on two overlapping trips - the same drivers as before these rules.
  - **Driver notes.** A note only when a trip loses or changes the driver its evidence load had: `CLASH` (another trip of the plan got that driver at an overlapping time; the note names that trip), `INACTIVE` (that driver is no longer active), and `TRIP_GONE` (a hand-set driver whose truck and trip the new plan does not have). Filling a trip that had no driver is not a note. `applyScenario` stores the notes with the names in `summaryJson.driverChanges`, audits them in `SCENARIO_CHOSEN` and returns them (the "Use instead" toast and the job's message count them; the dispatch screens show a job's message only while it runs or after it failed, so after a re-plan the dispatcher sees the yellow notes). Every apply replaces the stored notes, also with none: a re-plan's version starts with a copy of the previous version's summary, notes included (shown while the copy is in use), and its job's plan replaces them. A load change keeps them. The plan lists a note in yellow until the dispatcher sets that trip's driver - any driver, the same one with **Keep**, or "No driver" when the trip has a driver - and never again after (the row's marker decides, `driverSetByDispatcher`; a note that left the trip without a driver stays until a driver is picked for it, since a re-sent "No driver" writes no marker), and a `TRIP_GONE` note while the plan has no load for that trip ([3.7](#37-step-5-reviewing-the-plan)). A `TRIP_GONE` note is information about this version only: nothing brings that driver back later. A later plan with that truck and trip fills it in like any other trip, and the dispatcher picks the driver again.
  - These rules replaced a multi-step design after the sixth review of PR3 (owner decision, [7.4](#74-review-leads-found-while-writing-this-handbook)).
- **Clashes.** A clash the dispatcher sets by hand is allowed but shown (`driverClashes`, `driverClashNotes`). A re-plan or "Use instead" keeps it when a hand-set trip overlaps another hand-set trip of that driver or one of their frozen loads: the same warning. A driver RouteIQ fills in never creates a clash.
- `setLoadDriver` and `changeLoadStatus` in `plan-service.ts` are exported single-purpose wrappers that nothing calls; the route uses `updateLoad`.
- **Deleting a driver**: since the audit PR "Intake and master data" (F20, owner decision 8) a driver is **always deactivated, never deleted** (`DELETE /api/drivers/:id` answers `softDeleted: true`, `defaultOfTrucks` and a `warning` naming the trucks that still have them as default driver; the Drivers screen's button says **Deactivate**). PR1 deactivated only a driver on a load or a legacy shift and deleted the others, but its count raced with dispatch: a driver assigned and dispatched between the count and the delete lost the load (`PlanLoad.driverId` was `ON DELETE SET NULL`), and a delete cleared every truck's default driver without a word. A deactivated driver stays on their loads and as their trucks' default; new plans skip an inactive driver. The database refuses a delete of a driver still on a load or still a truck's default (`NO ACTION`, migration `20260930093000_master_data_no_orphans`). Switching a driver to inactive with Edit gives the same warning.

### 3.10 Step 8: exports

| Export | Route | Built by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Excel master workbook | `GET /api/runs/:id/export/excel` (`apps/web/app/api/runs/[id]/export/excel/route.ts`) | `getPlanDetail`, then `buildDispatchWorkbook` and `tenantAssumptions` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/workbook.ts`, ExcelJS) | Offered for every dispatch plan (`PlanDetail.isDispatchPlan`), also one in which every order is unserved and that has no load (audit F16: it had no Excel at all; its UNSERVED - EXCEPTIONS, RECONCILIATION and ASSUMPTIONS sheets matter most then). Sheets: SUMMARY, LOAD PLAN, one sheet per load ("T01 - L1": manifest and route), SKU LOADING SUMMARY, UNSERVED - EXCEPTIONS, RECONCILIATION, ASSUMPTIONS. File name `nmwc-dispatch-<depot>-<date>-v<version>.xlsx`. A superseded version carries a "do not load or dispatch" line. Since PR4: SUMMARY has a TIMETABLE CHECK section; a plan (or truck) whose times are not verified carries **TIMES NOT VERIFIED** at the top and in the page header of SUMMARY, LOAD PLAN and that truck's load sheets; LOAD PLAN has Payload kg, **Kg check** (the load's kg against its stops and the payload, with the 0.5 kg rounding tolerance of the dispatch check: OK / OVER PAYLOAD / OVER PAYLOAD at the case weights entered since planning / MISMATCH / SOME CASES HAVE NO WEIGHT) and Timing columns; the route table has a **Changed after planning** column; ASSUMPTIONS shows the settings stored with the plan in use ("Settings this plan (vN) was built and costed with", including whether the plan was outside the Oman + UAE routing map, `PlanSettings.outsideCoverage`), or, for a plan from before they were stored, today's settings under "Current settings, at export time …" (review F08). The web server's own `OSRM_URL` never appears there: it only draws the legacy Map tab. Since PR6 (scenario tests S01-S05): SUMMARY gives **Invoices (sales orders)** - distinct sales orders from the reconciliation (`invoiceCounts` in `reconcile.ts`: fully on trucks, partly (split), not planned; case ignored; lines without a sales-order number counted apart) - then **Delivery orders (one per customer branch)** (was "Total orders", which read as 200 for a day of 320 invoices), **Stops on trucks**, and "Delivery orders served / unserved"; the solver line is **Route search** in plain words ("Best plan found in the time allowed: the search stopped at its time limit ...") with the option and search seconds, never the raw OR-Tools code; the stored summary warnings are kept once (`computeSummary`; the routing note came back on the answer and on the option, so it was stored twice); the route table's **Notes** column adds the customer's access notes ("Access: ...") and the order notes ("Note: ...") that the PDF prints; ASSUMPTIONS states no setting the plan did not use: the road time factor reads "not used in this plan: every distance is a straight-line estimate ..." for a HAVERSINE plan or one with no road leg (under the earlier rule an OSRM plan whose legs all fell back keeps the earlier wording), the estimate multiplier and speed are listed for plans with estimated legs ("on the N leg(s) that could not be routed on roads"), a fuel price with no truck km per litre reads "not used in this plan", and the preferred-window penalty carries the currency |
| Driver sheets PDF | `GET /api/runs/:id/export/pdf`, optionally `?load=<loadId>` or `?truck=<truck id or code>` (`apps/web/app/api/runs/[id]/export/pdf/route.ts`) | `driverPackModel` (pure) then `renderDriverPackPdf` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/driver-pack.tsx`, @react-pdf/renderer and `qrcode`) | One A4 section per load: stops in order with ETA, window, address, notes, SKUs, sales orders, a pin link and QR code, and a box for cases received and signature. No costs or margins. Google Maps route links come from `routeLinks` in `driver-links.ts` (at most 9 waypoints per link). Characters the built-in font cannot print become `[?]` (`pdfSafe` in `apps/web/lib/dispatch/pdf-text.ts`). A superseded version shows "do not use". Since PR4 the destination, hours and truck are the ones the load was planned with; a correction made since prints under the stop ("Location updated after planning: new pin …" with a link to the new pin, "Receiving hours changed after planning …"); a truck-day whose times are not verified carries a TIMES NOT VERIFIED badge and banner. Driver name and phone, and the customer's access notes, stay live (contact data). Served inline |
| WhatsApp message per load | no route: a `wa.me` link built in the browser | `whatsappText`, `whatsappNumber`, `whatsappUrl` in `driver-links.ts`; local numbers get the country code from `phoneCountryCode(tenant.country)` | Greyed out on a superseded version and while optimizing. A message copied from a superseded version starts with `REPLACED_LINE`. Like the PDF sheet (PR4 review): a truck-day whose times are not verified starts with `TIMES_NOT_VERIFIED_LINE`, and under a stop each change made after planning is printed ("! Location updated after planning …") with the corrected pin ("New pin - ask the dispatcher which one to use: …"); the stop pin and route links stay the planned ones |

Both export routes first ask `isDispatchPlan` (`apps/web/lib/dispatch/legacy-runs.ts`: the plan has loads, a RECOMMENDED option or a later version; the plan screen reads the same rule as `PlanDetail.isDispatchPlan`, `isDispatchPlanShape`). A dispatch plan gets the dispatch export above; since audit F16 this no longer depends on the load count, which sent a plan with every order unserved to the legacy route sheet. The PDF stays driver sheets only: a dispatch plan without loads answers 404 `NO_LOADS` ("This plan has no loads, so there are no driver sheets. Its unserved orders are in the Excel export."), and the plan screen shows no PDF button for it. Only legacy runs fall back to the old route sheet: `buildRouteSheet` (`apps/web/lib/exports/route-sheet-data.ts`) with `buildRouteSheetExcel` (`apps/web/lib/exports/excel.ts`) or `buildRouteSheetPdf` (`apps/web/lib/exports/pdf.tsx`).

### 3.11 Step 9: late orders and plan versions

**Recording a late order.** There are two ways:

- upload a small file in Step 1 (confirm then asks for the late reason); or
- use "Late order" on the plan (`late-order-dialog.tsx`; the reason field reads "Reason for adding this order late (after the cutoff, or to a day already planned)" since PR6). It calls `POST /api/dispatch/late-order` (`apps/web/app/api/dispatch/late-order/route.ts`) with customer code, optional branch and name, priority, reason (at least 3 characters) and lines (product code, cases, sales order). In one transaction the route:
  - takes `lockIntake` (the same per-tenant lock as confirm, batch delete and the optimize start). The transaction has a 30 s timeout (maxWait 10 s); waiting longer for the lock (e.g. behind a large confirm) answers 409 `INTAKE_BUSY` ("try again in a moment"), not 500;
  - finds the customer case-insensitively (twins resolve like the file intake), or creates a stub (location required). An inactive customer is refused with 409 `CUSTOMER_INACTIVE` naming it ("Reactivate it in Customers or use another code");
  - finds or creates each product; an inactive product is refused with 409 `PRODUCT_INACTIVE`. A new product starts at 0 kg per case (unknown) and is listed in the answer's `productsWithoutWeight`;
  - refuses (409 `DUPLICATE_LINES`) a line whose sales order + product is already confirmed for that customer and date (`IntakeLineKey`, looked up over every case-variant twin id of the customer and of the product, since which twin is preferred can change; the file intake compares by code the same way), and (400) the same product twice on one sales order. The 409 says to record extra cases of a confirmed line without a sales-order number or under a new one;
  - creates one `Order` with its lines: `status VALIDATED`, `isLate` = after the cutoff or a plan is already applied, `lateReason`, `lateRecordedById`. `lateReason` and `lateRecordedById` are stored even when `isLate` is false. Line kg = cases × the product's case weight (0 = unknown), `weightFromMaster = true` (it follows later corrections of the case weight). One `IntakeLineKey` per line with a sales order.

  `date` must be a real calendar date (`isoDateSchema`). After the transaction commits, it writes audit `LATE_ORDER_RECORDED` through `audit()` and answers with `replanNeeded`, `locationRequired` and `productsWithoutWeight`. Nothing is planned yet. The dialog then offers "Re-plan now?", or asks for the location first; it warns about products without weight.

**Re-plan.** `POST /api/runs/:id/replan { reason, note?, allowMissingLocations?, allowMissingWeights?, expect? }` (`apps/web/app/api/runs/[id]/replan/route.ts`) runs `replan` in `start-optimize.ts`. Since PR3 (review F03) **every known reason to refuse is checked before a new version exists**, and the new version starts as a usable copy of the previous plan (copy-forward):

1. a plan of another day than the screen's `expect` answers 409 `DAY_MISMATCH`; a superseded plan (status or `supersededAt`) 409; a legacy plan 409 `LEGACY_PLAN`;
2. a plan not applied yet is simply optimized again in the same version (`startDispatchOptimize`);
3. a plan that is optimizing or has a job running answers 409;
4. otherwise it probes `buildDispatchRequest(parent)` and answers 409 `LOCATION_REQUIRED` or `WEIGHT_REQUIRED` (same `gate` as optimize, with `allowMissingLocations` / `allowMissingWeights`), **409 `NOTHING_TO_PLAN`** when every order of the day is already on a locked, loading or dispatched load ("To change a load, unlock it first"), and 400 when the depot has no active truck - all before any version is created. The probe plans weights from the product master in memory only and saves nothing: a refused re-plan leaves the parent (still the live plan) with its order, load and portion kg unchanged. The weights are saved by the child's job when it applies its plan (step 7), never when it fails;
5. `REOPTIMIZE` becomes `LATE_ORDER` when `pendingLateOrderIds(parent)` is not empty (late orders of the day that the applied scope does not contain);
6. the solve admission is reserved (429 / 503 leaves no version behind), then `createNextVersion` runs one transaction under the day lock and then `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the parent, and refuses a superseded or OPTIMIZING parent (or one with a job running). It:
   - creates a `RunPlan` with `version + 1`, `parentRunId`, `reason` and `reasonNote`;
   - copies **every** `PlanLoad` of the parent - PLANNED ones too - (new id, `carriedFromLoadId` = old id, same status, driver and times, and since PR4 the same `truckSnapshotJson`) with its `RouteAssignment` rows (with their `stopSnapshotJson`; rows planned before snapshots existed copy as SQL NULL through `copyRowData`).
