**Benchmarks and scripts** (not run in CI):

- `apps/solver/scripts/bench_dispatch.py <stops>` benchmarks the dispatch optimizer. Its docstring says to keep the 300-stop run in the release checklist.
- `apps/solver/scripts/bench_replan.py` compares two solver versions on re-plans around locked loads (PR5; run it for any change to how locked loads are planned).
- `apps/solver/scripts/bench_day1.py` benchmarks the legacy PyVRP solver.
- `apps/web/prisma/check-priority-drops.ts --run=<id>` checks the priority drop penalty for one run.
- `apps/web/prisma/check-saved-readings.ts` (audit PR A5 sixth review) counts, per company and read-only, the confirmed customers with a typed or pasted location (`MANUAL_LATLNG`, `GOOGLE_MAPS_URL`, with a saved point) whose saved text (`locationInput`) the parser of the commit it runs on reads as not exact, by reason, and the short links it cannot read again. Since the A5 seventh review a text is judged only when the saved point was read from it: it reads back to the saved point (to 6 decimals), or it holds the saved point as numbers but reads otherwise today (read differently before A5, such as a directions link read at its start). The others are counted apart and not judged by their text: no text kept, a text that reads as another point, the saved numbers as RouteIQ writes them that read as not exact, a text that cannot be read and does not hold the saved point; with how many of them have fewer than 4 decimals as stored. It names no customer. Checked on a throwaway database with writes refused (`default_transaction_read_only`). Unit tests: `check-saved-readings.spec.ts`.
- `apps/web/prisma/smoke-driver-flow.ts` was deleted in PR1 (the driver app is retired). It had written a synthetic driver "SMOKE-DRV" with a live PIN and proof rows into the `nmwc` tenant; the owner clean-up is in [`docs/SECURITY.md`](./SECURITY.md).

### 5.4 CI (GitHub Actions)

**`.github/workflows/ci.yml`** runs on pull requests to `main`, pushes to `main`, and manual dispatch.

| Job | Runs on | Steps |
|---|---|---|
| `web` ("Web (lint + typecheck + unit + integration)") | ubuntu-latest, service `postgis/postgis:16-3.4` on 5432 | 1. checkout, `pnpm/action-setup@v4`, Node from `.nvmrc` (22; pnpm cache), Python 3.11<br>2. `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`<br>3. `prisma generate`, then `prisma migrate deploy`<br>4. `tsc --noEmit`, then `next lint --max-warnings 0`<br>5. `vitest run tests/lib tests/tenant-isolation.spec.ts`<br>6. solver venv + `pip install -r requirements.txt`<br>7. start uvicorn on `127.0.0.1:8000`, polling `/health` for up to 30 s<br>8. `pnpm --filter @routeiq/web build` (since P5 `build-upload-parser.mjs` after `next build`; ends with `remove-public-source-maps.mjs` since A1)<br>9. (A1) build output check, `tsx scripts/check-build-output.ts`: no `'use server'` in the app's code, no Server Action in `.next/server/server-reference-manifest.json`, no `.map` file under `.next/static`; (P5) `.next/upload-parser/parse.cjs` is there, holds only the parser, SheetJS's `xlsx.mjs` and Papa Parse, and reads a two-line CSV when forked<br>10. `pnpm start`, polling `/api/health` for up to 60 s<br>11. (A1) on the running app: `/_next/image?...&w=1` answers 404 (400 if the image endpoint were on), a browser chunk answers 200 and its `.map` 404<br>12. `vitest run tests/integration`<br>13. on failure, the last 200 lines of `web.log` and `solver.log` |
| `solver` ("Solver (pytest)") | ubuntu-latest | Python 3.11, `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `pytest tests -v` |

- **Job environment:** names in [2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service); the values are CI-only placeholders in the workflow.
- **No road routing in CI:** `OSRM_URL` is not set, so the solver plans on estimated distances, and `TEST_EXPECT_OSRM` is not set.

**`.github/workflows/osrm.yml` ("OSRM image")** runs on PRs that touch `infra/osrm/**` or the workflow file, and on manual dispatch. It has a 60-minute timeout. It only validates; nothing is published.

1. Builds `infra/osrm`.
2. Prints the image size and `/data/BUILD_INFO`.
3. Starts the server and waits for `/nearest/v1/driving/58.3920,23.5680`.
4. Runs `infra/osrm/smoke-test.sh`: Ghala → Ruwi/Seeb/Barka/Nizwa within km bounds, Ghala → Khasab through the UAE, and a 100×100 table.
5. Checks an IPv6-bound server (`OSRM_BIND=::`), as used on Railway.

**Other repository automation:**

- `.github/dependabot.yml`: weekly updates for npm (grouped: radix-ui, next-stack, react, prisma, types), pip (`/apps/solver`) and github-actions.
- `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`: asks for the four test commands above and the tenant-isolation checklist: `TENANT_SCOPED_MODELS` in `lib/tenant.ts`, a row in `tests/integration/cross-tenant.spec.ts`, and use of `tenantDb()`.

**State on 25 Sep 2026 (`gh run list`):** every CI run on `main` and on the PR #25–#30 branches passed from `8f744d9` (24 Sep, PR #25's second commit) onward, including `f2f4099` (PR #30), which took 7 min 38 s. Typical duration is 5–8 minutes. The first CI run of PR #25 (`b50484e`) failed. The CI runs of the open Dependabot PRs also failed; they last ran between May and August 2026, before the September fixes, and have not been re-run.

### 5.5 Database migrations

The migrations are in `apps/web/prisma/migrations/` (provider `postgresql`, `migration_lock.toml`). `prisma migrate deploy` applies them in folder-name order.

| Folder | Added in | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `00000000000000_init_postgis` | `4e31804` (11 May 2026) | `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS postgis` inside a `DO … EXCEPTION` block, so databases without PostGIS still migrate (it only raises a NOTICE). Nothing queries PostGIS |
| `20260511114105_init` | `4e31804` | The v1 schema. Enums: Role, CapacityUnit, OptimizationMode, OrderStatus, RunStatus, PaymentType, UploadBatchStatus, RunJobStatus, UnservedReasonCode, DistanceProvider. Tables: Tenant, TenantConfig, User, PasswordResetToken, Depot, Truck, Driver, Region, Customer, Product, UploadBatch, Order, OrderLine, RunPlan, ScenarioResult, RouteAssignment, RunJob, ManualBaseline(+Assignment), UnservedOrder, AuditLog |
| `20260511151524_runjob_timestamptz` | `4e31804` | `RunJob.createdAt/startedAt/finishedAt` become `TIMESTAMPTZ(3)` |
| `20260511190329_module_c_driver_tracking` | `ebc9aaa` | `Driver.accessPinHash`; `DriverShift` (+ `DriverShiftStatus` enum), `TruckLocation`, `DeliveryProof` |
| `20260924090000_nmwc_dispatch_mvp` | `b50484e` (24 Sep 2026) | The dispatch MVP schema. **Enum values added:** `OSRM`, `SUPERSEDED`, 11 new `UnservedReasonCode` values. **New enums:** `LoadStatus`, `PlanReason`, `CustomerType`, `LocationSource`. **New columns** on Customer (windows, type, location source), Depot, Order, OrderLine (sales detail), Product, RouteAssignment, RunPlan (plan versions), TenantConfig, Truck (economics), UploadBatch. **New tables:** `CustomerTypeProfile`, `PlanLoad` (with lock states). **Replaced key:** the RouteAssignment unique key becomes `(runId, truckId, loadNo, sequenceInTruck, orderInStop)` |
| `20260924091500_order_priority_from_file` | `b50484e` | `Order.priorityFromFile` (a priority in the file overrides the customer's for that order) |
| `20260924093000_osrm_default_provider` | `9426307` | Default `TenantConfig.distanceProvider` becomes `OSRM`. A data update moves HAVERSINE tenants whose country is Oman/UAE (or blank) to OSRM. It is a separate migration because an enum value added with `ALTER TYPE` cannot be used in the same transaction |
| `20260925090000_split_deliveries` | `961c537` (PR #27) | `portionCases/portionWeightKg/portionLinesJson` on RouteAssignment and UnservedOrder; `TenantConfig.splitDeliveries` (default true) |
| `20260925120000_dispatch_timing_per_case` | `423750e` (PR #29) | `TenantConfig.loadingMinPerCase` and `serviceMinPerCase` (default 0) |
| `20260926090000_retire_driver_app_scrub_secrets` | stabilization PR1 | **Data only, idempotent** (no schema change): ends ACTIVE `DriverShift`s, clears `Driver.accessPinHash`, removes credential-hash keys from `AuditLog` JSON, and writes one `SECURITY_CLEANUP` audit row per affected tenant with the counts. Irreversible for the cleared hashes (they have no further use: the driver app is retired) |
| `20260926100000_intake_line_keys` | stabilization PR2 | New table `IntakeLineKey` (unique sales-order line identity, see [3.15](#315-data-model-used-by-the-dispatch-flow)), backfilled from every existing line with a sales order. Where the same line was already confirmed twice, the oldest line keeps the key (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) and the copies stay as they are. Additive; rollback is `DROP TABLE` |
| `20260926100100_unserved_order_fk_no_action` | stabilization PR2 | `UnservedOrder.orderId` foreign key from `ON DELETE CASCADE` to `NO ACTION` (added `NOT VALID`, then validated) |
| `20260926100200_order_line_weight_from_master` | stabilization PR2 (review fixes) | `OrderLine.weightFromMaster BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (constant default: no table rewrite). Guarded, repeatable backfill: `true` for lines at 0 kg and lines whose kg is cases × the product's current case weight (within 0.01 kg), i.e. lines intake weighed from the master. Additive; rollback is `DROP COLUMN` |
| `20260926110000_plan_load_driver_set_by` | stabilization PR3 (fourth review) | `PlanLoad.driverSetById TEXT` (foreign key to `User`, `ON DELETE SET NULL`, added `NOT VALID`, then validated) and `PlanLoad.driverSetAt TIMESTAMP(3)`, both nullable: the dispatcher's hand-set driver ([3.9](#39-step-7-a-driver-per-load)). No backfill (existing drivers count as filled in by RouteIQ until the dispatcher presses **Keep**). Additive; rollback is `DROP CONSTRAINT`, then `DROP COLUMN` both |
| `20260927090000_plan_snapshots_feasibility` | stabilization PR4 (review F08, F04) | `RouteAssignment.stopSnapshotJson`, `PlanLoad.truckSnapshotJson`, `RunPlan.feasibilityJson`: three nullable `JSONB` columns with no default (metadata-only on PG 11+, a brief lock, no rewrite). Existing rows stay NULL: the app shows live master data labelled as such and checks those plans structurally; the check is stored at the next load change. Old code ignores the columns, so an app rollback is safe; historical versions are never rewritten. Additive; rollback is `DROP COLUMN` |
| `20260928090000_plan_load_cost_json` | stabilization PR5 (review F17) | `PlanLoad.costJson`: one nullable `JSONB` column with no default (metadata-only on PG 11+, no rewrite): each load's cost breakdown under the one cost model. Existing rows stay NULL and keep their stored `operatingCost`; plans holding them are labelled as costed the earlier way (`costBasis` MIXED_LEGACY). Copied by re-plans NULL-safe (`copyRowData`). Old code ignores it, so an app rollback is safe. Additive; rollback is `DROP COLUMN` |
| `20260928090100_keep_imported_service_times` | stabilization PR5 (review fixes) | **Data only, idempotent** (no schema change): marks `Customer.serviceTimeConfirmed = true` where it was false, `avgServiceTimeMin` is above 0 and not the 10 min column default, and the customer type has no `serviceTimeMin` - the unloading times earlier imports stored without confirming them, which PR5's rule would otherwise replace by the Settings default. One `UPDATE Customer` audit row per changed customer (`afterJson.by` names the migration), so the rows can be found and reverted. Old code reads the flag the same way, so an app rollback is safe |
| `20260929090000_order_carry_over` | PR9 (bring forward) | `Order.carriedFromOrderId`, `carriedFromDate` (DATE), `carriedToOrderId`, `carriedAt`, `carriedById`: five nullable columns with no default (no table rewrite), unique indexes on `carriedFromOrderId` and `carriedToOrderId` (NULL on every existing row, so they cannot conflict), and three foreign keys added `NOT VALID` then validated (the two order links `ON DELETE NO ACTION`, `carriedById` `ON DELETE SET NULL`). No backfill. Old code ignores the columns (it would show carried originals as open again), so an app rollback is safe. Additive; rollback is `DROP CONSTRAINT` the three keys, `DROP INDEX` both, `DROP COLUMN` the five |
| `20260930093000_master_data_no_orphans` | Audit PR "Intake and master data" (F03, F20) | Four foreign keys from `ON DELETE SET NULL` to `ON DELETE NO ACTION`: `Order.depotId` and `UploadBatch.depotId` (-> `Depot`), `PlanLoad.driverId` and `Truck.defaultDriverId` (-> `Driver`), each added `NOT VALID` then validated. No column, type or data changes. Every existing value is NULL or points at an existing row (the old keys guaranteed it), so validation cannot fail; checked on a copy seeded before the migration (an order with a depot, one with none, a file, a load with a driver, a truck's default driver): the migration applied, deletes of the referenced depot and drivers were refused, unreferenced ones deleted, and a tenant delete in the `cleanupTenant` order still worked. NO ACTION (end of statement) rather than RESTRICT so a delete of a whole tenant, which cascades to both sides in one statement, is not blocked. Rollback: re-add the four keys with `ON DELETE SET NULL` (old code never relies on NO ACTION) |
| `20260930120000_orders_always_have_depot` | Audit PR A5 "Owner rules" (owner decision of 27 Sep 2026: every order has a depot) | **Data, then schema, in one transaction.** Takes its locks first: `Order`, `UploadBatch` and `Depot` ACCESS EXCLUSIVE, `Tenant` SHARE, in a retry loop (NOWAIT for about 30 s, then at most 5 s of waiting per try), so a busy app delays it but never makes it fail and there is no lock upgrade. Before the lock step, in statements of their own, it turns off `statement_timeout` (and `transaction_timeout` on PostgreSQL 17 and later) for its own transaction only, so a time limit set on the database or the role cannot cancel the wait (A5 second review: set inside the lock step it came too late, because PostgreSQL starts a statement's timer when that statement starts; with a 3 s limit on the database the deploy failed with 57014, then P3009). Its very first statement sets its transaction to READ COMMITTED, so a default isolation of REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE on the database or the role cannot fail it either (A5 fourth review: the first query took the one snapshot before the lock step, and an order without a depot changed by the old app meanwhile failed the backfill with 40001, then P3009). Adds `Depot.historyOnly BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (no rewrite) and the CHECK `Depot_historyOnly_not_active` (`NOT ("historyOnly" AND "active")`, A5 fifth review): the previous version keeps serving while the deploy goes live, and its Depots screen, which has no history-only check, could switch the new depot on; the new app then offered it for late orders and new plans and had no screen to switch it off. Prisma's schema has no CHECK constraints and `prisma migrate diff` leaves this one alone (checked: no drift). Fills in every NULL depot, best evidence first, only from the row's own company: (a) an order from its file; (b) an order from the one depot of the plans that held it (stops, unserved rows, the orders an option was made for, a manual baseline, the order it was brought forward to or from; two depots = a conflict, no answer); (c) a file from the one depot of its orders, then (a) again; (d) both from the company's only **active** depot, else its only depot (the depot whose plan already held them, `ordersInScopeWhere` before A5); (e) the rest to a per-company history-only depot `NO-DEPOT` (`NO-DEPOT-2`, ... when the code is taken in any letter case), "No depot (kept for history)", inactive, at one of the company's depots' coordinates (0, 0 without one). One `DEPOT_BACKFILL` audit row per company that changed ([3.13](#313-audit-log)); the totals are also NOTICEs (Prisma does not print them). Then `SET NOT NULL` on `Order.depotId` and `UploadBatch.depotId`; the foreign keys stay NO ACTION. Safe on existing rows: rows with a depot are never changed, nothing is deleted, each step touches only rows still without a depot, so a second run changes nothing. Tested on a throwaway database with every step, the conflicts, a company with no depot, one with two or more and one with a taken code, while another connection is still writing an order and an order file without a depot and changing another such order, on a database with 1 s timeouts and REPEATABLE READ by default, which the migration waits for and then fills in (`tests/migrations/orders-always-have-depot.spec.ts`, [5.3](#53-test-suites); without the lock step that test fails with P3018, because the backfill cannot see the uncommitted rows and SET NOT NULL then finds them). NMWC has one depot and its orders already carry it: nothing is filled in there. Rollback: `DROP NOT NULL` on both columns; the filled-in depots, the history-only depots, the column and its CHECK can stay (old code ignores the column; its Depots screen gets an error if someone switches a history-only depot on). A local database that applied this migration before the A5 fifth review lacks the CHECK: reset it, or add the constraint by hand |

**Read-only checks before deploying the stabilization PR2 migrations** (run them on production and give the results to the owner; none changes data):

```sql
-- 1. Sales-order lines confirmed twice (real double intakes already in plans; the backfill keeps the oldest)
SELECT o."tenantId", o."deliveryDate", upper(btrim(l."salesOrderNo")) AS so, o."customerId", l."productId", count(*) AS copies, sum(l.cases) AS cases
FROM "OrderLine" l JOIN "Order" o ON o.id = l."orderId"
WHERE l."salesOrderNo" IS NOT NULL AND btrim(l."salesOrderNo") <> ''
GROUP BY 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 HAVING count(*) > 1 ORDER BY 2 DESC;
-- 2. Live chosen scenarios whose scope names orders that no longer exist (shown "not reconciled" at their next load change)
SELECT r.id, r."runDate", r.version, s.id AS scope_order
FROM "RunPlan" r JOIN "ScenarioResult" sc ON sc.id = r."chosenScenarioId"
CROSS JOIN LATERAL jsonb_array_elements_text(sc."detailsJson"->'scope'->'orderIds') AS s(id)
WHERE r.status NOT IN ('SUPERSEDED', 'ARCHIVED') AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Order" o WHERE o.id = s.id);
-- 3. Open order lines at 0 kg (resolved at the next optimize when the product has a weight; else WEIGHT_REQUIRED)
SELECT p.code, p."weightPerCaseKg", count(*) AS lines, sum(l.cases) AS cases
FROM "OrderLine" l JOIN "Order" o ON o.id = l."orderId" JOIN "Product" p ON p.id = l."productId"
WHERE l."weightKg" <= 0 AND o.status NOT IN ('DISPATCHED', 'DELIVERED') AND o."deliveryDate" >= current_date
GROUP BY 1, 2 ORDER BY 4 DESC;
-- 3b. Open lines the weightFromMaster backfill will mark (they then follow later case-weight corrections)
SELECT count(*) AS lines FROM "OrderLine" l JOIN "Order" o ON o.id = l."orderId" JOIN "Product" p ON p.id = l."productId"
WHERE o.status NOT IN ('DISPATCHED', 'DELIVERED') AND o."deliveryDate" >= current_date
  AND (l."weightKg" <= 0 OR (p."weightPerCaseKg" > 0 AND abs(l."weightKg" - l.cases * p."weightPerCaseKg") <= 0.01));
-- 3c. Pre-release batches whose raw-row hash is still checked (no-SO files for dates still open)
SELECT b.id, b."fileName", b."deliveryDate" FROM "UploadBatch" b WHERE b.status = 'CONFIRMED' AND b."deliveryDate" >= current_date;
-- 4. Customer and product codes that differ only in letter case
SELECT "tenantId", upper(code) AS code, upper("branchKey") AS branch, array_agg(code) AS codes FROM "Customer" GROUP BY 1, 2, 3 HAVING count(*) > 1;
SELECT "tenantId", upper(code) AS code, array_agg(code) AS codes FROM "Product" GROUP BY 1, 2 HAVING count(*) > 1;
-- 5. Loads whose orders weigh more than the truck's payload
SELECT pl.id, pl."runId", t.code, t."capacityWeightKg", sum(coalesce(ra."portionWeightKg", o."totalWeightKg")) AS kg
FROM "PlanLoad" pl JOIN "Truck" t ON t.id = pl."truckId" JOIN "RouteAssignment" ra ON ra."loadId" = pl.id JOIN "Order" o ON o.id = ra."orderId"
WHERE t."capacityWeightKg" > 0 GROUP BY 1, 2, 3, 4 HAVING sum(coalesce(ra."portionWeightKg", o."totalWeightKg")) > t."capacityWeightKg" + 0.5;
```

**Read-only checks before deploying `20260930093000_master_data_no_orphans`** (audit PR "Intake and master data"; run on production and give the results to the owner; none changes data):

```sql
-- 1. Must be 0 in every column (the migration's validation would fail otherwise; the current keys make it 0 by construction)
SELECT
  (SELECT count(*) FROM "Order" o WHERE o."depotId" IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Depot" d WHERE d.id = o."depotId")) AS orders,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM "UploadBatch" b WHERE b."depotId" IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Depot" d WHERE d.id = b."depotId")) AS batches,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM "PlanLoad" l WHERE l."driverId" IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Driver" r WHERE r.id = l."driverId")) AS loads,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM "Truck" t WHERE t."defaultDriverId" IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Driver" r WHERE r.id = t."defaultDriverId")) AS trucks;
-- 2. Damage F03 may already have done (information; the migration neither needs nor changes them): orders and files without a depot
SELECT "tenantId", "deliveryDate", count(*) AS orders, sum("totalCases") AS cases FROM "Order" WHERE "depotId" IS NULL GROUP BY 1, 2 ORDER BY 2 DESC;
SELECT "tenantId", status, count(*) AS files FROM "UploadBatch" WHERE "depotId" IS NULL GROUP BY 1, 2;
```

With one active depot the orders of check 2 were in its plan (`ordersInScopeWhere` before audit PR A5); with two or more they were in none. Since audit PR A5 the migration `20260930120000_orders_always_have_depot` gives every one of them a depot (below), and the database refuses an order or file without one.

**Read-only checks before deploying `20260930120000_orders_always_have_depot`** (audit PR A5; run on production and give the results to the owner; none changes data). The migration cannot fail on these rows; the checks show what it will fill in:

```sql
-- 1. Orders and files without a depot, per company (no rows = nothing to fill in; NMWC should show none)
SELECT o."tenantId", count(*) AS orders_without_depot, count(*) FILTER (WHERE b."depotId" IS NOT NULL) AS from_their_file
FROM "Order" o LEFT JOIN "UploadBatch" b ON b.id = o."uploadBatchId" AND b."tenantId" = o."tenantId"
WHERE o."depotId" IS NULL GROUP BY 1;
SELECT "tenantId", status, count(*) AS files_without_depot FROM "UploadBatch" WHERE "depotId" IS NULL GROUP BY 1, 2;
-- 2. The depots of those companies: one active depot (or one depot) = the rest goes there; none, or two or more = to NO-DEPOT
--    (a company with no depot at all has no row here)
SELECT d."tenantId", count(*) FILTER (WHERE d.active) AS active_depots, count(*) AS depots, bool_or(upper(d.code) = 'NO-DEPOT') AS no_depot_code_taken
FROM "Depot" d
WHERE d."tenantId" IN (SELECT "tenantId" FROM "Order" WHERE "depotId" IS NULL UNION SELECT "tenantId" FROM "UploadBatch" WHERE "depotId" IS NULL)
GROUP BY 1;
-- 3. After the deploy: what was done, per company (also on the Audit log page, event "Depot filled in ...")
SELECT "tenantId", "afterJson" FROM "AuditLog" WHERE action = 'DEPOT_BACKFILL';
```

**Rules** (`docs/admin.md`, `CLAUDE.md` §14):

- Never edit a historical migration; add a new one.
- For destructive changes, use expand → migrate code → contract over two deploys.
- Take a manual backup before every deploy that carries a migration.

### 5.6 Deployment on Railway

The Railway project is **`routeiq`**, environment **`production`**, region EU West (Amsterdam). The state below comes from `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md` (checked 24 Sep 2026).

| Service | Source | Network |
|---|---|---|
| `web` | GitHub `main`, repo root, Nixpacks | the only public service (domain listed in `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md`) |
| `solver` | GitHub `main`, root `/apps/solver`, Dockerfile | private only: `solver.railway.internal` |
| `routeiq-osrm` | GitHub `main`, root `/infra/osrm`, watch path `/infra/osrm/**` | private only: `routeiq-osrm.railway.internal:5000`. No authentication, so it must never be public |
| `Postgres` | image `ghcr.io/railwayapp-templates/postgres-ssl:18`, volume `postgres-volume` (4.6 GB, about 220 MB used) | private `postgres.railway.internal`, **plus a public TCP proxy** (whether to keep it is an open follow-up) |

**Service settings live in the Railway dashboard.** Railway stops reading `railway.json` on 1 Dec 2026, so PR #26 (commit `4bc60d8`, merged 24 Sep 2026 as `af1a7fb`) deleted both remaining files, `apps/web/railway.json` and `apps/solver/railway.json` (`docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md`: "both files deleted"). `infra/osrm/railway.json` never reached `main`: it was added in `1a5c42f` and removed again in `ec619a4` on the PR #25 branch, because services created after 28 Aug 2026 can no longer opt into config files (`docs/OSRM_SETUP.md`). If a service is ever recreated, these values must be re-entered by hand.

| Service | Setting | Value |
|---|---|---|
| web | Build command | `corepack enable && pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm --filter @routeiq/web build` (the `build` script is `prisma generate && next build && node scripts/build-upload-parser.mjs && node scripts/remove-public-source-maps.mjs`; the last step since A1, the upload parser bundle `.next/upload-parser/parse.cjs` since P5, made with esbuild, a dev dependency) |
| web | Pre-deploy command | `pnpm --filter @routeiq/web db:migrate:deploy`, i.e. `prisma migrate deploy`. A failed migration stops the deploy, and the previous version keeps serving |
| web | Start command | `pnpm --filter @routeiq/web start` (`next start`, run in `apps/web`: since P5 it forks `.next/upload-parser/parse.cjs` from there for each upload) |
| web | Healthcheck | `/api/health`, timeout 300 s |
| web | Restart policy | On Failure (10 retries) |
| solver | Builder / start | `apps/solver/Dockerfile` (`python:3.11-slim`); CMD `uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port ${PORT}` |
| solver | Healthcheck | `/health` |
| routeiq-osrm | Builder | Dockerfile `/infra/osrm/Dockerfile` |
| routeiq-osrm | Healthcheck | `/nearest/v1/driving/58.3920,23.5680`, timeout 300 s |
| routeiq-osrm | Variables | `OSRM_BIND=::`, `PORT=5000` (pinned so the private URL never changes); `MAP_REFRESH` for the monthly refresh |

- **Automatic deploys from `main`.** `web` and `solver` redeploy from `main` (the `janitor-loop.ts` comment says "every push to main redeploys web"). `routeiq-osrm` rebuilds only when `infra/osrm/**` changes; a rebuild takes about 10 minutes. Web and solver **build independently**.
- **Variables.** The names are in [2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service); values live only in Railway.
  - **web:** `DATABASE_URL`, `NEXTAUTH_SECRET`, `NEXTAUTH_URL`, `SOLVER_URL`, `SOLVER_TOKEN`, plus the optional ones.
  - **solver:** `SOLVER_TOKEN`, `OSRM_URL` pointing at the private `routeiq-osrm.railway.internal:5000`, and `PORT` (injected by Railway).
- **Private networking.** Services are reached at `<service>.railway.internal`, which resolves over IPv4 and IPv6. OSRM and the solver have **no public domain and must never get one**.

**OSRM service** (`infra/osrm/`, `docs/OSRM_SETUP.md`):

- **Image.** `infra/osrm/Dockerfile` pins `osrm-backend:v26.9.0`. At **image build time** it downloads the Geofabrik GCC extract, clips it to Oman + UAE (bbox `51.5,16.4,60.0,26.6`), builds a car-profile MLD graph and writes `/data/BUILD_INFO`. The running container needs no volume and about 0.5–1 GB RAM.
- **Truck times:** the solver multiplies OSRM car durations by `roadTimeFactor` (default 1.25).
- **Option B, VM or on-prem:** `infra/osrm/docker-compose.yml` runs the same image bound to `127.0.0.1:5000`.
- **Monthly map refresh.** On Railway, set `MAP_REFRESH` to the current month (for example `2026-10`) and deploy; a plain redeploy can reuse cached layers and ship last month's map. On a Docker host, run `docker compose … build --pull --no-cache`.
- **Rollback.** Set `OSRM_URL` on the solver to an **empty value**; do not delete it. Plans fall back to estimated km with warnings. The OSRM service holds no RouteIQ data.

**Production cut-over and rollback** (`docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md`):

1. Take a manual Postgres backup (Postgres → Backups → New backup). This is the rollback point.
2. Merge to `main`. Web's pre-deploy step runs the migrations.
3. Wait until the **solver** deployment is Active before anyone plans.
4. Verify the deploy ([5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy)).

- **A migration fails with `P3009`.** Nothing is half-applied, because each migration runs in one transaction (checked for audit PR A5 with this repo's Prisma 5.22 on PostgreSQL 16: a failure in the last statement of a `migration.sql` rolled back its first one, a lock taken early was held to the end, an earlier migration of the same deploy stayed applied; the failing deploy answered `P3018` and left a `_prisma_migrations` row without `finished_at`, so the next deploy answered `P3009` until the resolve below; a `RAISE NOTICE` of the failing migration was not printed). From your machine, with `DATABASE_URL` set to the Postgres **public** URL (`DATABASE_PUBLIC_URL`, through the TCP proxy), run `pnpm --filter @routeiq/web exec prisma migrate resolve --rolled-back <migration_name>`, then redeploy.
- **Rollback is one-way once the pre-deploy step has run.** Restore the pre-merge backup, then redeploy the previous commit. **Never redeploy old code onto a migrated database.**
- **Node version on Railway (A1).** Nixpacks installs the Node major given by the service variable `NIXPACKS_NODE_VERSION` when it is set; otherwise the one `engines.node` in the root `package.json` names, now `22.x`; otherwise `.nvmrc`. Before A1 `engines.node` was `>=20.0.0`, and which major Nixpacks chose for it is not recorded in the repo. After the A1 deploy, check the setup line of the web build log (it names the package, for example `nodejs_22`); if it does not say 22, set `NIXPACKS_NODE_VERSION=22` on `web` and redeploy. Build on staging first where one exists (assessment risk for PR 1).
- **Web memory for uploads (P5).** Each file being read is a separate process next to the web process: about `UPLOAD_WORKER_MAX_HEAP_MB` (512) + 100-150 MB at most for a file near the caps, about 60-70 MB for NMWC's real files, and `UPLOAD_PARSE_CONCURRENCY` (2) of them at once. So the web service needs its own memory plus up to about 1.3 GB while two large files are read. The web process itself holds the rows of each file it handles: at most 200 cells a row and 2,500,000 cells per file, kept compact (P5 second review; the parser's answer is also capped at 128 MB, but that byte cap alone did not bound the rows: a CSV row of a million columns held 71 MB here). Measured: 20-22 MB for the widest legal files (12,400 rows x 200 columns, 50,000 x 49), 26 MB for NMWC's shape at the row limit, under 1 MB for NMWC's real files; main, which read files in the web process, held 142-147 MB for the widest ones. The web service's memory limit is not recorded in the repo: check it and the usage in Railway (web → Metrics) before the P5 deploy, and raise the limit or set `UPLOAD_PARSE_CONCURRENCY=1` if it is lower. If the container runs out anyway, the parser asks Linux to kill it first (`oom_score_adj` 1000). A Railpack move or a changed start command must keep `next start` running in `apps/web`, where it finds the parser bundle.
- **Planned later:** move `web` from Nixpacks (deprecated) to Railpack. Railpack reads `RAILPACK_NODE_VERSION`, `engines.node`, `.nvmrc` or `.node-version`, all 22 since A1; that change should still fix the root `packageManager` field.

### 5.7 Backups

- **Current backups.** Railway volume backups of `postgres-volume` run **daily (kept 6 days)** and **weekly (kept 27 days)** since 24 Sep 2026. Before then there were **no backups**. Manual backups were taken before the September restart and before the PR #25 merge.
- **Planned, not implemented:** a daily `pg_dump` to Cloudflare R2 (or similar) with 30-day retention and a quarterly restore drill (`docs/admin.md`, `CLAUDE.md` §14).
- **May 2026 outage** (`docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md`). Postgres was down from 12 May to 24 Sep 2026. The container was killed abruptly, and the stale `postmaster.pid` it left most likely blocked restarts until Railway gave up. The redeploy of `postgres-ssl:18` worked because its wrapper deletes a stale lock file. Disk full, a major-version mismatch and corruption were ruled out. What killed the container is unknown. `web` went down with it, because it cannot start without the database.

### 5.8 Health endpoint

Since audit PR4 (F15, owner decision 5) liveness and dispatch readiness are separate. Both run on the Node runtime with `force-dynamic`, are public (`middleware.ts` excludes `/api/health*` from auth) and answer `Cache-Control: no-store`.

- **`GET /api/health` = readiness** (`apps/web/app/api/health/route.ts`, `lib/health.ts`), Railway's deploy health check. It never starts an optimization.
- **`GET /api/health/live` = liveness** (`app/api/health/live/route.ts`): `{ ok: true, service: 'web' }`, HTTP 200 whenever the process answers; no database or solver call.

| Field of `/api/health` | Value | How it is computed |
|---|---|---|
| `status` | `ready` / `degraded` / `not_ready` | `overallReadiness`: `not_ready` (HTTP **503**) when the database is down or dispatch is `misconfigured`; `degraded` (HTTP **200**) when dispatch is only `degraded`; else `ready` (200). A deploy fails only on a definite fault, never because the solver is briefly unreachable |
| `ok` | `true` / `false` | `status === 'ready'`. Monitoring alerts on `ok: false` (a degraded answer is 200 with `ok: false`) |
| `db` | `up` / `down` | `checkDb()`: Prisma `SELECT 1` |
| `dispatch` | `{ status, reason, message }` | `checkDispatchReadiness`: `SOLVER_URL` missing (`SOLVER_URL_MISSING`) or `SOLVER_TOKEN` missing (`SOLVER_TOKEN_MISSING`) on web, (fourth review of audit PR4) a `SOLVER_URL` no call can use (`SOLVER_URL_INVALID`: no `http://` in front, `http//`, a hidden character or a space in it, another scheme, a user name and password, a `?` or `#` - it used to fail inside `fetch` and read as `SOLVER_UNREACHABLE`, 200, while every optimization failed), or (third review of audit PR4) a `SOLVER_TOKEN` that is not plain ASCII (`SOLVER_TOKEN_INVALID`: a hidden zero-width space, curly quotes, a non-breaking space or a line break inside it - no call can send it as the solver holds it) → `misconfigured` without calling the solver; else `GET ${SOLVER_URL}/ready` with `X-Solver-Token` and a 4 s timeout for the whole answer, body included (third review of audit PR4: it used to stop at the headers, so a body that stopped half-way kept `/api/health` waiting for minutes), without following a redirect (third review of audit PR4: a 3xx → `degraded` `SOLVER_URL_REDIRECTS`, never `ready`, because the optimize call does not follow one either), both values read by `solverEnv()` exactly as the optimize and road-line calls read them (review of audit PR4: the check used to trim them and drop a trailing slash while the calls did not, so `http://solver:8000/` or a token with a line break after it was `ready` while every optimization failed): 200 `ok: true` → `ready`; 401 → `misconfigured` (`SOLVER_TOKEN_REJECTED`: the two tokens differ); 403 → `degraded` (`SOLVER_ERROR`; second review of audit PR4: the solver never answers 403, so it came from a proxy, a firewall or a wrong host in front of it and the token was never checked); a `detail` "Solver not configured" (500) → `misconfigured` (`SOLVER_NOT_CONFIGURED`: the solver has no token); no answer or timeout → `degraded` (`SOLVER_UNREACHABLE`); 404 / 405 → `degraded` (`SOLVER_READY_UNSUPPORTED`: a solver deployed before `/ready`); any other answer → `degraded` (`SOLVER_ERROR`). `message` is a plain sentence; never a URL, a token or a solver body |
| `solver` | `up` / `down` / `misconfigured` | Kept for monitoring: `up` when dispatch is `ready`, `misconfigured` when it is, else `down` |
| `routing` | `{provider, status}` or `null` | Passed through from the solver's `/ready` (its cached `routing_status()`). `status` is `up`, `down` or `not_configured`. `null` when the solver did not answer `/ready`. **Never affects `ok`** |
| `email` | `configured` / `not_configured` | (PR1) whether `RESEND_API_KEY` is set, i.e. whether password-reset emails are sent. **Never affects `ok`** |

Before audit PR4 the check was the solver's public `/health` only: with `SOLVER_TOKEN` missing or wrong on web, health was `ok: true` while every optimization failed at once ("SOLVER_TOKEN not set" / 401); and an unreachable solver answered 503.

The solver's `GET /health` (`health()` / `routing_status()` in `apps/solver/main.py`) returns `{"ok": true, "routing": …}` and stays public (Railway's solver health check). Its `GET /ready` (audit PR4) runs `_check_token` and answers `{"ok": true, "service", "version", "max_concurrent_dispatch", "routing"}`: no optimization, no dispatch slot, no request body. With `OSRM_URL` empty routing is `{"provider":"HAVERSINE","status":"not_configured"}`. With `OSRM_URL` set it probes `${OSRM_URL}/nearest/v1/driving/58.3920,23.5680` with a 2 s timeout, caches the result for 60 s, and never returns the URL.

- **Monitoring recommendation** (`docs/OSRM_SETUP.md`): alert when `ok` is false (the `dispatch.reason` says why) and when `routing.status` is not `up` for more than 10 minutes. Nothing in the repo implements these alerts.
- **Startup log** (`lib/startup-checks.ts`): a production web without `SOLVER_URL` or `SOLVER_TOKEN` logs a `[config]` error at boot (audit PR4), and so does one whose `SOLVER_TOKEN` is not plain ASCII (third review of audit PR4) or whose `SOLVER_URL` no call can use (fourth review of audit PR4).
- **Live check on 25 Sep 2026** (before audit PR4): production returned `{"ok":true,"db":"up","solver":"up","routing":{"provider":"OSRM","status":"up"}}`, and an unauthenticated call to `/api/cron/janitor` returned 401.

### 5.9 Job failures and the janitor

The normal job path is in [2.7](#27-background-job-model) and [3.6](#36-step-4-optimize).

- **Failure.** `failJob()` in `apps/web/lib/jobs/dispatch-job.ts` sets the `RunJob` to `FAILED` with a message of at most 500 characters and `errorJson.reason` of `SOLVER_ERROR` or `UNKNOWN`, only while the job is still QUEUED or RUNNING. It sets `RunPlan` to `FAILED` only while the plan is `OPTIMIZING` with this job as `currentJobId` (PR3: never over READY or SUPERSEDED) and writes audit `OPTIMIZE_FAILED`. Since audit PR4 (F09) these three writes are one transaction under the plan row lock; if it fails, nothing changed and the janitor fails the job and its plan later. A re-plan version keeps the copy of the previous plan it started with ([3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)).
  - A solver 404 becomes "The route optimizer is being updated. Try again in a minute."
  - A solver 503 (more than `MAX_CONCURRENT_DISPATCH` solves at once) becomes "The route optimizer is busy with other plans right now. Optimize again in a minute." (PR3).
  - A solver 504 (`SolveAborted`) passes through the solver's `detail` message.
  - **Stale result** (PR3): a result for a version that is no longer `OPTIMIZING` with this job (superseded, reaped, another job) is not applied; the job becomes FAILED "Stale result: the plan changed while this optimization ran, so nothing was applied." with `errorJson.reason = 'STALE_RESULT'` (since audit PR4 in one transaction with its `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` row).
- **Poll and debug.** `GET /api/runs/[id]/status` returns the run plus its current job (status, `progressPct`, message, `errorJson`). `GET /api/runs/[id]/jobs/[jobId]/debug` downloads `requestJson`, `responseJson` and `errorJson` (SUPERVISOR and above since PR1). `RunJobStatus.CANCELLED` exists in the enum, but no code sets it.
- **Janitor.** `reapStuckJobs()` in `apps/web/lib/jobs/optimize-job.ts` together with `reapStaleShifts()` in `apps/web/lib/jobs/shift-janitor.ts`:
  - **Stuck jobs.** It fails any job `RUNNING` with `startedAt` older than **`STUCK_JOB_MS` = 15 min**, or `QUEUED` with `createdAt` older than 15 min. The comparison happens in Postgres with `NOW()`.
  - **Live jobs are skipped:** jobs still in the in-flight map are left alone.
  - **Safe update:** a conditional `updateMany`, so a job that just succeeded is not failed. **One transaction per job** (audit PR4, F09): the plan row lock (5 s lock timeout: a plan being saved is left to the next sweep), the job, its plan and the audit row. Before, the job and the plan were two writes: when the second failed the plan stayed OPTIMIZING behind a FAILED job, every later sweep skipped it (it looks only at QUEUED and RUNNING jobs), OPTIMIZE answered 202 with the dead job and started nothing, and RE-PLAN answered 409 "optimizing" - on a re-plan version even its locked loads could not be dispatched.
  - **Effect:** the job gets `errorJson.reason = 'STUCK'` and the message "No result after 15 minutes (the server restarted during the optimization). Optimize again." Its plan becomes `FAILED` only if it is still `OPTIMIZING` with this job as `currentJobId` (or none; PR3), and an `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` audit row is written. A re-plan version keeps its copied plan, usable. A job waiting for a solver slot is in the in-flight map, so it is never reaped while it waits.
  - **Stuck plans (audit PR4, F09):** after the jobs, `repairStuckPlans()` puts back to `FAILED` every plan `OPTIMIZING` whose current job is not QUEUED or RUNNING (it ended, or is missing; a plan with no job at all only 1 minute after it was created), with an `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` row, reason `STUCK_PLAN`, `repairedBy: JANITOR` (`repairEndedJobPlan`, one transaction under the plan row lock). This mends the rows the old janitor stranded. OPTIMIZE and RE-PLAN of such a plan do the same first, then start a new job (never a 202 with the dead job). The sweep answers `{ reaped, repaired }`.
  - **Reset stuck plan (audit PR4, owner decision 17):** a SUPERVISOR or above can fail a job lost by a restart (QUEUED or RUNNING, not in this web process, older than 2 minutes) at once instead of waiting 15 minutes: `POST /api/runs/:id/reset-stuck` (`resetStuckPlan` in `lib/dispatch/stuck-plan.ts`), the **Reset stuck plan** button under *Optimizing…* on the plan screen. The job becomes FAILED (`errorJson.reason = 'RESET'`, "Reset by a supervisor"), the plan FAILED, and a `PLAN_RESET` audit row names who did it. Refused (409) when the plan is not optimizing (`NOT_STUCK`), its job runs in this process (`JOB_RUNNING`: it ends by itself within 10 minutes) or started less than 2 minutes ago (`JOB_STARTING`).
  - **Driver shifts:** `DriverShift` rows `ACTIVE` for more than 18 h become `ABANDONED`.
- **How the janitor runs.**
  - **In-process:** every **60 s** (first sweep 5 s after boot), from `startJanitor()` in `lib/jobs/janitor-loop.ts`, started by `apps/web/instrumentation.ts`. Production has no cron service, so this is the main path. `ROUTEIQ_DISABLE_JANITOR=1` turns it off.
  - **Manual:** `POST` or `GET /api/cron/janitor` (`apps/web/app/api/cron/janitor/route.ts`) with header `X-Janitor-Token` or `Authorization: Bearer`. The token is `JANITOR_TOKEN` (outside production it falls back to `SOLVER_TOKEN`), compared with `constantTimeEqual` (`lib/janitor-auth.ts`).

### 5.10 Operating runbook pointers

From `docs/admin.md` unless stated.

- **"Optimization failed."** Download the debug JSON, then read the reason:
  - `SOLVER_ERROR` with HTTP 5xx: check the solver logs;
  - `SOLVER_ERROR` with HTTP 0: the solver is unreachable; check `SOLVER_URL` and private DNS. If the message says `SOLVER_TOKEN` "has a character that cannot be sent", paste the token again as plain text on web (third review of audit PR4). If it says `SOLVER_URL` "is not a usable address", set `SOLVER_URL` on web to `http://<solver private address>:<port>` as plain text; `/api/health` answers 503 `SOLVER_URL_INVALID` for the same value (fourth review of audit PR4);
  - `SOLVER_ERROR` with HTTP 301, 302, 307 or 308 ("answers with a redirect"): `SOLVER_URL` points at an address that redirects, for example an http-to-https edge; set it to the solver's private address (third review of audit PR4);
  - `STUCK`: the web restarted during the solve; optimize again;
  - `RESET`: a supervisor reset the plan (Reset stuck plan, audit `PLAN_RESET`); optimize or re-plan again;
  - HTTP 404: web was deployed before the solver; retry in a minute.
- **Solver logs** (`apps/solver/main.py`, `dispatch_solver.py`): two INFO lines per call, `optimize-dispatch run=… tenant=… stops=… trucks=… scenarios=…` at the start and `optimize-dispatch run=… done in X.Xs provider=…` at the end, plus one `dispatch run=… scenario=… status=… loads=… unserved=… km=… t=…` line per scenario and `post-solve run=…` lines. `docs/admin.md` still documents an older `run=... stops=... time_limit=...` format.
- **Rotate `SOLVER_TOKEN`** (policy: every 90 days). Set it on the solver and redeploy, set it on web and redeploy, then verify an optimize. In that order: since audit PR4 a web deploy whose token the solver refuses fails its health check (503 `SOLVER_TOKEN_REJECTED`) and the previous web keeps serving; between the two steps `/api/health` on the running web answers 503 and optimizations fail.
- **A plan stuck on "Optimizing…"** (audit PR4, F09): the plan screen says whether its optimization already ended (fixed by itself within a minute, or at once by a supervisor's **Reset stuck plan**; OPTIMIZE and Re-plan stay greyed out while the plan shows *Optimizing…*) or was lost by a restart (a supervisor presses **Reset stuck plan**, or the janitor fails it 15 minutes after it started). Read-only check: `SELECT p.id FROM "RunPlan" p LEFT JOIN "RunJob" j ON j.id = p."currentJobId" WHERE p.status = 'OPTIMIZING' AND (j.id IS NULL OR j.status NOT IN ('QUEUED','RUNNING'));` (expect no rows once the janitor has run).
- **Do not rotate `NEXTAUTH_SECRET`** except after credential exposure: rotating it logs everyone out.
- **Calibrate truck times** after go-live (`docs/OSRM_SETUP.md`): compare planned and actual trip durations (Ayun IVMS, OPERATION-PROJECT Lane A) and set `roadTimeFactor` to the median ratio.
- **Rate limits** are in memory (`lib/rate-limit.ts`, swept and capped) and reset on every web restart. The IP is the proxy-appended one (`lib/client-ip.ts`). Only these routes are limited, each by its own call:
  - credential sign-in (PR1, `lib/auth-credentials.ts`): 5 failures per IP + email in 15 min (a success clears it), 30 attempts per IP in 10 min (skipped when the IP cannot be resolved, so nobody shares an "unknown" bucket); 20 failures per account in an hour only write a `LOGIN_THROTTLED` audit row (no account lock);
  - admin password reset (`users/[id]/reset-password`): 30/h per admin (`withTenantApi` `rateLimitKey`);
  - `auth/signup`, `auth/forgot` and `auth/reset`: 5/min/IP (`LIMITS.auth`);
  - `orders/upload`, `customers/import` and `runs/[id]/baseline`: 60/h/user (`LIMITS.ordersUpload`);
  - every optimization start (`POST /api/dispatch/plan` with optimize, `POST /api/runs/[id]/replan`, `POST /api/runs/[id]/optimize`): the solve admission, not this limiter (PR3, `lib/dispatch/solve-admission.ts`, [2.7](#27-background-job-model)) - 15 starts per user and 30 per company per hour, `SOLVER_MAX_CONCURRENT` (2) solves at once in total and one less per company, at most 2 waiting per company, a shared queue of 10 that never refuses a company with nothing waiting (200 at most). The old `LIMITS.optimize` (30/h/tenant on `runs/[id]/optimize` only) was removed;
  - `locations/parse`: 120/min/user.

  Every other route has no rate limit. `withTenantApi` applies a limit only when a route passes `rateLimitKey`; only the admin password reset does (the unused `LIMITS.defaultAuthed` was removed in PR1).

### 5.11 Known operational gotchas

| # | Gotcha | Where | What to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Web must run exactly one replica.** The in-flight job map and the rate limits live in memory | `lib/jobs/optimize-job.ts` header, `README.md`, `docs/admin.md` | Never scale `web` horizontally until Redis/BullMQ locking replaces the in-memory map |
| 2 | **Every push to `main` redeploys web.** An optimization running in the old container is lost; its job stays RUNNING until the janitor fails it after 15 min | `lib/jobs/janitor-loop.ts`, `docs/admin.md` | Avoid merging while dispatchers are planning (evenings). Tell users to re-optimize after a `STUCK` failure |
| 3 | **Web and solver deploy independently.** A new web calling the old solver gets a 404 | `callDispatchSolver()` in `lib/solver-client.ts` | Wait until the solver deployment is Active |
| 4 | **Since audit PR4, web `/api/health` answers 200 `degraded` (`ok: false`) when the solver is down, unreachable or older than the web.** The web deploy goes ahead, and optimizations fail until the solver answers. Only a definite misconfiguration answers 503 and blocks the deploy (the previous version keeps serving): `SOLVER_URL` or `SOLVER_TOKEN` missing on web, a `SOLVER_URL` no call can use (for example without `http://`), a token that is not plain ASCII, the solver's 401, or a solver without its own token. (Before audit PR4 a solver that was down made it 503) | `lib/health.ts`, `app/api/health/route.ts` | Alert on `ok: false` and read `dispatch.reason` ([5.8](#58-health-endpoint)). Fix the solver, then optimize again |
| 5 | **Migrations are one-way.** Old code cannot read the migrated database | `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md` | Take a manual backup before each deploy that carries a migration. To roll back, restore the backup and redeploy the previous commit |
| 6 | **`P3009` recovery needs the Postgres public TCP proxy,** because `postgres.railway.internal` resolves only inside Railway | `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md` | Weigh this before removing the proxy (an open follow-up) |
| 7 | **No config-as-code.** Service settings exist only in the dashboard | `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md` | Re-enter them by hand if a service is recreated. Avoid `railway config migrate` and IaC: the CLI drops settings, and an IaC file plans to delete missing resources, including the database |
| 8 | **OSRM has no authentication, and covers only Oman and the UAE** | `docs/OSRM_SETUP.md`, `infra/osrm/Dockerfile` | Never give it a public domain. To roll back, blank `OSRM_URL`; do not delete it. For the monthly refresh, bump `MAP_REFRESH` |
| 9 | **Fixed in PR1 and PR5:** the web no longer calls any routing service. Since PR5 the legacy run-detail Map tab goes through the solver's private `/route-geometry` like the dispatch map (`/api/runs/[id]/load-geometry`), and `lib/road-routing.ts` is deleted | code | Nothing to set on web; `OSRM_URL` there does nothing |
| 10 | **The manual janitor route needs its own `JANITOR_TOKEN` in production** (PR1). Unset or empty = every call returns 401; `SOLVER_TOKEN` is never accepted there. Outside production an unset token falls back to `SOLVER_TOKEN`. The janitor spec sends `JANITOR_TOKEN`, else `SOLVER_TOKEN` | `lib/janitor-auth.ts` | Set a real, separate `JANITOR_TOKEN` on web if anything external calls the route. The in-process janitor is not affected |
| 11 | **`SOLVER_PARALLEL=0` disables every solver deadline and the time budget, and makes `/health` hang during a solve** | `docs/LOCAL_DEV.md`, `apps/solver/README.md`, startup warning in `main.py` (an error on Railway, PR4) | Never set it on a deployed solver |
| 22 | **`FEASIBILITY_GATE=warn` lets unverified trucks be dispatched** (PR4). It is the emergency switch if the gate ever blocks a correct plan (a bug in the check); the violations are still shown and audited | `lib/dispatch/feasibility.ts`, startup warning | Set it on web only for an emergency, report the case, and remove it again (web redeploys on the change). Before reaching for it: a problem on a LOCKED or LOADING load (for example one locked before the PR4 deploy with a stored `hardWindowOk = false`) is not fixed by Re-plan alone, which copies the load unchanged - put the load back to Planned (Back to locked, then Unlock; a later locked load of the same truck goes back first) and re-plan; the red box and the 409 name every load to put back, in order |
| 12 | **Solver worker processes use `spawn`.** Scripts that call `optimize_dispatch` need an `if __name__ == "__main__":` guard | `dispatch_solver.py`, `scripts/bench_dispatch.py` | Follow the bench script's pattern |
| 13 | **Postgres versions differ.** CI and local use PG 16 with PostGIS, but production runs **PG 18.6** on `postgres-ssl:18`. `docs/admin.md` ("Production uses the `postgis/postgis:16-3.4` Docker image"), the `init_postgis` migration comment and the `schema.prisma` header comment still say production uses the `postgis/postgis` image. `CLAUDE.md` §14 says something else: Railway's default Postgres template has no PostGIS, so the first migration must enable it | docs vs `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md` | Do not rely on PostGIS in production; the init migration tolerates its absence |
| 14 | **Python versions differ.** The Dockerfile and CI use 3.11; the local `.dev/setup.sh` venv uses 3.12 | `apps/solver/Dockerfile`, `ci.yml` | Reproduce CI failures on 3.11 |
| 15 | **pnpm versions differ.** `packageManager: pnpm@9.0.0`, but the lockfile was made with 9.15. Both `corepack enable` (Railway) and `pnpm/action-setup` (CI) follow `packageManager` | root `package.json` | Planned fix: the Railpack move in `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md` |
| 16 | **Windows:** Prisma generates into the wrong folder, the DLL is locked while `next dev` runs, and the CLI reads `.env` but not `.env.local` | `docs/LOCAL_DEV.md` | Run `prisma generate` by hand and keep both env files identical |
| 17 | **Service names in the docs are stale.** `.env.example`, `CLAUDE.md` §14 and `docs/admin.md` use `routeiq-web` / `routeiq-solver` (`routeiq-solver.railway.internal`), but the real services are `web` and `solver` | docs | Use the real names |
| 18 | **A code comment about timestamps is stale.** The comment in `reapStuckJobs()` says `RunJob.startedAt` is `TIMESTAMP` without a time zone, but it has been `TIMESTAMPTZ(3)` since `20260511151524_runjob_timestamptz`. The `NOW()` comparison is correct either way | `lib/jobs/optimize-job.ts` | Cosmetic |
| 19 | **The test fixtures README is stale.** `tests/fixtures/README.md` mentions `nmwc-normal.csv` and `nmwc-fixture-seed.sql`, which are not in the repo | `apps/web/tests/fixtures/` | Cosmetic |
| 20 | **The solver binds IPv4 only.** Its Dockerfile binds `0.0.0.0`, while the Railway notes say servers should listen on `::`. It works today because the private network resolves over IPv4 too | `apps/solver/Dockerfile` | Switch to `::` if Railway private networking ever becomes IPv6-only |
| 21 | **Everyone signs in again after the PR1 deploy, and at most every 12 h.** Cookies from before PR1 have no `authTime` / `pwf` claims | `lib/session-principal.ts` | Deploy PR1 after the morning dispatch; tell dispatchers to expect one sign-in per shift |

### 5.12 How to verify a deploy

1. **CI is green on the merged commit.** Run `gh run list --branch main --limit 3` and check both the `web` and `solver` jobs (5–8 minutes).
2. **A backup exists** from before the merge if the change adds a folder under `apps/web/prisma/migrations/` (Postgres → Backups → New backup).
3. **Railway deployments.** The `web` pre-deploy log shows `prisma migrate deploy` either applying the new migrations or finding none pending, and the deployment is **Active**. The `solver` deployment is **Active**. `routeiq-osrm` rebuilds only if `infra/osrm/**` changed.
4. **Health endpoint:** `curl -sS https://<web>/api/health`.
   - **Expected:** HTTP 200 and `{"ok":true,"status":"ready","db":"up","solver":"up","dispatch":{"status":"ready","reason":"OK",...},"routing":{"provider":"OSRM","status":"up"},...}` (since audit PR4; [5.8](#58-health-endpoint)). `GET /api/health/live` answers `{"ok":true,"service":"web"}`.
   - **HTTP 503 with `dispatch.reason`** `SOLVER_TOKEN_MISSING`, `SOLVER_TOKEN_INVALID`, `SOLVER_TOKEN_REJECTED`, `SOLVER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `SOLVER_URL_MISSING` or `SOLVER_URL_INVALID`: fix the variable on web or solver (the same `SOLVER_TOKEN` on both; for `SOLVER_TOKEN_INVALID`, paste it again as plain text; for `SOLVER_URL_INVALID`, set `SOLVER_URL` on web to `http://<solver private address>:<port>` as plain text).
   - **HTTP 200 with `status: degraded`**: the solver did not answer `/ready` in time (`SOLVER_UNREACHABLE`), or is older than the web (`SOLVER_READY_UNSUPPORTED`, until the solver deploy finishes), or `SOLVER_URL` answers with a redirect (`SOLVER_URL_REDIRECTS`: every optimization fails too; set it to the solver's private address), or something answered with an error (`SOLVER_ERROR`: a 5xx, or a 403 - the solver never answers 403, so check that `SOLVER_URL` is the solver's private address and not a proxy or another host).
   - **`routing.status = not_configured`:** `OSRM_URL` is missing on the solver.
   - **`routing.status = down`:** the solver cannot reach OSRM.
   - **HTTP 503 with `db: down`:** the database is down.
5. **Functional check.** Optimize a day at `/t/<slug>/dispatch`. The plan shows **Road km**, not "Estimated km", and has no routing warning. `GET /api/runs/<id>/status` shows the job `SUCCEEDED` and the plan `READY`. The solver log shows `optimize-dispatch run=… done in …s provider=OSRM`.
6. **Optional OSRM check.** From inside the private network, run `OSRM_URL=… sh infra/osrm/smoke-test.sh`.
7. **Janitor check.** Web logs should show no repeating `janitor: sweep failed`. A single one at boot is normal: the database may not be reachable yet, and the next sweep retries.
8. **Migration state (optional).** With the public database URL, run `pnpm --filter @routeiq/web exec prisma migrate status`. It should list every migration folder as applied.
9. **Security checks after PR1** (sign-in, `callbackUrl`, 410 driver routes, `email` in health, the client-IP check against a LOGIN audit row, read-only SQL checks and the owner clean-up): see section 7 of [`docs/SECURITY.md`](./SECURITY.md).
10. **After PR4 (snapshots and the feasibility gate).**
    - `_prisma_migrations` lists `20260927090000_plan_snapshots_feasibility`, and the three columns exist.
    - `FEASIBILITY_GATE` is unset (or `enforce`) on web; the web log has no "FEASIBILITY_GATE=warn" warning; the solver log has no `SOLVER_PARALLEL=0` warning.
    - At the next NMWC optimize: `RunPlan.feasibilityJson` is filled; the plan options show "Timing checked: Yes" for RECOMMENDED; no TIMES NOT VERIFIED on the driver sheets or the workbook; LOCK and DISPATCH work; the ASSUMPTIONS sheet title reads "Settings this plan (vN) was built and costed with".
    - After a late-order re-plan the carried loads keep their snapshots, and plans made before the deploy still open ("current customer data" under their stops) and can be re-planned.
    - Loads that were LOCKED or LOADING before the deploy and fail the check (the red box names them "locked or loading: a re-plan keeps it as it is"): put each back to Planned (Back to locked, then Unlock; the red box lists the truck's later locked loads first, in the order they unlock) and re-plan; do not switch the gate to warn for them.
    - Once NMWC enters a loading time per case, watch the `OPTIMIZE_SUCCEEDED` audit rows for scenarios whose `feasibility.status` is not VERIFIED.
11. **After PR5 (costs, routing, settings, audit).**
    - `_prisma_migrations` lists `20260928090000_plan_load_cost_json` and `20260928090100_keep_imported_service_times`, and `PlanLoad.costJson` exists.
    - Before the deploy (owner, read-only), unloading times. Every query counts customers whose type has no unloading time of its own (no type, or a type whose profile has none - not only untyped customers):
      - kept by the migration (it marks them confirmed): `SELECT count(*) FROM "Customer" c WHERE NOT c."serviceTimeConfirmed" AND c."avgServiceTimeMin" > 0 AND c."avgServiceTimeMin" <> 10 AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "CustomerTypeProfile" p WHERE p."tenantId" = c."tenantId" AND p."customerType" = c."customerType" AND p."serviceTimeMin" IS NOT NULL);`
      - switching to the Settings default (stored 10 min, the column default, never confirmed; a change only where the default is not 10): `SELECT count(*) FROM "Customer" c JOIN "TenantConfig" t ON t."tenantId" = c."tenantId" WHERE NOT c."serviceTimeConfirmed" AND c."avgServiceTimeMin" = 10 AND t."defaultServiceTimeMin" <> 10 AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "CustomerTypeProfile" p WHERE p."tenantId" = c."tenantId" AND p."customerType" = c."customerType" AND p."serviceTimeMin" IS NOT NULL);`
      - If the second count is above 0 and some of those customers really need 10 min, set their unloading time in the customer details on Daily dispatch (or re-import the customer file with the column) after the deploy; the customer page shows the time in use and its source. After the deploy, `SELECT count(*) FROM "AuditLog" WHERE "afterJson"->>'by' = 'migration 20260928090100_keep_imported_service_times';` equals the first count.
    - At the next NMWC optimize: the KPI operating cost equals the options table's day cost of the option in use, and the workbook's LOAD PLAN / TRUCK DAYS totals equal the summary; no "costed the earlier way" note on a fresh day; `distance_quality` is ROAD (RunJob `responseJson`); the solver log's `matrix … seconds=` is small (one call once `OSRM_TABLE_TILE` is raised).
    - A re-planned day's dashboard figures include its carried locked loads. A late-order re-plan (typically one or two new loads) ends READY, and no plan shows a "Cost check" warning.
    - The owner enters NMWC's real driver cost per hour and overtime (after / per hour, after at most the shift maximum) on Settings; the next `RunJob.requestJson.config` shows them.
    - `GET /api/audit?action=LOAD_DISPATCHED` returns only those rows; `?action=LOAD_DISPACHED` answers 400.
    - `/t/<slug>/runs/new` opens Daily dispatch; `GET /api/runs/<dispatch plan id>/route-geometries` answers 409.
    - Solver smoke test: a wrong `X-Solver-Token` answers 401 (not 500).
12. **After A1 (audit quick hardening).**
    - The web build log's setup line names Node 22 (for example `nodejs_22`); if not, set `NIXPACKS_NODE_VERSION=22` on web and redeploy ([5.6](#56-deployment-on-railway)). The build log has no "[@sentry/nextjs] The Sentry SDK has enabled source map generation" warning.
    - `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' 'https://<web>/_next/image?url=%2Ffavicon.ico&w=1&q=75'` prints 404 (it printed 400 while the image endpoint was on).
    - Pick any `/_next/static/chunks/*.js` from the login page's HTML; the same URL with `.map` added answers 404 (the owner check of the audit: before A1 every production build made these files).
    - Upload the usual order file on Daily dispatch: it is read as before. A workbook over the new caps is refused with a message that names the cap ("unpacks to more than 50 MB", "has 12 sheets; at most 10 can be read").
13. **After audit PR A5 (owner rules), the location rule.** No migration; the depot rule's checks are in [5.5](#55-database-migrations).
    - Before the deploy (owner, read-only; the box is the Oman + UAE default, use the company's own box if Settings has one). Customers confirmed at a point outside the delivery area - only the customer page's old PATCH could make one without asking, and such a point is planned: `SELECT t.slug, count(*) FROM "Customer" c JOIN "Tenant" t ON t.id = c."tenantId" WHERE c."locationVerified" AND c.lat IS NOT NULL AND (c.lat NOT BETWEEN 16.4 AND 26.6 OR c.lng NOT BETWEEN 51.5 AND 60.0) GROUP BY t.slug;`. Check each one on the map (Set location on the customer page). Customers LOW and not confirmed (blocked from the deploy on; expected 0, nothing ever stored LOW): `SELECT count(*) FROM "Customer" WHERE "geocodeConfidence" = 'LOW' AND NOT "locationVerified";`. Customers labelled HIGH, not confirmed, whose stored numbers have fewer than 4 decimals (an import before A5 stored any pair as HIGH; ADD LOCATION now lets a dispatcher confirm such a saved pin as it is, because the stored number cannot tell 23.58 from 23.5800): `SELECT t.slug, count(*) FROM "Customer" c JOIN "Tenant" t ON t.id = c."tenantId" WHERE NOT c."locationVerified" AND c."geocodeConfidence" = 'HIGH' AND c.lat IS NOT NULL AND (round(c.lat::numeric, 3) = c.lat::numeric OR round(c.lng::numeric, 3) = c.lng::numeric) GROUP BY t.slug;`. About 1 in 5 exact 4-decimal points shows here too (a last decimal 0); look at the listed ones on the map (Set location on the customer page).
    - Before the deploy (owner, read-only; A5 sixth review, corrected in the seventh). Customers confirmed from a pasted reading whose saved text reads as not exact today. ADD LOCATION keeps the text a location was read from (`Customer.locationInput`), so they can be found: a pair padded with zeros ("23.5800, 58.4100", read as exact before the owner decisions of 28 Sep 2026), Google's pin or degrees, minutes and seconds of a rough pair (before the sixth review), and any reading the screen before A5 let through (a map centre, fewer than 4 decimals, a directions link read at its start). They stay planned: a confirmed point is not judged again, and that does not change without the owner's decision ([7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)). From `apps/web` on the A5 commit: `DATABASE_URL=<the database> corepack pnpm@9.15.9 exec tsx prisma/check-saved-readings.ts`. It prints, per company: how many confirmed customers have a typed or pasted location; how many of them were **confirmed from a pasted reading** (the saved text reads back to the saved point, or holds it but reads otherwise today) and how many of those **read as not exact today**, by reason; how many short links it cannot read again (the address a short link led to is not kept); and, apart, the customers **not judged by their text**, because the saved point was not read from it: no text kept (a map click or two typed numbers on the customer page before A5, which never touched the text), a text that reads as another point (the point was set after it on the customer page before A5, or, before audit A2, the box was edited after Read), the saved numbers as RouteIQ writes them when a saved point is confirmed as it is (a stored 23.5850 reads "23.585"), and a text that cannot be read ("map pin"), with how many of them have fewer than 4 decimals as stored (a rough pair typed on the customer page, or an exact 4-decimal value ending in 0: the stored number cannot say which). Only the "read as not exact today" count is the 7.5 question; the "not judged" lines are not pasted readings. It writes nothing and names no customer.
    - After the deploy: ADD LOCATION with "23.58, 58.40" keeps **Save location** off with "This reading is not exact. Drop the pin on the customer's exact location, then save."; a click on the map turns it on and saves. The same for the link Google Maps opens when "23.58, 58.41" is typed into it and for `23°34'48.0"N 58°24'36.0"E`; a link to a real place saves as before. A full-precision link or "lat, lng" saves as before. The customer page shows **Set location** (no lat / lng boxes). A customer import of a file with 4 to 6 decimals lists no "location(s) not exact"; NMWC's own customer file reads 80 of 80 exact. ADD LOCATION on a customer whose saved pin ends in 0 (for example 23.5850) keeps **Save location** on.
14. **After P5 (uploads read in a separate process).** No migration.
    - Before the deploy: the web service's memory limit and usage (web → Metrics) leave room for about 1.3 GB more while two large files are read ([5.6](#56-deployment-on-railway)); else raise it or set `UPLOAD_PARSE_CONCURRENCY=1`. `UPLOAD_WORKER_MAX_HEAP_MB`, `UPLOAD_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS` and `UPLOAD_PARSE_CONCURRENCY` stay unset unless the owner chose other values.
    - The web build log shows `build-upload-parser: .next/upload-parser/parse.cjs (... KB from 10 files)`. After the start the web log shows `[upload-parse] the file reader works (startup check)` and no `[config]` line about the upload file reader or an `UPLOAD_*` value.
    - Check the usual order file on Daily dispatch: it is read as before, and the web log shows one line `[upload-parse] read <MB> MB in <ms> ms, parser peak <MB> MB (process <pid>): <rows> rows`. Import the customer file (Validate only): the same. `GET /api/health/live` keeps answering while a file is checked.
    - Rollback: redeploy the previous commit; nothing in the database changes.

## 6. History & decisions

All times are Gulf Standard Time (GST, Asia/Muscat, UTC+4), the same zone git records (`+04:00`). Facts marked **(per work log)** come from the operator's build notes and are not recorded in the repo. Everything else was checked against `git log --first-parent main`, the six merged pull requests (`gh pr list --state merged`), commit messages and `docs/`.
