Tests: `tests/lib/dispatch-load-state.spec.ts` (every pass, the candidate order, moved-least, the notes), `tests/lib/plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` (the re-plan job and "Use instead" give the same drivers and notes for the same evidence, in both truck orders; frozen loads; an inactive and a dropped hand-set driver; Keep ends a note in `getPlanDetail`; an older summary), `tests/lib/choose-scenario-route.spec.ts`, `tests/lib/driver-pack.spec.ts`, `tests/integration/plan-lifecycle-db.spec.ts`; the rules were checked by mutation (the two reviews below found rules no test pinned yet; they are pinned now). `tests/lib/repo-guards.spec.ts` keeps the removed promises (a dropped driver coming back, "Driver added" notes) and helper names out of the docs and `lib/`.
- **Review of the simplified driver rules, fixed before merge** (all confirmed findings, low):
  - *A note came back after "No driver"*: a note that had left a trip without a driver ended when the dispatcher picked a driver, and came back when the dispatcher then chose "No driver" (it cleared the marker). "No driver" is now marked too (who, when; not a hand-set driver), and the notes read that marker (`driverSetByDispatcher`), so a note ends for good once the dispatcher sets that trip's driver ([3.9](#39-step-7-a-driver-per-load)).
  - *The docs promised more than the code*: 3.9 and the guide said the drivers did not depend on the optimizer's trip order. They do on a tie (the same move, or no evidence load): the trip the optimizer lists first goes first, and it lists by truck code. The code follows the stable rule; the docs now say so, and `tests/lib/repo-guards.spec.ts` keeps the old wording out.
  - *Rules without a test that fails when they break*: the re-plan job reads its version's copies (with a driver the dispatcher set on a copy before the job started), not the previous version's loads; a tie keeps the optimizer's order, also on the first optimization of a day; a carried marker names the user who set the driver, not the one applying the plan; `TRIP_GONE` is in the `SCENARIO_CHOSEN` audit; an apply without notes replaces the stored ones (a re-plan's copied ones too). Each now has a test that fails under that mutation (`tests/lib/dispatch-load-state.spec.ts`, `tests/lib/plan-lifecycle.spec.ts`, `tests/lib/driver-pack.spec.ts`, `tests/integration/plan-lifecycle-db.spec.ts`).
- **Second review of the simplified driver rules, fixed before merge** (one medium, four low; no change to the rules or the code, only tests and docs):
  - *Frozen loads were tested only as LOCKED* (medium): pass 0 takes every status but PLANNED, but a change to LOCKED alone passed every test, and a mid-day re-plan could then give a driver a trip over that driver's loading, dispatched or completed load, without a note. `tests/lib/dispatch-load-state.spec.ts` now runs each frozen status in both truck orders (the filled-in trip moved onto it loses the driver with a `CLASH` note naming the frozen load; candidate 2 takes the driver of the truck's frozen Load 1 for its new Load 2), and the fake-database re-plan job in `tests/lib/plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` runs with each frozen status.
  - *Two conditions of candidate 2 had no test* (low): the driver of the truck's nearest trip must be active (a frozen load can carry a driver deactivated since), and a frozen load without a driver is not the nearest trip "that already has a driver". Both are in the unit tests now.
  - *No test checked the truck code of a truck the new plan leaves out* (low): `applyScenario` looks up the trucks of the evidence loads too, so a `TRIP_GONE` note (a **MIN_TRUCKS** option) or a `CLASH` note naming a frozen load names that truck by its code. The fake-database trucks now have codes that differ from their ids (T02 for `T2`), so the notes (returned and stored) and the yellow text must carry the code; the integration test checks the code of the `TRIP_GONE` truck.
  - *"No driver" could not end a note on a trip with no driver* (low): the guide and [3.7](#37-step-5-reviewing-the-plan) / [3.9](#39-step-7-a-driver-per-load) said it could. The Driver list sends nothing for the "No driver" already selected, and a re-sent `null` changes nothing. Within the rules (a note ends when the dispatcher sets that trip's driver), the docs now say such a note ends when a driver is picked for the trip; `tests/lib/plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` pins it.
  - *The guide promised a re-plan message with the note count* (low): the dispatch screens show a job's message only while it runs or after it failed. The guide now points to the yellow notes after a re-plan; the "Use instead" toast still counts them.

  The first three were checked by mutation: the new tests fail when pass 0 takes LOCKED loads only, when candidate 2 skips the active check or counts a frozen load without a driver, and when the evidence trucks are left out of the truck lookup. `tests/lib/repo-guards.spec.ts` keeps the two withdrawn promises out of the docs.
- **Tests**: `tests/lib/plan-lifecycle*.spec.ts`, `choose-scenario-route.spec.ts`, `solve-admission*.spec.ts`, `request-gate.spec.ts` (with the day loader), `plan-actions.spec.ts`, `dispatch-screen-guards.spec.ts`, `dashboard-live-plans.spec.ts`, `plan-status.spec.ts`; real PostgreSQL in `tests/integration/plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` (web + solver), `plan-lifecycle-db.spec.ts` (library level, solver faked; held row locks observed in `pg_stat_activity`) and `dashboard-db.spec.ts` (library level); janitor variants; solver `tests/test_admission.py`.
- **Owner diagnostics** (read-only, before and after deploy): stranded children `SELECT c.id FROM "RunPlan" c JOIN "RunPlan" p ON p.id = c."parentRunId" WHERE p.status = 'SUPERSEDED' AND c."chosenScenarioId" IS NULL;` (they keep working: unlock a load, then OPTIMIZE; loads already out can be marked completed); resurrected parents `SELECT id, status FROM "RunPlan" WHERE "supersededAt" IS NOT NULL AND status <> 'SUPERSEDED';` (now treated as superseded everywhere, the dashboard KPIs included; the owner decides whether to set them back to SUPERSEDED); duplicate live plans `SELECT "tenantId", "depotId", "runDate", count(*) FROM "RunPlan" WHERE status NOT IN ('SUPERSEDED', 'ARCHIVED') AND "supersededAt" IS NULL GROUP BY 1, 2, 3 HAVING count(*) > 1;`.

**Stabilization release, PR4 "Frozen snapshots and the dispatch feasibility gate".** Fixed (migration `20260927090000_plan_snapshots_feasibility`, three nullable JSONB columns):

- **F08**: every option keeps what it was computed with (`ScenarioDetails.inputs`: depot, trucks, stops, config, settings, job); loads and stops keep the truck and customer facts they were planned with (`PlanLoad.truckSnapshotJson`, `RouteAssignment.stopSnapshotJson`), copied by re-plans (NULL-safe); plan detail, map, driver sheets, WhatsApp and workbook show the planned facts and flag later corrections as "changed after planning", never switching silently ([3.7](#37-step-5-reviewing-the-plan)).
- **F04 A** (solver): `feasibility.check_scenario` checks every returned scenario (`DispatchScenario.feasibility`: VERIFIED / VIOLATED / UNVERIFIED, EXACT / ESTIMATED); when the post-solve stage does not run (out of time, no candidate, exception) the raw plan is re-timed exactly, else reported VIOLATED; "serves more" only for VERIFIED options ([4.11](#411-post-solve-stage)).
- **F04 B** (web): `checkPlanFeasibility` per truck-day, stored in `RunPlan.feasibilityJson` and enforced on LOCK / LOADING / DISPATCH (409 `TIMES_NOT_VERIFIED`); `FEASIBILITY_GATE=warn` as the audited emergency switch; TIMES NOT VERIFIED on the sheets and the workbook ([3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)).
- **L23** (minimal): a dead stage worker loses only its own task; no healthy RECOMMENDED re-check is aborted because a sibling's pid changed.
- **F01 item 4 / F02 inside the gate**: each load's physical kg against the payload it was planned with; cases planned with no weight reported as `KG_UNKNOWN`.
- **New issues 13 / 60**: the ASSUMPTIONS sheet comes from the settings stored with the plan (both halves since the review below: also the routing-coverage line, never the web's `OSRM_URL`).
- **New issue 22**: each scenario carries its own timing status; no false "not re-checked" note. **23**: a service start before the truck arrives is reported (`TRAVEL`), not silently repaired. **25**: `hard_window_ok = false` is gated. **28**: `SOLVER_PARALLEL=0` logs a warning outside development (an error on Railway) and is documented ([5.11](#511-known-operational-gotchas) #11). **31**: the `OPTIMIZER_DESIGN.md` text on returned timetables matches the behaviour.
- **Review of PR4, fixed before merge** (all confirmed findings; each has a test):
  - *Split part filled to its payload refused forever* (medium): the web rounded each part's line and order kg separately (12.35 kg cases: 12.4 + 9867.7 = 9880.1 kg on a 9880 kg truck the optimizer filled correctly), so the check blocked it on every re-plan. The parts of a split stop now add up exactly to the kg sent to the optimizer (largest-remainder rounding in `splitIntoParts`), and one rounding tolerance, `KG_ROUNDING_TOL` = 0.5 kg, is used by the check, the workbook's Kg check and `applyScenario`'s cross-check (parts stored before the fix pass too). `dispatch-split.spec.ts` runs the 12.35 kg x 800 case example for every 2-line and 2-order split.
  - *No-weight warning read today's product master* (medium): a 0-kg part whose product got a weight after locking lost its warning and passed as 0 kg (about 8000 kg physically on a 3000 kg truck). "Planned with no weight" is now read from the plan's rows (`portionLinesJson[].kgPerCase`; for older parts, a 0-kg line whose part weighs no more than its weighed lines), and a case weight entered since that puts the load over its payload blocks as `CAPACITY_KG_NEW_WEIGHT` ("OVER PAYLOAD at the case weights entered since planning" in the workbook).
  - *WhatsApp message without the change notes or TIMES NOT VERIFIED* (medium, a regression of this PR): it now prints each change made after planning with the corrected pin, and TIMES NOT VERIFIED first, like the PDF sheet.
  - *Re-plan advised for a problem on a locked or loading load* (low): a re-plan copies such a load unchanged. The 409 (`remedy`, `unlockFirst`), the red box, the load badge and the disabled Re-plan button now say to put that load back to Planned first ("Back to locked", then "Unlock"), then re-plan (`timingRemedy`).
  - *Inverted windows as a permanent "changed after planning"* (low): legacy windows that end before they start were planned as "any time"; `stopMasterChanges`, the check and the snapshots of older options apply the same `usableWindow` rule, so the day is no longer out of date after every re-plan.
  - *Truck corrections did not mark the day out of date* (low): the day overview counts trucks with PLANNED loads whose capacity or payload changed (`outdated.trucksChanged`, step 3 then offers RE-PLAN), and the check warns (`CAPACITY_CHANGED`) when a load not yet out no longer fits the corrected truck.
  - *Access notes frozen in the snapshot* (low): they are contact details, carried as they are now by the plan detail and printed so on the driver sheets, like the driver's name and phone (the snapshot keeps the planned notes for the record).
  - *This PR4 block was missing from 7.4* (low): added.
- **Second review of PR4 (after the rebase onto PR3's final head), fixed before merge** (all confirmed findings; each has a test that fails without the fix):
  - *Three integration tests pinned the old two-key `outdated`* (medium, CI only): `dispatch-weights.spec.ts` compared the day's `outdated` with `{ weightCases, inactiveOrders }`, so the two keys this PR added (`masterChanged`, `trucksChanged`) failed three tests. `day-overview.ts` now exports `UP_TO_DATE` (the shape, every count 0), the specs compare against it, and `day-overview-weights.spec.ts` pins that every path returns exactly its keys.
  - *The locked-load remedy named only the violating load* (low): with T01 L1 and L2 locked ("Lock all loads") and a problem on L1, "put load T01 L1 back to Planned first" was refused ("Unlock Load 2 of this truck first."). `timingRemedy` now adds every later LOCKED or LOADING load of the truck, latest first, to `unlockFirst`, the 409, the red box, the load badge and the "Re-plan is off" text; the red box's Re-plan is off while every blocking violation is on a locked or loading load (a re-plan would change none of them).
  - *The day screen said "up to date" while the check blocked the open rest of a split order* (low): a case weight entered after planning for an order partly on a frozen load was skipped by the day overview, so RE-PLAN was off while the PLANNED part got `CAPACITY_KG_NEW_WEIGHT`. The day overview now reads the case weight the PLANNED parts carry (`portionPlannedKgPerCase`, shared with `rowUnknownKg`) and counts them as out of date until a re-plan carries the weight now.
  - *3.8 said the gate never reads today's master data* (low, docs): the product case weight (`CAPACITY_KG_NEW_WEIGHT`) and the truck's capacity now (`CAPACITY_CHANGED`) are read live; 3.8 and the `feasibility.ts` header say so, and the test tables list the review's cases.
  - *The corrected-truck warning on a locked load named only that load* (low, found by the third review): with T01 L1 and L2 locked and T01's payload corrected below L1's load only, `CAPACITY_CHANGED` said "Put the load back to Planned and re-plan", and Unlock on L1 was refused ("Unlock Load 2 of this truck first."). The warning now builds its remedy like `timingRemedy`: the load plus every later LOCKED or LOADING load of the truck, latest first ("Put T01 L2, T01 L1 back to Planned first, in this order, and re-plan to use the new capacity."; `frozenReplanText` in `feasibility.ts`, reusing `unlockFirstText`).
  - *Rebase onto PR3's final head*: the job message keeps PR3's "N driver note(s) (see the plan)" before this PR's timing note (`jobMessage(scenario, preDrops, driverNotes)`), and the plan's yellow warnings keep PR3's driver notes next to this PR's "changed after planning" notes. Copy-forward (`copyRowData`) carries `truckSnapshotJson`, `stopSnapshotJson` and PR3's `driverSetById` / `driverSetAt` unchanged; `feasibilityJson` is recomputed for the copy.
- **Tests**: solver `tests/test_feasibility.py`, `test_dispatch.py` (worker death, pool canary); web `tests/lib/dispatch-feasibility.spec.ts`, `plan-feasibility-gate.spec.ts`, `plan-snapshots.spec.ts`, `dispatch-split.spec.ts`, `dispatch-workbook.spec.ts`, `driver-pack.spec.ts`, `day-overview-weights.spec.ts`; integration `dispatch-timing.spec.ts` (gate, locked-load remedy), `dispatch-mvp.spec.ts` 27-29 (snapshots, truck correction on the day screen, live access notes) and `dispatch-weights.spec.ts` (the day's `outdated`).
- **Still open** (not in PR4): a one-off script to backfill snapshots for plans made before the deploy from `RunJob.requestJson` (they are shown with today's data, labelled "current customer data", and checked structurally); an integration test that forces a solver stage failure on the running app (covered by the solver tests with `ROUTEIQ_TEST_FAIL_REPACK`); the proper L23 fix (one process per job, no private Pool attributes). By decision, not open: driver name and phone and the customer's access notes stay live (no contact snapshot).

**Stabilization release, PR5 "Costs, routing provenance and deadlines, effective settings, audit catalog, error contract, legacy cleanup".** Fixed (migration `20260928090000_plan_load_cost_json`, one nullable JSONB column):

- **F17** (L22; owner decision: DRIVER PAY = the WHOLE TRUCK DAY): one cost model, `apps/solver/costing.py` - the driver is paid from the first departure (or first frozen departure) to the last return, turnaround and waiting included, overtime after the configured hours on top. The same function prices the post-solve score's money (`load_repack.score`) and every scenario's loads, truck days and totals (`_build_scenario`, which checks that the loads add up); the routing model, the repack and the exact timing pay a truck with frozen loads from its last frozen return. Documented per-load allocation (each load owns the time since its truck's previous return; fixed cost on load 1). The web stores each load's breakdown (`PlanLoad.costJson`), and the summary / KPI, the options table (whole-day cost of each option), the workbook (label, breakdown, paid time, TRUCK DAYS sheet) and the dashboard (from the plan summary, so re-planned days count their carried loads) all add the same numbers up. Plans saved before are labelled as costed the earlier way (`costBasis` MIXED_LEGACY). The `dispatch_models.py` driver-rate comment is corrected.
- **F18** (L21, new issue 27): per-cell provenance of the road matrix, the truck factor on road cells only, `distance_quality` ROAD / MIXED / ESTIMATED, `estimated_legs` per load and `leg_estimated` per stop; each load's "Estimated km" follows its own legs (driver sheets), and the plan and workbook read "Road km (N legs estimated)"; `HARD_WINDOW_INFEASIBLE` / `SHIFT_LIMIT` say "(based on estimated distance)" when they rest on one.
- **F19** (new issue 29): a routing deadline (min(90 s, 20% of the budget)) through matrix construction, retries and response reading, per-attempt timeouts capped by the time left, one HTTP client per matrix, bounded parallel tiles, `OSRM_TABLE_TILE` / `OSRM_PARALLEL` from the environment, "road routing too slow" estimates instead of a shortened search; at most 600 stops (422; the web says so first).
- **F21** (new issues 61, 62, 66, 68): Settings shows only what the planner uses, with units and bounds equal to the optimizer's contract, the economics editable by TENANT_ADMIN, the old controls removed (API strict), an effective planner values panel from the same mapping as the request (`dispatchConfigFromTenant`), customer type defaults read-only; saves send only changed fields and refuse to overwrite a newer value (409 `SETTINGS_CHANGED`); out-of-range stored values refuse the optimize (409 `SETTINGS_OUT_OF_RANGE`); the country is a pick-list (Settings and sign-up); trucks and depots got their planner fields; the default service time applies to customers whose own time was never confirmed; the solver time limit is automatic.
- **F22**: the legacy Map tab's geometry goes through the solver's private `/route-geometry`; `lib/road-routing.ts` is deleted; dispatch plans answer 409; one `isDispatchPlan` predicate for the legacy page and routes.
- **F23** (L18, new issue 63): one audit catalog for writers (typed), API (zod, 400 for unknown filters) and page; Muscat days; the page shows errors and passes a limit.
- **L16**: `PlanError`, `RouteAdjustError` and `BatchRaceError` extend `HttpError` (`lib/http-error.ts`); the per-route try/catch blocks are gone.
- **Legacy cleanup**: `/runs/new` redirects to Daily dispatch, the dashboard buttons open it, the dead legacy optimize chain and uncalled plan-service helpers are removed, stale comments fixed (L10).
- **Docs and ops**: L29 (`--env-file`, dotenv pinned, wrong-token smoke test), L30 (see the row above), this handbook's 3.12, 3.13, 3.17, 4.3-4.5, 4.10, 2.8, 2.9 and backlog.
- **Tests**: solver `tests/test_costing.py` (golden whole-day case, frozen + new, score money = reported cost on random days, frozen truck paid from its return), `test_dispatch.py` (mixed matrix, estimated legs, `_submatrix` mask, estimate note on impossibility, slow and hanging OSRM bounded by the deadline, tile size from the environment, parallel = serial, 600 stops, the web bounds inside the Pydantic contract); web `dispatch-costs.spec.ts`, `tenant-settings.spec.ts`, `settings-route.spec.ts`, `audit-errors-geometry.spec.ts`, and additions to `dispatch-workbook.spec.ts`, `dashboard-live-plans.spec.ts`, `schemas.spec.ts`, `dispatch-customer-attrs.spec.ts`; integration `dispatch-costs.spec.ts`.
- **Review of PR5, fixed before merge** (all confirmed findings; each has a test):
  - *Cost check failed small optimizations with a 500* (high): each load's six parts were rounded to 0.001 OMR one by one, so a load drifted up to 0.003 OMR from its exact money, but `_build_scenario` allowed 0.001 per load and raised `CostError`, which `/optimize-dispatch` answered with 500 - deterministically, so a retry failed too. One- to three-load optimizations failed often (the reviewer: 5 of 40 late-order re-plans around a locked load), the core late-order case. Each load's total is now its exact money rounded once and its parts add up to it exactly (`costing.round_parts`), the check allows 0.0005 OMR per load, and a mismatch is logged and shown as a "Cost check" warning instead of failing the plan (`CostError` is gone) ([4.10](#410-extraction-and-reported-costs)). Tests: `test_costing.py` (the reviewer's one-load day, 16 random 1- and 2-load days fresh and around a locked load - two of them failed under the old rule -, 5,000 random part sets, a forced mismatch returns the plan with the warning); integration `dispatch-costs.spec.ts` checks the re-plan ends READY.
  - *Settings save refused over a stored overtime threshold* (low): a tenant whose shift maximum was lowered below the (then not editable) overtime threshold could not save even the company name. The rule is now checked only when a save changes one of the two, in the route and the form (`overtimeSaveProblem`); the page shows an amber note otherwise ([3.12](#312-settings-that-change-the-plan)).
  - *Handbook 3.4 still stated the old service-time rule* (low): corrected, with links.
  - *`distance_quality` MIXED after dropping the only off-road stop* (low): `_submatrix` now derives the quality (and `distance_is_estimated`) again from the kept legs, so a plan whose every leg is a road leg says ROAD (post-deploy check 11).
  - *Release benchmark not run, re-plans unmeasured* (low): measured, [`OPTIMIZER_BENCHMARK.md`](./OPTIMIZER_BENCHMARK.md) section 9. `bench_dispatch.py 300`: the same RECOMMENDED plan on PR4 and PR5 (12 trucks, 24 loads, 1,806.5 km, all served; the cost +1.6% only from the paid turnarounds). New `scripts/bench_replan.py` (two shapes of 8 days around locked loads, one cost model for both versions): nothing unserved either way; late-order re-plans give the same plan on 7 of 8 days and one day +24 OMR (one more truck, traced to the search's start bound for trucks with locked loads); with half the fleet locked PR5 is 0.6% cheaper with 2.8% fewer km. Over the 16 days the versions are within 0.8%, so the owner's whole-day rule stays in the search.
  - *No "*" on the Operating cost KPI of plans saved before PR5* (low): the plan screen and the workbook read the cost basis through one helper (`summaryCostBasis`: a summary without `costBasis` was costed the earlier way).
  - *Driver-rate contract comment on the wrong field* (low): the whole-truck-day JSDoc now documents `driver_cost_per_hour` in `packages/shared-types`.
  - *Unconfirmed imported service times replaced by the Settings default* (low): migration `20260928090100_keep_imported_service_times` keeps them; the pre-deploy count is widened to every customer whose type has no unloading time, with a remediation step ([5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy) check 11); the customer page shows the time in use and its source.
  - *Two "Large day" warnings above 350 stops* (low): the web's is dropped; the optimizer's names the limit.
  - *Workbook stated the new cost rules for plans costed the earlier way* (low): `planRules` (CURRENT, EARLIER, MIXED; from the option in use's cost version and the loads' breakdowns) words the ASSUMPTIONS rows (driver cost, overtime, road time factor, default service time), the NOTES and the TRUCK DAYS title by the rules the plan was made with.
- **Second review of PR5 (after the rebase onto PR3's final head and the rebased PR4), fixed before merge** (all confirmed findings; the code fix has a test that fails without it):
  - *The customer page warned about the 10 min column default* (low): `describeServiceTime` showed the amber "The stored 10 min was never confirmed ..." note for customers created by an order upload, a late order or a form without a time - nearly every typed customer from an order file (HYPERMARKET 35, SUPERMARKET 20, ...) - and following it pinned the customer to one time. The column default is now no note under a customer type's own time and a neutral line ("No confirmed unloading time of its own ...") under the Settings default, where it may still be an earlier import's real 10 min; other unconfirmed stored times keep the amber note. `CUSTOMER_SERVICE_COLUMN_DEFAULT` is one constant, checked against `schema.prisma` and migration `20260928090100` by `dispatch-customer-attrs.spec.ts`.
  - *Handbook said the web warns above 350 stops* (low, docs): since the first review only the optimizer adds the "Large day" warning (`dispatch_solver.LARGE_DAY_STOPS`); the timeouts table ([2.8](#28-timeouts-budgets-and-limits)) and [4.3](#43-request-and-response-contract) now say so, and name `lib/planner-bounds.ts` only for the 422 and the Settings text.
  - *Expected test counts still the pre-fix numbers* (low, docs): updated to solver 188, web unit 899 (52 files) on the rebased branch (790 at the first review fixes, before the rebase).
  - *Rebase onto PR3's final head and the rebased PR4*: `choose-scenario` keeps PR3's `driversChanged` answer without a try/catch (a `PlanError` is an `HttpError`); `applyScenario` and `setDriverTx` keep PR3's driver notes and hand-set marker (`driverSetById`, `driverSetAt`) and write their audit rows through `audit(..., tx)`; the dashboard keeps PR3's one-version-per-depot-and-day `LIVE_PLAN_IN_USE` and `PLAN_ORDERS_IN_SCOPE` and reads each plan's summary (PR5); the plan summary keeps PR3's `driverChanges` next to the cost totals; the plan screen keeps PR3's driver picker ("picked by hand", **Keep**) next to PR5's per-load cost tooltip and estimated km. Copy-forward (`copyRowData`) carries `costJson` with PR4's snapshots and PR3's hand-set driver, NULL-safe (`plan-feasibility-gate.spec.ts`). The migration table keeps timestamp order (PR3 `20260926110000` < PR4 `20260927090000` < PR5 `20260928090000`, `20260928090100`).
- **Still open** (not in PR5): a `CustomerTypeProfile` editor; dropping the deprecated `TenantConfig` columns (expand-then-contract); retiring the solver's PyVRP `/optimize`; loads locked out of order keep their planned cost share (documented, flagged on TRUCK DAYS); re-measure re-plans on NMWC's real days with locked loads, and if the one worse late-order case recurs, add a search without the start bound as an extra candidate (OPTIMIZER_BENCHMARK 9.2). A 150-stop synthetic day on PR5 and on PR4 gave the same plan (8 trucks, 9 loads, 805.3 km, 0 unserved); the reported cost rose 478.5 -> 479.8 OMR because depot turnaround and waiting are now paid. The 300-stop release benchmark and the re-plan comparison: [`OPTIMIZER_BENCHMARK.md`](./OPTIMIZER_BENCHMARK.md) section 9.

**Stabilization release, PR6 "Scenario findings: intake, messages and exports".** Five realistic-volume days (S01-S05, 320-450 invoices each) were run through the real web flow on `main` (PR1-PR3); each finding below was confirmed by two independent checks. Stacked on PR5 and the plan map fix (#36); no migration, no new API route (the changed routes keep their rows in `api-role-matrix.spec.ts`). Fixed:

- **B2 (S04, medium): a two-sheet workbook was cut to its first sheet without a word.** `S04_Orders.xlsx` (Orders 1,082 rows + LateOrder 116 rows, 6 urgent clinics) was accepted with no warning and the LateOrder sheet dropped. `parseUpload` now reads every sheet; the order upload refuses order rows on more than one sheet with 400 `MULTIPLE_SHEETS` naming each sheet and its rows ("Upload each sheet as its own file"), saves nothing, and reads a single order sheet wherever it is (`isOrderSheet`, the tenant's aliases included). There is no sheet choice in the screen or the API, so none was added. The customer import and baseline upload name the sheets they did not read ([3.3](#33-step-1-upload-and-validate-the-order-file)).
- **S04 (low): `LATE_REASON_REQUIRED` named the wrong reason.** A file for a day with a plan in use was told "These orders arrived after the planning cutoff". The message is now built from the stored reasons (`lateReasonRequiredMessage`), and the plan reason counts the plan's dispatched and locked loads (`planExistsReason`: "A plan (version 1) already exists for 2026-10-07 (2 of its loads are dispatched)."). The late-order dialog's field no longer says "after cutoff" only.
- **S05 (low): re-upload after a deactivation.** Lines already confirmed for a customer deactivated since were "Customer s05-c05 / MAIN is inactive" errors. They are now duplicates ("Already confirmed: ..., skipped. Customer S05-C05 / MAIN is inactive now: its confirmed orders are not planned until it is reactivated."; the same for a product); a new or changed line stays an error. Duplicate, amendment and inactive messages name the master codes, the refused confirm of a file with errors has the code `FILE_HAS_ERRORS`, and the "already confirmed" file error names the file's dates instead of "this date".
- **Excel (low, S01-S05):** the SUMMARY states invoices (distinct sales orders, `invoiceCounts`) next to delivery orders (one per customer branch; was "Total orders") and stops on trucks; the driver sheets' Notes column carries the access notes and order notes the PDF prints; ASSUMPTIONS no longer claims the x1.25 road time factor for a straight-line plan (PR5 had reworded it to "on road travel times (not on estimated legs)", which still listed it for plans with no road leg) and says when the fuel price was not used; the summary warnings are stored once (the workbook and screen already showed "Distances are ESTIMATED" once; the stored summary had it twice).
- **Raw solver status (low):** `ROUTING_PARTIAL_SUCCESS_LOCAL_OPTIMUM_NOT_REACHED` and the other OR-Tools names are shown in plain words on the options table ("Search" column), the Excel SUMMARY ("Route search"), the "no plan found" job message, and the `INFEASIBLE` unserved reason (the solver's `_no_plan_message`; reasons saved by an older solver are reworded by `withPlainSolverCodes`). `solver_status` itself is stored unchanged.
- **B4 (S03, low): the shortage reasons and warning counted cases only.** On the weight-bound Salalah day (loads 99.8 % full by kg) the warning said "4095 cases short today, but 4206 cases are unserved ... Re-plan to search again" - kg explained all but 64 kg, so a re-plan could not help. `_build_scenario` now also bounds the fleet by kg when every usable truck has a payload: the per-stop reason names kg ("shortage by weight", or "weight is the tighter limit today"), and the warning fires only when neither the case nor the kg shortage explains what is unserved and an unserved stop still fits the room left on some load (the second part since the review below; [4.12](#412-unserved-reason-codes-and-messages)).
- **Checked, not changed by PR6:** the Settings fields with no effect (report #11) were fixed by PR5 (F21); the whole fixed truck cost on each truck's first load (report #14) is PR5's documented allocation (TRUCK DAYS gives the per-truck totals); B1 (dispatched loads following master edits) is PR4's snapshots; B3, N1, N2 and the plan-quality items are not in this PR (B3 and N1: PR7).
- **Tests**: web `tests/lib/scenario-findings-pr6.spec.ts` (23 tests, the two routes on `fake-plan-db.ts`, which now also holds `uploadBatch`), updated `dispatch-order-intake.spec.ts` (duplicate message with master codes), `dispatch-workbook.spec.ts` (the renamed SUMMARY rows) and `plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` (no raw code in the "no plan found" message); integration `tests/integration/scenario-findings.spec.ts`; solver `test_dispatch.py` (a weight-only and a weight-bound shortage, the no-plan reason).
- **Review of PR6, fixed before merge** (all confirmed findings; each code fix has a test that fails without it):
  - *B4 only partly fixed on days with several loads* (medium): the "more than the shortage alone explains ... Re-plan to search again" check allowed one order of slack for the whole day, but packing whole stops leaves room on every load. The reviewer's day (6 trucks x 1 load of 600 cases / 6,500 kg, stops of 30 cases / 336 kg; each load 6,384 kg, 116 kg of room, every unserved stop 336 kg) still got the warning and its re-plan advice, which the dispatcher guide says cannot help; the real S03 day escaped it only because its mixed stop sizes left 78 kg of room in all. The shortage now also explains the day when no unserved stop fits, by cases and by kg, the room left on any load or on a load a truck did not use (`_fits_room_left` in `_build_scenario`); the warning stays when one does fit ([4.12](#412-unserved-reason-codes-and-messages)). Tests: `test_dispatch.py::test_weight_bound_shortage_over_several_loads_warns_nothing` (6 loads of 4 stops of 336 kg, 156 kg of room each), `test_repack.py::test_shortage_warning_counts_the_room_left_on_every_load` (the same day as a fixed plan) and `test_shortage_warning_stays_when_an_unserved_stop_fits_a_load` (a load with 492 kg of room, and a truck that did not load: the warning stays).
  - *The row limit counted sheets that are never read* (low): `parseUpload` added up the rows of every sheet, so 900 order rows next to a 49,500-row customer list were refused ("Too many rows: 50400 on 2 sheets") although stab-5-costs read them. The limit is now for the sheet that is read and names it; the other sheets are named in the warning as before ([3.3](#33-step-1-upload-and-validate-the-order-file)).
  - *The baseline upload dropped the skipped-sheet note* (low): the route built its own warnings without `parsed.warnings`, so this handbook's claim was not true. It now starts from them: the answer, `ManualBaseline.notes` ("Warnings: 1 - Only sheet ... was read ...") and the old run page's baseline tab (a warning message after the upload; it showed no warnings at all before) carry the note.
  - *Fixes tested only in their helpers* (low): new route and read-path tests on `fake-plan-db.ts` (which now also holds `product`, `intakeLineKey` and `manualBaseline`, and counts nested creates for `include._count`): `getPlanDetail` rewords a stored "(ROUTING_FAIL_TIMEOUT)" reason; the Excel export route writes "not used in this plan" for the fuel price when no load has fuel litres and the estimate settings "on the 2 leg(s) that could not be routed on roads" for a road plan with estimated legs; the order upload keeps the skipped-sheet note in the stored `validationJson.warnings`. All in `tests/lib/scenario-findings-pr6-review.spec.ts` (8 tests).
- **Still open** (not in PR6): a sheet choice on the upload screen; the post-solve "Loads were re-assigned after the route search: 9 -> 6 trucks ..." note is still worded for engineers; the unserved-reason codes in the SUMMARY's "Delivery orders unserved" note stay codes (the UNSERVED sheet gives the words); the overweight carton is still found only at optimize (report #18).

**Stabilization release, PR7 "Optimizer: truck count, search time, options trade-off"** (stacked on PR6, which is on PR5 and the plan map fix (#36); answers the confirmed findings B3, T1 and N1 of the Sep 2026 scenario re-test, five realistic days of 160-340 solver stops on main and on main + PR4 + PR5). No migration, no stored JSON shape changed (two optional response fields added):

- **B3** (medium): the truck count ignored the trucks of locked, loading and dispatched loads. `_build_scenario` counted `trucks_used = len({ld.truck_id for ld in loads})` over the NEW loads only, and `load_repack.score()` did the same. After a re-plan the options table and the job message understated the day's physical trucks (the targeted probe, T02 limited to one load: RECOMMENDED shown with 6 trucks but using 7, the alternatives 5 using 6, "10 loads on 6 trucks"), and MIN_TRUCKS, which ranks candidates by that count, could prefer "new loads on a fresh truck" over "new loads on trucks already out", using more physical trucks and paying a fresh truck's day. Fixed:
  - solver: `trucks_used` is the physical count (new loads' trucks + every truck with frozen loads, `_frozen_truck_ids`), also for NOTHING_TO_PLAN / NO_SOLUTION options; new optional `frozen_trucks` and `frozen_loads` on `DispatchScenario` (and in `packages/shared-types`); `LR.Day.frozen_trucks` and `score().trucks` count the same way, so MIN_TRUCKS' selection (`_GOALS`) and the "Loads were re-assigned ... A -> B trucks" note use physical trucks. A truck with frozen loads already never paid the "open a truck" fixed cost again (routing model and `_pricing`, MIN_TRUCKS' ×20 included); now documented and tested ([4.11](#411-post-solve-stage));
  - web: the options table counts each option's trucks from its stored loads + the kept loads' trucks (`physicalTruckCount` in `lib/dispatch/plan-options.ts`, so options saved by an older optimizer show the right number too) and its loads as kept + new ("(N new)"); the job message reads "N new loads + K kept (locked or dispatched) on M trucks" (`jobMessage`; the kept loads are read from the version's rows of `scope.frozenLoadIds`, so a truck deactivated after its load went out - not in the request, so not counted by the optimizer either - still counts, as on the plan screen); the Excel SUMMARY gets the same figures under **PLAN OPTIONS** (its "Physical trucks used" KPI already counted every load of the version).
- **T1** (quality): the automatic search time jumped from 20 s (≤ 200 stops) to 150 s (201-350), so a 200-stop day got 20 s and visibly different plans run to run, while 201 stops waited four minutes. Now monotone without the jump (`auto_time_limit`, `TIME_LIMIT_POINTS`): 20 s up to 120 stops (NMWC's usual 80-120-stop days unchanged), then straight lines to 50 s at 150 stops and 150 s at 200 (135 stops 35 s, 175 stops 100 s), 150 s from 200 to 350, 240 s above as before. It never gives a day size less than the pre-PR7 schedule (owner decision, see the review fixes): **121-200 stops get more** search time (150 stops +30 s, 175 stops +80 s, 200 stops +130 s), so their wall time for three options rises by roughly the extra search time × 1.5 plus a few seconds of re-check (about +45 s at 150 stops; about +3 min at 200, about the 4 min a 201-stop day already took); every other size keeps its time. The straight line suggested with the finding (20 s at 100 stops to 150 s at 350) would give a 120-stop day 30 s (+10 s) and every 201-349-stop day less than 150 s, so the line starts at 120 stops and reaches 150 s at 200. Every value fits the 540 s budget with the slowest road matrix (tested), and the web still waits 600 s ([2.8](#28-timeouts-budgets-and-limits), [4.7](#47-the-or-tools-routing-model)).
- **N1** (quality, transparency only): RECOMMENDED is often dominated by MIN_TRUCKS / MIN_DISTANCE on km and money because it also values early delivery of P1/P2 and preferred hours (a preference the table did not show: +1.6 to +37.2 OMR a day over the cheapest option on the re-test). What RECOMMENDED optimizes is **unchanged**. The options table gets a **Preference cost** column (window + early + continuity, OMR-equivalent; it replaces "Preferred-hours miss", which showed the window part only) and a **What it gains** column (`optionTradeoffs`): RECOMMENDED against the cheapest different plan ("vs MIN TRUCKS: P1/P2 delivered on average 40 min earlier, preference cost 19.5 OMR lower; but costs 35.4 OMR more, 87 km more, 1 more truck"), each alternative against RECOMMENDED, "Same plan as ..." for identical plans. The minutes compare service starts over the P1/P2 stops both options serve (the early priorities follow `early_preference_per_min`, default P1/P2). The Excel SUMMARY lists the same under PLAN OPTIONS. **Owner decision** ([7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)): RECOMMENDED stays as it is (priority-first timing, option (a)); the options table now shows the trade-off.
- **Tests** (each fails without its fix): solver `test_repack.py` (`test_auto_time_limits`, `test_auto_time_limit_schedule`: monotone for 1-600 stops, 80-120 stops at 20 s, 200-350 at 150 s, the budget; `test_auto_time_limit_never_below_the_pre_pr7_schedule`: no size of 1-600 stops gets less than the pre-PR7 schedule (PR7 review, owner decision; fails on PR7's first schedule from 201 stops); `test_score_counts_the_trucks_of_frozen_loads`: score and MIN_TRUCKS' ranking with two trucks already out and one fresh, and no ×20 fixed cost on the trucks already out; `test_min_trucks_uses_the_trucks_already_out`: end to end, MIN_TRUCKS' selection - the trucks already out cost more per km, so RECOMMENDED puts both new loads on the fresh truck and MIN_TRUCKS must still pick the two trucks already out, 2 physical trucks against RECOMMENDED's 3; PR7 review: its first fixture had equal truck costs, so no candidate left the trucks already out idle and it passed on the old code except for the new response fields), `test_dispatch.py` (`test_trucks_used_counts_the_trucks_of_frozen_loads`: the S04 probe shape, and a day with nothing to plan; `test_web_search_time_schedule_is_the_solver_schedule`, PR7 review); web `tests/lib/plan-options.spec.ts` (14 tests, see [5.3](#53-test-suites)), `tenant-settings.spec.ts` (the Settings search-time row and the web copy of the schedule, never below the pre-PR7 schedule, PR7 review) and `plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` (the re-plan job's message counts a kept load whose truck is no longer in the request); integration `dispatch-costs.spec.ts` extended (the late-order re-plan's chosen option counts the locked load's truck and load like the KPI; the job message).
- **PR7 review fixes:**
  - *Settings still stated the old search time* (medium): the "Search time" row of the effective planner values read "20 s up to 200 stops, 150 s up to 350 stops" from a web constant PR7 had not touched, so for every 122-299-stop day it named another time than the optimizer used (a 200-stop day: 20 s stated, 70 s used by PR7's first schedule). The schedule is now in `planner-bounds.json` (`searchTimeSec`: points, small-day and large-day seconds, and examples); the row is built from its web copy (`searchTimeText`, `autoTimeLimitSec`), a pytest checks the JSON against `auto_time_limit` for 1-600 stops and a vitest checks the web copy and the row text.
  - *The B3 end-to-end test did not test MIN_TRUCKS' selection* (low): see Tests above; it now fails on the pre-PR7 solver at the MIN_TRUCKS assertion (the old count picked "both loads on the fresh truck").
  - *The T1 docs said 121-299-stop days get more time* (medium, docs): PR7's first schedule, (120, 20 s), (150, 30 s), (300, 150 s), gave 201-299-stop days less than the old flat 150 s (201 stops 71 s, 240 stops 102 s); the docs were corrected and the question put to the owner.
  - *201-299-stop days got less search time than before* (owner decision): those are the sizes that still had not converged in the re-test (S03, 240 stops, 317.7-377.3 km between runs on 150 s), so the schedule may never give any day size less time than the pre-PR7 one. The points are now (120, 20 s), (150, 50 s), (200, 150 s): 150 s from 200 up to 350 stops. `test_auto_time_limit_never_below_the_pre_pr7_schedule` (solver) and `tenant-settings.spec.ts` (the web copy) fail if any size of 1-600 stops gets less; the former "at most 1 s more per extra stop" guard became monotone + never below the pre-PR7 schedule + the budget. Corrected here, in [2.8](#28-timeouts-budgets-and-limits), [4.7](#47-the-or-tools-routing-model), `OPTIMIZER_DESIGN.md`, `OPTIMIZER_BENCHMARK.md`, `DISPATCHER_GUIDE.md` and `admin.md`.
  - *Older plans compared "preference cost" that was only preferred hours* (low): plans saved without `preference_penalties` (every plan made on main) fell back to `objective.window_penalty` under the name "preference cost", so a row could read "preference cost 11.6 OMR lower ... but P1/P2 delivered on average 68 min later". `preferenceFigures` now keeps the total unknown for them; the trade-off line compares the whole preference cost only when both options report it, else the "preferred-hours cost"; the screen marks such a figure "(hours only)" and the Excel prints "preferred hours only".
  - *Options km was the new loads only* (low): trucks, loads and day cost were the whole day, km not (S04b's in-use row: 436.05 km against the KPI's 476.3). Each option's km is now the whole day (`dayKm`, kept + new, "(new X)" alongside, as for the day cost), on the screen and in the Excel; the trade-off differences are unchanged.
- **Not in PR7** (still open from the re-test): N2 (no "plan from now" in a same-day re-plan: the early-delivery preference still counts from the shift start), identical options are still shown as separate rows (now labelled "Same plan ..."), early-delivery rates listed under ASSUMPTIONS, and repeating S01 / S03 / S04c to separate code effects from search luck (T1 changes their search time: S01 and S02 ran at 197-200 stops on 20 s, now about 144-150 s; S04 at 190 stops 130 s; S03 at 240 stops and S05 at 338 stops keep 150 s, and S03's km varied 317.7 to 377.3 between runs on 150 s, so T1 does not settle it). B2, B4 and the raw solver status codes (the options table's "Search" column) are fixed by PR6, below which PR7 is stacked.

**Stabilization release, PR8 "Same-day planning starts from now"** (branch `stab-8-replan-from-now`, made on PR5 and rebased onto PR7 (#40), which is on PR6 (#38), the plan map fix (#36) and PR5; answers N2, which PR7 listed as open; scenario run of 27 Sep 2026, finding S04 / N2, high, confirmed by two reviewers). A behaviour change, kept on its own in this PR; **approved by the owner when PR8 was merged** (see Owner decisions below). No migration, no new or changed API route; the review fixes below add one optional solver field (`loading_from_min`).

- **The finding.** A re-plan made at 09:00 for today (late order "received at 09:00") planned new loads to leave at 06:00: 5 of the 10 new loads left before 09:00, 5 of the 6 late clinics were "delivered" before their orders existed, and a phone order entered through the late-order dialog was served at 06:12. RouteIQ had no notion of the current time: the request carried only the first departure setting (`buildDispatchRequest`). Not fixed by PR4 or PR5 (checked on `stab-5-costs` before the change), nor by PR6 or PR7 (PR7 lists N2 as not in it).
- **Fixed.** When an OPTIMIZE or a re-plan is built on its own delivery day (company timezone, Asia/Muscat for NMWC; never the server's UTC day), no new load leaves before **now + preparation**, where the preparation time is the existing **Turnaround between loads** setting (30 min for NMWC; no new setting, so no migration). `sameDayPlanFrom` (`lib/dispatch/plan-from.ts`, pure) computes it; `buildDispatchRequest` sends it as `shift_start_min` when it is later than the first departure setting and the depot opening (at most 24:00). The optimizer already applies that field to every truck together with its frozen loads, so: locked, loading and dispatched loads keep their times; a truck still out (back at 09:57) leaves again only after its return + turnaround + loading per case; the P1 / P2 early-arrival preference counts from the new start ([3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions), [4.6](#46-truck-days-and-prefilters)). A plan for a future day is byte-for-byte the request it was.
- **Said plainly.** A plan warning (the first of the optimizer's warnings; the plan screen can show other notes above it): "Planned from 09:30 (now 09:00 + 30 min preparation): the plan is for today, so no new load leaves the depot before 09:30. Locked, loading and dispatched loads keep their times; a truck still out leaves again only after it is back and turned around." (or "... The depot closes at 22:00, so no new load can leave today: open orders stay unserved."). The plan's stored settings keep it (`PlanSettings.planFrom`), the Excel ASSUMPTIONS sheet has a row "Planned from (plan made on the delivery day)" next to the shift start, and the `OPTIMIZE_STARTED` audit row has `planFromMin`. Settings: the First departure and Turnaround hints and the effective values panel say the turnaround is also the same-day preparation time.
- **Testable clock.** `buildDispatchRequest(..., { now })`, `startDispatchOptimize(..., { now })` and `replan(..., { now })` (9th argument) take the time; the routes pass nothing (the clock).
- **Tests.** Web unit `same-day-plan.spec.ts` (15; three fail without the change: the 09:30 start and its warning, the turnaround setting / depot opening, and the new plan's rules flagging a 09:00 load): same-day at 09:00 gives 09:30, the next day unchanged, dispatched loads and truck availability sent unchanged, T01 back at 09:57 still needs its turnaround, the 06:00 dispatched load is not reported, timezone and end-of-day edges, ASSUMPTIONS and Settings texts. Solver `test_dispatch.py` +2 (the optimizer honours the start after a load back at 09:57, VERIFIED; the S04c clinic window becomes `HARD_WINDOW_INFEASIBLE`). Integration `same-day-plan-db.spec.ts` (real database, faked optimizer): the plan made the day before is unchanged; the 09:00 re-plan keeps the dispatched load, starts new loads at 09:30 or later, says so, and its new loads lock.
- **Owner decisions.** (1) The behaviour change: **approved by the owner when PR8 was merged**. Still open ([7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)): (2) the preparation time is the turnaround setting; a separate "same-day preparation" setting would need an additive column (say if NMWC's picking for a late order takes longer than a turnaround). (3) "Now" is taken when the optimization is started; there is no manual "plan from" time on the screen.
- **Still open** (not in PR8): a load that has left but is still PLANNED in RouteIQ is planned again from now (dispatch loads before re-planning; the dispatcher guide says so); a LOCKED load whose departure is already in the past keeps its time without a note; the day screen does not show the start time before OPTIMIZE (the plan warning does after); the web dispatch gate words a same-day early departure as "before the shift start (09:30)".

**PR8 review fixes** (same branch, `stab-8-replan-from-now`; review of the PR8 diff, three findings each confirmed by two reviewers; none is about code PR4 or PR5 changed, so none was fixed there - checked on this branch first). One additive, optional solver field; no migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged).

- *Same-day preparation left out the loading minutes per case for trucks already at the depot* (medium). PR8 sent now + turnaround as `shift_start_min`, but the solver adds the loading per case only after a frozen return (`load_repack._first_departure_s`, `dispatch_solver._truck_days`). A truck idle all morning, or one back before now, left at 09:30 with a 700-case load whose 35 min of loading (at 0.05 min per case) were never counted, while a truck back at 09:00 left at 10:05 with the same load: two trucks of one plan timed by different rules, and departure times the warehouse cannot meet. The web gate did not flag it. **Fixed with a solver change:** on a plan built on its delivery day the web sends the time it is made as `config.loading_from_min` (`planDayNowMin` in `lib/dispatch/plan-from.ts`, `buildDispatchRequest`; `DispatchConfig.loading_from_min` in `packages/shared-types` and `apps/solver/dispatch_models.py`, optional, default None). The solver treats it like a frozen return for the loading gap only (`TruckDay.loading_from_s`, `TruckDay.ready_s` = the later of the two; no trip used, no shift anchor, driver pay unchanged): the route search's start bound, the repack's departure range, the exact timing and `timing_ok` ([4.6](#46-truck-days-and-prefilters)). The solver's check (`feasibility.py`) and the web gate (`feasibility.ts`, `PlanRules.loadingFromMin` from the truck snapshot, only when set, so every other plan's rules and `inputHash` are unchanged) check the same rule as `TURNAROUND` ("... the plan was made at 09:00 on its delivery day, so loading starts then: the truck needs 65 min to reload and load 700 cases: ready 10:05"); where it is the later bound only it is reported (no second `EARLY_DEPARTURE` / turnaround line for the same load in the web gate). The same holds on a plan made on the delivery day before its first departure (05:15 for 06:00): there is no "Planned from", but the time is still sent, the plan warns "Planned on the delivery day at 05:15: loading starts now, so a new load leaves no earlier than now + 30 min turnaround + 0.04 min per case of its load - a full 800-case truck at 06:17, although the first departure is 06:00." when a full truck of the depot would be later than the first departure, and ASSUMPTIONS gets a "Loading from (plan made on the delivery day)" row. The "Planned from" warning and ASSUMPTIONS row name the loading per case; `PlanSettings.loadingFromMin` and `loadingFromMin` in the `OPTIMIZE_STARTED` audit keep the time. Settings: the Turnaround and Loading per case hints and the effective values notes say loading counts from now for every truck on the delivery day. With no loading per case nothing changes in the timetable (now + turnaround is the first departure already). **Deploy the solver before the web**: an older solver ignores the field (Pydantic drops unknown fields), and the web gate then refuses the loads it times too early. Tests: solver `test_dispatch.py` (idle, back at 09:00, back at 08:00: each 700-case load at 10:05 or later, VERIFIED; the next-day plan unchanged), `test_feasibility.py` (hand-built `TURNAROUND` from `loading_from_min`, only the later rule reported after a frozen load back earlier), `test_repack.py` (all three scenarios of a same-day day keep the rule; `assert_plan_rules` checks it) - all three fail on the solver without the fix; web `same-day-plan.spec.ts` (+5; the request, the 05:15 case, the gate timing an idle truck and trucks back at 09:00 / 08:00 the same, the texts; three PR8 tests updated: a 09:00 load on the new rules is now the loading-from `TURNAROUND`, the 09:30 on-time load becomes 09:33, the ASSUMPTIONS text) - eight tests fail without the fix; integration `same-day-plan-db.spec.ts` (the request carries 09:00; an optimizer that ignores it is refused by the gate and a re-plan by one that honours it locks).
- *The dispatcher guide said "Planned from" is the top yellow line of the plan* (low, docs). The plan screen shows the timing warnings above the plan warnings, and inside them the notes about outdated weights, changed master data and drivers come before the optimizer's warnings (`plan-detail.ts`), so the note is often not first. The guide now says "in a yellow note ... (other notes, such as weights or drivers changed, can be above it)"; the handbook ([3.6](#36-step-4-optimize), [3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions), this section) says "a plan warning (the first of the optimizer's warnings ...)". The code is unchanged (moving the note to the top would still leave the timing warnings above it). `repo-guards.spec.ts` refuses the two old phrasings in `docs/` and `lib/`.
- *The solver test table gave `test_dispatch.py` 47 tests* (low, docs). It had 66 (190 overall) before these fixes; `test_repack.py` said 29 of 32, and `test_costing.py` (30) and `test_admission.py` (3) had no row. The table in [5.3](#53-test-suites) now lists every file with the number pytest collects (67, 20, 33, 27, 13, 30, 3 = 193 with these fixes), and `repo-guards.spec.ts` counts the tests from the files (each `def test_`, times its parametrize values) and fails when the table differs.
- **Owner decisions.** (1) Counting the loading per case from now on every truck of a same-day plan, including a plan made on the delivery day before the first departure: **approved by the owner when PR8 was merged** (the alternative, no solver change, was to only say in the texts that loading is not counted for trucks at the depot). Still open ([7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)): (2) a load the warehouse has already started loading before a same-day re-plan should be marked Loading (or locked) first; otherwise it is planned again with its loading counted from now.
- **Still open** (not in these fixes): the route search assumes 80% of a full truck for the first loading on a same-day plan, as it does after a frozen return, so a tight receiving window may be dropped by the search and then placed by the repack as an optional one-stop load (only when the post-solve stage runs); the window prefilter's "earliest possible arrival" does not add the loading time.
- **Rebase onto PR7** (on PR6, the plan map fix and PR5; `git rebase --onto origin/main`, main at `8d69c26`): conflicts only in the imports of `lib/dispatch/workbook.ts` (PR6's `invoiceCounts` / `solverStatusText` kept next to PR8's `plan-from` imports) and in the docs (the dispatcher guide's Optimize step, and the handbook's 2.3 solver-test row, the 5.3 counts, spec and solver-test tables and this 7.4, which keep PR6 / PR7's text and add PR8's after it). Merged without a conflict and checked: `dispatch_solver.py` keeps PR7's `auto_time_limit` / `TIME_LIMIT_POINTS` and physical `trucks_used` / `frozen_trucks`, PR6's kg shortage and plain no-plan reason, and PR8's `loading_from_min` / `TruckDay.ready_s`; `load_repack._identical_trucks` groups by `loading_from_s` as well; the ASSUMPTIONS sheet keeps PR6's "not used in this plan" rows and PR8's "Planned from" / "Loading from" rows; the job message keeps PR6's plain solver status and PR7's "N new loads + K kept (locked or dispatched) on M trucks" (PR8 adds no text to it: its note is the plan warning). The early-delivery preference, and with it the early part of PR7's **Preference cost** (and so the preference-cost part of *What it gains*), counts from the same-day start, because the web sends that start as `shift_start_min` and the solver prices early arrival after `shift_start_min` in the route search, the repack score and `preference_penalties`. New tests pin the combination: solver `test_dispatch.py::test_same_day_early_preference_counts_from_the_same_day_start`, and `same-day-plan-db.spec.ts` checks the re-plan's job message ("2 new loads + 1 kept (locked or dispatched) on 2 trucks"). Counts after the rebase: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Rebase review fixes** (review of the rebased branch, three findings, all low):
  - *A same-day plan made at or after the depot closing blamed locked loads* (low, solver). The same-day start (now + turnaround) at or past `depot.close_min` leaves no truck usable, and `_prefilter` gave every order "No truck has shift time left after its locked/dispatched loads." and the plan "No truck has capacity left for new loads.", also when no truck had a locked or dispatched load (before PR8 that branch was reached only through frozen loads or truck availability). **Fixed** in `dispatch_solver._no_usable_truck` ([4.6](#46-truck-days-and-prefilters)): the reason names what closes the trucks - the start for new loads at or after the closing ("Planned on the delivery day from 18:15: the depot closes at 18:00, so no new load can leave today."; "the delivery day ends at 24:00" on a depot open all day), trucks outside their available hours, or frozen loads (the old wording, kept only then); `_truck_days` and the reason share `_new_load_start_min` / `_truck_hours`, so the two cannot drift apart. Test: `test_dispatch.py::test_no_usable_truck_reason_names_what_closes_the_trucks` (seven cases; six fail without the fix, the frozen-loads case pins the old wording).
  - *The handbook disagreed with itself on whether the same-day start changes PR7's What it gains line* (low, docs). `compare()` in `lib/dispatch/plan-options.ts` builds the line from the P1/P2 minutes-earlier figure (service starts, independent of the start) and the preference-cost difference (whose early part counts from the start). [3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions) and the bullet above now agree: the preference-cost part of *What it gains* counts from the same-day start, the minutes-earlier figure does not.
  - *The docs still called PR8 a change awaiting the owner's approval* (low, docs). Recorded as approved by the owner when PR8 was merged, following PR7: the [6.2](#62-decisions) row, this section's header and Owner decisions, and [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner), which keeps the sub-questions that are still open (a separate preparation setting, a manual "plan from" time, loading that started before the plan).
  - `repo-guards.spec.ts` refuses the old wordings of the last two in `docs/` and `lib/`. Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).

**Bring forward (PR9)** (branch `feat-bring-forward`, on main `83d8174`: PR1-PR8 and the plan map fix; a feature approved by the owner for the NMWC demo of 27 Sep 2026). One additive migration (`20260929090000_order_carry_over`), one new API route (`/api/dispatch/carry-over`, GET any / POST PLANNER, in the role matrix), no solver change.

- **The gap.** An order not delivered on its day (unserved, on a load that never left the depot, or on a day never planned) stayed on its own delivery date: the next day's plan never saw it, so NMWC re-keyed it or lost it.
- **Built.** The day screen of day D lists the depot's orders of [D-7, D-1] whose cases were not delivered (never after today; today's own in a group of their own, unticked), with why, and "Bring forward to <D>" carries the selected ones in one transaction under the intake lock ([3.11](#311-step-9-late-orders-and-plan-versions)): a copy on D with the open cases (same customer, sales orders, products and weight per case; linked by `carriedFromOrderId` / `carriedFromDate`), the original marked (`carriedToOrderId` / `carriedAt` / `carriedById`) and out of its day's scope (`ordersInScopeWhere`), its day's plan versions unchanged. On D the copies are planned by OPTIMIZE, or wait like late orders for RE-PLAN (reason LATE_ORDER) around the frozen loads. Marked on the day screen, the plan's stops and unserved list, the driver sheets and the Excel workbook (load sheets, unserved sheet, a **CARRIED OVER** part on SUMMARY). Audit `ORDERS_CARRIED_OVER`.
- **Safety around it.** An order carried at most once (unique links + the intake lock + `checkSelection`: a second run, or two at the same time, carries nothing new); the list the dispatcher saw is the expected state (409 `CARRY_OVER_CHANGED` otherwise); an order of today only when ticked on its own (409 `TODAY_NOT_SELECTED` otherwise); a load of the earlier day still holding a carried order cannot be locked, loaded or dispatched (409 `ORDERS_CARRIED`: it would be delivered twice); an optimize start whose open orders were carried meanwhile answers 409 `ORDERS_CHANGED`; a sales-order line already on D, or open on two earlier days, is never added twice; the originals' file cannot be deleted (409 `BATCH_CARRIED`); the dashboard counts a carried order once, on its new day.
- **Review fixes (PR9 review).** (1) The window never reaches the company's today (`carryWindow`, preview and `bringForward` alike; changed since by the owner's decision on today's orders, below: capped at today, today's in their own group, unticked): the day screen opens on tomorrow, and on 27 Sep the 28 Sep screen listed and ticked every 27 Sep order still at the depot - loads locked the night before, afternoon trips, waiting late orders - so one click took today's orders off their trucks and blocked those loads (409 `ORDERS_CARRIED`); a later day listed orders not due yet as "never planned". Offering today's leftovers the same evening is an owner question ([7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)). (2) A re-plan after the rest of a split order was carried while its other part was dispatched reconciled "uploaded 100 != planned 60 + unserved 0" for good, so every lock, load and dispatch of that day was refused: such an order now reconciles against the parts the version holds (`carriedHeldCases`). (3) `bringForward` takes the day locks before reading the live plans and checks, under their row locks, that they are still the live ones (409 `PLAN_BUSY`), so a re-plan committing between the read and the lock can no longer leave a load dispatchable unseen. (4) The dashboard subtracts the carried cases (the copy's) from the original's day, so cost per case no longer counts them twice. (5) The Orders list (`GET /api/orders`, the upload page) marks carried originals and copies and leaves carried originals out of an open-status filter. (7) "Re-plan this day to take them off" was refused when nothing else was open (`NOTHING_TO_PLAN` "every load has left the depot", or `NO_ORDERS` "upload orders first"): the 409 now says to leave a load holding only carried orders (`carriedLoadRemedy`), a re-plan with nothing else open says the orders were brought forward (`nothingLeftCarried`), and the plan screen's Re-plan is off for it (`replanWork`). (8) The part of a split order on a dispatched or completed load is no longer marked "carried over" (plan, driver sheet, Excel): only loads that never left are (`carriedLoadShows`). Finding (6) was the same as (1).
- **Second review fixes (PR9).** (1, high) *A sales-order line was carried a second time once its newer copy had been carried or delivered*: the check compared only open orders of the window and the lines on D, so after sales re-entered a line for the next day and that newer order was brought forward (or dispatched, or re-entered for today or a later day, or on another depot's order, or blocked itself), the older order was offered again, ticked, and the line was delivered and invoiced twice (a split example: 2000 cases for a 1000-case order). Now any order with the same line (sales order, customer, product) after the candidate's date, in any depot and state, blocks it (`laterLinesOf`, `SAME_LINE_LATER` "was entered again for ..."), in the preview and inside `bringForward`. (2) *Unticked orders were ticked again whenever the list reloaded* (after a 409, a partial carry, an OPTIMIZE or RE-PLAN, a new file), and a bring forward cannot be undone: the panel now keeps what the dispatcher unticked (`carrySelected`). (3) *A day that is over was offered, pre-ticked and accepted*: a D before the company's today lists nothing and answers 409 `DAY_OVER`. (4) *The earlier day's Step 3 said "unlock it first" or "every load has left the depot"* because of a load holding only brought-forward orders: the day overview gives `loadsOfDay`, and Step 3 uses `dayNothingLeftText`, the server's wording. (5) *A LOCKED or LOADING load holding only carried orders was said to be "never loaded" and to "need nothing"*, though in NMWC's flow it was loaded at night (the case the owner asked about): the 409, the plan badge, Step 3 and `NOTHING_TO_PLAN` now say to unload those cases before the later day's loads are picked, then put the load back to Planned. (6) *While D's first optimization ran, Bring forward stayed on and the toast said OPTIMIZE would plan the copies*: the button is off while D's plan is optimizing or queued, and a carry made meanwhile answers `replanNeeded` + `optimizing` ("RE-PLAN to add them when it finishes").
- **Tests.** Web unit `carry-over.spec.ts` (33) and `carry-over-day.spec.ts` (4), `plan-lifecycle-start.spec.ts` +2; each behaviour was checked to fail without it (scope filter, waiting late order, load gate, start check, day overview, plan detail, driver sheet, workbook, deactivation warning). Integration `carry-over.spec.ts` (real database, faked optimizer; typechecked, run with the integration suites): the owner's scenario end to end, the re-plan around a locked load, concurrency. The review fixes added `carry-over.spec.ts` +14 (window, locks, split re-plan, marks, remedy, dashboard, Orders list) and `plan-lifecycle-start.spec.ts` +3 (`NOTHING_TO_PLAN` with the reason, unlock advice only for a load still holding an order of the day), each checked to fail without its fix, and a fourth integration test (a split order, its day re-planned after the carry). The second review fixes added `carry-over.spec.ts` +18 (the same line entered again, in the pure check and on the in-memory database with two depots; `DAY_OVER`; a day being optimized; the selection kept across reloads; Step 3's words; the loaded-load remedy), `carry-over-day.spec.ts` +1 (`loadsOfDay`) and `plan-lifecycle-start.spec.ts` +1 (the unload advice in `NOTHING_TO_PLAN`), each checked to fail without its fix, and a fifth integration test (the same line entered again for the next day) plus DAY_OVER, `loadsOfDay` and Step 3 checks in the first. The owner's decision on today's orders added `carry-over.spec.ts` +9 (the window, today's candidates and texts, the default selection and kept choices, `TODAY_NOT_SELECTED` in the pure check, on the in-memory database and through the route, the carry at night with today's locks, the load gate's today words, the heading, warning, question and toast, the panel's group and the day screen's today), each checked to fail without its change (20 mutations), and a sixth integration test (the evening carry of today's orders) with today's group checked in the first and fifth. The review of the owner decision added `carry-over.spec.ts` +2 (the rows' labels and the button's count; the plan detail's today and the plan version page's badge) and extended three (the today remedy's exact words for LOCKED and LOADING, the 409 with a LOADING load holding other orders, the question's words, the panel and plan screen sources), each checked to fail without its fix (19 mutations; the question given the POST body is also a type error), and the sixth integration test checks the badge.
- **Owner decision: today's orders (PR9, after the second review).** NMWC loads tomorrow's trucks tonight after 20:00, and the dispatcher plans tomorrow in the evening including today's leftovers; the review's cap at yesterday meant today's undelivered orders could not be brought into tomorrow until midnight. Now `carryWindow` caps the window at today: today's orders qualify exactly like an earlier day's (on a load of today that has not left - PLANNED, LOCKED, LOADING -, unserved, or not planned yet; never on a DISPATCHED or COMPLETED load) and are marked `ofToday`. The day screen lists them after the earlier days in their own group, "Today (27 Sep) - may still leave today", with the warning "Today's loads that have not left yet may still go out today; tick only orders you know will not be delivered today", **unticked by default** (earlier days stay ticked; the panel keeps the dispatcher's choices across reloads). The POST carries an order of today only when it is sent with `today: true` (ticked in that group): otherwise 409 `TODAY_NOT_SELECTED`, nothing carried - never implied by selecting the earlier days. D stays later than the order's day (never today to today). Carrying one is safe for today's plan: the original is closed on today like an earlier day's (its plan rows unchanged), today's day and live plan are locked during the carry, and a load of today still holding it cannot be locked, loaded or dispatched (409 `ORDERS_CARRIED`), with words that say today: re-plan today for its other orders, or unlock it (unload a loaded one) - also on the plan badge and the toast after the carry. The "why" says "has not left the depot yet" / "not planned yet" for today's orders.
- **Review of the owner decision (PR9).** (1) *A LOADING load of today was told to "Unlock it (put it back to Planned)"*, but a LOADING load has only "Back to locked" and "Dispatch" (LOADING to PLANNED answers 409): the today words now say "Put it Back to locked, then Unlock it" for a LOADING load, and, for every loaded one, that a later locked or loading load of the same truck must be unlocked first (`carriedLoadRemedy`; the toast adds "a loading one goes Back to locked first"). (2) *The question before a carry of today's orders said they "come off today's plan"* while today's plan rows stay exactly as they were (the load keeps the order - that is why it is blocked): it now says they are closed on today (today's plan stays as it is, for the record). (3) *Some of the Today group's screen behaviour had no test*: the rows' labels and the button's count are pure helpers now (`carryWhyLabel`, `carryButtonSuffix`), the question takes the listed orders by type (their `ofToday` is required), and the panel test pins the warning outside the closed list and the question's argument. (4) *The plan version page's badge used the earlier-day words for a load of today* (only the day screen passed `today` to `PlanView`), while Lock / Loading / Dispatch on that page answered with the today words: the plan detail now gives the company's `today` (`getPlanDetail` takes the clock), and the badge's title (`carriedLoadTitle`) uses the day screen's today, else the plan's own.
- **Owner decisions made in PR9** (for review): (1) orders on a load that was never marked Dispatched count as not delivered and are ticked by default: the dispatcher unticks a load that really left (the list names the load and its status); (2) a day's orders that arrived after its plan was made and were never planned count as "never planned" and are listed; (3) the copy is late like a late order (after the cutoff of D, or D already has a plan in use), so a re-plan adds it with plan continuity; (4) the priority is kept (no bump); (5) when the same sales-order line is open on two earlier days only the newest is carried, and a line already confirmed for D is not carried; (second review) nor one also on an order of any later date, in any depot and any state (brought forward, dispatched, delivered): the older order is listed with the reason, and a separate delivery of the same line is entered as a late order; (6) the "carried over from" date is the date the order was first due, also after a second carry; (7) the preview is readable by every role, like the day overview; (8) the original keeps its order status (no new enum value), `carriedToOrderId` is the mark; (9, PR9 review; changed by 13) never a day not due yet; (10, PR9 review) a load of an earlier day holding only brought-forward orders is left as it is (history), not re-planned away; (11, second review) when such a load is LOCKED or LOADING it was loaded: the app says to unload its cases (back to stock, or tell the warehouse) before the later day's loads are picked, and to put it back to Planned - moving the loaded truck itself onto the later day is not built; (12, second review) orders are brought forward only to today or a later day; (13, owner) today's orders are listed for a later day in their own group, unticked, with the warning, and carried only when ticked on their own - a load of today still holding one says re-plan today or unlock.
- **Still open** (not in PR9): no "undo bring forward" (a copy cannot be removed in the app, like any planned order); orders are not brought forward by themselves (always the dispatcher's click); the WhatsApp message does not say "carried over"; a window other than 7 days and a priority bump would be owner settings ([7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)). A load of an earlier day holding only brought-forward orders stays in that day's live plan (it can never be dispatched with them; a loaded one is unloaded and put back to Planned; a re-plan with nothing else open has nothing to plan); a "clean-up" version without it would need an optimization with no stops, which the web never sends. (The review fixed the reconciliation of a re-plan after a split order's rest was carried: see Review fixes.)

**Audit of 27 Sep 2026, PR A2 "Dispatch screen: never save the wrong thing"** (branch `audit-2-dispatch-screen`, on main `bc10d7a`: PR1-PR9 with Bring forward; the consolidated assessment's PR 2, owner decision 9 as approved). **Fixed: audit F06, F07, F13, F16, F17**, and in the A2 review the F06 residual (a point read from earlier text) and the docs' wrong claim that an import clears an unloading time; in the second A2 review the docs' wrong claim that the map and the WhatsApp messages follow a corrected pin, and a receiving window sent one end at a time. No migration, no solver change, no new API route; three routes changed behaviour, not their roles (`PATCH /api/customers/[id]`, `GET /api/runs/[id]/export/excel`, `GET /api/runs/[id]/export/pdf`; noted in `api-role-matrix.spec.ts`).

- **F06 (medium): a slow map-link Read for customer A could save A's pin onto customer B.** Read for A (a short link can take seconds), Cancel, open B: A's answer filled B's dialog and Save wrote it to B. The verifiers found a worse variant: the Enter key started a Read while one was running (the Read button was disabled, Enter was not), so A's late answer replaced B's own. **Fixed** in `location-dialog.tsx` with `location-requests.ts` (pure): every Read and Save carries the dialog generation (open, close, another customer) and the input generation (typed text, a pin dropped by hand); an answer whose generation changed is dropped and a running Read is aborted; one request at a time for the button and the Enter key alike; a late Save answer is reported for its own customer and never closes or changes the dialog on screen; the saved input text is the text the point was read from ([3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details)).
- **F07 (medium): the Details dialog saved broken hours and unloading text, and confirmed what nobody touched.** "06:90" became 07:30, "10 min", "ten" or a blank box became 0 minutes, and every Save sent the priority and unloading time as shown, defaults included, which the server marked as confirmed. **Fixed** (owner decision 9): times through the shared strict `parseHhmm` (the dialog's lax `toMinutes` is removed); unloading time whole minutes 0 to 480; blank = the customer-type or Settings default, not confirmed (`avgServiceTimeMin: null`: the column default stored with `serviceTimeConfirmed = false`); an explicit 0 allowed; only changed fields sent (`customer-details.ts`; a changed window with both its ends, second review); a default priority or unloading time opens as a default (the day overview now sends `serviceSource`); 24:00 reads back. The API takes those fields as JSON numbers only (a coerced "" used to be 0).
- **F13 (medium): after a pin or hours correction, the plan below, its badges and the WhatsApp text stayed out of date** until the page was reloaded (a READY plan does not poll; LOCKED loads, the usual case when a driver phones about a wrong pin, are not in the day's `outdated` count). **Fixed:** after either dialog saves, the day is refreshed and the plan is read again in place (`afterCustomerSaved`, `PlanView` `reloadSignal`; never a remount, which would close an open late order), so its orange "changed after planning" notes, the **Changed after planning** badge, its warnings and the WhatsApp texts' "New pin - ask the dispatcher" line show at once. The stop itself keeps the pin it was planned with (its pin link, the route link and the map) until the load is re-planned, a LOCKED one after it is unlocked (second A2 review: this bullet and the guide said the map and the messages switch to the new pin; they never did, see [3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details)).
- **F16 (low): a dispatch plan with every order unserved had no dispatch Excel.** The screen offered no export and the route chose the legacy route sheet by the load count alone. **Fixed:** both export routes use `isDispatchPlan`, and the plan screen `PlanDetail.isDispatchPlan` (`isDispatchPlanShape`, the same rule, kept equal to `DISPATCH_PLAN_WHERE` by a test). The PDF stays driver sheets only: no button without loads, and the route answers 404 `NO_LOADS` for such a plan instead of a legacy route sheet ([3.10](#310-step-8-exports)).
