- **F17 (low): impossible DMS coordinates were accepted with high confidence.** 23°99'00"N became 24.65°N (a real point in Oman), and whole degrees counted as exact. **Fixed** in `parseDms` (`location-input.ts`): minutes and seconds below 60, seconds need minutes, latitude within 90° and longitude within 180°, else refused with the reason; whole degrees (LOW) or whole minutes (MEDIUM) need the pin confirmed (`needsPin`); the same inside Google Maps links and on `PUT /api/customers/:id/location` with `{ input }` (422).
- **Review fixes (A2 review, each confirmed by two reviewers).** No migration, no route changed.
  - *F06 residual (low): after the text was changed, Save still stored the point read from the previous text.* Only the requests in flight were bound to the input; a preview already on screen stayed valid. Read "23.600100, 58.400200" (found, high confidence), replace it by "23.700300, 58.500400" and Save without reading again: the green "Found 23.600100, 58.400200" box stayed next to the new text, and the PUT sent the old point, which the route marked verified with HIGH confidence. Text typed but never read did the same with a customer's existing pin (saved as `MAP_PIN` under the unread link). **Fixed** in `location-dialog.tsx`: the preview is out of date whenever the text in the box is not the text it was read from (`textUnread`). It turns grey, the dialog says "Text changed - press Read" (or "Press Read to find this point before saving"), and Save location is off, in the button and in `save()`, until a Read of the text on screen comes back. A pin the dispatcher drops by hand is still saved (the note says Save keeps that pin). The saved input is only ever text that was read (a hand pin without a Read is stored as "map pin"). Found while fixing it: "Confirm & save" after a 422 `OUTSIDE_AREA` stayed on for the next point read or dropped by hand, so a new point outside Oman/UAE was saved without being asked about. A new point now puts the button back to "Save location" ([3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details)).
  - *The docs said a customer import can clear an unloading time back to the default* (low, docs): this block's "Still open" bullet and the branch's owner notes. An import cannot: a blank cell (or no column) keeps the stored time and its confirmation, a number always confirms, and no import path writes `serviceTimeConfirmed = false`. Only the day screen's Details dialog, with the box emptied (`avgServiceTimeMin: null`), puts a customer back on the default. Corrected here and in [3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details). `customer-import-service-time.spec.ts` pins the import side, integration `customers-import.spec.ts` checks a blank cell on the real database, and `repo-guards.spec.ts` refuses the old wording in `docs/` and `lib/`.
  - Tests: `dispatch-location-dialog.spec.ts` +5 (scenario 3 from the reviewers' `.dev/scratch-a2-v1/ui-v1/a2-residual.spec.tsx`: text changed after a Read; a failed Read of the new text; text never read; a hand pin; "Confirm & save" reset), each checked to fail without its part of the fix (9 mutations: the rule in the button, in `save()`, or both; unread text sent as the input; either reset of "Confirm & save"; the grey preview; the note; the note hidden while the Read runs). The reviewers' harnesses (`ui-v1` F06 scenarios, the skeptics' S1, S2, S2-a and S2-b) show Save off and no PUT on react 18.3.1 and on Next's bundled React. `customer-import-service-time.spec.ts` (5) passes before and after (it pins the code the docs now describe); the new `repo-guards.spec.ts` pattern fails on the old handbook.
- **Second review fixes (second A2 review, each confirmed by two reviewers).** No migration, no route changed, no solver change.
  - *The docs promised that a corrected pin reaches the map and the WhatsApp messages at once* (medium, docs). The guide's ADD LOCATION bullet, this block's F13 bullet and [3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details) said so; the code never did. A plan keeps the pin each stop was planned with (`getPlanDetail` reads the stop snapshot), so after the reload the map, the stop's pin link and the route link are the planned ones; only the orange note, the **Changed after planning** badge and the "New pin - ask the dispatcher which one to use: <link>" line in the WhatsApp text and the driver sheet are new (`whatsappText` says so: never switched silently). The usual case is a driver phoning about a wrong pin on a LOCKED load: a dispatcher who trusted the guide re-sent the message believing its links now went to the new pin, and did not unlock and re-plan. **Corrected** in the guide (ADD LOCATION: what the refresh shows, and that the plan still goes to the old pin until the load is re-planned, a locked one unlocked first), in the F13 bullet above, in [3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details) and in the comments of `dispatch-client.tsx` and `dispatch-screen-reload.spec.ts`. `repo-guards.spec.ts` refuses the old claims in `docs/`, `lib/` and the dispatch screen's code.
  - *Details sent one end of a receiving window alone* (low). `detailsPatch` compared and sent each end on its own, and `PATCH /api/customers/:id` fills a missing end from the stored row. The day screen does not poll outside an optimization, so the dialog can open with hours changed since by another dispatcher or tab: 06:00-10:00 on screen, 12:00-15:00 stored since, end set to 16:00 sent `{hardWindowEndMin: 960}` and saved 12:00-16:00, a hard window nobody typed (the optimizer then treats the customer as closed before 12:00). Before A2 every Save sent all four window values, so only whole-window overwrites were possible; owner decision 9 (do not confirm an untouched priority or unloading time) never needed the ends split. **Fixed** in `customer-details.ts`: when either end of a window changed, both ends are sent as the dialog shows them; type, priority and unloading time stay only-when-changed. The route still accepts one end (other callers), unchanged ([3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details)).
  - Tests: `dispatch-customer-details.spec.ts` 19 to 21 (the "only the fields that changed" and "a changed receiving hour" cases now expect the pair; +2 with the real dialog and the real PATCH route on a customer whose stored window differs from the form's: 12:00-15:00 stored, end set to 16:00 saves 06:00-16:00; 06:00-08:00 stored, start set to 09:00 saves 09:00-10:00 instead of a 400). Reverting the fix fails 5 of them. `dispatch-screen-reload.spec.ts` 7 to 8: after the reload the stop's WhatsApp link is the planned pin, the new pin is only on the "New pin" line, the route link is unchanged and the map gets the planned point (fails when the map or the message is switched to the new pin: 2 mutations, `plan-view.tsx` and `whatsappText`). `repo-guards.spec.ts` +1 (fails on the old guide, handbook and `dispatch-client.tsx` comment). The existing integration test `dispatch-mvp.spec.ts` step 27 already pins the planned pin, window and map link of a LOCKED load after a pin correction on the real database.
- **Tests** (each fails without its fix, checked by reverting the fix): web unit `dispatch-location-dialog.spec.ts` (10; 15 with the review fixes), `dispatch-customer-details.spec.ts` (19; 21 with the second review), `dispatch-screen-reload.spec.ts` (7; 8 with the second review), `dispatch-export-routes.spec.ts` (5), `dispatch-location-input.spec.ts` +7, `day-overview-weights.spec.ts` +1. The component tests drive the real dialogs, `DispatchClient` and `PlanView` through `tests/lib/hook-host.ts` (no DOM; `vitest.config.ts` now compiles JSX with the automatic runtime), reusing the verifiers' scenarios (`.dev/audit-verify/ui-v1`, `ui-v2`, `intake-v1`, `intake-v2`). Integration `dispatch-screen-db.spec.ts` (real database, faked optimizer, no web server; typechecked, run with the integration suites). Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Still open** (not in this PR): a Read result that needs a pin (`needsPin`) can still be saved as shown: Save is the confirmation, with the point on the map and the yellow note (unchanged design; say if a pin move should be required for LOW confidence; **answered by the owner on 27 Sep 2026**: "its a standing rule locations should always be correct as part of sop no item will be delivered without location", a rule the system enforces, audit PR A5 below and [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner): since A5 such a reading needs a pin placed by hand, in the dialog and in the route); the Customers page's own map editor (`customer-editor.tsx`, one customer per page) was not part of F06 (since audit PR A5 it opens the same ADD LOCATION dialog); only the day screen's Details dialog, by emptying the unloading box (it sends `avgServiceTimeMin: null`), puts a customer back on the customer-type or Settings default: a customer import can give a new own time but cannot clear one, because a blank cell keeps the stored time (corrected in the A2 review; this bullet used to say an import could).

**Audit of 27 Sep 2026, PR 3 "Intake and master data"** (branch `audit-3-intake-master`, on main `bc10d7a`; the external audit of the production build, verified independently, with the owner's approved defaults, decisions 6-8 and 10). One migration (`20260930093000_master_data_no_orphans`: four foreign keys to NO ACTION, safe on existing rows, [5.5](#55-database-migrations) with the owner's read-only pre-check), no new API route (the changed routes keep their roles; noted in `api-role-matrix.spec.ts`), no solver change.

| Item | Status | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| **F02** (medium) merged rows lost priority and notes | **Fixed** | Rows with the same sales order, customer branch, product and date: the highest priority of the rows (P1 wins; a first row without a priority no longer hides a P1 on the next row), every distinct note (line and order, each once), in any row order; each row kept with the check (`mergedRows`) and a warning naming the rows when they differ ([3.3](#33-step-1-upload-and-validate-the-order-file)). A file checked before this release with such a line is refused at confirm with `STALE_VALIDATION` (upload again). Owner question still open: can NMWC's ERP repeat the same sales order and product? |
| **F04** (low) partial money looked complete | **Fixed** | A merged line's sales value and margin are known only when every row has them (a blank is unknown, never 0), so the order's money stays unknown and money-based scoring never uses a subtotal; a warning names the blank rows |
| **F05** (low) import overwrote a pin verified meanwhile | **Fixed** | The import writes coordinates with an update conditional on "still not verified", checked by PostgreSQL on the current row; the kept count comes from those updates ([3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details)) |
| **F03** (low; medium with a 2nd depot) deleting a depot orphaned its orders | **Fixed** | A depot that anything refers to (trucks, regions, plans, orders, order files) is deactivated, never deleted; counted under the depot's row lock, a database refusal answered by deactivating; the database refuses such deletes (NO ACTION); the dialog's title, text and button use the same rule and words (`lib/master-data-delete.ts`); deactivating (also with Edit) warns that its open orders keep the depot and wait for it; a file checked for a depot deactivated since is refused at confirm (`MASTER_CHANGED`) |
| **F20** (low) a driver delete raced with dispatch | **Fixed** | A driver is always deactivated, never deleted (screen button **Deactivate**); the trucks that keep them as default driver are named, never cleared; the database refuses a delete of a driver on a load or set as a default (NO ACTION) ([3.9](#39-step-7-a-driver-per-load)) |
| **F24** (low) Customers page toggle without failure handling | **Fixed** | `runInlineUpdate`: saved = the server's values; refused (only the app's 4xx answer) = rolled back with the error; not confirmed (no answer, a 5xx, or an answer that is not the app's JSON) = reloaded from the server (a GET), or rolled back and marked **not confirmed** until the page reloads; never re-sent, never thrown (no error card); the table re-syncs with the server's data after each refresh |
| **F25** (low) midnight availability did not round-trip | **Fixed** | `lib/dispatch/time-input.ts`: 1440 shows as 00:00 in an "until" field, 00:00 / 24:00 save as 1440, a field left as loaded keeps its stored minutes ([3.12](#312-settings-that-change-the-plan)) |
| **F26** (low) optional fields could not be cleared | **Fixed** | `clearable()` in `lib/schemas.ts`: in a save, left out = unchanged, empty or null = cleared. A driver's phone clears; a region's depot clears and a region is created without one (the form sends null for "No depot"); the same rule for a depot's address, a truck's description and a customer's branch, region, address and access notes |

- **Tests** (each fails without its fix, checked by reverting it): `intake-merged-rows.spec.ts` (16: both row orders, a first row without priority, notes, provenance and warning, money, the confirmed order, the old-merge and depot re-checks), `master-data-delete.spec.ts` (12: the depot route under its lock, the P2003 fallback, the PATCH warning, the page and dialog; since the second review also the Drivers screen's words and the Edit forms' warnings, which before had no test), `customer-import-locations.spec.ts` (3), `customer-inline-update.spec.ts` (16 with the review fixes), `time-input.spec.ts` (6), `clear-semantics.spec.ts` (8 with the second review's "No depot" check), `api-guards-runtime.spec.ts` (drivers: never deleted, truck defaults named, phone cleared). Integration (library level, real PostgreSQL, typechecked; run with the integration suites) `master-data-db.spec.ts`: merged rows through upload and confirm, the depot with orders (deactivated, file refused, database refusal), the import race forced with a row lock, drivers on a dispatched load and never used, clearing phone and region depot; `driver-app-retired.spec.ts` now expects an unused driver to be deactivated. The verifiers' own experiments (`.dev/audit-verify/intake-v1`, `auth-v1/f20.spec.ts`) were re-run on a throwaway database with this branch: each case now shows the fixed behaviour (P1 with both notes in both orders, the depot deactivated and the late file refused, the pin kept and counted, the phone and region depot cleared, no driver ever deleted in 200 natural races).
- **Review fixes (audit PR 3).** (1, low) *F24 was only half closed: a server error was shown as "nothing was saved"*. `runInlineUpdate` re-read the row only when the request failed with no answer; any other error answer rolled the row back with "Update failed: nothing was saved." (or "Internal server error"). A 5xx does not mean nothing was saved: `PATCH /api/customers/:id` stores the change and only then writes the audit entry and counts the open orders (an error there answers 500 after the change is stored), and a gateway 502 / 504 during a redeploy or a timeout can come after the app saved. Reproduced with the verifier's harness on the React build bundled with Next (`.dev/scratch-a3-v1/ui24/f24-gateway.spec.tsx`): the server had the customer inactive, the screen showed it active, no reload, no mark. Now only the app's 4xx answer (its JSON error: not allowed, invalid, not found, a conflict) is a refusal; a 5xx, or an answer that is not the app's `{ data, error }` JSON (a proxy's HTML page, an empty body, also with status 200 or 408), is handled like no answer: the row is reloaded with a GET and shows what the server has ("The server answered with an error (502): the change may or may not have been saved. The row now shows what the server has saved."); if that fails, or its answer has no row, the row goes back and is marked **not confirmed**. The same harness now shows the saved value in all three cases (504, 502, 500 after the change was stored). The PATCH itself was left as it is: the audit entry in the same transaction would not cover a gateway error or the open-order count, which the client now handles for every case. (2, low, docs) *The dispatcher guide's "Customers page" bullet had taken over two customer-import sentences* (the 480-minute service-time rule and **Validate only**): they are back under **Customer import**, and the Customers page bullet covers only the inline toggles (with the server-error case). (3, low, docs) *The guide said a file checked before the update "asks to be checked again"*, which read as every such file: it now says only a file with the same sales order and product on two rows is refused at **Add** (`mergedTheOldWay`).
- **Review fixes tests.** `customer-inline-update.spec.ts` +9 (a 409 and a 400 as refusals without a reload; 502 and 504 with an HTML body, 500 after the change was stored and 503 with an empty body, each reloaded to the saved value and never "nothing was saved"; a 5xx whose reload fails, or answers without the row, marked "not confirmed"; a 200 and a 408 that are not the app's JSON reloaded), checked by mutation (6, 2 and 1 tests fail when a 5xx is taken as a refusal, when an answer that is not the app's JSON is trusted, and when a reload without the row is trusted); `repo-guards.spec.ts` +3 (the guide's customer bullets and the old-merge sentence; all three fail on the previous guide).
- **Second review fixes (audit PR 3).** (1, low, tests) *The screen side of F20 / F03 had no test*: nothing read the Drivers table or the driver and depot Edit forms, so putting them back as they were on main left every check green: the Drivers dialog would again promise "A driver never used on a load is deleted; that cannot be undone" and the toast say "deactivated instead of deleted" (the API always deactivates), and the Edit forms would drop the answer's warning (the open orders waiting for an inactive depot, the trucks that keep an inactive driver as default). `master-data-delete.spec.ts` now reads the three files: the Drivers button, dialog and toast say **Deactivate** and never speak of deleting, and the Drivers Deactivate and both Edit forms show `data.warning`. No behaviour change. (2, low, docs) *The dispatcher guide said to choose "No depot" for a region, but the form's option read "— none —"*. The region form's option is now **No depot** (the words of the guide, the F26 row above and the tests), and the guide says "choosing **No depot** as a region's default depot".
- **Second review fixes tests.** `master-data-delete.spec.ts` +2 (the Drivers screen's words; the warning toasts): the first fails with `drivers-table.tsx` from main, and also when only its old dialog sentence or only its old toasts are put back; the second fails with any one of the three files from main. `clear-semantics.spec.ts` +1 (the region form's empty option is "No depot" and the guide's *Clearing a field* bullet names it): fails with the old option and with the old guide sentence.
- **Not in this PR:** a screen to move orders that already lost their depot (check 2 in [5.5](#55-database-migrations); **answered by the owner on 27 Sep 2026**, "no, all orders must have depots linked to them": audit PR A5 gives every such order and file a depot and the database requires one, see below); an "On hold" state for masters (owner policy 19).

**Audit PR4 "Recovery, races, readiness"** (branch `audit-4-recovery`, on main `bc10d7a`: PR1-PR9; the external audit of 27 Sep 2026, its verified assessment `.dev/audit-2026-09-27/VERIFIED-ASSESSMENT.md`, PR 4 of its plan). No migration. Two new API routes (`POST /api/runs/[id]/reset-stuck` SUPERVISOR, `GET /api/health/live` public; both in the role matrix), one new solver endpoint (`GET /ready`, token-protected). Owner decisions applied as approved: 5 (health gate), 17 (Reset stuck plan), 18 (email reset off until this lands).

- **F09 - Fixed.** *A crash between the janitor's two writes left a plan "optimizing" for ever* (medium): the janitor failed the job, then updated the plan separately; when the second write failed the plan stayed OPTIMIZING behind a FAILED job, later sweeps ignored it, OPTIMIZE answered 202 with the dead job and started nothing, RE-PLAN answered 409 "optimizing", and on a re-plan version even locked loads could not be dispatched. Now:
  - `reapStuckJobs`, `failJob` and the stale-result path write the job, the plan and the `OPTIMIZE_FAILED` audit row in ONE transaction under the plan row lock (plan, then job: the success path's order). A failed write changes nothing; the next sweep retries ([5.9](#59-job-failures-and-the-janitor)).
  - Rows already stranded are repaired: the janitor's `repairStuckPlans` puts every plan OPTIMIZING behind an ended or missing job back to FAILED (`OPTIMIZE_FAILED`, reason `STUCK_PLAN`), and an OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN request for such a plan does the same first, then starts real work (the screens disable both buttons while the plan shows *Optimizing…*: there the janitor or **Reset stuck plan** does it; see the review fixes below). A 202 now always names a job the database has QUEUED or RUNNING (never an ended job, never the in-flight map alone); OPTIMIZING without one is 409 `PLAN_STUCK` (decided on the locked plan row since the review fixes).
  - **Reset stuck plan** (owner decision 17): SUPERVISOR and above, `POST /api/runs/:id/reset-stuck` and a button under *Optimizing…* on the plan screen when the server says the plan is stuck (`PlanDetail.stuck`). It also fails a job lost by a restart (not in this web process, older than 2 minutes) instead of waiting 15 minutes; refused while the job really runs here or just started. Audited `PLAN_RESET` (who, the job, the kind, an optional note). The dispatcher guide says what the dispatcher sees and does.
- **F10 - Fixed.** *A retired reset link could overwrite a newer password, and a link and an admin reset could deadlock* (low for NMWC: email reset is off): issuing a link, using one and an admin reset now lock the user's row first (`lockUserCredentials`, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`), always user then links; a link is consumed only while unused and unexpired, checked in the `DELETE` (0 rows = refused). The verifiers' deadlock (link-then-user against user-then-link) cannot happen any more. Email reset may now be switched on (decision 18, owner action: `RESEND_API_KEY` + `RESEND_FROM`).
- **F11 - Fixed.** *Two admins demoting or deactivating each other at the same instant left the company without an admin*: `PATCH /api/users/:id` locks the company's `Tenant` row (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which never blocks rows that only reference the tenant), re-reads the user, re-checks "another active admin remains", and writes the change and its audit row in one transaction (409 on a 5 s lock wait). The second of two such requests is refused (400).
- **F15 - Fixed.** *`/api/health` was green with the solver token missing or wrong*: `/api/health` is now dispatch readiness (`lib/health.ts`): definite misconfiguration - `SOLVER_URL` / `SOLVER_TOKEN` missing on web, the solver's 401, or a solver without its own token - is 503 `not_ready` (the deploy fails, the previous version serves); an unreachable or older solver is 200 `degraded` with `ok: false` (alert, do not block: decision 5). The check is one authenticated `GET /ready` on the solver (the token check and nothing else, pytest-covered); it never optimizes. `/api/health/live` is process-only liveness. A production web without `SOLVER_URL` / `SOLVER_TOKEN` also logs a `[config]` error at boot ([5.8](#58-health-endpoint)). Since the review fixes the check and the solver calls read both values one way (`solverEnv`).
- **F19 - Fixed.** *The Docker OSRM health check only ran `osrm-routed --version`*: `infra/osrm/docker-compose.yml` now runs `healthcheck.sh` (mounted read-only): a Muscat `/nearest` query that must answer HTTP 200 with `"code":"Ok"` within 4 s, through curl, wget or bash's `/dev/tcp` (the image has no curl); `start_period` 60 s. `infra/osrm/.gitattributes` keeps it LF on Windows checkouts. `osrm.yml` runs it inside the built image (passes on the live graph, fails on a dead port). Checked on the branch against fake servers (healthy, `NoSegment`, HTTP 500, hung, no listener) with the curl and bash clients; not run in Docker here. Railway is unaffected (its own `/nearest` health path).
- **F21 - Fixed.** *A plan read or an export racing "Use instead" could mix two options* (screen and Excel: summary and chosen option of A, loads of B, reconciliation "ok"): `getPlanDetail` reads every row in ONE REPEATABLE READ transaction on the tenant-scoped client (`PLAN_DETAIL_TX`: 5 s wait, 20 s limit, no locks, nothing slow inside; the exports render after it returns). The tenant scope holds inside the transaction (tested).
- **Tests.** Real PostgreSQL race tests in `tests/integration/recovery-races-db.spec.ts` (19; the verifiers' schedules from `.dev/audit-verify/{authv2,auth-v1,lifecycle1,lifecyclev2}`, forced with a query-extension barrier and a temporary trigger): run on a throwaway database on the branch, all green; with each fix reverted its group fails (F10 5 of 6, F11 3 of 4, F09 5 of 6 - the new reset passes either way -, F21 2 of 3; the remaining ones are controls). Unit: `health.spec.ts` (14), `stuck-plan.spec.ts` (15, two of them static checks of the plan screens), `plan-lifecycle-start.spec.ts` +6, `plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` +2, `api-guards-runtime.spec.ts` +2 (the lock orders), `ops-config.spec.ts` +1; solver `test_security.py` +4. Each fails without its fix (checked by reverting).
- **Deploy notes.** No migration. Deploy the solver with or before the web (a web meeting an older solver answers `degraded`, never 503). The merge touches `infra/osrm/**`, so Railway's `routeiq-osrm` (watch path) rebuilds; the Dockerfile is unchanged, so its layers should come from cache, and the running server keeps serving until the new one passes its health check. After the deploy the janitor repairs any plan still stranded within a minute (read-only check in [5.10](#510-operating-runbook-pointers)).
- **Still open** (not in this PR): a load change on a stuck re-plan version still answers "wait for the running optimization" until the repair runs (at most a minute); the rate of `/ready` calls follows the public `/api/health` (no cache); no index on `RunPlan.status` was added (the sweep scans the plan table once a minute for the few OPTIMIZING rows; fine at today's size, an additive index if it grows).

**Audit PR4 review fixes** (same branch, `audit-4-recovery`; review of the audit PR4 diff: five findings, each confirmed by two reviewers, three distinct problems, all low; each fix has a test that fails without it, checked by reverting). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged), no solver change.

- **Fixed.** *OPTIMIZE answered a false 409 `PLAN_STUCK` when the plan's job ended during the request* (low; a regression of the F09 fix). `unstickIfEnded` read the plan, counted the active jobs and, when the job had ended in between and moved the plan to FAILED or READY itself, found nothing to repair and handed back the plan row read before (still OPTIMIZING). `startDispatchOptimize` then answered 409 `PLAN_STUCK` ("a supervisor can reset it now") for a plan that was FAILED or READY, and a supervisor's Reset got 409 `NOT_STUCK`. RE-PLAN of a first version went the same way. The verifiers forced it with a barrier (`.dev/scratch-a4-v1/stale-stuck`) and also saw it without one (their lifecycle F09 scenario, `verif-l1`: RE-PLAN right after a retry). Now `unstickIfEnded` always reads the plan again after the job checks, and the `PLAN_STUCK` answer before the start transaction is gone: 409 `PLAN_STUCK` is decided only inside the start transaction, on the locked plan row, where a job's end and its plan's status are always seen together (every way a job ends writes both under the plan row lock) ([3.6](#36-step-4-optimize)). Tests: `plan-lifecycle-start.spec.ts` +5 (the job fails or saves its plan between the plan read and the count; it fails between the count and the job lookup; a RE-PLAN of a version holding a plan whose job fails meanwhile creates the next version; a plan really stuck still answers `PLAN_STUCK`), `recovery-races-db.spec.ts` +2 on real PostgreSQL (the verifiers' barrier schedule, and the job saving its plan after the count). The verifiers' own experiments, re-run on a throwaway database against the branch: `stale-stuck` answers 202 with a new job in the FAILED and READY variants (409 `PLAN_STUCK` before); the unforced F09 scenario gave `PLAN_STUCK` in 3 of 3 runs before the fix and in 0 of 5 after.
- **Fixed.** *`/api/health` said `ready` while every optimization failed, for a `SOLVER_URL` ending in `/` or a `SOLVER_TOKEN` with a line break after it* (low; two findings). The readiness check trimmed both values and dropped trailing slashes; `callDispatchSolver` and `callRouteGeometry` sent them as they were. With `http://solver:8000/` every optimization posted `//optimize-dispatch` (404, shown as "The route optimizer is being updated") while `/api/health` was green; the old `/health` check had answered 503 for the same URL. A token with a line break after it was `ready` while no call could even be sent (invalid header). Now one function, `solverEnv()` in `lib/solver-env.ts`, reads both values for the optimize call, the road-line call, the readiness check and the startup log: spaces and line breaks around them and trailing slashes on the URL are dropped, and a value that is empty after that is "not set". The check asks exactly the URL and sends exactly the token an optimization uses, and a trailing slash or a pasted line break no longer stops optimizing ([2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service), [5.8](#58-health-endpoint)). Tests: `health.spec.ts` +4 (the real check and the real calls against one in-process HTTP server that matches paths exactly, as FastAPI does). The verifiers' `health-slash` experiment now reaches `/ready` and `/optimize-dispatch` with the same token, and the optimize call succeeds.
- **Fixed (wording).** *The stuck-plan text, the dispatcher guide and this handbook said OPTIMIZE or RE-PLAN resets a stuck plan, but both buttons are disabled while the plan is OPTIMIZING* (low; two findings). The plan screen's text for a plan behind an ended or missing job now says RouteIQ resets it by itself within a minute, or a supervisor can press **Reset stuck plan** now, then optimize or re-plan again. The dispatcher guide and this handbook ([2.7](#27-background-job-model), [5.10](#510-operating-runbook-pointers)) say the same. The buttons were not enabled for a stuck plan (the findings' other option): the state lasts at most a minute (the janitor sweep), the plan screen reloads every 2.5 s while it shows *Optimizing…* so the buttons come back by themselves, and an OPTIMIZE enabled on a plan shown as optimizing would add a screen state that no DOM test covers. The server still resets such a plan first when an OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN request reaches it. Tests: `stuck-plan.spec.ts` +3 (no stuck text names OPTIMIZE or RE-PLAN, with a static check that both buttons are off while optimizing; the new wording; the guide).
- **Deploy notes.** No migration, no solver change. Behaviour changes: a `SOLVER_URL` with a trailing slash, or a `SOLVER_TOKEN` with spaces or a line break around it, now works (before, every optimization failed); the text under *Optimizing…* for a stuck plan changed; OPTIMIZE never answers `PLAN_STUCK` for a plan whose job has just ended.

**Audit PR4 second review fixes** (same branch, `audit-4-recovery`; second review of the audit PR4 branch: three findings, each confirmed by two reviewers, all low; each fix has a test that fails without it, checked by reverting). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged), no solver change. Everything else the reviewers re-checked on the branch held (F09 fault experiments, F21 0 of 550 racing reads mixed, F10 / F11 races).

- **Fixed.** *The plan screen briefly said "the server restarted" and showed **Reset stuck plan** when a normal optimization finished during a plan read.* `getPlanDetail` reads the plan and its job from one REPEATABLE READ snapshot (F21) but asked the in-flight map at the END of the read. A job more than 2 minutes old that saved its plan (job SUCCEEDED, plan READY) and left the map while the read ran was still OPTIMIZING / RUNNING in the snapshot and no longer live, so it read as `JOB_LOST`, resettable. For one reload (the screen reloads every 2.5 s) the dispatcher saw *This optimization stopped without a result (the server restarted while it ran)* and a supervisor the button; pressing it answered 409 `NOT_STUCK` (nothing changed). The reviewers forced it with a barrier and measured it without one: 140 of 200 reads racing a job's end showed it. Now `getPlanDetail` asks the in-flight map once, before the snapshot starts, and hands that answer to the stuck check. A job in the map then is either still active in the snapshot (really optimizing) or already ended there (plan READY or FAILED, never stuck); a job started after the question is under 2 minutes old, so never "lost". A job really lost by a restart (not in the map, older than 2 minutes) is still `JOB_LOST` ([2.7](#27-background-job-model)). Tests: `stuck-plan.spec.ts` +2 (the race: no stuck line; controls: a live job is not stuck, a job not in the map and 3 minutes old is `JOB_LOST`, a plan behind an ended job is `JOB_ENDED` whatever the map says); `recovery-races-db.spec.ts` +1 on real PostgreSQL (the reviewers' barrier schedule: the job commits SUCCEEDED / READY and leaves the map before the read's job lookup; typechecked). The reviewers' own experiments (`.dev/scratch-a4-v2/flash`, `sk2-flash`), re-run on a throwaway database against the branch: the forced race gives no stuck line (`JOB_LOST` with the fix reverted), and the unforced one 0 of 200 false "lost" reads (138 of 200 with the fix reverted).
- **Fixed (wording).** *The admin runbook still said OPTIMIZE and Re-plan clear a stuck plan* (`docs/admin.md`, *When a run is stuck "Optimizing"*; the one place the first review's wording fix missed). It now says both buttons stay greyed out while the plan shows *Optimizing…*: the janitor puts it back within a minute, or a supervisor presses **Reset stuck plan** now; an OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN request that reaches the server (for example from an API call) still resets such a plan first. Step 2 now says **Reset stuck plan** also covers a plan whose job has already ended, not only a job lost by a restart. Test: `stuck-plan.spec.ts` +1 (the runbook's stuck section: no "do the same first", the greyed-out buttons, the janitor or Reset stuck plan).
- **Fixed.** *`/api/health` answered 503 `not_ready` for a 403 from `/ready`* (owner decision 5 lists only a missing token, the solver's 401 and "not configured" as definite misconfiguration). The solver never answers 403 (`_check_token` raises only 401 or 500 "Solver not configured"), so a 403 comes from something in front of it - a proxy, a firewall, a wrong host - and the token was never checked. It is now `degraded` (`SOLVER_ERROR`, HTTP 200, `ok: false`: alert, do not block the deploy); only the solver's 401 is `SOLVER_TOKEN_REJECTED` ([5.8](#58-health-endpoint), [5.10](#510-operating-runbook-pointers)). The `SOLVER_ERROR` message now says "the route optimizer, or a proxy in front of it". Tests: `health.spec.ts` +2 (a 403 with a JSON or an HTML body is `degraded`, the 401 stays `misconfigured`; `GET /api/health` answers 200 `degraded` for a 403). The reviewers' `sk2-403` probe (a 403 "proxy", plain text and JSON) re-run against the branch: `degraded` 200 (503 before).
- **Deploy notes.** No migration, no solver change, no new or changed route. Behaviour changes: no false stuck line or **Reset stuck plan** button when an optimization finishes while the plan screen reloads; a 403 from something in front of the solver no longer blocks a web deploy (it shows `degraded`, `ok: false`: monitoring alerts).

**Merge of main into audit PR4** (same branch; main `22fe298` with audit A2 #42 and A3 #43; a merge commit, no rebase). Conflicts only in the imports of `lib/dispatch/plan-detail.ts` (A2's `isDispatchPlanShape` kept next to PR4's `stuck-plan` and `isOptimizing`), the header comment of `api-role-matrix.spec.ts` (A2's and A3's notes, then PR4's) and this handbook (the 5.3 counts line; the integration table, A3's `master-data-db.spec.ts` row before PR4's; this 7.4, A2 and A3 before PR4). Merged without a conflict and checked: `getPlanDetail` sets A2's `isDispatchPlan` inside PR4's REPEATABLE READ read; the plan screen keeps A2's Excel export for a plan without loads and PR4's **Reset stuck plan**. PR4 has no migration; main's (`20260930093000_master_data_no_orphans`) changes nothing it relies on, and `recovery-races-db.spec.ts` passes on a database with it. The branch's own change set is unchanged by the merge (compared line by line). Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).

**Audit PR4 third review fixes** (same branch, `audit-4-recovery`, after the merge of main; third review of the branch: five findings, each confirmed by two reviewers, one medium and four low; each code fix has a test that fails without it, checked by reverting each part on its own). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged), no solver change. The last fix commit before it (`093c0e5`: the plan read asks the in-flight map before its snapshot; the admin runbook wording; a 403 on `/ready` is only `degraded`) was part of the review.

- **Fixed.** *`/api/health` said `ready` while every optimization failed, for a `SOLVER_URL` that answers with a redirect* (medium; F15 not fully closed). The readiness check used `fetch`, which follows a redirect and sends the token on to the address it names. The optimize call (`node:http`) never follows one. Behind an http-to-https edge (Caddy answers 308; a Railway public domain given as `http://`), `/api/health` answered `ready` (`ok: true`, `solver: up`) while every OPTIMIZE and RE-PLAN failed with "Solver returned HTTP 301". Now the check asks with `redirect: 'manual'`, and any 3xx is `degraded` `SOLVER_URL_REDIRECTS`: HTTP 200, `ok: false`, so monitoring alerts; the message says to set `SOLVER_URL` to the solver's own address. It is `degraded`, not 503, like a 403 from something in front of the solver: owner decision 5 lists only a missing token, the solver's 401 and "not configured" as definite faults (owner question in [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)). The road-line call does not follow a redirect either, so no call sends the token to the address a redirect names. The optimize call's error now names `SOLVER_URL` instead of "HTTP 301". Production is not exposed today: `SOLVER_URL` is the solver's private address, and the solver has no public domain ([2.6](#26-runtime-architecture), [5.8](#58-health-endpoint)). Tests: `health.spec.ts` +3 (301, 302, 303, 307 and 308 from a faked fetch, and a browser-style `opaqueredirect`; `GET /api/health` answers 200 `degraded`; an in-process edge that redirects to the in-process solver: the check is `degraded`, the optimize and road-line calls fail with that status, and the solver behind the redirect gets no call). Checked against the real solver on a scratch port: direct `ready`; through a 301 or 308 edge `degraded` `SOLVER_URL_REDIRECTS` (`ready` before).
- **Fixed.** *A `SOLVER_TOKEN` with a character outside plain ASCII read as "solver unreachable" (200, deploy allowed), or even `ready`, while every optimization failed* (low). Trimming does not remove a zero-width space, curly quotes, a non-breaking space or a line break inside the token. `fetch` refuses such a token before sending, so the check said `SOLVER_UNREACHABLE`, a network problem, and the deploy went ahead; `node:http` refused it too ("Invalid character in header content"). A non-breaking space is worse: `fetch` sends it as one byte and `node:http` as two (UTF-8), so with the same value on the solver the check was `ready` while every optimization got 401. Now `tokenCanBeSent()` in `lib/solver-env.ts` accepts only plain ASCII (letters, digits, punctuation, a space or a tab inside it). Any other token is `misconfigured` `SOLVER_TOKEN_INVALID`: HTTP 503, so the deploy fails and the previous version serves, as for a missing token (decision 5). A production boot logs a `[config]` error, and the optimize and road-line calls refuse the token before sending anything, with a message that says to paste it again as plain text ([2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service)). Tests: `health.spec.ts` +3 (five such tokens: `misconfigured`, the solver not called, the startup log; `GET /api/health` answers 503 and never shows the token; the real check and the real calls against the in-process solver holding the same value: nothing is sent, and the optimize error names `SOLVER_TOKEN`). Against the real solver: the working token with a zero-width space after it is 503 `SOLVER_TOKEN_INVALID`.
- **Fixed.** *The readiness timeout stopped at the response headers* (low). The 4 s timer was cleared as soon as the headers arrived, and the body was then read with no deadline (up to undici's 300 s, the same as Railway's health-check timeout). A proxy or worker that sent the headers and part of the body, then stalled, kept `/api/health` waiting instead of answering `degraded`. Now one deadline covers the headers and the body; a body that is not complete in time is `degraded` `SOLVER_UNREACHABLE` ([2.8](#28-timeouts-budgets-and-limits)). Tests: `health.spec.ts` +2 (a faked body that stops half-way; a real server that sends 8 of 60 bytes and stalls: `degraded` within the timeout, still pending after 3 s before the fix).
- **Fixed (docs).** *Gotcha 4 in [5.11](#511-known-operational-gotchas) still said `/api/health` answers 503 when the solver is down* (low; the row came from before audit PR4). It now says 200 `degraded` with `ok: false`: the web deploy goes ahead, and only a definite misconfiguration is 503. What to do: alert on `ok: false`, read `dispatch.reason`, fix the solver.
- **Fixed (docs).** *[2.2](#22-the-web-app) still gave 63 unit and 25 integration spec files after the merge of main* (low). Now 74 and 27. The list of integration specs that run on the real database without the web server now names all eight; it missed `dashboard-db` and `service-time-migration` before the merge, and `dispatch-screen-db` and `master-data-db` after it.
- **Guards.** `repo-guards.spec.ts` +3: the two counts in 2.2 against the files on disk, 2.2's list against every integration spec whose header says the web server and the solver are not used, and gotcha 4. Each fails on the handbook before this fix.
- **Checked on the branch.** Web unit, type check and lint pass. The full integration suite passes: 235 tests in 27 files, with `recovery-races-db.spec.ts` (22) in it for the first time, running in the same process as all the others, against a throwaway database and the web and solver started on spare local ports. Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Deploy notes.** No migration, no solver change, no new or changed route. Behaviour changes: a web whose `SOLVER_TOKEN` is not plain ASCII now fails its deploy health check (503 `SOLVER_TOKEN_INVALID`, the previous version keeps serving) instead of passing as `degraded`; a `SOLVER_URL` that redirects shows `degraded` `SOLVER_URL_REDIRECTS` instead of `ready`, and the road-line call no longer follows a redirect; `/api/health` answers within about 4 s even when the solver's answer stalls half-way. Before deploying, check that the web's `SOLVER_TOKEN` is plain ASCII (a generated base64 or hex token is).

**Audit PR4 fourth review fixes** (same branch, `audit-4-recovery`; fourth review of the branch at `53c9398`: three findings, each confirmed by two reviewers, one medium and two low; two of them are the same fault, so two distinct problems; each code fix has a test that fails without it, checked by reverting each part on its own). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged), no solver change.

- **Fixed.** *An unusable `SOLVER_URL` read as "solver unreachable" (200 `degraded`, deploy allowed) while every optimization failed* (medium; two findings; the same kind of fault as the token fixed in the third review, for the URL). The readiness check only asked whether `SOLVER_URL` was set. A value without `http://` - Railway's private domain pasted alone (`routeiq-solver.railway.internal:8000`, which the URL parser reads as the scheme `routeiq-solver.railway.internal:`), `127.0.0.1:8000` or `localhost:8000` -, `http//host:8000`, or a zero-width space after it made `fetch` throw, and every throw was `SOLVER_UNREACHABLE`: HTTP 200, a message that sent the admin to the solver service, and a web redeploy with the broken value passed Railway's health check and replaced the working version (on main the old check answered 503 for it). Every OPTIMIZE and RE-PLAN then failed before anything was sent ("Protocol not supported", "Invalid URL"), the road lines failed, and the startup log said nothing. A URL with a user name and password was the opposite case: `fetch` refuses it (health "unreachable", road lines failed) while the optimize call sent it. Now `solverUrlUsable()` in `lib/solver-env.ts`, next to `tokenCanBeSent()`: the value must be plain ASCII without spaces, read by the URL parser as `http:` or `https:`, without a user name or password, and without `?` or `#` (the paths the calls add would go into the query or the fragment). Any other value is `misconfigured` `SOLVER_URL_INVALID`: HTTP 503, so the deploy fails and the previous version serves, as for a missing `SOLVER_URL` (owner decision 5: a definite misconfiguration on the web; the same rule as `SOLVER_TOKEN_INVALID`). The message says to set it to `http://<solver private address>:<port>` as plain text and never shows the value. A production boot logs a `[config]` error; the optimize call refuses before sending, with a message that names `SOLVER_URL`; the road-line call answers `failed` without a call. Forms the URL parser reads as the same address (`HTTP://host`, `http:host:port`, a path, IPv6) still work ([2.9](#29-environment-variables-by-service), [5.8](#58-health-endpoint), [5.10](#510-operating-runbook-pointers), [5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy); `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT.md`, `docs/admin.md`, `.env.example`). Tests: `health.spec.ts` +3 (eleven such values against a faked fetch: `misconfigured`, 503, the solver not called, the startup log; six usable forms stay `ready`; `GET /api/health` answers 503 and never shows the value; the real check and the real calls against the in-process solver for six values: nothing is sent and the optimize error names `SOLVER_URL`, while `HTTP://` and `http:host:port` reach all three paths). Reverting the check in `health.ts`, the startup log, the optimize call, the road-line call, or any one of the four rules fails at least one of them.
- **Fixed (docs).** *The handbook still gave old counts, and said every spec is listed in [5.3](#53-test-suites) when 17 were not* (low; already wrong on main, not caused by the merge). [2.2](#22-the-web-app) said 60 route handlers (there are 64 route files), 5.3's unit heading 32 files (74), sections 1 and 2.2 said 10 and 9 migrations (19) and 26 models (27: `IntakeLineKey` was missing from the list). 5.3's tables did not name 10 unit specs (`dispatch-weights`, and nine from stabilization PR3: `plan-lifecycle`, `plan-lifecycle-start`, `plan-status`, `solve-admission`, `solve-admission-routes`, `dashboard-live-plans`, `request-gate`, `plan-actions`, `dispatch-screen-guards`) or 7 integration specs (`customers-import`, `intake-confirm`, `dispatch-heavy`, `plan-lifecycle`, `plan-lifecycle-db`, `dashboard-db`, `service-time-migration`); five of them were named in 5.3 only in the counts line, or as the other table's spec of the same file name. The counts are corrected, each missing spec has a row, and the integration table's lead-in says which specs need the web server.
- **Guards.** `repo-guards.spec.ts` +2: every route-file, `withTenantApi`, migration, model and enum count in sections 1 and 2 against the files on disk and the schema; 5.3's unit count, and every spec file named in its own part of 5.3 (unit or integration). Each fails on the handbook before this fix, and again with each stale phrase put back on its own.
- **Checked on the branch.** Web unit (1345), type check and lint pass. The full integration suite passes again: 235 tests in 27 files, `recovery-races-db.spec.ts` (22) among them, against a throwaway database with the built web (`next start`) and the solver on spare local ports. That web answers `/api/health` 200 `ready` with `SOLVER_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, and 503 `SOLVER_URL_INVALID` (never the value) with the solver's private domain without `http://` or with a zero-width space after the URL; its boot log has the `[config]` error. Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Deploy notes.** No migration, no solver change, no new or changed route. Behaviour change: a web whose `SOLVER_URL` no call can use now fails its deploy health check (503 `SOLVER_URL_INVALID`, the previous version keeps serving) instead of passing as `degraded` "unreachable". Before deploying, check that the web's `SOLVER_URL` starts with `http://` (production: the solver's private address and port) and has nothing pasted after it.

**Audit A1 "Quick hardening"** (branch `audit-1-hardening`, on main `bc10d7a`: PR1-PR9; assessment PR 1 of the external audit of 27 Sep 2026, verified in `.dev/audit-2026-09-27/VERIFIED-ASSESSMENT.md`, with the owner-approved defaults of decisions 4 and 16). No migration, no new or changed API route, no solver change.

- **F01, first part: fixed** (the rest of F01 is the Next.js 16 upgrade, assessment PR 9). Next.js 14 gets no more security fixes, and several advisories without a 14.x fix sit in the image endpoint, which answers without login.
  - `next.config.js` sets `images: { unoptimized: true }`: the app uses no `next/image`, and with it Next.js 14 answers `/_next/image` with 404 before reading any parameter (`next-server.js`, `handleNextImageRequest`).
  - The unused `experimental.serverActions.bodySizeLimit` is gone.
  - No Server Actions until the upgrade (owner decision 4): `build-hardening.spec.ts` fails on any `'use server'` in apps/web or packages/ (tests and scripts excepted), and the new CI step after `next build` (`scripts/check-build-output.ts`) fails on the directive, on any action in `.next/server/server-reference-manifest.json` and on any `.map` under `.next/static`. A second CI step asks the running app: `/_next/image?...&w=1` must answer 404 (400 while the endpoint was on), a browser chunk 200 and its `.map` 404.
- **Side item, public source maps: fixed.** Sentry 8 turns on source maps for every production build (`hidden-source-map`: no comment in the JS, but the files stay in `.next/static`, which Next.js serves to anyone). The `disableServerWebpackPlugin` / `disableClientWebpackPlugin` options in `next.config.js` were Sentry 7 options that Sentry 8 ignores, so "no upload without a DSN" never held. Now `sourcemaps.disable` is set unless `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`, `SENTRY_ORG` and `SENTRY_PROJECT` are all set, and `sourcemaps.deleteSourcemapsAfterUpload` is always on (Sentry's deletion step runs even when the upload fails). Sentry deletes only `*.js.map`: a local build with an upload configured (pointed at an unreachable local address, so it failed) left two `.css.map` files, so `pnpm build` now ends with `scripts/remove-public-source-maps.mjs`, which removes every `.map` under `.next/static` (Railway's build command runs this script). Checked on local production builds: main's config left **65** `.map` files in `.next/static` (the build check fails, listing them); this branch leaves none, without an upload and with one (both pass).
- **Node 22 LTS pinned.** Root `engines.node` `22.x` (was `>=20.0.0`), `.nvmrc` and `.node-version` `22`, CI `setup-node` from `.nvmrc` (was 20). Node 20 reached end of life on 30 Apr 2026. How Railway's Nixpacks picks the version, and what to check after the deploy: [5.6](#56-deployment-on-railway), [5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy). A newer Node installs locally with pnpm's "Unsupported engine" warning.
- **E2, quick fix: partly fixed by this first version** (the review below found crafted files of a few KB that still blocked the app for minutes or crashed it, and a worst case far above the "about 2 s" this block gave; fixed there). The real isolation is assessment PR 5, a worker thread with a memory cap and a timeout that really stops it. Owner decision 16: 10 MB and 50,000 rows kept; 50 MB unpacked and 10 sheets added.
  - The 10 s "parse timeout" is removed: the parse is synchronous, so its timer could only run after the parse had finished. Comments and docs say so.
  - `lib/workbook-guard.ts` (`checkWorkbookZip`) walks the .xlsx zip the way SheetJS does and unpacks each part with zlib under a hard cap, so the size is measured, never taken from the headers (SheetJS allocates by the header size and unpacks to the end of the stream whatever it says). Refused before SheetJS sees the file: over 50 MB unpacked, over 1,000 parts, a part whose header size is not its real size, a password-protected part, an archive that is not an Excel workbook (OpenDocument, Numbers, an archive inside; SheetJS would unpack those further).
  - The sheet count comes from a first SheetJS pass that reads only the workbook's sheet list (`bookSheets`); more than 10 sheets are refused before any sheet is read.
  - Each sheet is read to at most `READ_ROWS` = 50,100 rows (`sheetRows`; CSV: Papa `preview`). A sheet cut there is never shortened without a word: the sheet that is read is refused, another is named "N rows or more" in the warning.
  - SheetJS now gets a Buffer view of the upload: given a Uint8Array it copied the rest of the file for every part it unpacked (5,000 small parts: 8.4 s in the sheet-list pass alone; with a Buffer 65 ms).
  - Measured on the maintainer's machine with `parseUpload` (event loop blocked / memory): a 606 KB workbook unpacking to 200 MB, more than 3 min before, **31 ms** now (refused); the same with headers claiming 4 KB, 0.7 s reading 0 rows before, 28 ms refused now; a 138 KB workbook unpacking to 45 MB (1.15 M rows; not the worst the caps allow, see the review block below), 9.0 s and 877 MB before, **1.9 s and 382 MB** now; a 120,000-row sheet 0.65 s → 0.60 s; a 9.8 MB CSV of 1.29 M tiny rows 2.4 s → 0.56 s. Local order files (the real-data file of 26 Sep and the file of 25 Sep in `.dev/`, the `orders-150.xlsx` fixture, the S04 / S05 scenario files): 0.08-0.72 MB unpacked, 10-16 parts, 1 sheet, 11-154 ms, far under the caps.
  - Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see: the new refusal messages (above); a sheet with 50,000 rows or fewer that goes on past row 50,100 (empty rows, or an Excel used range that runs on) is refused with "If the rows below your data are empty, delete them"; empty lines in a CSV count towards the 50,100 lines read; RouteIQ's own plan workbook (20-22 sheets) cannot be uploaded back (no upload expects it).
- **Tests** (each checked to fail without its fix, by reverting that part): `upload-limits.spec.ts` (19): without the zip check 6 fail, without `sheetRows` 5, without the sheet count 1, with a Uint8Array 1, without `preview` 1, ignoring the cut 3, with a timer armed again 1. `build-hardening.spec.ts` (16): main's `next.config.js` fails 5, a `'use server'` in `lib/` fails the repo guard, main's `package.json`, `ci.yml` and no `.nvmrc` fail the Node and CI checks, a build script without the map removal fails 1. The CI script was run on real local builds: main's config fails (65 maps), a probe page with an inline Server Action fails twice (the directive and the manifest id), this branch passes, also through `pnpm build` with a failing Sentry upload (the removal step took the 2 CSS maps Sentry left). Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Owner actions.** Build on staging first if one exists, then check the Node version in the web build log ([5.6](#56-deployment-on-railway)); after the deploy, the two 404 checks of [5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy) step 12 (the source-map check is also the audit's read-only production check); check that NMWC's largest real order and customer files pass (only local copies were measured). The app binds to localhost on the Windows PC (decision 4, applied in A1 v2: the dev script is `next dev -H 127.0.0.1`; use `next start -H 127.0.0.1` for a local production run).
- **Still open.** The parse still runs on the event loop (assessment PR 5); what a file near the caps still costs is in the review block below (5-11 s; this block said "about 2 s", measured on one file). Everything else in F01 waits for the Next.js 16 upgrade (assessment PR 9). (P5 later fixed this: every upload is read in a separate process with a time limit and a heap cap; see its block, the last one.)

**A1 review fixes** (same branch, `audit-1-hardening`; review of the A1 diff: five findings, each confirmed by two reviewers with their own files, in `.dev/scratch-a1-v1/`). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged: the three upload routes answer 400 with the message, as before), no solver change. All in `lib/workbook-guard.ts` (`guardSpreadsheet`, `checkWorkbookZip`) and `lib/csv.ts` (`parseExcelSheets`, `sheetRanges`, `pickSheet`).

- *E2 was not closed: a 1.6-4 KB workbook still blocked the app for minutes or crashed it, because the sheet's range width was never capped* (high). `sheet_to_json` visits every cell of a sheet's range and, with `defval`, gives every row a key per column; the width comes from the size record or the right-most cell, and `sheetRows` cuts only rows. The reviewers' 1,632-byte file with `<dimension ref="A1:XFD2000"/>` and 2 rows took 28 s and 641 MB (A1:XFD50000: still running at 120 s); one header cell in column XFD with 200 rows 6.1 s, with 2,000 rows a V8 out-of-memory crash (which drops every request in flight and can leave a day on "optimizing", F09). A 43 KB workbook with a 16,384-column header row took 33 s; ten sheets of 50,000 rows 6 s. **Fixed:** before any sheet is turned into rows, each sheet's range (decoded as `sheet_to_json` decodes it) is checked (`sheetRanges`): a range wider than 200 columns, or ranges adding up to more than 2,500,000 cells over all sheets, is first cut to the last row and column that hold a cell (only empty trailing columns, the `__empty` keys, and blank rows go; within the caps a range is walked as before); what is still over is refused, naming the sheet ("Sheet "Orders" has 16,384 columns; at most 200 can be read. Delete the columns you do not need, or save only the sheet you need as a new workbook or as CSV, and upload that." / "This workbook is too large to read: its sheets span N cells (rows x columns); at most 2,500,000 can be read. ..."). The size record is still read, so a sheet that goes on past row 50,100 is still refused, never cut. Now: A1:XFD2000 and A1:XFD50000 read as their 2 rows in 20-27 ms; the XFD-cell files refused in 46 / 210 ms; the 16,384-column header refused in 0.15 s; 200 columns x 50,000 rows (8.8 s) refused in 0.3 s.
- *The "archive inside" refusal and the 50 MB cap could be bypassed: the check read part names from the zip's central directory, SheetJS reads them from each part's local header* (medium). A part named "[Content_Types].xml" in the directory and "Index.zip" in its header passed the check (1 part, 1.2 MB), and SheetJS opened it as a workbook of its own: the reviewers' 1.2 MB file unpacked to about 400 MB inside SheetJS (14.6 s, 1.7 GB; the inner workbook alone is refused in 31 ms). **Fixed:** `checkWorkbookZip` reads each part's name from its local header and refuses a part whose two names differ ("damaged"); the checks use that name as SheetJS finds parts: by path with "/" or "\" (a "META-INF\manifest.xml" made SheetJS read the file as OpenDocument) and by file name with a trailing "/" dropped ("Index.zip/"); a name with a control character is refused as damaged. The reviewers' file is refused in 3 ms.
- *A 1.7 KB workbook with one hyperlink over a large range blocked the app for minutes* (medium). SheetJS makes a cell object for every address of a link's range (`parse_ws_xml_hlinks`), whatever `sheetRows` says. A1:T200000: 5.6 s and 977 MB; A1:XFD1048576 (17 billion cells): still running at 180 s. The same holds in the other formats: an .xls link record is looped over cell by cell (a 4 KB .xls with one link over 65,536 x 65,536 cells: about half an hour; 2,000 x 2,000 took 2 s), and so is a link in a binary (.xlsb) sheet, which SheetJS reads from any worksheet part whose name ends in ".bin", also inside an .xlsx. **Fixed:** before SheetJS reads the file, the link ranges are added up and more than 200,000 cells in all are refused ("This workbook has links over N cells; at most 200,000 can be read. Remove the links (in Excel: select the cells, right-click, Remove Hyperlinks), save it and upload it again."): in every part of an .xlsx, read the way SheetJS reads them (its tag and attribute patterns, UTF-8 or a UTF-16 part, namespace prefixes, any case, "_" suffixes; a test compares the count with the cells SheetJS makes for each variant), and in the link and tooltip records of an .xls workbook stream. An address too long for a number (hundreds of letters) decodes to Infinity, and SheetJS's loop over it never ends: it counts as endless. Binary parts are hidden from SheetJS (a new central directory without them is appended to the bytes it reads; it needs none of them for an .xlsx: printer settings, macros, embedded objects), and a renamed .xlsb is refused ("This is an Excel binary workbook (.xlsb), which cannot be read. ..."). Both link files are refused in 4-5 ms, the .xls in 20 ms.
- *The E2 docs promised a worst case of about 2 s* (medium, docs). That figure came from one 45 MB single-sheet file; files that passed every cap blocked the app for 6-33 s or more. `docs/SECURITY.md` section 10, `DISPATCHER_GUIDE.md`, `admin.md`, this handbook ([2.2](#22-the-web-app), [2.8](#28-timeouts-budgets-and-limits), [3.3](#33-step-1-upload-and-validate-the-order-file), [7.2](#72-known-limitations), the A1 block above) and the `csv.ts` / `workbook-guard.ts` comments now give the measured cost of the largest files the caps allow (below) and say that only PR 5 bounds the time. `repo-guards.spec.ts` refuses the old wordings in `docs/` and `lib/`.
- *A blank but formatted first sheet was picked and refused where main read the data sheet* (low). A sheet cut at row 50,100 counted as holding data whatever its first rows were, so a first sheet with no value and a used range to row 60,000 (formatting) was chosen over the customer list on sheet 2 and refused with "Too many rows ... delete the empty rows"; an order upload named it as "(0 rows or more)". **Fixed:** a sheet whose first 50,100 rows hold no value is empty, cut or not: it is not read, not a candidate and not named (`parseExcelSheets` keeps only sheets with rows, `pickSheet` counts only rows with a value). The chosen sheet that goes on past row 50,100 is still refused.
- **Found while fixing (same cause: SheetJS does far more work than the file's size; not in the review):**
  - *SheetJS's other readers.* It picks its reader from the first bytes, whatever the file's name or type: a 763-byte flat OpenDocument file named orders.xls (one cell repeated 1,000 x 2,000 times) took 7.7 s, and larger ones run out of memory; its web-page reader compares every cell with every merged cell (0.7 MB: 1.9 s, growing with the square). **Fixed:** `guardSpreadsheet` mirrors SheetJS's choice of reader (`sheetjsReader`, the same tests in the same order as `readSync`) and lets it read only a zip that is an Excel workbook, an .xls (a compound file with a Workbook or Book stream, or a bare BIFF stream) and text it reads as CSV; text that starts with "<" (after spaces, line breaks or a byte-order mark, also UTF-16) is refused as "a web page or an XML file", SYLK, DIF, Lotus, dBASE, RTF and SocialCalc files as "not an Excel workbook". A CSV sent as Excel (Windows browsers do) is still read by SheetJS, as before (the A1 review's SYLK test wrongly refused a CSV whose first header is exactly "ID"; the A1 v2 review below fixed that, and the crafted-.xls hole too).
  - *Millions of cells.* SheetJS's CSV reader tries every value as a number and as a date: a 9.5 MB CSV sent as Excel with 5 million cells took 20 s; a dense 39 MB .xlsx (2.55 million cells) took 11 s before the range check could refuse it. **Fixed:** both are counted before SheetJS reads them (the CSV's separators and line breaks, the .xlsx's cell tags that do not close themselves, so formatted empty cells never count) and more than 2,500,000 are refused ("This file is too large to read: it has about N cells ...") in 60-350 ms.
  - *Array formulas.* With formulas on, SheetJS compares every array-formula cell with every array formula before it (20,000 rows, a 275 KB file: 2.6 s, growing with the square). **Fixed:** `cellFormula: false`; formulas are not used, their saved values are read as before (20,000 rows: 0.33 s). A formula cell without a saved value (some generators write none) now reads as blank; it read as 0.
  - *Comments.* SheetJS checks every comment already on a cell before adding one: 20,000 on one cell took 0.4 s, and 50 MB of them would take minutes. **Fixed:** more than 10,000 comments are refused before SheetJS reads the workbook.
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** the new refusals above, each saying what to do (NMWC's real files are far from all of them); a formula without a saved value reads as blank instead of 0; a blank formatted sheet is ignored instead of refused; a sheet over the column or cell cap loses only its empty trailing "__empty" columns (seen only in "Found columns: ..."). The local real files (the real-data order file of 26 Sep, the file of 25 Sep, their CSV export as `text/csv` and as Excel, the customer CSV, the late-order CSV) give the same rows, sheet and warnings as main; RouteIQ's own 26-sheet plan workbook is refused for its sheets, as in A1.
- **What a file near the caps still costs** (the maintainer's machine; times vary by about a third from run to run): an .xlsx of 50,000 rows x 49 columns (0.19 MB, 37 MB unpacked, just under 2,500,000 cells) 9-10 s and about 1 GB of memory; the same rows as CSV sent as Excel 7-11 s and 1.2 GB; ten sheets of 50,000 rows x 3 columns 5-6 s; a 45 MB sheet of 1.15 M rows (refused for its rows) 1.8 s; NMWC's shape at the row limit (50,000 rows x 15 columns) 2.1-2.5 s. Every upload is still parsed on the event loop; only the worker of assessment PR 5 bounds the time. SheetJS has many readers: these checks cover the ways found so far in which a small file makes it do large work, not every way that may exist.
- **Tests** (`upload-limits.spec.ts`, + 27, now 46), each checked to fail with its fix reverted, by a script that reverts one mechanism at a time and runs only the tests that cover it: the range caps 4, the local name 1, the foreign names 1, the link count in .xlsx parts 3, in .xls 1, the binary parts 2, the formats 2, the CSV cell count 1, the .xlsx cell count 2, the comment count 1, `cellFormula` 1, the blank sheet 2. The whole-sheet link, the 65,536 x 65,536 .xls and the binary sheet with a whole-sheet link were not run reverted (they run for minutes or out of memory); their smaller siblings were. The endless range was run reverted as a script: still running after 20 s. The one test that passes reverted is meant to: a range within the caps is walked as before. Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Owner decisions.** New caps and rules beyond decision 16, applied as defaults because the high finding needed them: 200 columns per sheet, 2,500,000 cells, links over 200,000 cells, 10,000 comments, and an Excel upload must be an .xlsx, an .xls or CSV. 2,500,000 cells keeps 50,000-row files up to 50 columns; 1,000,000 would cut the worst case to about 4 s and 0.5 GB but refuse 50,000-row files over 20 columns. Questions in [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner).
- **Still open.** Every upload is parsed on the event loop: a file near the caps blocks every user for 5-11 s and needs about 1 GB (assessment PR 5). Checking NMWC's largest real files is still the owner action of the A1 block. (P5 later fixed this: every upload is read in a separate process with a time limit and a heap cap; see its block, the last one.)

**A1 v2 review fixes** (same branch, `audit-1-hardening`; a second review of the A1 diff against the verified assessment, four findings each confirmed by two reviewers with their own files in `.dev/scratch-a1-v2/`). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged; the three upload routes still answer 400 with the message). No solver change. In `lib/workbook-guard.ts` (`sheetjsReader`, `guardSpreadsheet`, new `checkCompoundFile` and `looksLikeSylk`) and `apps/web/package.json`.

- *E2 was still open for an old .xls: XLSX.CFB.read itself could be made to use memory and time that grow with the square of the file's size* (high). The `cfb` branch of `guardSpreadsheet` called `XLSX.CFB.read` before any check. SheetJS's `make_sector_list` starts at `dir_start` and, wrapping, walks and copies a FAT chain from every sector it has not yet seen as a chain start; a later walk can still walk *through* a sector an earlier one reached. In a file whose chains are stored in descending sector order (sector s points to s-1) every sector starts a walk down through all the sectors below it, so the total is about D^2/2 for D sectors, though each sector still has one predecessor and it reads as one ordinary chain. Measured on this branch in guard mode (`.dev/scratch-a1-v2/cfb/run.ts`): 0.5 MB 0.14 s / 358 MB, 1 MB 0.57 s / 1.1 GB, 2 MB 2.6 s / 4.1 GB; projected about 48 GB at 7 MB and about 100 GB at the 10 MB limit, so the container is killed and every request in flight is dropped. (On main the same 1 MB file crashes V8, so this is not a regression, but SECURITY.md section 10 (then numbered 9), this handbook and the code claimed an upload's work is bounded, which for .xls it was not.) **Fixed:** `checkCompoundFile` reads the header, the 109 header FAT addresses and the DIFAT chain (bounded, cycle-detected), then replays `make_sector_list`'s walk order counting sectors only (no copies) and refuses the file as damaged once the total passes a few times the sector count - a well-formed file's chains are disjoint, so its total is the sector count. It also refuses a directory of more than 16,384 entries (SheetJS's `build_full_paths` is quadratic in that). The crafted 2,000-sector file (about 1 MB) is refused in a few ms; without the check it takes about 0.6 s and 1.1 GB. A real .xls (ascending chains, as Excel and SheetJS write) is unchanged.
- *Regression: a CSV whose first column header is "ID", sent as Excel, was refused as "not an Excel workbook"* (medium; two skeptics wrote it up separately). `sheetjsReader` returned `other` (refused as SYLK) for any file beginning with the bytes "ID". SheetJS does not: it routes such a file to `read_wb_ID`, which tries its SYLK reader and, when that throws (with WTF off, as `lib/csv.ts` reads), falls back to its CSV reader. A CSV whose first header is "ID" (`ID,Customer Code,...`, or `ID;...` with data rows, or a tab file, or `IDNo,...`) throws in the SYLK reader on the first record and is read as CSV. Chrome and Edge on a Windows PC with Excel send every .csv as application/vnd.ms-excel, so a dispatcher's order, customer or baseline CSV with a leading "ID" column arrived on the Excel path and was wrongly refused (main reads it; the advice to "save it as CSV" brought back the same refusal). Reproduced in `.dev/scratch-a1-v2/idcsv/` and `regress/`; a 91 KB branch-errors CSV in Downloads starting "ID,S" was one real case. NMWC's current files (headers "SO Number..." and "code,...") were not affected. **Fixed:** `looksLikeSylk` treats a file starting with "ID" as SYLK (refused) only when its first ";"-separated field is exactly "ID" *and* the next non-empty record's type is a SYLK record type (ID, E, B, O, W, P, NN, C, F); otherwise it is delimited text, bounded by `checkDelimitedText`, and SheetJS reads it as CSV. A genuine SYLK file (`ID;PWXL` then `P;`/`C;` records) stays refused.
- *Owner decision 4 (bind to localhost on the Windows PC) was only documented; `next dev` still listened on 0.0.0.0* (low). The Next.js 14 CLI defaults `-H` to 0.0.0.0, and the dev script was `next dev`, so the Windows-only critical advisory could be reached from the LAN whenever the app ran locally. **Fixed:** the dev script is `next dev -H 127.0.0.1`; `start` (Railway) keeps no `-H`. `http://localhost:3000` still works, so `TEST_BASE_URL` and the integration run are unaffected. `build-hardening.spec.ts` pins that the dev script binds 127.0.0.1 and that `start` has no `-H`. `docs/SECURITY.md` section 10 and `docs/LOCAL_DEV.md` now state the rule as applied, including `next start -H 127.0.0.1` for a local production run.
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** an "ID"-column CSV sent as Excel is read again (it was refused on this branch, never in production); the new crafted-.xls refusal (a real .xls is unaffected); no other change.
- **Tests**, each checked to fail with its fix reverted (by disabling one mechanism at a time and running only its tests): `upload-limits.spec.ts` + 5 (a descending-chain .xls refused as damaged in ms, a real .xls still read; an "ID"-column comma CSV read with key "id"; a tab file, an "IDNo" header and a semicolon "ID" CSV read; a real SYLK file still refused), `build-hardening.spec.ts` + 1 (the dev binding). Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Owner decisions.** Decision 4's localhost part is applied as its approved default. Refusing a crafted compound file and the "ID"-CSV rule are bug fixes to the A1 caps, not new limits (the .xls structural check has no dispatcher-visible cap; the largest real files still pass). The alternative of refusing every .xls (an owner decision NMWC has not made) was not taken.
- **Still open.** The event-loop cost is unchanged (assessment PR 5). Checking NMWC's largest real files remains the A1 owner action. (P5 later fixed this: every upload is read in a separate process with a time limit and a heap cap; see its block, the last one.)

**A1 v3 review fixes** (same branch, `audit-1-hardening`; a third review, of the A1 v2 fix `5ac4019` and, with fresh eyes, of the merged head `76ce2f5`: six findings, each confirmed by two reviewers with their own files). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged; the three upload routes still answer 400 with the message). No solver change. In `lib/workbook-guard.ts` (`sheetjsReader`, new `idFileReader`, `scanXmlPart`, `checkWorkbookZip`), `lib/csv.ts` (`parseExcelSheets`, new `MAX_METADATA`, `MAX_PEOPLE`) and the test fixtures.

- *A long number format on many cells ran the web process out of memory* (high). SheetJS made the display text of every number cell, parsing the cell's format again for each one (`safe_format`; `safe_format_xf` for .xls), though the app reads raw values (`sheet_to_json` with `raw: true`), and nothing capped a format's length. A 162 KB .xlsx within every cap (50,000 rows x 44 number cells in one 255-character format, Excel's maximum) ran for about 90 s and died with "JavaScript heap out of memory" at about 4 GB; 5,000 rows took 11-13 s and 2 GB; a 4 KB file with one very long format on 300 cells ran out of memory too. **Fixed:** `parseExcelSheets` reads with `cellText: false` (both passes), so SheetJS makes no display text. Values are unchanged (raw). Header names come from `format_cell`, which formats a header cell on its own: in General for an .xlsx, so a number in a header row with a custom format now reads as "2026" where it read "2,026.00" (an .xls header cell keeps its own format; NMWC's headers are text). Now 50,000 rows x 44 cells in the 255-character format take 10.9 s and 0.94 GB, the same as with no format (10.0 s, 0.90 GB); the test's 2,000 x 10 file 0.1 s instead of 0.9 s. The one place a cell's own format is still applied is an .xls header cell, at most 200 per sheet: ten sheets of 200 header cells in one 65,535-character format (the most an .xls format record holds) took 6.9 s in `sheet_to_json`, within what a file near the caps costs.
- *`xl/metadata.xml` made SheetJS do work that grows with the square of a small part* (high). For each future-metadata block SheetJS looks through every metadata type (`parse_xlmeta_xml`), and neither was counted. 100,000 of each (a 15 KB upload, 4.8 MB unpacked) took 28-30 s through `parseUpload`, 200,000 of each 138 s; about 1,000,000 of each fit under the 50 MB cap (about an hour). **Fixed:** `scanXmlPart` counts metadata types and future-metadata blocks, and more than 1,000 of either is refused before SheetJS reads the workbook ("This workbook has N metadata entries or more; at most 1,000 can be read. Save only the sheet you need as a new workbook or as CSV and upload that."). Excel writes one or two; the real fixture has one of each. A 10 KB file with 20,000 of each (about 4 s in SheetJS) is refused in milliseconds.
- *A threaded-comment person list multiplied the comments* (medium). For each threaded comment SheetJS looks up its author in the whole person list (`sheet_insert_comments`); comments were capped at 10,000, people not at all. 9,900 threaded comments by an author missing from 2.9 million people (a 142 KB upload) took 109 s; 1,000 against 1,000,000 took 3-11 s. **Fixed:** a person list of more than 1,000 is refused before SheetJS reads the workbook ("This workbook lists N comment authors or more; at most 1,000 can be read. ..."); with 10,000 comments the lookups stay under 10^7 (10,000 x 10,000 took 0.5 s).
- *Self-closing cells with a type got past the cell cap* (low). The count skipped every tag that closes itself, on the view that it holds no value. But SheetJS keeps a piece of a row as a cell when its tag has a type (`<c t="b"/>` is a FALSE cell) or a `<v>` follows the tag (`<c r="A1" s="1"/><v>1</v>`, `<c/><v>1</v>`), and it also cuts at `<c/` (`<c/t=b>`). A 133 KB .xlsx of 4.5 million `<c t="b"/>` (43 MB unpacked) took 13-22 s and 1.1 GB before the range check refused it; other variants took up to 36 s and 1.8 GB. **Fixed:** `scanXmlPart` finds, in one pass, where SheetJS cuts a sheet into pieces (each cell start and row end) and counts each piece with a type or a value mark as one cell; it may count a piece too many, never one too few. A formatted empty cell (`<c r="B2" s="1"/>`) is still not a cell, so formatting alone never refuses a workbook; an open tag with neither (`<c r="A9"></c>`, which SheetJS does not make) is no longer counted. A test compares the count with the cells SheetJS makes for twelve variants; a 26 MB sheet of 2.7 million `<c t="b"/>` is refused in about 0.6 s. The one-pass count takes about 0.3 s on a 45 MB sheet (the old one about 0.2 s). Pieces SheetJS drops still cost it time: 11 million `<c/>` (45 MB unpacked, a 160 KB file) took 6.1 s, within the 5-11 s a file near the caps costs.
- *A semicolon CSV with an "ID" header whose first ID is "C" or "F" was still refused* (low). `looksLikeSylk` took any second record that starts with a SYLK record type for SYLK. SheetJS reads a file as SYLK only when every record is valid SYLK, so `ID;Code;Qty` then `C;5;10` ("5" is not a C field), `F;maths;40`, or `E;5;10` then `1;x;2` are CSV to SheetJS, and were refused here.
