While confirming it, the reviewers found the reverse, which was a hole: the check read only the first 4,096 bytes and did not decode SheetJS's escapes, so "ID", 5,000 empty lines, then `C;Y5000000;X1;K1` (5 KB), or `ESC $3;Y3000000;...` (24 bytes; ESC "$3" decodes to "C") passed as CSV while SheetJS read them as SYLK and allocated millions of rows (0.3-0.8 s and 0.6-1.1 GB; 20,000,000 rows ran out of memory). SheetJS also does the work of every SYLK record before the one it gives up on, so `C;Y5000000;...` followed by a CSV line costs the same. **Fixed:** `idFileReader` replays SheetJS's SYLK reader over the whole file, record by record and field by field (the record types, and the field codes of C and F records, whose unknown values make it throw with WTF on), up to the first record or field it throws on. It counts the reader's costly steps: a row field grows the sheet to that row, a width field loops over its columns, a value after a format checks the whole format, and one step per record. The file is read as CSV when the reader gives up within 100,000 steps (a few ms and about 10 MB; a CSV takes a handful). It is refused as "not an Excel workbook" when it reads as SYLK, when the reader would do more, or when a record after the first holds an ESC character (no CSV has one). All the reviewers' files: the CSVs are read as SheetJS reads them, the others refused in milliseconds; a real SYLK file stays refused. Only the errors WTF raises are replayed: where SheetJS gives up earlier on another error (a value before any row, `ID;Qty` then `C;K5`), the file is refused here though SheetJS would read it as CSV.
- *Handbook 7.3 "Expected counts" still ended at A1's 1205* (low, docs). The merge added 1340 to [5.3](#53-test-suites) only; 7.3 was already behind the A1 v2 count (1211), and PR8, PR9, A2 and A3 never updated it. **Fixed:** 7.3 names 1211, the merge's 1340 and this block's 1350, and says that [5.3](#53-test-suites) holds the latest.
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** two new refusals (metadata entries, comment authors) and more cells counted in crafted files; a semicolon "ID" CSV whose first ID is a SYLK letter is read; a number in a header row with a custom number format reads in General. The local files (the order files of 25, 26, 28 and 30 Sep as .xlsx and CSV, sent as CSV and as Excel; the customer, product, truck and late-order CSVs; `orders-150.xlsx`; the S04 / S05 scenario workbooks; a plan workbook) give the same rows, sheet, warnings or refusal as `76ce2f5`.
- **Tests** (`upload-limits.spec.ts`, + 10, now 61), each checked to fail with its fix reverted, by a script that disables one mechanism at a time and runs only its tests: the SYLK replay (the A1 v2 check put back) 3, `cellText` 1, the metadata cap 2, the person cap 2, the cell count (the A1 v2 count put back) 3 (the two new tests and one changed expectation in an existing test). Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Owner decisions.** The new caps (1,000 metadata entries of each kind, 1,000 comment authors) and the 100,000-step SYLK budget are applied as defaults, like the A1 review's caps: the high findings need them, and no real file comes near them. Question in [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner).
- **Still open.** Every upload is parsed on the event loop: a file near the caps still blocks every user for 5-11 s and needs about 1 GB (assessment PR 5). SheetJS has many readers and parts; these checks cover the ways found so far in which a small file makes it do large work, not every way that may exist. Checking NMWC's largest real files remains the A1 owner action. (P5 later fixed this: every upload is read in a separate process with a time limit and a heap cap; see its block, the last one.)

**A1 v4 review fixes** (same branch, `audit-1-hardening`; a fourth review, of the A1 v3 head `35c5b3a` with fresh eyes and against main: three findings, each confirmed by two reviewers with their own files). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged; the three upload routes still answer 400 with the message). No solver change. In `lib/workbook-guard.ts` (`checkWorkbookZip`, new `sheetReads` with its copies of SheetJS's lookups, new `sameSheetList`; `guardSpreadsheet` also returns the sheet list), `lib/csv.ts` (`parseExcelSheets`), the test fixtures and the docs.

- *Several sheets could name one worksheet part, so SheetJS read it once per sheet and every per-part cap was multiplied by up to 10* (high). The caps counted each part of the zip once. SheetJS finds each sheet's part through the workbook's relationships: the sheet's `r:id`, then the target tried as "xl/" + target, as it is, and next to the relationships folder, through a lookup that ignores capitals and takes "\" for "/". Nothing stopped ten sheets from naming one part. A 121 KB upload of 50,000 rows x 49 columns in one part, inside every cap, took 9-12 s and 0.7-0.9 GB when one sheet named it; named by ten sheets it blocked the app for 94-108 s and reached 3.4 GB before the range check refused it, and with a 2 GB heap the process ran out of memory. Any planner could send it to the three upload routes. **Fixed:** `sheetReads` replays how SheetJS's `parse_zip` finds each sheet's part (`[Content_Types].xml`, the workbook part, its relationships, `safe_parse_wbrels`, the three tries and the same lookup), and two sheets that read one worksheet part are refused as damaged before SheetJS reads the file. `parseExcelSheets` also checks that the sheet list SheetJS reads first is the one the guard replayed (`sameSheetList`), so the replay can never count other parts than the ones SheetJS reads without the file being refused.
- **Found while fixing** (the same cause: a part SheetJS reads more than once, counted once). For each sheet SheetJS also reads its relationships, every part they name as comments or threaded comments, once per spelling of the target (`comments1.xml` and `Comments1.xml` are two reads of one part, and each adds the comments to the cells again), and the drawing of notes (VML) that its `<legacyDrawing>` names; it reads an external link each time `[Content_Types].xml` lists it. Measured before the fix: a 3 KB file whose sheet named one comments part of 5,000 comments 20 times took 8.8 s (SheetJS checks every comment already on a cell before adding one, so 256 spellings of 10,000 comments would take hours); a 51 MB drawing of 400,000 notes shared by ten sheets (a 1.1 MB upload) 58 s, against 4.3 s for one sheet; a 45 MB comments part shared by ten sheets 4.7 s and 1.4 GB, against 0.7 s; a 20 MB external link listed 200 times 1.9 s, growing with the listings. **Fixed:** the replay counts every read, and a part read again counts again against every cap: its size (more than 50 MB read in all is refused with "its sheets read the same parts again"), its cells, links, comments, metadata entries and comment authors. The check stops at the first cap passed, so the replay itself stays within the caps. Where SheetJS would stop with an error the replay goes on, so it may count reads that never happen (the file is then refused, where SheetJS would fail on it anyway), never fewer. A differential check on 15,000 random crafted workbooks (sheet ids shared, missing, or named `__proto__`; targets in every spelling; comments and drawings shared; links listed again; the workbook in another folder) compared, part by part, what SheetJS read with what the replay counted for the 12,500 the guard did not refuse, and found no part that SheetJS read more often; with the comment or the drawing count switched off, it found under-counts at once.
- *Chart sheets made cells that the guard never counted and that ignore the row limit* (medium). For a chart sheet SheetJS reads the chart of its drawing and makes one cell for every point cached in it, with no `sheetRows` limit. A 3.1 MB upload whose chart caches 1,300,000 points (the guard counted 1 cell) read as a sheet of 1.3 million rows in 5-5.5 s and 1 GB; one point at index 4,294,967,294, in a 1.7-2.8 KB upload, made SheetJS walk an array of 4.3 billion slots for about 3 minutes. **Fixed:** a workbook with a chart sheet, a dialog sheet or a macro sheet is refused before SheetJS reads it: "This workbook has a chart sheet (or a dialog or macro sheet), which cannot be read. Delete that sheet, or save only the sheet you need as a new workbook or as CSV, and upload that." A chart placed on a worksheet is not a chart sheet and reads as before.
- *After the merge, the audit status line at the top of this handbook still said only A1 was fixed* (low, docs). A2 and A3 are merged in main too. **Fixed:** the line names A2 and A3 with their findings, and `repo-guards.spec.ts` checks that it names every audit PR that has a block in 7.4.
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** a workbook with a chart sheet (a tab that holds only a chart) is refused with a message that says what to do; crafted files get the refusals above. The local files (the order files of 25, 26, 28 and 30 Sep as .xlsx and CSV, sent as CSV and as Excel; the customer, product, truck and late-order CSVs; the plan and scenario workbooks: 370 files, each read with and without the order-sheet test) give the same rows, sheet, warnings or refusal as `35c5b3a`.
- **Tests** (`upload-limits.spec.ts` + 12, now 73; `repo-guards.spec.ts` + 1): seven ways two sheets name one part (SheetJS itself shown to read the part for both), ten sheets naming one 49-column part refused in milliseconds, sheets with their own parts in any spelling still read, the sheet list read as SheetJS reads it (namespaces, quotes, escapes, UTF-8) and a different list refused; a comments part named 3, 5, 6 and 20 times (the 3 KB file refused in milliseconds) and one shared by two sheets; a 6.5 MB drawing of notes shared by ten sheets; an external link listed 60 times; chart, dialog and macro sheets refused, a chart on a worksheet read; the status line. Ten of the twelve and the status-line test failed before the fix; two check that normal files still read. Each mechanism was switched off in turn and the A1 v4 tests run: the refusal of two sheets on one part makes 2 fail, counting a part again 5, the chart-sheet refusal 2, the sheet-list check 1, the comment reads 3, the drawing reads 1, the external-link reads 1. Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Owner decisions.** Refusing chart, dialog and macro sheets is applied as a default, like the A1 review's caps: NMWC's files have none, and the upload screens read only worksheets. Question in [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner). The rest are bug fixes to the A1 caps (a part read again counts again; no new cap).
- **Still open.** Every upload is parsed on the event loop: a file near the caps still blocks every user for 5-11 s and needs about 1 GB (assessment PR 5); with these fixes that is again the most the caps allow. SheetJS has many readers and parts; these checks cover the ways found so far in which a small file makes it do large work, not every way that may exist. Checking NMWC's largest real files remains the A1 owner action. (P5 later fixed this: every upload is read in a separate process with a time limit and a heap cap; see its block, the last one.)

**A1 v5 review fix** (same branch, `audit-1-hardening`; a fifth review, of the A1 v4 head `53c58b8`: one finding, confirmed by two reviewers with their own files). No migration, no new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged; the three upload routes still answer 400 with the message). No solver change. In `lib/workbook-guard.ts` (`sheetReads`) and the tests.

- *The replay numbered the sheets SheetJS finds by number differently from SheetJS, so it counted another sheet's parts and never the last sheet's* (high). When the workbook's relationships do not name a sheet (so `safe_parse_wbrels` gives up), SheetJS reads the sheets from `xl/worksheets/sheet1.xml`, `sheet2.xml`, ... or, when `xl/worksheets/sheet.xml` holds text (its "Numbers iOS" numbering), from `sheet.xml`, `sheet1.xml`, ... It tests the part's text as it reads it (`getzipdata`, which decodes a UTF-16 byte-order mark); `sheetReads` tested the part's size. A part of only a byte-order mark (the 2 bytes FF FE; also 00 FE FF, or FF FE and one byte) has a size but no text, so the replay counted `sheet.xml`, `sheet1.xml` ... `sheet(N-1).xml` while SheetJS read `sheet1.xml` ... `sheetN.xml`: the last sheet's relationships, comments, threaded comments and drawing of notes were never counted again. A 3.5 KB upload whose one sheet names a comments part of 5,000 comments under 20 spellings passed the guard (the same file without the 2-byte part is refused, "15,000 comments or more") and blocked the app for 10-22 s while SheetJS put 100,000 comments on one cell. The `xl/workbook.xml` fallback (a `[Content_Types].xml` that names no workbook part) had the same size test; there it could only count reads SheetJS never makes. **Fixed:** both are taken from the part's text as SheetJS reads it (`sheetjsText`, which already copies SheetJS's byte-order-mark handling). The reviewers' files are refused for their comments in milliseconds; a `sheet.xml` with text still gives SheetJS's "Numbers iOS" numbering. The suggested extra check (refuse when a numbered part the replay reads is missing) was not added: it would not have caught this case, where every part the replay read existed. A test instead compares, for seven kinds of `sheet.xml` part and one and two sheets, the part SheetJS reads with the one whose comments the guard counts.
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** none for a real file. The fix changes only a workbook with an `xl/worksheets/sheet.xml` part or a `[Content_Types].xml` that names no workbook part; none of the 62 local workbooks (61 in `.dev`, the repo's fixture) has either. Crafted files of this shape are refused.
- **Tests** (`upload-limits.spec.ts` + 3, now 76), all three failing before the fix; each half of the fix, put back on its own, makes its own tests fail (the numbering 2, the workbook fallback 1). Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Owner decisions.** None: a bug fix to the A1 caps, no new cap.
- **Still open.** As after A1 v4: every upload is parsed on the event loop (assessment PR 5), and these checks cover the ways found so far in which a small file makes SheetJS do large work, not every way that may exist. (P5 later fixed this: every upload is read in a separate process with a time limit and a heap cap; see its block, the last one.)

**Audit PR A5 "Owner rules: correct locations, every order has a depot"** (branch `audit-5-owner-rules`, on main `22fe298`, which has A2 #42 and A3 #43). The owner's answers of 27 Sep 2026 (evening) to the questions A2 and A3 left open, recorded word for word in [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner). Both are standing rules the system **enforces**, not reports.

- **Depot rule** (owner, on A3's question whether to add a read-only check for orders or order files without a depot: "no, all orders must have depots linked to them"). One migration, `20260930120000_orders_always_have_depot` ([5.5](#55-database-migrations), with the owner's read-only pre-check): every order and order file without a depot gets one, best evidence first (its file; the one depot of the plans that held it; for a file, the one depot of its orders; the company's only active depot, else its only depot; the rest a per-company history-only depot `NO-DEPOT` "No depot (kept for history)"), one `DEPOT_BACKFILL` audit row per company that changed, then `Order.depotId` and `UploadBatch.depotId` are NOT NULL. It takes its locks first and retries instead of failing, so it cannot fail on real data or on a busy app (a failed migration blocks every later deploy, `P3009`); the migration test proves the lock with a write still running when the migration starts (A5 review). Nothing changes for NMWC: one depot, and every order already has it. What the app now enforces:
  - **An order file is for one depot, never a guessed one** (`intakeDepot` in `intake-server.ts`, [3.3](#33-step-1-upload-and-validate-the-order-file)): the depot chosen on Daily dispatch must be active (422 `DEPOT_NOT_ACTIVE`); without a choice (the legacy Upload orders page) the company's only active depot is used; none, or two or more, 422 `DEPOT_REQUIRED` with what to do. Before, the first active depot by code was taken silently, so a company with two depots could file orders under the wrong one.
  - **The history-only depot gets nothing new** (`Depot.historyOnly`): a file on it is refused at confirm (`batchDepotProblem`, never "reactivate the depot"); `PATCH /api/depots/:id` refuses to make it active and the truck and region routes refuse to use it (422 `DEPOT_HISTORY_ONLY`, `lib/master-data-delete.ts` words); the Depots screen shows it as **History only**, its Edit form has a note instead of the Active switch and it has no Delete button (it always has orders, so Delete could only deactivate it again); the onboarding truck picker lists active depots only (it listed every depot). The other pickers already listed active depots only (dispatch screen, Trucks, Regions), and planning, the late order, Bring forward and `POST /api/runs` already require an active depot.
  - **Every creation path passes a depot**, checked: intake confirm (the file's depot, `confirmIntake`), the late order (`depotId` required by its schema, an active depot, `late-order/route.ts`), a Bring forward copy (`carryCopyData`, the active depot on screen), the upload (`intakeDepot`); the synthetic seed (`prisma/seed-synth.ts`) did not and now does. The Prisma types make a new path without a depot a compile error.
  - **Dead null-depot handling removed, no behaviour change for orders with a depot**: the "orders without a depot when the company has one active depot" branch of `ordersInScopeWhere` (and its depot count per call), of the Bring forward window (`windowOrders`) and of the dashboard's `PLAN_ORDERS_IN_SCOPE`; the file delete's "orders without a depot block on any depot's OPTIMIZING plan"; `findSameConfirmedFile`, `batchDepotProblem`, `revalidateIntake` and `confirmIntake` take a depot, never null.
  - **What looks different after the deploy**: files that had no depot now show in the day screen's file list of the depot they were given (`day-overview.ts`) and count in its duplicate-file check (`findSameConfirmedFile`); a company with two or more active depots can no longer use the legacy Upload orders page (the answer says to use Daily dispatch); an inactive or unknown depot for an upload is 422 instead of 400.
  - **Deviations from the design** (each with its reason): step (d) uses the company's only **active** depot before its only depot, like `ordersInScopeWhere` did, so orders without a depot stay on the plan that already held them when a company has one active depot and inactive ones; the history-only depot is a column (`historyOnly`), not a code pattern, because a company may already have a real depot coded NO-DEPOT and PATCH could otherwise reactivate it at made-up coordinates; the legacy upload keeps the one-active-depot default (NMWC's Upload orders page keeps working) and refuses only when there is no depot to choose or more than one; the migration also writes audit rows, because Prisma does not print NOTICEs and there is no console access to production.
  - **Tests** (each fails without its change, checked by reverting it): web unit `orders-have-depot.spec.ts` (14), `intake-merged-rows.spec.ts` +1 (and the history-only depot in the confirm re-check), `dashboard-live-plans.spec.ts` (the scope SQL is exactly the plan's depot); fixture orders in `carry-over.spec.ts` got their depot (the planner reads only the depot's orders now); integration (library level, real PostgreSQL) `orders-have-depot-db.spec.ts` (6), `dashboard-db.spec.ts` without its depot-less order, and the fixture depots in `cross-tenant.spec.ts`, `legacy-data.spec.ts` (both run against a local web server) and `runs.spec.ts` (typechecked; it needs the solver); the migration test `tests/migrations/orders-always-have-depot.spec.ts` (7, on a throwaway database, [5.3](#53-test-suites)).
  - **Not in this part:** `GET /api/orders?depotId=` still filters by the customer's region depot, not `Order.depotId`; `POST /api/runs` counts `totalOrders` without a depot filter (legacy, display only); a screen to move orders from the history-only depot to a real one (they are history: nothing plans them).
- **Location rule** (owner, on A2's question whether a LOW-confidence reading must be moved by hand before it is saved: "its a standing rule locations should always be correct as part of sop no item will be delivered without location"). Recorded in [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner). No migration and no schema change. What the app now enforces ([3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details)):
  - **Typing or pasting a location** (L1). In ADD LOCATION a reading that is not exact (`needsPin`: every LOW reading and the MEDIUM ones - map centre, fewer than 4 decimals (since the owner decision of 28 Sep 2026 of the zeros at the end one counts, as in the import: "A5 owner decisions" below), degrees and minutes only, swapped, outside the area) or that could not be read keeps **Save location** off, with "This reading is not exact. Drop the pin on the customer's exact location, then save.", until the dispatcher clicks the map or drags the pin. Before, Save was the confirmation and stored the reading as read, verified HIGH. The customer's saved pin (the dialog opens on it) is saved again as it is only when it is exact. The customer page's **Set location** opens the same dialog; its map with typed lat / lng boxes saved through `PATCH` as verified HIGH with no check at all (not 0,0, not the area). A2's guarantees are unchanged (late answers dropped, one request at a time, Save off until the text on screen is read).
  - **The server checks every save itself** (L2, `PUT /api/customers/:id/location`). A reading is read again with the same parser and no network call (`checkManualLocation`; a short link from the `resolvedUrl` the Read returned) and refused with 422 `PIN_REQUIRED` (with the plain remedy) or 422 `LOCATION_MISMATCH`; the source and confidence stored are the parser's, a hand pin is `MAP_PIN` HIGH. `PATCH /api/customers/:id` refuses coordinates (400 `USE_SET_LOCATION`), `POST /api/customers` refuses a pair that needs a pin (422 `PIN_REQUIRED`). The audit row `CUSTOMER_LOCATION_SET` keeps working and records the confidence and the check.
  - **Bulk paths** (L3). The customer import stores no pair that needs a pin: a new customer gets no coordinates (LOCATION REQUIRED), an existing one keeps its own when the file points at the same place (else, since the A5 review, its unconfirmed saved point is marked LOW and blocks until the pin is placed), and the row is listed with the reason (`locationsNotSaved`), not as an error. A verified location is still never overwritten (F05). Order-file stubs (`intake-server.ts`, `geocodeConfidence 'MISSING'`) and late orders (`late-order/route.ts`) store no coordinates at all: nothing to change there.
  - **Planning** (L4). A location that is LOW and never confirmed is treated like an invalid one (`isUnverifiedLowLocation`, `locationBlocksDelivery` in `customer-attrs.ts`): blocking `INVALID_LOCATION` on the day screen (the card shows "Saved location is not exact (low confidence). Drop the pin on the customer's exact location."), OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN ask first (409 `LOCATION_REQUIRED`), "anyway" leaves its orders unserved with that reason. Confirmed locations and HIGH / MEDIUM imports not confirmed yet are planned as before (`LOCATION_UNVERIFIED` stays a note, by owner decision of 28 Sep 2026, [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)). Before this PR nothing stored LOW; since the A5 review the customer import marks an unconfirmed saved point LOW when the file points elsewhere, also for a customer already on a plan (handled at LOCK / LOADING / DISPATCH since the second review, next bullet).
  - **Every path that puts an order on a truck or tells a driver where to go** (L5), checked one by one. The check that holds everywhere is `buildDispatchRequest` (`plan-service.ts`, `locBad = locationBlocksDelivery(c, area)`): an order of a customer without a usable location is never a stop, it is left unserved (`MISSING_COORDINATES` / `INVALID_LOCATION`), and `startDispatchOptimize`'s `gate` answers 409 `LOCATION_REQUIRED` unless the dispatcher says "optimize anyway". Stops (`RouteAssignment`) are created in two places only, `applyScenario` (from the stored option the optimizer returned for that request, with the planned pin in `stopSnapshotJson`) and `createNextVersion` (a verbatim copy of the parent's loads and stops, snapshots included); `route-adjust.ts` only updates, and refuses dispatch plans. So OPTIMIZE, RE-PLAN with locked / loading / dispatched loads kept (the frozen loads keep the pin they were planned with; the open orders go through `buildDispatchRequest` again), same-day plans (`plan-from.ts` changes only times), Bring forward copies and late orders (new `Order` rows, planned only by OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN), split orders (split after the per-customer location check) and the driver PDF, Excel and WhatsApp text (the planned pin from the snapshot) hold without a new check. A customer's coordinates can never be cleared (a blank is "keep" in every route and in the import), so a planned stop never loses its pin, **but its customer can lose a usable location after planning** (A5 second review): the A5 review made the customer import mark an unconfirmed saved point LOW when the file points elsewhere (and a changed delivery area can put a point outside it), and neither the snapshots (they compare only the pin and the hours) nor the load status changes looked at it, so such a stop could be locked, loaded and dispatched, also after "Use instead" or from a failed re-plan's copy, while the import said "Nothing is delivered to them". Now the load status changes LOCK / LOADING / DISPATCH (`changeStatusTx`) check the customers of the load as they are now with the same predicate (`locationGate`, 409 `LOCATION_REQUIRED`, [3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)), and the day counts such customers on PLANNED loads as out of date (`outdated.locationBlocked`, RE-PLAN offered). Verified points and HIGH or MEDIUM points inside the area pass as before, so the 28 Sep demo's locked loads are not affected. The other path with no check was the legacy whole-run dispatch (`runs/[id]/dispatch`, May-2026 runs of the previous planner): it now answers 409 `LOCATION_REQUIRED`, dispatching nothing, while a customer on the run has no correct location.
  - **What looks different after the deploy:** ADD LOCATION asks for a hand pin for a reading that is not exact (and for a saved MEDIUM or LOW point, or a saved point outside the delivery area that nobody confirmed); the customer page has **Set location** instead of an editable map; an import lists "location(s) not exact" instead of storing them; `PUT { input }` answers `PIN_REQUIRED` where it answered `CONFIRM_ON_MAP`. For NMWC's customers (every location HIGH, 4 to 6 decimals) nothing changes in planning, the demo's locked loads or a re-plan.
  - **Deviations from the design** (each with its reason): (1) the saved point sent back unchanged is checked (confirmed as it is only when verified, or HIGH and inside the company's area; since the A5 review the digits of the stored number are not counted), because the dialog opens on it and saved it as a hand pin, verified HIGH, which would defeat L1 and L4; (2) a "hand pin" exactly on the point of a reading that needs a pin is refused (it was not moved); (3) `GEOCODER` is no longer accepted as a source; (4) `{ input }` alone answers `PIN_REQUIRED`, not `CONFIRM_ON_MAP` (one code; the A2 F17 test changed with it); (5) `PATCH /api/customers/:id` refuses coordinates with 400 instead of dropping them (zod would drop them silently and the page would think it saved); (6) `POST /api/customers`, not named in the design, refuses a pair that needs a pin (422) and one coordinate alone (400; it used to store the one); (7) the import keeps an **existing** customer's saved coordinates when the file's pair needs a pin (the design said leave them empty): a re-import never erases, and an Excel cell that lost its trailing zeros would otherwise turn a good point into LOCATION REQUIRED; since the A5 review it keeps them usable only when the file points at the same place (within the file's precision, or 150 m), else it marks them LOW (kept on the map as the start for the hand pin, blocked like LOCATION REQUIRED); new customers get none, and every such row is listed; (8) a check was added to the legacy run dispatch, which no new plan can reach but old runs can; (9) the day screen, `buildDispatchRequest`, that dispatch and (since the second review) `locationGate` share one predicate, `locationBlocksDelivery`.
  - **Tests** (each fails without its change, checked by reverting it, 19 reverts): web unit `location-rule.spec.ts` (19), `location-rule-routes.spec.ts` (23), `dispatch-location-dialog.spec.ts` +11, `customer-import-locations.spec.ts` +7 (and its F05 tests now use 4-decimal coordinates); integration (library level, real PostgreSQL) `location-rule-db.spec.ts` (7), `master-data-db.spec.ts` (4-decimal coordinates in the F05 race), `dispatch-screen-db.spec.ts` (F17 answers `PIN_REQUIRED`).
  - **Not in this part:** a way to move a verified point outside the area back to unverified (the pre-deploy check in [5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy) lists them); an Excel lat / lng cell in General format still loses its trailing zeros (Excel itself shows 23.585; the remedy says to format the cells as text).
- **A5 review fixes** (two independent reviewers, 28 Sep 2026; each fix has a test that failed first, and each guard was reverted once to see its test fail):
  - **The migration test could drop another database.** Its default name was `routeiq_migtest` and it accepted any `*_migtest` name, then ran `DROP DATABASE ... WITH (FORCE)`. Now the default is `routeiq_a5_migtest` and only `routeiq_a5_..._migtest` names are accepted (`tests/migrations/db-name.ts`, unit test `migration-test-db-name.spec.ts`); the documented command no longer names the database.
  - **The migration test did not prove the lock step.** It passed with the lock block deleted, because its second connection only read `Order`. Now that connection writes an order and an order file without a depot and commits only after the migration started: the deploy waits, then gives both a depot (step d). With the lock block deleted the deploy fails with P3018 (23502, `depotId` contains null values), checked.
  - **An exact saved pin ending in 0 was called "not exact".** The dialog and the server read the stored number again as text, and "23.5850" is stored as 23.585 (about 1 in 5 of NMWC's 4-decimal points). Now both use `savedPointProblem`: verified, or HIGH and inside the area. HIGH is only written for an exact reading or a hand pin since A5; the pre-A5 imports labelled HIGH with fewer than 4 decimals are listed by a read-only check in [5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy) check 13.
  - **A saved pin outside the area kept Save on, and the server refused it with the wrong reason.** The dialog judged it with no area. Now the day screen (`serviceArea` from the day overview) and the customer page (`tenantServiceArea`) give the dialog the company's area; Save stays off with "This saved location is outside the delivery area and was never confirmed. ..." (or "... has latitude and longitude swapped ..."), the server answers with the same reason, and the day card says "Drop the pin on the customer's exact location." instead of "Confirm or correct it on the map".
  - **An import could leave an old point in use when the file pointed elsewhere.** A coarse pair about 12 km from an unconfirmed saved point kept that point, HIGH, planned. Now the saved point is kept usable only when the file agrees with it (within its precision, or 150 m); otherwise it is marked LOW and the row says "The file points about 12 km from the saved location."
  - **The import screen did not say the rule, and its reasons described things that did not happen** ("they were swapped back. Please confirm on the map", "Confirm the pin" on a screen without a pin). Now the column hints give the rule, the reasons are the import's own words, and each row says what happens to the customer's location.
  - **Excel lat / lng cells lost their trailing zeros** (the Not in this part item above): a cell showing 23.5850 now counts as 4 decimals (`decimalTextColumns`).
  - **Deviations** (each with its reason): a pre-A5 "HIGH" import with fewer than 4 decimals can be confirmed as it is by a dispatcher who looks at it (the stored number cannot tell 23.58 from 23.5800; the read-only check lists them); a contradicting file marks the saved point LOW instead of emptying it (the dispatcher still sees where it was when placing the pin, and LOW blocks like LOCATION REQUIRED); the Excel fix uses the shown text only when it is the same number with more decimals (a cell showing fewer decimals than it holds keeps its own); the migration test's name rule is stricter than `routeiq_a5_<suffix>` (it must also end in `migtest`).
- **A5 second review fixes** (two independent reviewers, 28 Sep 2026; each fix has a test that failed first, and each guard was reverted once to see its test fail):
  - **A stop whose customer lost its usable location after planning could still be locked, loaded and dispatched** (medium, three reports). The A5 review made the customer import mark an unconfirmed saved point LOW when the file points elsewhere, and the import said "Nothing is delivered to them until then", but `changeStatusTx` had no location check and the day did not count it as out of date (the snapshots compare only the pin and the hours): the load went out to the point the import had just called unusable, also after "Use instead", from a failed re-plan's copy and when the load was locked before the import. Now LOCK, LOADING and DISPATCH refuse such a load (`locationGate`, 409 `LOCATION_REQUIRED` naming the customers, with what to do for the load's status, [3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)), and the day counts such customers on PLANNED loads (`outdated.locationBlocked`: step 3 says why and offers RE-PLAN, [3.5](#35-step-3-the-day-overview)). The legacy run dispatch's comment ("Daily dispatch plans never hold such a stop") and the L5 bullet above are corrected.
  - **An import row kept a saved point that is itself blocked and called it kept** (low): a file agreeing with a point an earlier import had marked LOW, or with an unconfirmed point outside the area, said "The location it already has is kept." with no warning. Now it is `SAVED_LOCATION_NOT_USABLE`: "Its saved location is not exact or is outside the delivery area, so it is not used either: its orders are not planned or sent out until someone drops the pin on the map.", with its own warning; the point is not marked again.
  - **The import's words promised more than the system did**: "Nothing is delivered to them until then" is now "Their orders are not planned or sent out until then." (what planning and `locationGate` enforce; a load already on the road is not called back).
  - **The migration could still fail on a database or role with a `statement_timeout`** (low; production's default is none): the lock step turned the timeout off inside itself, too late for its own wait (57014, P3018, then P3009). Now separate statements before it turn off `statement_timeout`, and `transaction_timeout` on PostgreSQL 17 and later ([5.5](#55-database-migrations)). The migration test runs with 1 s timeouts on its database and a writer holding on for 2.5 s; with the old step it fails with 57014, checked.
  - **The migration test failed as soon as a later migration was added** (low, not in CI): it required the new migration to be the newest folder. Now it applies the folders up to `20260930093000`, then the new one, and only requires the new one to come right after it (`tests/migrations/folders.ts`, unit tests in `migration-test-db-name.spec.ts`).
  - **Deviations** (each with its reason): the gate covers LOCKED and LOADING loads too, not only PLANNED ones (one reviewer suggested PLANNED only, to keep the demo's locked loads safe; the owner's rule is about delivery, and a locked load held a stop the import had just called unusable; verified, HIGH and MEDIUM points inside the area pass, so the demo is not affected); "Use instead" is not refused (its loads are PLANNED, so the gate stops them before they are locked, and the day asks for a RE-PLAN); the kept-but-blocked rows get a warning of their own instead of being added to the "file points elsewhere" count (the reason is different); `transaction_timeout` is also turned off (not in the report; the same failure on PostgreSQL 17 and later, and production runs 18; checked only on 16, where the statement does nothing).
  - **Tests** (each failed before its fix; the gate, the day count and the import's `geocodeConfidence` read were each removed once to see their tests fail): web unit `plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` +9 (the gate on LOCK, LOADING and DISPATCH, unlock never refused, the controls that pass, the remedy words), `day-overview-weights.spec.ts` +3 (`locationBlocked`), `dispatch-screen-reload.spec.ts` +1 (the out-of-date box and RE-PLAN on), `customer-import-locations.spec.ts` +5 (`SAVED_LOCATION_NOT_USABLE`, the new words), `migration-test-db-name.spec.ts` +3 (`migrationFoldersBefore`); integration (library level, real PostgreSQL) `location-rule-db.spec.ts` +2 (plan, import, refuse; "Use instead"; a load locked before the import); the migration test with 1 s timeouts (failed with 57014 before the fix).
  - **Not in this part:** a note on the plan view or the driver sheets for a stop whose customer lost its usable location (the day card is red, step 3 says why, and the lock answer names the customers); a way to call back a load already dispatched.

- **A5 third review fixes** (two independent reviewers, 28 Sep 2026; each fix has a test that failed first, and each guard was reverted once to see its test fail):
  - **Following the lock refusal's own advice sent the load out to the old, flagged point** (medium). The refusal and the guide said "drop the pin, then try again" for a locked or loading load. A stop keeps the pin it was planned with until a re-plan, and `locationGate` only looked at the customer as it is now: after the pin was dropped, LOADING and DISPATCH went through, and the stop, its route link and the WhatsApp text still went to the point the system had called not usable (with a "New pin - ask the dispatcher" line). A PLANNED load could be locked the same way without its RE-PLAN. Now the remedy says to unlock (a loading load goes Back to locked first) and RE-PLAN, and the gate refuses a stop still planned at a point that was replaced while it was not usable (409 `STOP_PIN_REPLACED`, [3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)). The proof is the `CUSTOMER_LOCATION_SET` audit row of the change (its "before" point, judged with `locationBlocksDelivery`), so the location route now writes the change and its row in one transaction, and the customer import writes the same row when an exact pair replaces a saved point that was not usable (the import's own remedy says "or fix the file", which led to the same stale stop).
  - **A directions link was read at its start** (medium). `/maps/dir/<start>/<place>/...`: Google puts the start in the path as coordinates when the route starts at "Your location" or a dropped pin, and the path rule took the first waypoint whose coordinates it found, as exact. ADD LOCATION showed it HIGH with Save on, and the server agreed, so the dispatcher's or salesman's own position could be saved as the customer's verified location (three waypoints: the middle one). Now only the end of the route is read, and only when it is a point with a start before it (blank or not); otherwise the link needs a pin (the map centre, MEDIUM, "This directions link does not end at a point..."), or it is not read. The data part (`!3d!4d`, `!1d!2d`) of a directions link is not read. The rule predates A5.
  - **A classic `?ll=` link (the map centre) was read as exact** (low). `ll` is the centre of the view, like `center`, `sll` and `/@lat,lng`, which already needed a pin; now it does too, with the map-centre warning (so do `center` and `sll`, which had none). The handbook listed `ll=` among the exact inputs; corrected in [3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details).
  - **The customer page and the Customers list showed a blocked saved point like a usable one** (low). Now both say so, with the day card's test and words (`locationIssue`): a red note on the customer page and **needs pin** in the list, counted at the top.
  - **The OPTIMIZE / RE-PLAN question called every blocked customer "no location ... location missing"** (low; already wrong for 0,0 and outside-area points, A5 made it apply to every LOW point). It now says "a saved location that cannot be used" for those, with both counts when mixed.
  - **Deviations** (each with its reason): a directions link whose end is a point is still read as exact (like `destination=`), not always sent to a pin (the reviewer's simpler option), because the end of the route is where the link goes and Google's own "directions to here" link is `/dir//<point>`; its destination is not read from the data part (which waypoint a `!2m2!1d!2d` block belongs to is not reliable), so such a link needs a pin; the gate refuses only a stop at a point that was not usable when it was replaced, not every stop whose pin changed after planning (that would end the owner's frozen-plan-facts default for ordinary corrections, [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner); the owner kept that default on 28 Sep 2026, "Allow, driver gets new pin"); the import's row is written after its update, outside a transaction (the import is not one transaction; a failed row stops the import with an error), unlike the location route's.
  - **Tests** (each failed before its fix; the stale-stop check, the import's row, the transaction, the parser rules, the page and list notes and the question were each removed once to see their tests fail): web unit `plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` +9 (two refusals, the remedy words, six controls), `location-rule-routes.spec.ts` +7, `dispatch-location-input.spec.ts` +5 (and the `?ll=` test now expects a pin), `location-rule.spec.ts` +3, `customer-import-locations.spec.ts` +1; integration (library level, real PostgreSQL) `location-rule-db.spec.ts` +2 (the reviewer's locked-load scenario end to end, and the second customer file), and its PLANNED-load test now expects the refusal after the pin and the new pin after RE-PLAN.
  - **Not in this part:** a directions link's destination read from its data part; a way to see on the plan view which stops are held by `STOP_PIN_REPLACED` before pressing Lock (the answer names them).

**Merge of main into audit PR A5** (same branch, `audit-5-owner-rules`; main `2a20cd7` with audit PR4 #45 and A1 #44; a merge commit, no rebase). Four files had conflicts; each keeps both sides.

- `lib/csv.ts`. A1 rewrote the upload read: the workbook guard, the caps, no parse timeout, and `cellText: false`, so SheetJS makes no display text. A5 read the lat / lng decimals a cell shows from that display text, with a second `sheet_to_json(raw: false)`; with `cellText: false` that call would format every cell of the sheet (the cost A1 v3 removed) and would no longer see the decimals. Every A1 guard, cap and refusal is kept as on main. A5's rule is kept with a narrower mechanism (`takeShownText`), used only when the customer import asks for it: an .xlsx or .xls is read with `cellNF` (each cell keeps a reference to its number format; nothing is formatted while the file is read), and a CSV sent as Excel with `cellText` (SheetJS's CSV reader keeps each cell's own text and formats nothing; not for a file whose first record SheetJS's SYLK reader takes as "ID", which formats cells as it goes). (Changed by the A5 fifth review, below: only a file whose SYLK part formats a value is read without the text; a CSV whose first header is "ID" is read with it.) Only the lat and lng number cells are formatted, with a format of at most 64 characters (`MAX_SHOWN_FORMAT`: a coordinate format takes 1 to 1.5 microseconds a cell, a 64-character one up to about 5, a 255-character one up to about 40). (Corrected by the A5 fourth review, below: a 64-character format SheetJS cannot apply took about 50 microseconds a cell, and every sheet and every column folding into "lat" was formatted; now each format is tried once per upload, on the sheet that is read.) Then every cell's format and text are taken off again, so the rows read exactly as on main. A cell with a longer format counts its number's own decimals: a location that then has too few is not saved, and the pin is placed by hand.
- `lib/dispatch/plan-service.ts`. `ordersInScopeWhere` keeps A4's client parameter (the plan detail reads in one snapshot) and A5's body (every order has a depot, so there is no depot count); the parameter is now unused (`_db`).
- `tests/lib/api-role-matrix.spec.ts`: both notes, A4's two routes first. This handbook: the 5.3 counts paragraph (both sides' sentences, then this merge's); the 5.3 integration rows (A5's `master-data-db` row and A4's new `recovery-races-db` row); [5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy) check 12 is A1's and A5's becomes check 13 (its two references updated); A5's 7.4 blocks moved after main's.
- **Also changed with the merge** (no conflict, but no longer true or complete): the counts main's repo guards check (20 migrations; 80 unit and 29 integration spec files; `location-rule-db` and `orders-have-depot-db` named with the specs that use no web server); the audit status line at the top (A4 and A5 named, A1's blocks no longer the last ones); the notes that say number formats are never applied (the `csv.ts` row of 2.2, the SheetJS rule in 7.3, `docs/SECURITY.md` section 9).
- **Tests** (`customer-import-locations.spec.ts` +3, each failing with its part of `takeShownText` removed: the CSV text, the 64-character cap, taking the formats off again): a CSV sent as Excel keeps "23.5850"; only the lat / lng cells are formatted, and a longer format is not applied; a date format on another column is not applied, so the rows read as without the lat / lng columns.

**A5 fourth review fixes** (same branch, `audit-5-owner-rules`, on the merge `a89e4cf`; a fourth review with fresh eyes: five findings, each confirmed by two reviewers with their own files; each fix has a test that failed first). No new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged), no schema change, no solver change; the migration gains one statement.

- *A customer import within every A1 cap could block the web process for 40 to 108 s* (high). The merge's `takeShownText` formatted every lat / lng number cell with `XLSX.SSF.format`, which parses the format again for every cell: a 64-character format SheetJS cannot apply ("#," 32 times, within `MAX_SHOWN_FORMAT`) took about 50 microseconds a cell, where the merge had measured 1 to 5 with formats that work. It did so on every sheet, also the ones `pickSheet` then does not read, and on every column whose header is "lat" or "lng" in any letter case or with spaces after it, which `normalizeKeys` then folds into one. Ten sheets of 50,000 rows (83 KB) took 40 s through `POST /api/customers/import` against 5.7 s on the orders path; 44 such columns (222 KB) 108 s. Any planner could send it (Validate only too), and meanwhile the app answered nothing, `/api/health` included. **Fixed** (`lib/csv.ts`): nothing is formatted while the file is read. `readWorkbook` keeps, for each sheet, a reference to the number format (or a CSV cell's text) of the decimal columns' number cells (`shownSources`), only in the LAST column of each name (the one the rows keep); `parseUpload` works out what they show on the sheet it reads only (`showDecimals`, after the row limit). Each number format is tried once per upload, on the number 1 (-1 for a negative number; `ShownFormats`, `zerosShown`): a format that shows "1.0000" shows 4 decimals; a date, a percentage, text around the number, a scale, a condition or a format SheetJS cannot apply adds nothing (the number's own decimals count). At most 20 formats are tried in one upload (`MAX_SHOWN_FORMATS`), each at most 64 characters. `parseExcelSheets` no longer takes the decimal columns. Measured on the maintainer's machine (files built with the repo's zip fixtures, 50,000 rows, with the decimal columns against without): one sheet of lat, lng in the format SheetJS cannot apply 0.78 s against 0.60 s (3.1 s before); ten such sheets 6.9 s against 5.7 s (43.6 s before, with 1,000,000 calls of `SSF.format`; now one); 44 "lat" / "LAT" columns, 30,000 rows, 7.6 s against 7.0 s. What is left per cell (a reference kept for the two kept columns of every sheet, and every cell's format taken off again) is about a microsecond, the same whatever the format.
- *An ordinary correction of a point confirmed where it was refused the load as "planned at a point that was not usable"* (medium). `stopsAtReplacedPoints` refused a stop when ANY `CUSTOMER_LOCATION_SET` row of its customer had a blocked "before" point at the planned pin, whether or not that change moved the customer off it. ADD LOCATION writes such a row when a dispatcher confirms a LOW point by typing its own coordinates (read as exact, saved verified HIGH at the same point, as the dialog allows): the stop was then planned at a confirmed point, and a hand correction about 1 km away made LOCK answer 409 `STOP_PIN_REPLACED` ("its old point, which was not usable"), a locked load being sent through unlock and RE-PLAN; also for a usable point planned, flagged by a customer file, confirmed where it was, then corrected, and for a flagged point replaced, set back by hand, then corrected. **Fixed:** only the change that replaced the planned point counts. A row whose "after" is the same point (a confirmation in place) is skipped, and of the rows that moved the customer off the planned point the newest decides; rows of the same millisecond count together (refused when one of them blocked). Such a correction now gets the owner default (frozen plan facts, the "New pin" note); the K5 and K6 refusals stand. A move off a usable point by a customer import writes no row, so the newest recorded row stands for it: that can only refuse more, never less.
- *The import screen's advice made rough locations exact* (medium). The lat hint and `DISPATCHER_GUIDE.md` said "format the cell as text or to show 4 decimals". A number holds no trailing zeros, so 23.58 in a cell formatted 0.0000 shows 23.5800, and the import took the shown text as 4 decimals: stored HIGH from the import and planned with only the "not confirmed" note, against the owner's rule. **Fixed:** a number format adds one zero at most (23.585 shown as 23.5850 still counts 4 decimals, as before; 23.58 shown as 23.5800 counts 3, 23.6 shown as 23.6000 counts 2: not saved, listed as "Fewer than 4 decimals."). The page hint, the import's warning, its result box and the guide now say to type or paste each coordinate with all the decimals it really has and, if Excel drops a trailing zero, to format the column as Text before typing or pasting. A CSV, also one sent as Excel, is read as the text in the file, as before (the two ways a browser sends a .csv give the same result). (Corrected by the A5 fifth review, below: the one-zero rule held for number formats only, so the CSV Excel saves from those same cells, a typed CSV and a Text cell read "23.5800" as 4 decimals; and a semicolon CSV whose first header is "ID" lost its trailing zeros only when sent as Excel.)
- *ADD LOCATION's notes said "Confirm the pin" or "move the pin if needed" while Save stayed off until a pin placed by hand* (low). The parser's warnings (fewer than 4 decimals, the map-centre and `?ll=` links, swapped, outside the area, degrees and minutes only) were older than the owner's rule. **Fixed:** they say only what is wrong with the reading ("Coordinates have fewer than 4 decimals (accurate to ~100 m or worse).", "This link only gives the map centre, not a pin.", "Latitude and longitude looked swapped; they were swapped back.", ...); the dialog's red note says what to do. The import's first location warning says "will not be saved" on Validate only or when the file has errors, "were not saved" otherwise, like the warning after it; the result box too.
- *The migration failed (P3018, then P3009) when the database's or the role's default isolation is REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE and the old app changed an order without a depot while the migration waited for its locks* (low: production uses READ COMMITTED, and NMWC has no order without a depot). Its first query took the transaction's one snapshot before the lock step, so the backfill failed on the changed order (40001, "could not serialize access due to concurrent update"), or did not see a new one that SET NOT NULL then found (23502). **Fixed:** `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED;` is the migration's first statement ([5.5](#55-database-migrations)); the header's "a busy app delays the migration; it never makes it fail" now holds for the isolation setting too. The migration test sets REPEATABLE READ on its database and its writer also changes an order without a depot: before the fix it failed with P3018 and 40001, checked.
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** a rough lat / lng pair formatted in Excel to show more decimals is listed as not exact (the pin is placed by hand); a load whose stop is at a point confirmed where it was and later corrected locks, with the "New pin" note; ADD LOCATION's notes and the import's words. NMWC's customer file (4 to 6 decimals) reads as before.
- **Deviations** (each with its reason): what a format shows is taken from SheetJS itself, once per format on the number 1, not from a pattern for fixed-decimal formats (the reviewers' first suggestion), so "#,##0.0000", "0.0000_)", a "[Red]" section and quoted empty text read as before; a format with a condition ("[>=100]") is not read, since it shows numbers differently by size. Of the reviewers' two options for the rough pairs, "one zero at most" was taken rather than "only when the number already has 3 decimals": both give the same verdict at 4 decimals, and with it a 3-decimal number formatted with 6 decimals reads 23.5850, not 23.585000, so the file's own precision (the "file agrees with the saved point" check) is not overstated. The references of the sheets that are not read are still kept until the sheet is picked (about 0.25 s for 500,000 cells, measured), rather than reading the picked sheet a second time (which would read its parts twice, against A1's caps). A workbook where two columns fold into one name (lat and "LAT ") is not refused: the rows keep the last one, as they always did; refusing it would be a new rule for every upload, left for the owner.
- **Tests** (each was run before its fix and failed; the old gate was also put back once to see the new integration test fail): web unit `customer-import-locations.spec.ts` +5 (each format tried once, also one SheetJS cannot apply; only the sheet that is read and the kept column; at most 20 formats; rough pairs formatted to show 4 or 6 decimals stay not exact; the warning's tense and remedy) and the new page hint, `dispatch-location-input.spec.ts` +7 (no reading that needs a pin asks to confirm it), `dispatch-location-dialog.spec.ts` +1 (the dialog tests use the parser's real answer and check that nothing asks to confirm), `plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` +4 (two controls that lock: confirmed in place then corrected, set back then corrected; two refusals that stand); integration (library level, real PostgreSQL) `location-rule-db.spec.ts` +1 (the reviewers' scenarios A and B end to end; 409 `STOP_PIN_REPLACED` with the old gate); the migration test (REPEATABLE READ and the writer's update; 40001 before the fix). Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Not in this part:** parsing uploads in a worker (assessment PR 5; a file near the caps still blocks the app for seconds); refusing a workbook whose columns fold into one name. (P5 later did the first: see its block.)

**A5 fifth review fixes** (same branch, `audit-5-owner-rules`, on `d61cb5d`; seven findings, each confirmed by two reviewers with their own files; each fix has a test that failed first, and each part of each fix was put back once to see its tests fail). No new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged), no solver change; the A5 migration gains a CHECK constraint (the Prisma schema cannot express one; no drift).

- *A rough location padded to 4 decimals still got in as exact when the sheet was saved as CSV* (medium). The fourth review's one-zero rule was in `lib/csv.ts`, for number formats only. Excel, like SheetJS, writes each CSV cell as it shows it, so a sheet with 23.58 / 58.41 formatted 0.0000 was refused as .xlsx ("Fewer than 4 decimals.") and stored as exact (HIGH, from the import) from the CSV Excel saves from it, sent as text/csv or as Excel; a typed CSV and an Excel Text cell holding "23.5800" too. The customer was then planned and loaded at a point good to about 1 km, against the guide and [3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details). **Fixed:** the customer import counts one zero at the end of a coordinate's decimals, whatever the file (`countedText` in `lib/dispatch/import-location.ts`, used by `readImportedPair` for the precision, the reason and the file's own tolerance): 23.5850 is 4 decimals, 23.5800 and 23.580000 are 3. `lib/csv.ts` now gives a number cell the whole text its format shows (23.58 in 0.0000 is "23.5800", no longer "23.580"), so the rule is in one place and every file gets the same verdict and the same reason: "Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts)." when the zeros made the difference, "Fewer than 4 decimals." otherwise. The guide says so.
- *A semicolon CSV whose first header is "ID" lost its trailing zeros only when the browser sent it as Excel* (low). SheetJS reads a file that begins with "ID" with its SYLK reader first, and the merge read every such file without its cells' text, since that reader formats values as it goes. Sent as Excel (Chrome and Edge on a PC with Excel), "ID;...;23.5850;58.4150" read 23.585 and the location was not saved; sent as text/csv it was. **Fixed:** the SYLK reader formats a value only after a format record gave it a number format, and the guard's SYLK replay now says whether it does (`idFileFormatsCells` in `lib/workbook-guard.ts`, the same walk as `idFileReader`, up to the same record or field); only such a file, never a CSV, is read without the text. With the text SheetJS throws a TypeError on a format it cannot apply in that pass ("Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'indexOf')"), which is why the exclusion stays for those files. Measured on the maintainer's machine: a semicolon "ID" CSV of 50,000 rows x 15 columns sent as Excel 2.6-3.1 s without the decimal columns and 2.9-3.1 s with them, the same as a comma CSV of those rows; the most formatting the SYLK budget allows (7,000 formatted values) 0.33-0.36 s either way, since it is not done.
- *An ordinary correction of a pin that confirmed a flagged point a few metres away was refused* (medium). A pin dropped exactly on a flagged saved point is refused (422 `PIN_REQUIRED`), so a hand pin confirms it a few metres off; the gate counted that pin as the same place and the load locked. `stopsAtReplacedPoints` matched a change's "before" to the planned point within 5 cm, so the later correction of that confirmed pin (its "before" 6 m from the planned point) was never counted, the older flagged row decided, and LOADING was refused with 409 `STOP_PIN_REPLACED` ("its old point, which was not usable"), a locked load being sent through unlock and RE-PLAN, against the owner default for ordinary corrections. **Fixed:** the planned point's place is `PIN_MOVED_M` (50 m) around it, the distance at which the gate and the sheets' "New pin" note count a pin as the same place: a change counts when it started in that place and ended outside it; one that ended in it too (a confirmation where it is, typed or by a hand pin within 50 m) moved nothing ([3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)). A flagged point replaced by a pin more than 50 m away is still refused, whatever came after.
- *The fourth review's "confirmed where it was" skip was reached by no test* (low): removing it left every test green, since a newer row always decided. It is now the "ended in it too" rule above, and two tests fail without it (a confirmation and its correction recorded in the same millisecond, which count together).
- *The customer import wrote its location-change row outside the change, and judged it on the customer as read at the start of the request* (low). A restart or a failed insert between the two calls left the customer at the new pair with no row (importing the same file again did not write it either), and a point another customer file marked LOW after the import's read was replaced with no row; either way LOCK, LOADING and DISPATCH passed a stop still planned at the flagged point. **Fixed:** a row that needs the location-change row, or whose customer changed since the import read it, is written in one transaction with that row, the customer locked (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) and judged as it is then; every other row is still one statement, now conditional on the customer being as read (not confirmed, same point, same confidence), so "no row needed" holds for the row as it is when written. `PUT /api/customers/:id/location` records its "before" point from the locked row too (it used its read at the start of the request).
- *The import told the dispatcher to set on the map locations the customer keeps as usable* (low). Rows listed with `kept` = `SAVED_LOCATION` (a confirmed point, or a file that points at the same place, as when Excel drops a trailing zero from a list saved again) were counted into "N location(s) ... were not saved. Set them on the map ..." and the result box's "No item is delivered without a correct location", though those customers are planned and sent out as before. **Fixed:** the warning and the box (`locationsNotSavedSummary` in `import-form.tsx`) ask for a pin only for the rows whose customer has no usable location after the import; the others get their own line: "N location(s) in the file are not exact, but each of these customers keeps the location it already has, which is used as before: nothing to do."
- *While the previous version was still serving, its Depots screen could switch the new history-only depot on* (low). The pre-deploy migration commits while the previous version keeps serving, and its depot PATCH has no history-only check; switched on, the new app offered NO-DEPOT on the day screen (first, so as the default depot), for late orders and new plans, and had no screen to switch it off. **Fixed** in the database: the A5 migration adds the CHECK `Depot_historyOnly_not_active`, so no version of the app can make a history-only depot active ([5.5](#55-database-migrations)).
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** a coordinate with two or more zeros at the end (23.5800) is not exact in any customer file, with the reason saying why (about 1 in 100 real 4-decimal values per axis; its pin is placed by hand, or the file gives more decimals); a semicolon "ID" CSV reads the same whichever way the browser sends it; a load whose flagged stop was confirmed by a hand pin a few metres off, then corrected, goes on with the "New pin" note; the import's words for rows that keep a usable location; the previous version's Depots screen gets an error when switching the history-only depot on during a deploy.
- **Deviations** (each with its reason): the one-zero rule covers Excel Text cells and typed CSVs too, not only CSVs saved from formatted cells: a file cannot tell padding from typed zeros, and a workbook and the CSV saved from it must give one verdict; ADD LOCATION and `POST /api/customers` still read a typed "23.5800" as 4 decimals (one point typed by a dispatcher; left for the owner, [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner); the owner decided "Same rule everywhere" on 28 Sep 2026, done in "A5 owner decisions" below). The "ID" CSV is read with its text (the reviewers' first option), but files whose SYLK part formats a value keep the exclusion, decided by the guard's replay before the read rather than by reading again after SheetJS throws. The import's locked transaction is used only for the rows that need it: one per row of a master list of thousands would add several round trips to each. The depot finding is fixed by a CHECK in the not yet deployed A5 migration, not by adding `historyOnly: false` next to every `active: true` depot read (seven places, and every new one) or a screen to switch the depot off: the state can no longer happen. The location route re-reads its "before" under the lock but still judges a saved point sent back unchanged on its read at the start of the request.
- **Tests** (each was run before its fix and failed; each part of each fix was put back once to see its tests fail): web unit `customer-import-locations.spec.ts` +9, `plan-lifecycle.spec.ts` +8, `upload-limits.spec.ts` +3, `location-rule-routes.spec.ts` +1; integration (library level, real PostgreSQL) `location-rule-db.spec.ts` +1 (the reviewers' scenario end to end; 409 `STOP_PIN_REPLACED` with the old gate); the migration test +1 (the old migration let the history-only depot be switched on). Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Not in this part:** counting one zero in ADD LOCATION and the new-customer API ([7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner); done after the owner's decision, "A5 owner decisions" below); re-judging a saved point sent back unchanged under the location route's lock (a race of milliseconds); the HTTP integration suites other than `customers-import` and `cross-tenant` (not re-run; no solver in this environment).

**A5 owner decisions of 28 Sep 2026** (same branch, `audit-5-owner-rules`, on `3ec43ee`). The owner answered the two questions the A5 reviews had left open, recorded in [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner). No new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged), no schema or migration change, no solver change.

- *(b) "Same rule everywhere": a pair a dispatcher types or pastes counts one zero at the end, like the customer import.* Before, ADD LOCATION's Read (`POST /api/locations/parse`), the server's check on Save (`PUT /api/customers/:id/location`) and `POST /api/customers` counted every zero, so "23.5800, 58.4100" (a rough 23.58 copied from a cell formatted to show 4 decimals) read as exact, HIGH, and was saved as a confirmed location, while the same pair in a customer file was refused. **Done:** `countedText` moved from `import-location.ts` to `location-input.ts`, so there is one rule and one implementation: `parseLocationInput` counts each written coordinate with it (`pairPrecision`) and the import calls the same function (`readImportedPair`, whose reason for this case is the parser's warning, `FEW_DECIMALS_ZEROS_WARNING`). When the zeros made the difference, the reading's warning is "Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts)." and the dialog's note and the server's 422 say "Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts). Drop the pin on the customer's exact location." (`notExactMessage`, used by `pinRequiredMessage`; the dialog's preview does not repeat the reason while the note shows it; a "hand pin" exactly on such a pair gets the same words). `POST /api/customers` refuses such a pair with "This location is not exact. Fewer than 4 decimals (only one zero at the end counts). Create the customer without coordinates, then set its location on the map (Set location on the customer page)." "23.5850, 58.4105", "23.58500, 58.41050" and 6-decimal pairs (also "23.585900, 58.405900", 5 counted) are exact as before.
- *Links: the pair written in a link counts the same way; Google's own pin keeps its digits.* The decision asked whether machine-made Google Maps links keep their own digits. Padding can reach the pair a link carries as text (`geo:`, `q=`, `query=`, `destination=`, `daddr=`, `/search/`, `/place/<pair>`, the end of a `/dir/` link): `/search/` and `q=` carry the text searched for, as it was typed, so a padded pair typed into a search comes back in the link with its zeros; and other programs build such links and `geo:` URIs with a fixed number of decimals (Java and Android `String.format("%f")` always prints 6, JavaScript `toFixed(6)` too, a spreadsheet `TEXT(A2,"0.000000")`), so a rough 23.58 comes out "23.580000", 6 decimals, read as exact before. A link cannot say which of those wrote it, so all of them count one zero at the end, like typed text. Google's own pin (`!3d...!4d...`) is the marker's position as Google writes it, not text anyone typed or formatted: its digits are not counted, as before. (Corrected in the A5 sixth review, below: Google writes the pin, with no zeros at the end, but it is not always a marker Google placed. Google repeats a coordinate searched for as the pin, so the pin now needs 4 decimals, counted as written.) A map centre needs a pin whatever its digits. RouteIQ's own driver links (`driver-links.ts`, `coordText`) print the number without padding, so a link copied from a driver sheet reads as before.
- *(c) "Allow, driver gets new pin": a pin corrected by more than 50 m after planning does not block the load.* No code change: this is the behaviour since PR4's frozen plan facts, kept and now recorded as the owner's decision ([3.4](#34-step-2-customer-locations-and-details), [3.8](#38-step-6-load-states)). The load can be locked, loaded and dispatched; the WhatsApp text and the driver sheet show the planned stop with "New pin - ask the dispatcher which one to use" and a link to the new pin. Only a stop still planned at a point that was not usable when a new pin more than 50 m away replaced it is refused (409 `STOP_PIN_REPLACED`, A5 third to fifth reviews).
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** ADD LOCATION asks for a hand pin for "23.5800, 58.4100" (or "23.580000, 58.410000", or a link carrying such a pair as text), with the reason; Save location stays off until the pin is placed, and the server refuses the reading if it is sent anyway. `POST /api/customers` (no screen uses it) refuses such a pair with the reason. Nothing changes for pairs with 4 or more decimals as counted, for Google's own pins (until the A5 sixth review, below), for saved points, or for planning and loads.
- **Tests** (each was run before the change and failed; each part of the change was put back once to see its tests fail: the typed pair, the query parameters, the `geo:` URI, the path pair, the end of a directions link, the message, the dialog's note and preview, the hand-pin message, the import's use of the shared check): web unit `dispatch-location-input.spec.ts` +25 (padded pairs typed, pasted and in each kind of link; controls that still read exact, including 6-decimal pairs and Google's own pin; the import and the parser give the same verdict and words), `location-rule-routes.spec.ts` +15 (the Read route, the Save route however the pair is sent, `POST /api/customers`, with controls), `dispatch-location-dialog.spec.ts` +6 (the note, the preview, the hand pin, with controls). Tests changed, and why: `location-rule-routes.spec.ts` "the text as sent is read: "23.5800" has 4 decimals; stored HIGH" expected `POST /api/customers` to create "23.5800", "58.4000" as an exact location; it is now three refusals with the reason, plus four controls. `dispatch-location-dialog.spec.ts` "text changed while reading" typed "23.6100, 58.4100" and had the fake server answer it as exact, which the server no longer does; it types "23.6150, 58.4150" (one zero at the end, exact). Comments and a label only (the saved-point rule is unchanged, since the stored number cannot say how it was written): the `ZERO` fixture in `location-rule-routes.spec.ts`, a case label in `location-rule.spec.ts` and a comment in `location-rule-db.spec.ts` said "23.5850, 58.4000" or "23.5800, 58.4000" was read as exact. Counts: [5.3](#53-test-suites).
- **Not in this part:** customers saved from a padded pair before this change (confirmed by a dispatcher, or HIGH imports from before the A5 fifth review) are not judged again: a saved point is judged by what is stored (`savedPointProblem`), and a confirmed point is never judged again. (Corrected in the A5 sixth review: this said the stored number cannot say how many decimals it was written with. That holds for imports, whose text is not kept, but ADD LOCATION keeps the text a location was read from in `Customer.locationInput`, so the customers a dispatcher confirmed from a padded pair can be found; the owner now has a read-only count and the question, below and in [7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner).) Not checked: whether Google writes its own pin with fewer than 4 decimals when someone searches a rough coordinate in Google Maps and shares the place (for example `!3d23.58!4d58.41`); the pin's digits are not counted, before or after this change. (Answered in the A5 sixth review: it does.)

**A5 sixth review fixes** (same branch, `audit-5-owner-rules`, on `629f2f7`). Three findings, each confirmed by two skeptics; the first two are the same defect seen from two sides. No new or changed API route (the role matrix is unchanged), no schema or migration change, no solver change; one new owner-run read-only script.

- *A rough coordinate searched in Google Maps and shared was read as exact* (medium, two findings). Google Maps repeats a coordinate searched for. "23.58, 58.41" (or "23.5800, 58.4100") typed into Google Maps opens as `https://www.google.com/maps/place/23%C2%B034'48.0%22N+58%C2%B024'36.0%22E/@23.58,58.41,17z/data=!3m1!4b1!4m4!3m3!8m2!3d23.58!4d58.41` (seen live by a skeptic on 28 Sep 2026; Google drops the zeros at the end), and Google shows the point as 23°34'48.0"N 58°24'36.0"E. Google's pin (`!3d...!4d...`) was read with no decimals check (`!3d23.5!4d58.4` too), and degrees, minutes and seconds were always exact. So that link, the short link Google shares for it, the same link without its data part and the DMS text all read HIGH with no hand pin. The Save route's check agreed and stored a point good to about 1 km as confirmed, while the same pair typed was refused. Main read the pin and the DMS the same way; the owner's rule and "Same rule everywhere" make it a defect. **Fixed** in `location-input.ts`: (1) the pin needs 4 decimals, counted as Google wrote them (`precision` in `parseGoogleMapsUrl`), with the warning "Coordinates have fewer than 4 decimals (accurate to ~100 m or worse)."; (2) degrees, minutes and seconds whose two coordinates are both within 0.05" of a value with 3 decimals or fewer (`dmsIsRoughPair`, in `dmsBase`) read MEDIUM with `needsPin` and "These degrees, minutes and seconds are 23.58, 58.41 written another way: fewer than 4 decimals (accurate to ~100 m or worse).", typed or in a link (`?q=`, `/place/<DMS>`, the end of a directions link). Save location stays off until the pin is placed by hand, and the server refuses the reading however it is sent (the Read, `{ input }`, `{ lat, lng, input }`, a short link with its `resolvedUrl`, a "hand pin" exactly on it).
- *Decision (b) and the pin's digits.* The owner decided that machine-made Google Maps links keep their own digits unless padding can reach them. Padding does not reach Google's pin: Google writes the shortest number (a searched "23.5800, 58.4100" comes back as `!3d23.58!4d58.41`), so the pin's digits are counted as written, not cut to one zero at the end. For every pin Google writes, the two counts are the same. What reaches the pin is the rough value itself, and the 4-decimal check catches it. The price: a coordinate search whose 4th decimal is 0 ("23.5850, 58.4105") comes back from Google with 3 decimals and needs its pin placed by hand (typed, it is exact). A real place marker has 6 or 7 decimals, so this happens to a real marker about once in 10,000 per coordinate.
- *Degrees, minutes and seconds: both coordinates, within 0.05".* A value with 3 decimals is always a whole number of tenths of a second (0.001° is 3.6"), so Google's DMS text of a rough pair matches it exactly. A real point written to a tenth of a second lands on such a value once in 36 per coordinate, and on both about once in 1,300 (whole seconds: once in 324); its pin is then placed by hand. Whole degrees written with seconds (23°00'00"N 58°00'00.0"E) now need a pin; they read HIGH before.
- *The handbook said the customers saved from a padded pair cannot be found* (low). The owner decisions' "Not in this part" said the stored number cannot say how it was written, so such customers are not judged again. ADD LOCATION keeps the text a location was read from (`Customer.locationInput`; main did too), so the customers a dispatcher confirmed from "23.5800, 58.4100" (before the owner decisions), from a rough Google pin or DMS text (before this review), or from any reading the screen before A5 let through (a map centre, fewer than 4 decimals) can be found. **Corrected** (the sentence above), and the owner gets a read-only count for the check before the deploy ([5.12](#512-how-to-verify-a-deploy) step 13): `apps/web/prisma/check-saved-readings.ts` reads the saved text of every customer confirmed from a pasted reading (`MANUAL_LATLNG`, `GOOGLE_MAPS_URL`) with the parser of the commit it runs on and the company's delivery area, and prints per company how many read as not exact today, by reason, and how many short links it cannot read again (the address a short link led to is not kept). It writes nothing and names no customer. Whether to flag them is the owner's decision ([7.5](#75-open-questions-for-the-owner)); nothing changes for them meanwhile. The reason stands for imports, whose text is not kept. (Corrected in the A5 seventh review, below: the count judged the saved text without looking at the saved point, so customers placed on the map on main's customer page, whose text is empty or older, were counted as not exact pasted readings.)
- **Behaviour changes a dispatcher can see:** ADD LOCATION asks for a hand pin, with the reason, for a Google Maps link (or its short link) whose pin has fewer than 4 decimals, and for degrees, minutes and seconds of a pair with fewer than 4 decimals; the server refuses them if sent anyway. A link to a real place and the degrees, minutes and seconds of a real point read as before. Nothing changes for saved points, imports, planning or loads.
- **Deviations** (each with its reason): the pin's digits are counted as written, not cut to one zero at the end (the second finding's option; the first said cutting is harmless, which is true for every pin Google writes; counting as written is decision (b)); the DMS check needs both coordinates (the second finding's rule: with either one, about 1 real point in 18 would need a hand pin), so a search with one rough coordinate ("23.58, 58.4059") still reads exact as Google's DMS text, though its link is caught (its pin has 2 decimals); the owner's count is a script with the parser, not SQL, because it must read links and DMS text; it runs like `check-priority-drops.ts`.
